historical_completion_claim_quarantined: >-
  Sections 60--75 develop the recovered Sobolev idea on the one continuing VPI
  field. Every normalized row has the same kinetic-energy mass and reconstructs
  the same critical height; signed second descent cancels every completed scale
  and time face before positivity. The first heat current is exactly the motion
  of one complete Gaussian material-pair layer. Diffuse octave breadth has a
  datum-finite occupancy bound, while concentrated layer growth has an exact
  deformation--loss charge. At one fixed heat depth the derivative rows form an
  exact Poisson partition of the same kinetic energy, so the terminal ultraviolet
  diagonal becomes the sole depth boundary m=infinity. The adjacent source--loss
  square is exact and its H-normalized unweighted form is datum-finite. The
  subsequent signed depth and helical descents retain that normalized
  remainder after common faces cancel; taking positive atom squares earlier
  restores the uncontrolled critical tangent and is not used. The exact nonlinear tangent
  is now centered simultaneously against the energy and helicity invariant
  directions, proving that critical strengthening requires a genuine
  scale--handedness defect before it enters the fixed-depth packing. Centering
  the depth source against its simultaneous loss distribution now proves a
  datum-finite bound for all narrow-depth positive logarithmic growth; any
  surviving record remainder is forced into a genuine multi-depth interaction.
  Helicity conservation then proves that its ultraviolet source enters both
  helical sectors equally up to a datum-paid lower-order error; the one-triad
  determinant identifies the surviving interaction as within-helicity radial
  separation inside a heterochiral triad.  The same fact is now expressed
  without per-triad selection by the spectral measure of curl: the complete
  nonlinear source has zero mass and zero signed-frequency barycenter, hence
  is the second derivative of one signed curvature current.  Critical
  production is exactly twice that current at the helicity origin.  The
  source action already paid by the energy law controls every positive-log
  episode whose curvature remains in a fixed signed-frequency radius.
  Therefore any unbounded record tail must carry the same positive curvature
  through arbitrarily high frequencies on both helical sides; bounded-frequency
  rebuilding, handedness switching, and a one-sided ultraviolet source have
  all been removed from the remaining packing.  Capped absolute-frequency
  tests then identify the two-sided ultraviolet term as one exact boundary
  current.  A second q-scale difference converts its nested tail layers into
  positive compact two-octave stocks.  After division by their physical
  radius these stocks, their losses, and their source actions sum exactly to
  the kinetic-energy mass, kinetic-energy dissipation, and the already paid
  normalized source action.  Summing the descended chain to its true
  ultraviolet boundary now recovers the actual cumulative fine-side flux and
  proves a datum-finite Copson action for that flux.  Completing the local
  source--loss square identifies its unique fast equality direction and proves
  that every such receiving direction has a simultaneous compensating trough
  in the same all-field source.  The radial distance joining that trough to its
  receiver is returned exactly to the material-pair commutator in (TL.856).
  Sections 83--86 place its longitudinal and transverse parts in one completed
  tangent and identify the remaining critical burden as the first-receiving
  positive boundary of the original Sobolev stock.  Sections 87--88 then use
  one fixed higher normalized Sobolev row to construct the common critical
  moving-face kernel.  Its critically weighted operator number is strictly
  smaller than its positive stock normalization.  The connection loss is
  summed on the whole translated first-receiving complex before localization,
  so one convolution tail is never reused.  Section 89 applies both the exact
  receiver law and the matched-kernel law to that same joined measure; the
  maximum of their pointwise minimum is a strict q-ladder coefficient.  Initial
  row and connection stocks are
  paid by H(0), every finite shared face cancels, the coarse face vanishes, and
  the simultaneous ultraviolet/time face remains on the favorable side of the
  finite recurrence.  Section 90 inserts that datum-finite first-receiving sum
  into the exact critical trace, proving the uniform H^{1/2} height bound
  (TL.918)--(TL.919) and the endpoint L3 continuation relay for the same
  fixed-viscosity solution.

# The exact VPI moving-band throughput--lifetime law

## 1. One continuing fluid and one spectral readout

Let \(u\) be one smooth, decaying solution of

\[
\partial_tu+\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)=\nu\Delta u,
\qquad \nabla\cdot u=0,
\tag{TL.1}
\]

on a time interval \([0,T]\).  The Leray-projected quadratic term in (TL.1)
contains the simultaneous transport, incompressibility and pressure response of
that same velocity field.  The radial Fourier variables below are readouts of
this evolution; they are not independently evolving shells or sources.

Write

\[
\mathcal E(t):=\frac12\|u(t)\|_2^2,
\qquad
\mathcal E_0:=\mathcal E(0),
\qquad
\mathcal D(t):=\|\Lambda^{3/2}u(t)\|_2^2.
\tag{TL.1a}
\]

Use a unitary Fourier convention and define the kinetic-energy density on the
frequency sphere of radius \(k\) by

\[
e(k,t):=\frac12\int_{|\xi|=k}|\widehat u(\xi,t)|^2\,d\sigma(\xi).
\tag{TL.2}
\]

Let \(B(u,u)=\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)\), and define its signed energy-transfer
density and the forward flux through \(k\) by

\[
T(k,t):=-\operatorname{Re}\int_{|\xi|=k}
\widehat{B(u,u)}(\xi,t)\cdot\overline{\widehat u(\xi,t)}\,d\sigma(\xi),
\tag{TL.3}
\]

\[
\Pi(k,t):=\int_k^\infty T(q,t)\,dq.
\tag{TL.4}
\]

Because the complete quadratic term does no net kinetic-energy work,

\[
\int_0^\infty T(k,t)\,dk=0.
\tag{TL.5}
\]

Thus positive \(\Pi(k,t)\) means net transfer by the complete current from
frequencies below \(k\) to frequencies above \(k\).  No positive part is taken.

## 2. Local conservation in scale and time

Taking the Fourier energy balance in (TL.1) gives

\[
\partial_te(k,t)=T(k,t)-2\nu k^2e(k,t).
\tag{TL.6}
\]

Since \(\partial_k\Pi=-T\), this is the exact scale--time continuity law

\[
\boxed{
\partial_te+\partial_k\Pi=-2\nu k^2e.
}
\tag{TL.7}
\]

It says exactly what the physical picture requires.  Kinetic energy can enter
or leave a scale region only through the signed current \(\Pi\), while the same
history loses \(2\nu k^2e\) there.  No scale region creates kinetic energy.

## 3. Moving-band receiver theorem

Let \(0<a(t)<b(t)<\infty\) be absolutely continuous scale boundaries and set

\[
S_{a,b}(t):=\int_{a(t)}^{b(t)}e(k,t)\,dk,
\qquad
L_{a,b}(t):=2\nu\int_{a(t)}^{b(t)}k^2e(k,t)\,dk.
\tag{TL.8}
\]

The current through a moving boundary must be measured relative to that
boundary:

\[
J_a(t):=\Pi(a(t),t)-a'(t)e(a(t),t),
\qquad
J_b(t):=\Pi(b(t),t)-b'(t)e(b(t),t).
\tag{TL.9}
\]

The terms involving \(a'\) and \(b'\) remove energy that only changes side
because the scale marker moved.  Applying Reynolds transport to (TL.7) yields

\[
\boxed{
S_{a,b}'(t)+L_{a,b}(t)=J_a(t)-J_b(t).
}
\tag{TL.10}
\]

Consequently, on every interval \([s,t]\),

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^tJ_b(r)\,dr
=\int_s^tJ_a(r)\,dr
+S_{a,b}(s)-S_{a,b}(t)
-\int_s^tL_{a,b}(r)\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.11}
\]

This is the exact receiver law:

\[
\text{outgoing signed throughput}
=\text{incoming signed throughput}
+\text{one-time storage released}
-\text{final storage}
-\text{simultaneous irreversible loss}.
\tag{TL.12}
\]

In particular,

\[
\int_s^tJ_b
\le \int_s^tJ_a+S_{a,b}(s)-\int_s^tL_{a,b}.
\tag{TL.13}
\]

No independent donor, receiver or viscous continuation was introduced.  All
terms in (TL.11) are boundary, storage and loss readings of (TL.1).

## 4. Exact non-recounting under concatenation

Let

\[
0=K_0(t)<K_1(t)<\cdots<K_N(t)
\tag{TL.14}
\]

be moving boundaries.  Apply (TL.10) to every adjacent band
\((K_j,K_{j+1})\).  The current \(J_{K_j}\) is outgoing for one band and incoming
for its neighbour, with the same sign and value.  It therefore cancels exactly
when the bands are summed.  Writing

\[
S_{0,N}:=\int_{K_0}^{K_N}e\,dk,
\qquad
L_{0,N}:=2\nu\int_{K_0}^{K_N}k^2e\,dk,
\tag{TL.15}
\]

gives

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^tJ_{K_N}
=\int_s^tJ_{K_0}
+S_{0,N}(s)-S_{0,N}(t)
-\int_s^tL_{0,N}.
}
\tag{TL.16}
\]

Hence internal transfer cannot be recounted as fresh power at each interface.
Changing the number, spacing or motion of the readout boundaries does not
change (TL.16).

## 5. Viscous lifetime of a receiver

For the moving tail

\[
E_{>K(t)}(t):=\int_{K(t)}^\infty e(k,t)\,dk,
\tag{TL.17}
\]

there is no outgoing current at infinity.  Equations (TL.7)--(TL.9) give

\[
E_{>K}'(t)+2\nu\int_{K(t)}^\infty k^2e(k,t)\,dk=J_K(t).
\tag{TL.18}
\]

Since \(k\ge K(t)\) on the tail,

\[
\boxed{
E_{>K}'(t)+2\nu K(t)^2E_{>K}(t)\le J_K(t).
}
\tag{TL.19}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
E_{>K(t)}(t)
\le{}&e^{-2\nu\int_s^tK(q)^2dq}E_{>K(s)}(s)\\
&+\int_s^t
e^{-2\nu\int_r^tK(q)^2dq}J_K(r)\,dr.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.20}
\]

For fixed \(K\), the receiver memory is \(O((\nu K^2)^{-1})\).  For a moving
front, its memory is the accumulated clock
\(2\nu\int K^2dt\).  Equation (TL.20), rather than an isolated heat comparison,
is the lawful throughput-versus-lifetime statement for the complete history.

## 6. Exact relation to critical height

The critical height is the first moment of the same energy density:

\[
H(t)=\frac12\|u(t)\|_{\dot H^{1/2}}^2
=\int_0^\infty k e(k,t)\,dk.
\tag{TL.21}
\]

Equivalently, by layer cake,

\[
\boxed{
H(t)=\int_0^\infty E_{>K}(t)\,dK.
}
\tag{TL.22}
\]

The ultraviolet critical tail is

\[
H_{>K}(t)
=K E_{>K}(t)+\int_K^\infty E_{>q}(t)\,dq.
\tag{TL.23}
\]

Thus the no-Zeno endpoint is not a hard Fourier cutoff.  It is the uniform
critical-tail statement

\[
\lim_{K\to\infty}\sup_{t<T_*}H_{>K}(t)=0.
\tag{TL.24}
\]

Together with the energy bound on frequencies below one fixed \(K\), (TL.24)
bounds \(H\).

## 7. The remaining central inference, now in native coordinates

Equations (TL.11), (TL.16) and (TL.20) prove all of the following without a
proxy action:

1. every finer scale region is a receiver of the complete signed current;
2. its outgoing signed throughput cannot exceed incoming throughput plus its
   one-time storage minus simultaneous irreversible loss;
3. internal transfers cancel and cannot be counted again as new energy;
4. its retained energy is filtered by its own viscous lifetime.

What these identities do not establish by themselves is (TL.24).  For fixed
boundaries \(q\), \(J_q=\Pi(q,\cdot)\).  The initial contribution in (TL.20)
has a uniformly vanishing critical tail because \(u_0\in\dot H^{1/2}\).
The exact remaining statement is therefore a bound on the current that enters
the lifetime filter:

\[
\boxed{
\lim_{K\to\infty}\sup_{t<T_*}
\left[
K\!\int_{0}^{t}
e^{-2\nu K^2(t-r)}J_K(r)\,dr
+\int_K^\infty\!\int_{0}^{t}
e^{-2\nu q^2(t-r)}J_q(r)\,dr\,dq
\right]=0.
}
\tag{TL.25}
\]

The source in (TL.25) is not freely prescribed.  It is the signed current of
the same velocity field in (TL.3)--(TL.4).  The next proof step must use that
constitutive coupling to show that the complete transfer cannot concentrate
successively larger power into successively shorter viscous memories.  Merely
replacing \(J_K\) by its positive part would discard the cancellation proved in
(TL.16) and reopen recounting.

## 8. The current's exact mixed-helicity geometry

The current in (TL.25) is further constrained by the geometry of the same
field.  Let

\[
u=u_++u_-,
\qquad
\operatorname{curl}u_\pm=\pm\Lambda u_\pm,
\tag{TL.26}
\]

be the two orthogonal helical readouts, and define

\[
R_\pm:=\|\Lambda^{1/2}u_\pm\|_2^2,
\qquad
D_\pm:=\|\Lambda^{3/2}u_\pm\|_2^2.
\tag{TL.27}
\]

The Euler part of the complete current conserves kinetic helicity.  Therefore
its critical production is equal in the two readouts:

\[
\boxed{
R_\pm'+2\nu D_\pm=P_H,
}
\qquad
P_H:=-\langle B(u,u),\Lambda u\rangle.
\tag{TL.28}
\]

The production also has the exact one-field triple-product form

\[
\boxed{
P_H
=2\int_{\mathbb R^3}
(u_++u_-)\cdot
(\Lambda u_+\times\Lambda u_-)\,dx.
}
\tag{TL.29}
\]

Thus pure handedness cannot produce critical radial growth.  Both orientations
must participate simultaneously in the same velocity field.

Set

\[
C(t):=2\min\{R_+(t),R_-(t)\}.
\tag{TL.30}
\]

Away from ties, let \(D_\wedge\) be the \(D_\pm\) belonging to the smaller
\(R_\pm\).  At a tie choose the larger \(D_\pm\), because (TL.28) makes that
sector's forward derivative the smaller one.  The derivative formula for the
minimum then gives, almost everywhere,

\[
\boxed{
\frac12C'(t)+2\nu D_\wedge(t)=P_H(t).
}
\tag{TL.31}
\]

On \(C>0\), put

\[
\kappa_\wedge^2:=\frac{D_\wedge}{\min(R_+,R_-)}
=\frac{2D_\wedge}{C}.
\tag{TL.32}
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\frac12C'+\nu\kappa_\wedge^2C=P_H.
}
\tag{TL.33}
\]

Finally, layer cake and (TL.3)--(TL.5) give

\[
\boxed{
P_H(t)=\int_0^\infty\Pi(k,t)\,dk.
}
\tag{TL.34}
\]

Indeed,

\[
\int_0^\infty\Pi(k,t)\,dk
=\int_0^\infty kT(k,t)\,dk
=-\langle B(u,u),\Lambda u\rangle.
\tag{TL.35}
\]

Combining (TL.33) and (TL.34),

\[
\boxed{
\frac12C'(t)+\nu\kappa_\wedge(t)^2C(t)
=\int_0^\infty\Pi(k,t)\,dk.
}
\tag{TL.36}
\]

This is the constitutive geometry missing from an arbitrary shell schedule:
the total critical radial throughput can be retained only as common
opposite-helicity content, and that content is simultaneously filtered by its
own mean-square-frequency lifetime.  Equivalently, for \(s<t\),

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
C(t)
={}&e^{-2\nu\int_s^t\kappa_\wedge(q)^2\,dq}C(s)\\
&+2\int_s^t
e^{-2\nu\int_r^t\kappa_\wedge(q)^2\,dq}
\left(\int_0^\infty\Pi(k,r)\,dk\right)dr.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.37}
\]

Equations (TL.16), (TL.20) and (TL.37) now retain all three facts at once:
energy cannot be recounted across interfaces, fine energy has a \(k^2\)
lifetime, and critical strengthening requires simultaneous mixed-helicity
participation.  The remaining contraction cannot be a free shell-flux bound
or an assumed alignment loss; it must follow from the triple product (TL.29)
during the same lifetime appearing in (TL.37).

## 9. Critical throughput has one lawful gain term

The non-amplification statement must distinguish energy throughput from
critical throughput.  Multiplying (TL.7) by \(k\) gives

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t(ke)+\partial_k(k\Pi)
=\Pi-2\nu k^3e.
}
\tag{TL.38}
\]

The term \(\Pi\) on the right is radial work: the same kinetic energy acquires
more critical weight when the complete transfer moves it outward in frequency.
It is not a new energy source.

For a moving band, define its critical storage and loss by

\[
S^H_{a,b}(t):=\int_{a(t)}^{b(t)}k e(k,t)\,dk,
\qquad
L^H_{a,b}(t):=2\nu\int_{a(t)}^{b(t)}k^3e(k,t)\,dk.
\tag{TL.39}
\]

Reynolds transport applied to (TL.38) gives

\[
\boxed{
(S^H_{a,b})'+L^H_{a,b}
=aJ_a-bJ_b+\int_a^b\Pi(k,t)\,dk.
}
\tag{TL.40}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\int_s^t b(r)J_b(r)\,dr
={}&\int_s^t a(r)J_a(r)\,dr
+S^H_{a,b}(s)-S^H_{a,b}(t)\\
&-\int_s^tL^H_{a,b}(r)\,dr
+\int_s^t\int_{a(r)}^{b(r)}\Pi(k,r)\,dk\,dr.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.41}
\]

Thus the exact critical receiver statement is:

\[
\begin{aligned}
\text{outgoing critical throughput}
={}&\text{incoming critical throughput}
+\text{one-time critical storage released}\\
&-\text{final critical storage}
-\text{simultaneous critical loss}
+\text{radial work of the same transfer}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.42}
\]

Dropping the radial-work term would incorrectly turn kinetic-energy
conservation into critical-height conservation.  Summing (TL.40) over adjacent
bands cancels all internal boundary currents, while the radial-work terms join:

\[
\sum_j\int_{K_j}^{K_{j+1}}\Pi\,dk
=\int_{K_0}^{K_N}\Pi\,dk.
\tag{TL.43}
\]

Over all frequencies this is exactly (TL.34), hence exactly the
mixed-helicity balance (TL.36).  The central no-Zeno inference is consequently
not a second energy ledger.  It is the following same-transfer statement:

\[
\boxed{
\text{every unit of radial work appears exactly once as}
\quad
\text{common-content increase or simultaneous lifetime loss}
}
\tag{TL.44}
\]

Equation (TL.36) proves the equality at each time; the unresolved strict part is
to prove that the resulting common mixed-helicity content cannot compress its
release into successively shorter, successively stronger radial-work pulses
before its own lifetime loss acts.

### 9.1 Absorb the radial work into the complete critical current

Equation (TL.40) is useful for displaying how kinetic-energy transfer creates
critical weight.  The same fact also has an exact conservative form.  Define
the complete critical current through \(K\) by

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal Q(K,t)
:=\int_K^\infty q\,T(q,t)\,dq
=K\Pi(K,t)+\int_K^\infty\Pi(q,t)\,dq.
}
\tag{TL.44a}
\]

The second equality follows by Fubini.  Since
\(\partial_K\mathcal Q=-KT(K,t)\), (TL.6) becomes

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t(Ke(K,t))+\partial_K\mathcal Q(K,t)
=-2\nu K^3e(K,t).
}
\tag{TL.44b}
\]

Thus radial work has not been discarded; it has been incorporated into the
current that transports the critical moment itself.  In particular,

\[
\mathcal Q(0,t)=P_H(t),
\qquad
\lim_{K\to\infty}\mathcal Q(K,t)=0.
\tag{TL.44c}
\]

For a moving boundary define the relative critical current

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal J_K^H(t)
:=\mathcal Q(K(t),t)-K'(t)K(t)e(K(t),t).
}
\tag{TL.44d}
\]

Reynolds transport in (TL.44b) now gives the source-free moving-band law

\[
\boxed{
(S^H_{a,b})'(t)+L^H_{a,b}(t)
=\mathcal J_a^H(t)-\mathcal J_b^H(t).
}
\tag{TL.44e}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t\mathcal J_b^H(r)\,dr
=\int_s^t\mathcal J_a^H(r)\,dr
+S^H_{a,b}(s)-S^H_{a,b}(t)
-\int_s^tL^H_{a,b}(r)\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.44f}
\]

This is exactly the signed, causal, non-recounting law at critical scale:

\[
\boxed{
\text{outgoing complete critical throughput}
=\text{incoming complete critical throughput}
+\text{one-time critical storage}
-\text{final storage}
-\text{simultaneous irreversible loss}.
}
\tag{TL.44g}
\]

For adjacent moving bands, every internal \(\mathcal J_{K_j}^H\) cancels
exactly.  No radial work, internal transfer or moving-boundary sweep can be
counted again at the next interface.

For the moving critical tail

\[
H_{>K(t)}(t):=\int_{K(t)}^\infty k e(k,t)\,dk,
\tag{TL.44h}
\]

(TL.44e) gives

\[
H_{>K}'(t)
+2\nu\int_{K(t)}^\infty k^3e(k,t)\,dk
=\mathcal J_K^H(t).
\tag{TL.44i}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
H_{>K}'(t)+2\nu K(t)^2H_{>K}(t)
\le\mathcal J_K^H(t),
}
\tag{TL.44j}
\]

and hence

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
H_{>K(t)}(t)
\le{}&e^{-2\nu\int_s^tK(q)^2dq}H_{>K(s)}(s)\\
&+\int_s^t
e^{-2\nu\int_r^tK(q)^2dq}\mathcal J_K^H(r)\,dr.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.44k}
\]

Unlike (TL.20), this is already the lifetime filter for the critical tail
itself.  It is the exact mathematical realization of “every finer critical
structure is a leaky receiver, not a new source.”

## 10. Exact first-record power floor

The lifetime law already forces the power escalation associated with a finer
active scale.  Fix \(K>0\) and abbreviate

\[
E_K(t):=E_{>K}(t),
\qquad
\mathcal L_K(t):=2\nu\int_K^\infty k^2e(k,t)\,dk.
\tag{TL.45}
\]

Let \([s,t]\) be a last-passage doubling interval for this same tail:

\[
E_K(s)=A,
\qquad
E_K(t)=2A,
\qquad
A\le E_K(r)\le2A
\quad(s\le r\le t).
\tag{TL.46}
\]

Integrating the exact fixed-boundary case of (TL.18) gives

\[
\int_s^t\Pi(K,r)\,dr
=A+\int_s^t\mathcal L_K(r)\,dr.
\tag{TL.47}
\]

Since \(\mathcal L_K\ge2\nu K^2E_K\), (TL.46) yields

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t\Pi(K,r)\,dr
\ge A\bigl(1+2\nu K^2(t-s)\bigr).
}
\tag{TL.48}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{t-s}\int_s^t\Pi(K,r)\,dr
\ge \frac{A}{t-s}+2\nu K^2A.
}
\tag{TL.49}
\]

Writing \(Q:=KA\) for the critical strength already present at the start of
the doubling,

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{t-s}\int_s^t\Pi(K,r)\,dr
\ge \frac{Q}{K(t-s)}+2\nu KQ
\ge2\nu KQ.
}
\tag{TL.50}
\]

This is the exact throughput-versus-lifetime ratchet:

\[
\boxed{
\text{doubling a fixed amount of critical tail at frequency }K
\text{ requires average kinetic-energy power at least }2\nu KQ.
}
\tag{TL.51}
\]

It covers both temporal regimes without introducing them as separate cases.
If the event is shorter than the viscous lifetime, the storage term
\(Q/[K(t-s)]\) is larger.  If it is longer, the \(2\nu KQ\) term is the power
continuously lost while the tail remains active.  Thus temporal compression
does not lower the required power.

For an absolutely continuous moving front \(K(r)\), the same proof with
(TL.18), \(K_*:=\inf_{[s,t]}K(r)\), and the relative current \(J_K\) gives

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{t-s}\int_s^tJ_K(r)\,dr
\ge \frac{A}{t-s}+2\nu K_*^2A.
}
\tag{TL.52}
\]

The next inference is therefore no longer qualitative.  A finite-time Zeno
front carrying a nonvanishing critical amount \(Q\) would force a sequence
\(K_n\to\infty\) of same-history power pulses whose averages grow at least like
\(2\nu K_nQ\).  The remaining use of the full VPI coupling is to prove that
the triple product (TL.29) cannot supply that unbounded sequence from the
finite initial field while retaining the daughter as the next productive
current.

### 10.1 Direct critical-tail version

The complete critical current gives the sharper version without converting
critical strength back to kinetic energy.  Let \([s,t]\) be a last-passage
doubling interval for a fixed critical tail:

\[
H_{>K}(s)=A,
\qquad
H_{>K}(t)=2A,
\qquad
A\le H_{>K}(r)\le2A.
\tag{TL.53}
\]

Integrating (TL.44i) with fixed \(K\) yields

\[
\int_s^t\mathcal Q(K,r)\,dr
=A+2\nu\int_s^t\int_K^\infty k^3e(k,r)\,dk\,dr.
\tag{TL.54}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{t-s}\int_s^t\mathcal Q(K,r)\,dr
\ge\frac{A}{t-s}+2\nu K^2A
\ge2\nu K^2A.
}
\tag{TL.55}
\]

For a moving front, replace \(\mathcal Q\) by
\(\mathcal J_K^H\) and \(K\) by \(K_*=\inf_{[s,t]}K(r)\):

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{t-s}\int_s^t\mathcal J_K^H(r)\,dr
\ge\frac{A}{t-s}+2\nu K_*^2A.
}
\tag{TL.56}
\]

Hence any fixed threshold \(A_*>0\) defines an active scale only where the same
VPI history can supply average complete critical throughput at least
\(2\nu K^2A_*\).  A datum-dependent upper bound for these last-passage averages
would immediately give

\[
K\le
\left(
\frac{\mathcal P_*(u_0,\nu)}{2\nu A_*}
\right)^{1/2},
\tag{TL.57}
\]

which is an upper-frequency bound for structures carrying the fixed critical
threshold \(A_*\).  It is not a hard Fourier cutoff: arbitrarily fine weak
content is compatible with (TL.57).  What (TL.57) would exclude is critical
strength persisting beyond a datum-controlled scale, which is the no-Zeno and
bounded-\(H\) bridge.  The current \(\mathcal J_K^H\), rather than a positive
shell flux or an externally prescribed power, is the exact quantity on which
that datum bound must be proved.

## 11. The complete critical current is a deformation commutator

The constitutive restriction on the current can be stated without assigning
independent dynamics to Fourier shells.  Let \(\chi\in C^\infty([0,\infty))\)
be nondecreasing, with \(\chi=0\) on \([0,1]\) and \(\chi=1\) on
\([2,\infty)\), and define the self-adjoint multiplier

\[
M_K:=\Lambda\chi(\Lambda/K),
\qquad
H_K^\chi:=\frac12\langle u,M_Ku\rangle .
\tag{TL.58}
\]

Its complete critical current is

\[
\mathcal Q_\chi(K,t)
:=-\langle \mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u),M_Ku\rangle .
\tag{TL.59}
\]

This is a reconstructive smoothing of the sharp critical tail:

\[
\boxed{
H_{>2K}(t)\le H_K^\chi(t)\le H_{>K}(t).
}
\tag{TL.59a}
\]

Testing (TL.1) against \(M_Ku\) gives its exact same-history receiver
balance,

\[
\boxed{
(H_K^\chi)'(t)
+2\nu\int_0^\infty k^3\chi(k/K)e(k,t)\,dk
=\mathcal Q_\chi(K,t).
}
\tag{TL.59b}
\]

Put \(L_u f=u\cdot\nabla f\).  Since \(M_Ku\) is divergence free, the
Leray projection can be removed in (TL.59).  Since \(L_u\) is skew-adjoint
on \(L^2\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal Q_\chi(K,t)
=\frac12\langle[L_u,M_K]u,u\rangle .
}
\tag{TL.60}
\]

Equivalently, with \(A_K=M_K^{1/2}\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal Q_\chi(K,t)
=\langle[L_u,A_K]u,A_Ku\rangle .
}
\tag{TL.61}
\]

Thus rigid carriage of the complete velocity field contributes no critical
throughput.  The current is exactly the failure of material transport to
commute with the critical scale reading.  In physical terms, only deformation
of the same velocity field relative to its scale geometry can feed the
receiver.

### 11.1 A remote donor cannot directly service an arbitrarily fine receiver

This commutator gives a quantitative version of the preceding statement.  Let

\[
v=P_{\le L}u,
\qquad w=P_{>L}u,
\qquad 0<L\le K/8,
\tag{TL.62}
\]

The \(v\)-\(v\) interaction has frequency below \(K/4\) and is invisible to
\(M_K\).  Define the complete remote cross-interaction by

\[
\mathcal Q_{\chi,\mathrm{rem}}(K)
:=-\left\langle
\mathbb P\bigl(v\cdot\nabla w+w\cdot\nabla v\bigr),M_Ku
\right\rangle .
\tag{TL.62a}
\]

The first cross term has the transport-commutator symbol

\[
\bigl(m_K(\xi)-m_K(\zeta)\bigr)\,\zeta,
\qquad
m_K(\xi)=|\xi|\chi(|\xi|/K),
\qquad
\xi=\eta+\zeta,\quad |\eta|\le L.
\tag{TL.63}
\]

Whenever this symbol is nonzero, \(|\xi|\) and \(|\zeta|\) are comparable
and at least \(K/2\).  The mean-value theorem gives

\[
|m_K(\xi)-m_K(\zeta)|\,|\zeta|
\le C_\chi |\eta|\sqrt{|\xi||\zeta|}.
\]

The second cross term already contains \(\nabla v\); its two remaining
frequencies are comparable for the same support reason.  The combined
Coifman--Meyer/paraproduct estimate is

\[
\boxed{
|\mathcal Q_{\chi,\mathrm{rem}}(K,t)|
\le C_\chi\|\nabla P_{\le L}u(t)\|_\infty
H_{>K/2}(t).
}
\tag{TL.64}
\]

No independent low-frequency source has been introduced: \(P_{\le L}u\) is
one readout of the same field in (TL.1).  Bernstein and the kinetic-energy
identity give

\[
\|\nabla P_{\le L}u(t)\|_\infty
\le C L^{5/2}\|u(t)\|_2
\le C L^{5/2}\|u_0\|_2.
\tag{TL.65}
\]

On one viscous memory interval

\[
I_{K,t}:=
\left[\left(t-\frac{\theta}{\nu K^2}\right)_+,t\right],
\]

(TL.64)--(TL.65) therefore imply

\[
\boxed{
\int_{I_{K,t}}
|\mathcal Q_{\chi,\mathrm{rem}}(K,s)|\,ds
\le
\frac{C_\chi\theta\|u_0\|_2L^{5/2}}
{\nu K^2}
\sup_{s\in I_{K,t}}H_{>K/2}(s).
}
\tag{TL.66}
\]

Writing

\[
\mathcal Q_\chi
=\mathcal Q_{\chi,\mathrm{rem}}
+\mathcal Q_{\chi,\mathrm{near}},
\qquad
\mathcal Q_{\chi,\mathrm{near}}
:=-\langle\mathbb P(w\cdot\nabla w),M_Kw\rangle ,
\tag{TL.66a}
\]

the first term is now datum-controlled over the receiver lifetime by
(TL.66).  The second is not a new source: it is the portion of the same
commutator whose advecting content has already reached frequencies above
\(L\).

Consequently, for every scale choice \(L=L(K)\) satisfying

\[
L(K)\le K/8,
\qquad
\frac{L(K)^{5/2}}{K^2}\longrightarrow0,
\tag{TL.67}
\]

the direct contribution of all frequencies below \(L(K)\) vanishes relative
to the receiver's critical storage during one \(K^{-2}\) viscous lifetime.
An arbitrarily fine active receiver therefore cannot be serviced directly by
one fixed coarse part of the initial field.  Any nonvanishing terminal current
must be carried forward by velocity content whose own frequency also tends to
infinity.  This is a proved same-history relay requirement, not an assumed
vortex ancestry.

### 11.2 Why frequency scaling alone cannot finish the contraction

The remaining relay is scale critical.  For a divergence-free field \(w\)
supported in one fixed-width annulus, Sobolev, Holder and interpolation give

\[
\begin{aligned}
|\langle\mathbb P(w\cdot\nabla w),\Lambda w\rangle|
&\le \|w\|_6\|\nabla w\|_3\|\Lambda w\|_2\\
&\le C\|\Lambda^{1/2}w\|_2
       \|\Lambda^{3/2}w\|_2^2.
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.68}
\]

The simultaneous viscous critical loss is
\(\nu\|\Lambda^{3/2}w\|_2^2\).  Hence the ratio between the largest
general nonlinear service allowed by (TL.68) and the loss depends on

\[
\frac{\|\Lambda^{1/2}w\|_2}{\nu},
\tag{TL.69}
\]

not on the annulus frequency.  Both service and loss acquire the same
\(K^2\) factor under a critical rescaling.  The exact lifetime law therefore
rules out remote coarse servicing, but a local high-frequency relay can only
be excluded by a further property of the complete VPI interaction geometry.
The required property is not the slogan that the Laplacian has two derivatives
while transport has one; at fixed critical strength that derivative count is
exactly balanced.

Equations (TL.60), (TL.66), and (TL.68) locate the remaining inference on the
correct object.  It must prove that a succession of local commutator currents
cannot retain scale-independent productive coupling through every shrinking
viscous memory.  A bound on an externally prescribed flux, or a viscosity-only
comparison, would not address that same-field interaction.

### 11.3 The surviving current requires a migrating critical population

The near term in (TL.66a) has an exact critical absorption threshold.  Fix a
homogeneous Littlewood--Paley decomposition and set

\[
\beta_{>L}(t)
:=\sup_{\{j:2^j>L\}}
2^{j/2}\|\Delta_j u(t)\|_2,
\tag{TL.70}
\]

\[
\mathfrak D_K^\chi(t)
:=2\int_0^\infty k^3\chi(k/K)e(k,t)\,dk
=\|\Lambda^{3/2}\chi(\Lambda/K)^{1/2}u(t)\|_2^2.
\tag{TL.71}
\]

Apply the critical paraproduct estimate to the divergence-free field
\(w=P_{>L}u\) and the multiplier \(M_K\).  In every low--high row,
\(\|S_{j-1}w\|_\infty\) is bounded by
\(C\beta_{>L}2^j\); in every high--high row the output sum is geometric.
Consequently,

\[
\boxed{
|\mathcal Q_{\chi,\mathrm{near}}(K,t)|
\le C_\chi\beta_{>L}(t)\mathfrak D_K^\chi(t).
}
\tag{TL.72}
\]

Combining (TL.59b), (TL.64), and (TL.72) gives the joined inequality

\[
\boxed{
(H_K^\chi)'
+\bigl(\nu-C_\chi\beta_{>L}\bigr)\mathfrak D_K^\chi
\le
C_\chi\|\nabla P_{\le L}u\|_\infty H_{>K/2}.
}
\tag{TL.73}
\]

This is not a comparison between separate mechanisms.  It is one
decomposition of the complete VPI current according to which frequencies of
the same field participate in the interaction.

Let

\[
\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}:=\frac{\nu}{2C_\chi}.
\tag{TL.74}
\]

On every interval where \(\beta_{>L}\le\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}\), (TL.65),
(TL.71), and \(k\ge K\) on the support of \(\chi(k/K)\) imply

\[
\boxed{
(H_K^\chi)'
+\nu K^2H_K^\chi
\le
C_\chi L^{5/2}\|u_0\|_2 H_{>K/2}.
}
\tag{TL.75}
\]

In particular, at every time for which \(H_K^\chi>0\),
\((H_K^\chi)'>0\), and
\(\beta_{>L}\le\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}\),

\[
\boxed{
\frac{H_{>K/2}}{H_K^\chi}
>
\frac{\nu K^2}
{C_\chi L^{5/2}\|u_0\|_2}.
}
\tag{TL.76}
\]

The second branch in the following prose disappears once the receiver carries
a fixed positive critical amount.  Indeed, when \(K/2>L\), the definition of
\(\beta_{>L}\) and finite overlap of the dyadic decomposition give

\[
\int_{K/2}^{2K}k e(k,t)\,dk
\le C_{\mathrm{LP}}\beta_{>L}(t)^2.
\tag{TL.77}
\]

Since \(\chi=1\) above \(2K\),

\[
\boxed{
H_{>K/2}(t)
\le H_K^\chi(t)
+C_{\mathrm{LP}}\beta_{>L}(t)^2.
}
\tag{TL.78}
\]

Fix \(A_*>0\), choose any \(L(K)\) satisfying (TL.67), and suppose
\(H_K^\chi(t)\ge A_*\).  If
\(\beta_{>L}\le\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}\), then (TL.78) bounds the left side of
(TL.76) by

\[
1+\frac{C_{\mathrm{LP}}\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}^2}{A_*},
\]

whereas the right side of (TL.76) tends to infinity.  Hence there is
\(K_*=K_*(u_0,\nu,A_*,\chi,L)\) such that

\[
\boxed{
\begin{gathered}
K\ge K_*,
\qquad H_K^\chi(t)\ge A_*,
\qquad (H_K^\chi)'(t)>0
\\[2mm]
\Longrightarrow
\beta_{>L(K)}(t)>\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}.
\end{gathered}
}
\tag{TL.79}
\]

Equation (TL.79) is the fixed-strength migration theorem.  A critical
ultraviolet receiver can strengthen only while the same velocity field already
contains a viscosity-sized critical population at a frequency that also tends
to infinity.  Neither a fixed coarse donor nor release from a bounded adjacent
collar can produce the event.

It follows that positive fine-tail growth at a scale satisfying
\[
C_\chi L^{5/2}\|u_0\|_2\ll\nu K^2
\]
has only two lawful same-field causes:

1. \(\beta_{>L}>\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}\), so a viscosity-sized critical
   population has already migrated beyond the intermediate scale \(L\); or
2. below a fixed receiver threshold, the parent tail \(H_{>K/2}\) is larger
   than the receiver \(H_K^\chi\) by the reciprocal of the small coefficient
   in (TL.75), so the event is release of already-present nearby critical
   storage rather than direct service from a fixed coarse donor.

Therefore a fixed coarse population cannot remain the productive source of a
terminal front.  Every continuing front must carry forward either a
super-threshold critical population or, only below fixed critical strength, a
quantitatively larger adjacent storage layer.  Equations (TL.73)--(TL.79)
prove this migration requirement from the complete current; they do not yet
prove that the migrating local population loses enough productive coupling
from one successor lifetime to the next.

### 11.4 The migrating population has datum-finite scale--time occupancy

The migration alternative in (TL.79) is tied directly to the initial energy.
If \(\beta_{>L}(t)>\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}\), then for some dyadic
\(2^j>L\),

\[
2^{j/2}\|\Delta_j u(t)\|_2>\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}.
\]

Therefore the same field's enstrophy obeys

\[
\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2
\ge c_{\mathrm{LP}}L\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}^2.
\tag{TL.80}
\]

The kinetic-energy identity consequently gives

\[
\boxed{
\left|
\left\{t\in(0,T):
\beta_{>L}(t)>\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}
\right\}
\right|
\le
\frac{C_{\mathrm{LP}}\mathcal E_0}
{\nu L\beta_{\mathrm{abs}}^2}
\le
\frac{C_{\mathrm{LP},\chi}\mathcal E_0}{\nu^3L}.
}
\tag{TL.81}
\]

For the fixed-strength rising set

\[
\mathcal R_{A_*}(K)
:=
\left\{
t:
H_K^\chi(t)\ge A_*,
\ (H_K^\chi)'(t)>0
\right\},
\tag{TL.82}
\]

(TL.79)--(TL.81) yield, for \(K\ge K_*\),

\[
\boxed{
|\mathcal R_{A_*}(K)|
\le
\frac{C(u_0,\nu,\chi)}{L(K)}.
}
\tag{TL.83}
\]

For example, after fixing one reference frequency, one may take
\(L(K)\asymp K^\alpha\) with any \(0<\alpha<4/5\).  Then

\[
\int_{K_*}^{\infty}
|\mathcal R_{A_*}(K)|\,\frac{dK}{K}
<\infty.
\tag{TL.84}
\]

This is a datum-finite all-scale occupancy law for actual positive
fixed-strength rebuilding.  It is stronger than a statement that every
individual time slice is smooth: the set on which a critical receiver can
strengthen shrinks quantitatively as its scale becomes finer.  It does not by
itself count isolated scale--time crossings; converting (TL.84) into exclusion
of a Zeno graph requires the same VPI current to give each successor
strengthening nonvanishing scale--time thickness.

### 11.5 The datum gives strong time-tightness of the critical ultraviolet tail

The energy identity gives a second exact restriction on any terminal relay.
Put

\[
\mathcal E_{>K}(t):=\int_K^\infty e(k,t)\,dk,
\qquad
\mathcal K_{>K}(t):=\int_K^\infty k^2e(k,t)\,dk.
\tag{TL.85}
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz in the same spectral measure gives

\[
\boxed{
H_{>K}(t)^2
\le \mathcal E_{>K}(t)\mathcal K_{>K}(t)
\le \mathcal E_0\mathcal K_{>K}(t).
}
\tag{TL.86}
\]

Define the tail of the actual kinetic-energy loss measure by

\[
\mathfrak e_\nu(K;T)
:=2\nu\int_0^T\mathcal K_{>K}(t)\,dt.
\tag{TL.87}
\]

The energy identity says that this is the restriction to frequencies above
\(K\) of one finite measure and hence

\[
0\le \mathfrak e_\nu(K;T)\le\mathcal E_0,
\qquad
\mathfrak e_\nu(K;T)\longrightarrow0
\quad(K\to\infty).
\tag{TL.88}
\]

Combining (TL.86)--(TL.88),

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T H_{>K}(t)^2\,dt
\le
\frac{\mathcal E_0}{2\nu}\,
\mathfrak e_\nu(K;T)
\longrightarrow0.
}
\tag{TL.89}
\]

In particular, for every fixed \(A_*>0\),

\[
\boxed{
\left|\{t\in(0,T):H_{>K}(t)\ge A_*\}\right|
\le
\frac{\mathcal E_0}{2\nu A_*^2}
\mathfrak e_\nu(K;T)
\longrightarrow0.
}
\tag{TL.90}
\]

Thus a terminal critical population cannot occupy arbitrarily fine scales for
a positive amount of ordinary time.  This is stronger than merely knowing
that the total dissipation is finite, because its right side is the vanishing
ultraviolet tail of that finite measure.  It still permits a graph whose time
thickness collapses together with its viscous lifetime.  The causal task is
therefore to connect the positive current required by (TL.55) to the actual
participation concentration needed to achieve such a collapse.

### 11.6 A surviving annular self-relay must concentrate into wavelength-scale volume

The concentration forced by a fast local relay can be stated exactly before
introducing a vortex label.  Let

\[
w_K=P_{[K/2,2K]}u,
\qquad
E_K=\|w_K\|_2^2,
\qquad
A_K=K E_K,
\tag{TL.91}
\]

where the annular projector is a fixed smooth readout of the same velocity
field.  When \(w_K\ne0\), define its effective participation volume and local
service Reynolds number by

\[
V_K^{\rm eff}
:=\frac{\|w_K\|_2^6}{\|w_K\|_3^6},
\qquad
\operatorname{Re}_K^{\rm part}
:=\frac{\|w_K\|_3^3}
{\nu K\|w_K\|_2^2}.
\tag{TL.92}
\]

For a field of essentially constant velocity amplitude on a region of volume
\(V\), the first quantity is comparable to \(V\).  No region is selected in
its definition.

The critical work of the annular self-interaction obeys

\[
\begin{aligned}
\left|
\left\langle
\mathbb P(w_K\cdot\nabla w_K),\Lambda w_K
\right\rangle
\right|
&\le
\|w_K\|_3\|\nabla w_K\|_3\|\Lambda w_K\|_3\\
&\le C K^2\|w_K\|_3^3,
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.93}
\]

whereas its simultaneous critical heat satisfies

\[
\nu\|\Lambda^{3/2}w_K\|_2^2
\ge c\nu K^3E_K.
\tag{TL.94}
\]

Consequently, if the self-interaction supplies at least a fixed fraction
\(\theta>0\) of this lifetime loss, then

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Re}_K^{\rm part}\ge c_\theta,
\qquad
V_K^{\rm eff}
\le
\frac{C_\theta A_K}{\nu^2K^3}.
}
\tag{TL.95}
\]

Bernstein supplies the opposite geometric inequality

\[
\boxed{
V_K^{\rm eff}\ge cK^{-3}.
}
\tag{TL.96}
\]

Thus, when \(A_K\) is of viscosity size, an annular self-relay capable of
competing with its own loss is forced, up to constants, into one
wavelength-scale participation volume.  At larger critical strength the exact
excess factor is \(A_K/\nu^2\).  This is the formal version of the physical
fact that a distributed fine oscillation has too little velocity contrast to
service itself before its lifetime expires: survival requires simultaneous
spatial concentration of the same velocity field.

The initial datum roots this statement.  Bernstein and any one Sobolev norm
above the critical index give, for \(s>1/2\),

\[
\operatorname{Re}_K^{\rm part}(u_0)
\le
\frac{C}{\nu}K^{1/2}\|P_{[K/2,2K]}u_0\|_2
\le
\frac{C\|u_0\|_{\dot H^s}}{\nu}
K^{1/2-s}
\longrightarrow0.
\tag{TL.97}
\]

Hence a smooth datum contains no ultraviolet annular population able to
self-service against its viscous lifetime.  Any such population later in the
history must be produced by the complete VPI current already constrained by
(TL.44f), (TL.66), and (TL.79); it is not a pre-existing independent source.

The annular volume in (TL.92) is only a history-preserving diagnostic of one
row of that current.  Equations (TL.95)--(TL.97) do not replace the full
near-current by a shell model.  In the concentrated, annularly self-serviced
branch satisfying their hypotheses, they require wavelength-scale
participation concentration while (TL.89) forces its ordinary-time support to
zero.  The diffuse and many-row causal-allocation branches remain separate and
are not converted into this annular scenario.

### 11.7 Diffuse octave proliferation is exponentially time-thin

The concentration conclusion has a complementary whole-spectrum branch.  For
one fixed homogeneous Littlewood--Paley decomposition put

\[
a_j(t)=2^j\|\Delta_j u(t)\|_2^2,
\quad
H_d=\sum_j a_j,
\quad
M_d=\sup_j a_j,
\tag{TL.98}
\]

\[
E_d=\sum_j2^{-j}a_j,
\qquad
K_d=\sum_j2^ja_j.
\tag{TL.99}
\]

These are, up to the fixed decomposition constants, the kinetic energy,
critical height, largest critical octave and enstrophy of the same field.
There are universal constants \(c_0,C_0>0\) such that

\[
\boxed{
E_dK_d
\ge c_0H_d^2
\exp\!\left(c_0\frac{H_d}{M_d}\right).
}
\tag{TL.100}
\]

To see this, normalize \(p_j=a_j/H_d\).  Each atom has mass at most
\(M_d/H_d\).  Choose a lower quarter-quantile and an upper quarter-quantile.
They must be separated by at least a universal multiple of
\(H_d/M_d\), apart from a fixed number of lattice sites.  The lower quarter
contributes at least \(2^{-j_-}/4\) to \(\sum2^{-j}p_j\), while the upper
quarter contributes at least \(2^{j_+}/4\) to \(\sum2^jp_j\).  Their product
is therefore exponentially large in the required separation.  When
\(H_d/M_d\) is bounded, ordinary Cauchy--Schwarz supplies the same inequality
after changing the constants.

The energy identity and Littlewood--Paley equivalence now give

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T
H_d(t)^2
\exp\!\left(c_0\frac{H_d(t)}{M_d(t)}\right)dt
\le
\frac{C_0\mathcal E_0^2}{\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.101}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\boxed{
\left|
\left\{t:
H_d(t)\ge h,
\ \frac{H_d(t)}{M_d(t)}\ge N
\right\}
\right|
\le
\frac{C_0\mathcal E_0^2}{\nu h^2}e^{-c_0N}.
}
\tag{TL.102}
\]

Thus diffuse proliferation at large critical height is exponentially
time-thin in its effective octave count.  Separately, a locally self-serviced
annular concentration satisfying (TL.95) obeys that theorem's volume
restriction.  These are conditional branches, not an exhaustive simultaneous
geometry: a sequence of diffuse record instants can still occupy shrinking
time neighborhoods, and the many-row causal allocation remains unclassified.
Consequently (TL.89)--(TL.102) do not reduce closure solely to a concentrated
successor handoff; the temporal and ancestry bridges remain open.

### 11.8 At a concentrated record, participation volume collapses quadratically in the record height

The preceding concentration law becomes datum-scaled at a critical record.
Suppose an annular self-relay satisfies the hypothesis of (TL.95) and carries
a fixed fraction of the current critical height,

\[
A_K(t)\ge\vartheta H(t),
\qquad 0<\vartheta\le1.
\tag{TL.103}
\]

Since \(E_K\le C\mathcal E_0\) and \(A_K=KE_K\),

\[
K\ge\frac{A_K}{C\mathcal E_0}.
\]

Substitution into (TL.95) gives the scale-free datum consequence

\[
\boxed{
V_K^{\rm eff}(t)
\le
\frac{C_{\theta,\vartheta}\mathcal E_0^3}
{\nu^2H(t)^2}.
}
\tag{TL.104}
\]

Thus the concentrated alternative to octave proliferation has a literal
physical meaning: as the record grows, the velocity population capable of
servicing that record must occupy an effective volume tending to zero at
least quadratically in the record height.  This is not inferred from a freely
chosen vortex core; it follows from the same annular current, its simultaneous
loss, and the total energy inherited from the datum.

There is also a datum-finite ordinary-time bound on such states.  Interpolation
and the energy identity give

\[
\int_0^T\|u(t)\|_3^4dt
\le
C\sup_{t\le T}\|u(t)\|_2^2
\int_0^T\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2dt
\le\frac{C\mathcal E_0^2}{\nu}.
\tag{TL.105}
\]

On the active self-relay set, (TL.93)--(TL.95) imply
\(\|w_K\|_3^3\ge c_{\theta}\nu A_K\).  Hence

\[
\boxed{
\left|
\left\{t:
H(t)\ge h
\text{ and a }\vartheta\text{-concentrated annular self-relay is active}
\right\}
\right|
\le
\frac{C_{\theta,\vartheta}\mathcal E_0^2}
{\nu^{7/3}h^{4/3}}.
}
\tag{TL.106}
\]

Equations (TL.102), (TL.104), and (TL.106) cover the diffuse representation
and the locally self-serviced part of the concentrated representation.
Spreading a large record over many octaves has exponentially vanishing time;
placing a fixed fraction in one locally productive octave forces both
participation volume and time to zero by explicit datum laws.  A concentrated
octave that is storage rather than self-service must receive its gain through
the adjacent part of the complete current.  That is precisely the temporal
handoff still to be controlled, not an additional source: can the one VPI
history move these increasingly concentrated states forward quickly enough
that every successor is generated before the preceding receiver has lost its
content?

### 11.9 Every comparable-scale handoff inherits the same volume collapse

The concentration conclusion is not confined to a block interacting with
itself.  Let \(v_1,v_2,v_3\) be three smooth frequency readouts of the same
velocity field, all supported at frequencies comparable to \(K\), and define

\[
\mathcal T_K(v_1,v_2,v_3)
:=\left|
\left\langle
\mathbb P(v_1\cdot\nabla v_2),\Lambda v_3
\right\rangle
\right|.
\tag{TL.107}
\]

The pressure response remains inside \(\mathbb P\).  Holder and Bernstein
give the exact comparable-frequency estimate

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal T_K(v_1,v_2,v_3)
\le CK^2
\|v_1\|_3\|v_2\|_3\|v_3\|_3.
}
\tag{TL.108}
\]

Put

\[
E_i=\|v_i\|_2^2,
\qquad
V_i=\frac{\|v_i\|_2^6}{\|v_i\|_3^6}.
\tag{TL.109}
\]

Retain the actual absolute geometric efficiency of this same row by setting

\[
\gamma_{123}
:=\frac{\mathcal T_K(v_1,v_2,v_3)}
{CK^2\|v_1\|_3\|v_2\|_3\|v_3\|_3},
\qquad 0\le\gamma_{123}\le1,
\tag{TL.109a}
\]

with \(\gamma_{123}=0\) when the denominator vanishes.  This number contains
the actual phase, orientation, pressure-projection and spatial-overlap loss of
the joined interaction; it is not prescribed independently.

Since \(\|v_i\|_3=E_i^{1/2}V_i^{-1/6}\) and every \(E_i\) is bounded by
a fixed multiple of \(\mathcal E_0\), any handoff row satisfying

\[
\mathcal T_K(v_1,v_2,v_3)
\ge\theta\nu K^2A
\tag{TL.110}
\]

must obey

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\left(V_1V_2V_3\right)^{1/3}}
{\gamma_{123}^2}
\le
\frac{C_\theta\mathcal E_0^3}{\nu^2A^2}.
}
\tag{TL.111}
\]

Thus a loss of productive coupling does not evade the concentration law: it
forces the raw participation volume to collapse by the additional factor
\(\gamma_{123}^2\).  In particular, at least one of the three simultaneous
participants has effective volume no larger than the right side.  Equation
(TL.111) applies to the adjacent low--high, high--low and high--high rows after
the remote part has been removed by (TL.66); it does not call any row an
independent source.  If a bounded number of comparable rows supplies a fixed
fraction of a record current with \(A\asymp H\), one of those actual rows
satisfies

\[
\boxed{
\min_i V_i
\le
\frac{C\mathcal E_0^3}{\nu^2H^2}.
}
\tag{TL.112}
\]

If instead the current is divided among an unbounded number of individually
weak octave rows, that fact alone does not place it in the diffuse critical-mass
branch.  Equations (TL.100)--(TL.102) control such a branch only when the
corresponding height-to-row-count ratio has the required size.  The many-row
causal allocation case therefore remains unclassified by the present
decomposition.

Thus (TL.112) is a conditional spatial restriction for a bounded-row relay (or
for a separately certified diffuse alternative), not the complete spatial half
of the successor bridge.  A temporal no-return theorem must still show how
replacement of one vanishing-volume participant by the next is charged to the
same finite history.

## 12. An exact one-slot adjoint factorization of a critical record

For the fixed solution \(u\), define on divergence-free vector fields the
frozen-advecting-slot operator

\[
\mathcal L_{u(t)}v
:=-\mathbb P(u(t)\cdot\nabla v)+\nu\Delta v.
\tag{TL.113}
\]

The same velocity field satisfies

\[
\partial_tu=\mathcal L_{u(t)}u.
\tag{TL.114}
\]

Thus (TL.113) is not a second fluid or the full linearized equation.  It is the
exact linear operator obtained by leaving the advected velocity slot open in
the quadratic equation, and it happens to satisfy (TL.114).  It is not the
Frechet tangent of the Navier--Stokes vector field
\(\mathcal N(u):=-\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)+\nu\Delta u\): the latter is

\[
D\mathcal N[u]v
=-\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla v+v\cdot\nabla u)+\nu\Delta v,
\tag{TL.113a}
\]

so the deformation row \(-\mathbb P(v\cdot\nabla u)\) is absent from
\(\mathcal L_u\).

Fix \(s<t\) and a divergence-free terminal covector \(\psi_t\).  The backward
adjoint for this one-slot factorization is the solution of

\[
\partial_r\psi
+\mathbb P(u(r)\cdot\nabla\psi)
+\nu\Delta\psi=0,
\qquad
\psi(t)=\psi_t.
\tag{TL.115}
\]

The projected transport operator is skew-adjoint in \(L^2\).  Equations
(TL.114)--(TL.115) consequently give the exact same-history pairing law

\[
\boxed{
\langle u(r),\psi(r)\rangle
=\langle u(t),\psi_t\rangle
\quad(s\le r\le t).
}
\tag{TL.116}
\]

The adjoint also has an exact irreversible norm law.  Multiplying (TL.115) by
\(\psi\) gives

\[
\boxed{
\|\psi(s)\|_2^2
+2\nu\int_s^t\|\nabla\psi(r)\|_2^2\,dr
=\|\psi_t\|_2^2.
}
\tag{TL.117}
\]

Hence this one-slot adjoint preimage is \(L^2\)-contractive when it is followed
backward.  Advection and the simultaneous pressure projection can rearrange
its frequency and geometry, but cannot create adjoint \(L^2\) mass; the only
change in (TL.117) is the same viscous loss.

### 12.1 A doubling forces one octave of one-slot adjoint-covector motion

Let \(t\) be the endpoint of a first-record doubling interval \([s,t]\),

\[
H(t)=2H(s)>0,
\tag{TL.118}
\]

and choose the terminal critical unit covector

\[
\psi_t
=\frac{\Lambda u(t)}{\sqrt{2H(t)}}.
\tag{TL.119}
\]

It satisfies

\[
\|\psi_t\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2=1,
\qquad
\langle u(t),\psi_t\rangle=\sqrt{2H(t)}.
\tag{TL.120}
\]

Propagate it by (TL.115).  The pairing law and critical duality at the earlier
endpoint give

\[
2H(t)
=|\langle u(s),\psi(s)\rangle|^2
\le 2H(s)\|\psi(s)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2.
\]

Using (TL.118),

\[
\boxed{
\|\psi(s)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2\ge2.
}
\tag{TL.121}
\]

Define the inverse-frequency mean of a nonzero covector by

\[
\bar k_\psi(r)
:=\frac{\|\psi(r)\|_2^2}
{\|\psi(r)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2},
\tag{TL.122}
\]

and the terminal critical mean frequency of the velocity by

\[
\kappa(t)
:=\frac{\|\Lambda u(t)\|_2^2}
{\|\Lambda^{1/2}u(t)\|_2^2}.
\tag{TL.123}
\]

Equations (TL.117), (TL.119), and (TL.121) imply

\[
\bar k_\psi(t)=\kappa(t),
\qquad
\boxed{
\bar k_\psi(s)\le\frac12\kappa(t).
}
\tag{TL.124}
\]

Thus every forward critical doubling has an exact backward one-slot preimage
statement: the terminal critical direction, followed backward through the
one-slot adjoint factorization, must move at least one octave toward lower mean
frequency.  This is not inferred from a shell label and it does not compare
the fluid with a heat-only continuation.  It is forced by the pairing of the
same state with this factorized adjoint preimage.

### 12.2 The octave motion has a non-recounting kinetic-energy charge

Put

\[
X(r):=\frac12\|\psi(r)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2.
\tag{TL.125}
\]

Differentiating with (TL.115) and then integrating backward gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
X(s)-X(t)
=\int_s^t\bigl[
&\langle\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla\psi),\Lambda^{-1}\psi\rangle\\
&-\nu\|\Lambda^{1/2}\psi\|_2^2
\bigr]dr.
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.126}
\]

The first term is the one-slot adjoint transport current.  Integration by
parts, incompressibility, Sobolev, and Young give

\[
\begin{aligned}
|\langle\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla\psi),\Lambda^{-1}\psi\rangle|
&\le C\|\nabla u\|_2
\|\Lambda^{1/2}\psi\|_2\|\psi\|_2\\
&\le\frac\nu2\|\Lambda^{1/2}\psi\|_2^2
+\frac C\nu\|\nabla u\|_2^2\|\psi\|_2^2.
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.127}
\]

By (TL.117), \(\|\psi(r)\|_2\le\|\psi_t\|_2\).  Combining
(TL.126)--(TL.127) with the kinetic-energy identity

\[
\mathcal E(s)-\mathcal E(t)
=\nu\int_s^t\|\nabla u(r)\|_2^2\,dr
\tag{TL.128}
\]

yields

\[
\boxed{
X(s)-X(t)
\le
\frac C{\nu^2}
\|\psi_t\|_2^2
[\mathcal E(s)-\mathcal E(t)].
}
\tag{TL.129}
\]

For (TL.119), \(\|\psi_t\|_2^2=\kappa(t)\).  Equations
(TL.120)--(TL.121) give \(X(s)-X(t)\ge1/2\).  Therefore every first-record
doubling obeys the datum-native charge

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E(s)-\mathcal E(t)
\ge c\frac{\nu^2}{\kappa(t)}.
}
\tag{TL.130}
\]

For pairwise disjoint first-record doubling intervals
\(J_j=[t_j,t_{j+1}]\), the charges are portions of the same decreasing
kinetic energy and cannot be recounted.  Hence

\[
\boxed{
\sum_j\frac1{\kappa(t_{j+1})}
\le\frac{C\mathcal E_0}{\nu^2}.
}
\tag{TL.131}
\]

Equations (TL.124) and (TL.131) are temporal record/preimage estimates in this
note that compare each record with the one-slot adjoint preimage of its terminal
covector under the
one-slot factorization.  They rule out an infinite record sequence whose
terminal critical mean frequencies have nonsummable reciprocals.  The only
remaining sequence would have to push the factorized adjoint covector
through successively faster octave descents with
\(\sum_j\kappa(t_{j+1})^{-1}<\infty\).  Closing (TL.24) now requires proving
that this residual accelerated descent is incompatible with the same
transport current in (TL.126), rather than postulating a separate per-shell
power ceiling.

### 12.3 Exact logarithmic split: deformation or loss, with no reset

The residual octave descent has an exact two-term payment on that same adjoint
covector.  Reverse the time parameter by

\[
\varphi(\tau):=\psi(t-\tau),
\qquad 0\le\tau\le t-s.
\tag{TL.132}
\]

Then

\[
\partial_\tau\varphi
=\mathbb P(u(t-\tau)\cdot\nabla\varphi)+\nu\Delta\varphi.
\tag{TL.133}
\]

Put

\[
Z=\|\varphi\|_2^2,
\qquad
A=\|\varphi\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2,
\qquad
B=\|\Lambda^{1/2}\varphi\|_2^2,
\qquad
D=\|\Lambda\varphi\|_2^2,
\tag{TL.134}
\]

and retain the signed one-slot transport current

\[
\mathfrak C_\varphi
:=\left\langle
\mathbb P(u(t-\tau)\cdot\nabla\varphi),
\Lambda^{-1}\varphi
\right\rangle.
\tag{TL.135}
\]

The projected transport is \(L^2\)-skew, so

\[
\boxed{
Z'=-2\nu D,
\qquad
A'=2\mathfrak C_\varphi-2\nu B.
}
\tag{TL.136}
\]

The adjoint inverse-frequency length is

\[
\ell_\varphi:=\frac AZ=\frac1{\bar k_\varphi}.
\tag{TL.137}
\]

Consequently,

\[
\boxed{
(\log\ell_\varphi)'
=\frac{2\mathfrak C_\varphi}{A}
-2\nu\frac BA
+2\nu\frac DZ.
}
\tag{TL.138}
\]

The spectral moment ratios are ordered:

\[
\frac DZ\ge\frac BA.
\tag{TL.139}
\]

Indeed, if \(m_q=\int|\xi|^q|\widehat\varphi(\xi)|^2d\xi\), then
(TL.139) is \(m_2m_{-1}\ge m_1m_0\), the monotonicity of the ratios of
the log-convex moment sequence \(m_q\).  Thus diffusion coarsens the
*normalized* adjoint spectrum, but it can do so only while (TL.136)
removes actual \(L^2\) stock.

Keeping the signs in (TL.138) gives the one-sided identity

\[
(\log\ell_\varphi)'
\le
\frac{2[\mathfrak C_\varphi]_+}{A}
- (\log Z)'.
\tag{TL.140}
\]

For a doubling interval, (TL.124) says that
\(\ell_\varphi(t-s)/\ell_\varphi(0)\ge2\).  Integration of (TL.140)
therefore gives

\[
\boxed{
\log2
\le
2\int_0^{t-s}
\frac{[\mathfrak C_\varphi(\tau)]_+}{A(\tau)}\,d\tau
+
\log\frac{\|\psi_t\|_2^2}{\|\psi(s)\|_2^2}.
}
\tag{TL.141}
\]

This is a signed backward-adjoint dichotomy in non-resetting form.
The first term is positive scale change seen by the one-slot projected
transport.  The second is the actual fraction of terminal adjoint stock
irreversibly lost before reaching the earlier preimage.  This remains an
identity of the factorization, not a material-ancestry law.

The law telescopes without resetting the covector.  Let

\[
H(t_N)=2^NH(t_0)
\tag{TL.142}
\]

along successive first-record doublings, and propagate the *single* terminal
unit covector

\[
\psi_N=\frac{\Lambda u(t_N)}{\sqrt{2H(t_N)}}
\tag{TL.143}
\]

all the way back to \(t_0\).  Pairing conservation, critical duality, and
\(L^2\) contraction give

\[
\|\psi(t_0)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2\ge2^N,
\qquad
\|\psi(t_0)\|_2^2\le\kappa(t_N).
\tag{TL.144}
\]

Hence the total inverse-frequency length grows by at least \(2^N\).
Applying (TL.140) once over \([t_0,t_N]\), rather than separately on the
subintervals, yields

\[
\boxed{
N\log2
\le
2\int_{t_0}^{t_N}
\frac{[\mathfrak C_{\psi,N}(r)]_+}
{\|\psi_N(r)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2}\,dr
+
\log\frac{\kappa(t_N)}
{\|\psi_N(t_0)\|_2^2}.
}
\tag{TL.145}
\]

The conserved pairing also gives

\[
2H(t_N)
\le2\mathcal E_0\|\psi_N(t_0)\|_2^2,
\tag{TL.146}
\]

and therefore

\[
\boxed{
N\log2
\le
2\int_{t_0}^{t_N}
\frac{[\mathfrak C_{\psi,N}]_+}
{\|\psi_N\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2}\,dr
+
\log\!\left(
\frac{\mathcal E_0\kappa(t_N)}{H(t_N)}
\right).
}
\tag{TL.147}
\]

Equation (TL.147) identifies the accelerated branch without a shell schedule.
An infinite record history must force at least one of two quantities to
diverge on one and the same backward adjoint trajectory:

1. the normalized positive one-slot transport action in (TL.147); or
2. the excess terminal frequency ratio
   \(\mathcal E_0\kappa(t_N)/H(t_N)\).

The second quantity is not fresh power: it is the endpoint record's own
critical content occupying frequencies finer than the minimum forced by its
kinetic energy.  The next contraction step must show that generating this
excess frequency is itself registered by the first term or by the already
telescoping \(L^2\) loss, so that the two alternatives cannot be used to
recount the same descent.

### 12.4 A majority-band one-slot adjoint removes irrelevant ultraviolet dust

The terminal mean \(\kappa(t)\) in (TL.147) can be made large by an
arbitrarily small amount of critical mass at a much higher frequency than the
record population.  That is a defect of the full terminal covector, not a
physical alternative.  The one-slot adjoint law can instead be applied to a
finite band carrying a prescribed majority of the actual record.

Fix

\[
\frac12<q<1.
\tag{TL.148}
\]

At a terminal time \(t\) with \(H(t)>0\), the finite critical spectral
measure admits a bounded radial interval

\[
B=[K_-,K_+],
\qquad 0<K_-<K_+<\infty,
\tag{TL.149}
\]

such that, for the orthogonal Fourier projection \(P_B\),

\[
H_B(t)
:=\frac12\|\Lambda^{1/2}P_Bu(t)\|_2^2,
\qquad
q_B(t):=\frac{H_B(t)}{H(t)}\ge q.
\tag{TL.150}
\]

Sharp projections can be replaced by smooth inner approximations and the
identities below recovered by \(L^2\) convergence.  Define the terminal
majority covector

\[
\psi_{B,t}
:=\frac{\Lambda P_Bu(t)}{\sqrt{2H_B(t)}}.
\tag{TL.151}
\]

It has the exact normalization and pairing

\[
\|\psi_{B,t}\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2=1,
\qquad
\langle u(t),\psi_{B,t}\rangle=\sqrt{2H_B(t)}.
\tag{TL.152}
\]

Propagate this one covector backward by the one-slot adjoint (TL.115).  If
\(H(t)=2H(s)\), pairing conservation and critical duality give

\[
2H_B(t)
\le 2H(s)\|\psi_B(s)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2,
\]

and therefore

\[
\boxed{
\|\psi_B(s)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2
\ge 2q_B(t)\ge2q.
}
\tag{TL.153}
\]

The terminal mean frequency seen by this covector is

\[
\kappa_B(t)
:=\|\psi_{B,t}\|_2^2
=\frac{\|\Lambda P_Bu(t)\|_2^2}
{\|\Lambda^{1/2}P_Bu(t)\|_2^2},
\qquad
K_-\le\kappa_B(t)\le K_+.
\tag{TL.154}
\]

Applying (TL.129) to \(\psi_B\) and using
\(X(s)-X(t)\ge q-\tfrac12\) yields the majority-population charge

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E(s)-\mathcal E(t)
\ge
c\left(q-\frac12\right)
\frac{\nu^2}{\kappa_B(t)}.
}
\tag{TL.155}
\]

Thus a remote trace of terminal ultraviolet mass can no longer lower the
charge by inflating the frequency denominator: \(\kappa_B\) belongs to a
band carrying at least \(q\) of the terminal critical state.

The same refinement applies to the logarithmic no-reset law.  Suppose

\[
H(t_N)=2^NH(t_0),
\]

choose one terminal band \(B_N\) satisfying (TL.150) at \(t_N\), and
propagate the single \(\psi_{B_N,t_N}\) all the way to \(t_0\).  Then

\[
\|\psi_{B_N}(t_0)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2
\ge q\,2^N.
\tag{TL.156}
\]

Writing \(Z_{B_N}(t_0)=\|\psi_{B_N}(t_0)\|_2^2\), integration of
(TL.140) over the whole interval gives

\[
N\log2+\log q
\le
2\int_{t_0}^{t_N}
\frac{[\mathfrak C_{\psi,B_N}(r)]_+}
{\|\psi_{B_N}(r)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2}\,dr
+\log\frac{\kappa_{B_N}(t_N)}{Z_{B_N}(t_0)}.
\tag{TL.157}
\]

The same conserved pairing now gives

\[
Z_{B_N}(t_0)
\ge\frac{H_{B_N}(t_N)}{\mathcal E_0}
\ge\frac{qH(t_N)}{\mathcal E_0}.
\]

Consequently,

\[
\boxed{
N\log2
\le
2\int_{t_0}^{t_N}
\frac{[\mathfrak C_{\psi,B_N}]_+}
{\|\psi_{B_N}\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2}\,dr
+\log\left(
\frac{\mathcal E_0\kappa_{B_N}(t_N)}
{q^2H(t_N)}
\right).
}
\tag{TL.158}
\]

The endpoint term has a direct stored-energy meaning.  Put

\[
E_B(t):=\frac12\|P_Bu(t)\|_2^2,
\qquad
\lambda_B(t):=\frac{H_B(t)}{E_B(t)},
\qquad
W_B:=\log\frac{K_+}{K_-}.
\tag{TL.159}
\]

Moment ordering and the support of \(P_B\) imply

\[
1\le\frac{\kappa_B}{\lambda_B}\le e^{W_B}.
\tag{TL.160}
\]

Since \(q_B=H_B/H\), there is the exact factorization

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\mathcal E_0\kappa_B}{H}
=q_B\frac{\mathcal E_0}{E_B}
\frac{\kappa_B}{\lambda_B}.
}
\tag{TL.161}
\]

Combining (TL.158)--(TL.161) gives the fully representative bound

\[
\boxed{
N\log2
\le
2\int_{t_0}^{t_N}
\frac{[\mathfrak C_{\psi,B_N}]_+}
{\|\psi_{B_N}\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2}\,dr
+\log\frac{\mathcal E_0}{E_{B_N}(t_N)}
+W_{B_N}+2|\log q|.
}
\tag{TL.162}
\]

Equation (TL.162) removes the artificial far-tail branch from (TL.147).
An accumulated critical record is now registered by three properties of its
terminal majority covector: positive one-slot transport action, loss of its
adjoint \(L^2\) stock, or
growth of the logarithmic scale width carrying the majority.  These are not
three complete-carrier facts.  They are one-slot transport, an adjoint-stock
ratio, and terminal scale geometry.

For the throughput--lifetime program this identifies the precise remaining
contraction.  A record population may use less kinetic energy as it moves to
finer scale, but (TL.44f) says that its delivered critical throughput must
still pass through the one signed receiver current, while (TL.157) supplies a
separate one-slot adjoint transport-or-stock-loss identity.  The required
final step is to connect these and obtain a datum-bound for the right side of
(TL.162) without treating the three readings as independent ledgers.

All identities in this section are therefore exact support laws for the
one-slot factorization.  Because (TL.113) omits the deformation row displayed
in (TL.113a), they do not constitute the complete material or Frechet
predecessor required by the VPI goal.

## 13. The fixed-time affine-chord VPI receiver and its participating-energy lifetime

The spectral current above is a reconstructive readout of the full velocity
field.  An exact receiver law also exists on a fixed-time all-intermediate
affine-chord reconstruction.  This reconstruction uses the same velocity field
at each time, but its chord coordinates are not persistent material labels.

For a center \(z\), chord \(R=\rho n\), and every intermediate fraction
\(0<\theta<1\), put

\[
y=z-\theta\rho n,
\qquad
x=z+(1-\theta)\rho n,
\tag{TL.163}
\]

and retain the affine velocity residual

\[
C_{\theta,\rho,n}(z)
=u(z)-\bigl[(1-\theta)u(y)+\theta u(x)\bigr].
\tag{TL.164}
\]

After integration over \(z,n,\theta\) with the positive weight
\([\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)\rho^2]^{-1}\), denote its square, center-gradient
square, and signed radial current by

\[
\mathfrak M(\rho,t),
\qquad
\mathfrak G(\rho,t),
\qquad
\mathfrak F(\rho,t).
\tag{TL.165}
\]

These are the all-intermediate cocycle quantities, not a selected pair.  The
complete pressure, transport, incompressibility, geometry and viscosity rows
give the exact integrated law

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\mathfrak M
+\rho^{-4}\partial_\rho(\rho^4\mathfrak F)
=-2\nu\mathfrak G,
\qquad
\mathfrak M,\mathfrak G\ge0.
}
\tag{TL.166}
\]

The radial divergence in (TL.166) contains the critical work created by
changing chord length.  Absorb that work into the complete chord-critical
current

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(\rho,t)
:=\mathfrak F(\rho,t)
-4\int_\rho^\infty\frac{\mathfrak F(s,t)}s\,ds.
}
\tag{TL.167}
\]

For a smooth decaying history the improper integral is the same finite radial
boundary appearing in the critical-height balance.  Since

\[
\partial_\rho\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}
=\partial_\rho\mathfrak F+\frac4\rho\mathfrak F,
\]

(TL.166) becomes the source-free carrier law

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\mathfrak M
+\partial_\rho\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}
=-2\nu\mathfrak G.
}
\tag{TL.168}
\]

Its endpoints recover the complete global current:

\[
\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(\infty,t)=0,
\qquad
\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(0,t)=4P_H(t),
\tag{TL.169}
\]

while

\[
\int_0^\infty\mathfrak M\,d\rho=4H,
\qquad
\int_0^\infty\mathfrak G\,d\rho=2D.
\tag{TL.170}
\]

Thus integration of (TL.168) is exactly
\(H'+\nu D=P_H\).

Let \(a(t)<b(t)\) be positive absolutely continuous chord lengths and set

\[
\mathsf S_{a,b}(t)
:=\int_{a(t)}^{b(t)}\mathfrak M(\rho,t)\,d\rho,
\qquad
\mathsf L_{a,b}(t)
:=2\nu\int_{a(t)}^{b(t)}\mathfrak G(\rho,t)\,d\rho.
\tag{TL.171}
\]

The relative carrier current through a moving chord boundary is

\[
\mathsf J_c(t)
:=\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(c(t),t)
-c'(t)\mathfrak M(c(t),t).
\tag{TL.172}
\]

Reynolds transport in (TL.168) gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf S_{a,b}'+\mathsf L_{a,b}
=\mathsf J_a-\mathsf J_b.
}
\tag{TL.173}
\]

Hence, on every interval \([s,t]\),

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t\mathsf J_b\,dr
=\int_s^t\mathsf J_a\,dr
+\mathsf S_{a,b}(s)-\mathsf S_{a,b}(t)
-\int_s^t\mathsf L_{a,b}\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.174}
\]

Adjacent chord-length bands share the identical boundary current with
opposite orientation, so every internal \(\mathsf J_c\) cancels.  Equation
(TL.174) is therefore an exact signed moving-band balance on this fixed-time
affine reconstruction.  It does not by itself provide labelled ancestry or the
requested causal contraction across successors.

### 13.1 The same carrier identifies the energy that participates in its loss

The lifetime in (TL.174) is not assigned from the chord label \(\rho\) alone.
It is determined by the kinetic energy actually participating in the same
affine defect.  Since translations and affine interpolation commute with
\(\Lambda\), define

\[
\mathfrak E(\rho,t)
:=\mathfrak M[\Lambda^{-1}u](\rho,t).
\tag{TL.175}
\]

Equivalently, \(\mathfrak E\) is obtained from (TL.165) by replacing
\(C\) with \(\Lambda^{-1}C\).  The gradient square already satisfies

\[
\mathfrak G(\rho,t)
=\mathfrak M[\Lambda u](\rho,t).
\tag{TL.176}
\]

Hilbert-space interpolation on the single combined
\((z,n,\theta)\)-carrier gives the exact pointwise-in-\(\rho\) inequality

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak M(\rho,t)^2
\le\mathfrak E(\rho,t)\mathfrak G(\rho,t).
}
\tag{TL.177}
\]

Indeed,
\(\langle\Lambda^{-1}C,\Lambda C\rangle=\|C\|_2^2\), and one
application of Cauchy--Schwarz after all intermediate points have been retained
gives (TL.177).  No separately selected donor and receiver occur.

The participating-energy stock has an exact datum ceiling over all logarithmic
chord scales.  Applying the all-intermediate logarithmic endpoint identity to
\(\Lambda^{-1}u\), first with homogeneous cutoffs and then by monotone
limit, yields

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^\infty\mathfrak E(\rho,t)\frac{d\rho}{\rho}
=\frac{4}{3\pi}\mathcal E(t)
\le\frac{4}{3\pi}\mathcal E_0.
}
\tag{TL.178}
\]

Thus \(\mathfrak E\) is not a freely reusable capacity.  It is a positive
all-scale decomposition of the kinetic energy remaining in this one fluid.

### 13.2 Exact throughput floor over the receiver's own lifetime

For a dyadic physical chord band \([r,2r]\), set

\[
\mathsf S_r
:=\int_r^{2r}\mathfrak M\,d\rho,
\qquad
\mathsf G_r
:=\int_r^{2r}\mathfrak G\,d\rho,
\qquad
\mathsf E_r
:=\frac1r\int_r^{2r}\mathfrak E\,d\rho.
\tag{TL.179}
\]

Integrating (TL.177) once more in \(\rho\) gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf S_r^2
\le r\mathsf E_r\mathsf G_r,
\qquad
\mathsf G_r
\ge\frac{\mathsf S_r^2}{r\mathsf E_r}.
}
\tag{TL.180}
\]

The convention is that \(\mathsf S_r=0\) whenever \(\mathsf E_r=0\).
Moreover, for every base length \(R>0\), the dyadic bands partition the
positive chord axis and (TL.178) gives

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}\mathsf E_{2^jR}(t)
\le\frac{8}{3\pi}\mathcal E(t)
\le\frac{8}{3\pi}\mathcal E_0.
}
\tag{TL.181}
\]

This is the finite all-scale layer generated by the datum: each
\(\mathsf E_r\) is the kinetic energy participating in the corresponding
all-intermediate chord deformation, and the same kinetic energy cannot be
assigned at full value to every dyadic chord scale.

For fixed \(r\), (TL.173) and (TL.180) now give the carrier-native nonlinear
receiver inequality

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf S_r'
+\frac{2\nu\mathsf S_r^2}{r\mathsf E_r}
\le
\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(r)-\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(2r).
}
\tag{TL.182}
\]

The corresponding instantaneous viscous memory satisfies

\[
\boxed{
\tau_{\rm VPI}(r,t)
:=\frac{\mathsf S_r}{2\nu\mathsf G_r}
\le\frac{r\mathsf E_r}{2\nu\mathsf S_r}.
}
\tag{TL.183}
\]

This is the exact form of the physical lifetime statement.  When the
participating energy of a critical population obeys
\(\mathsf E_r\asymp r\mathsf S_r\), (TL.183) is the familiar
\(r^2/\nu\) clock.  When it does not, (TL.183) retains the actual excess
surrounding-field participation instead of silently replacing that
participation by an isolated vortex.

Finally, let \([s,t]\) be a last-passage doubling interval for this same
carrier band,

\[
\mathsf S_r(s)=A,
\qquad
\mathsf S_r(t)=2A,
\qquad
A\le\mathsf S_r(q)\le2A.
\tag{TL.184}
\]

Integrating the equality (TL.173) and then using (TL.180) yields

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\int_s^t
\bigl[\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(r,q)
-\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(2r,q)\bigr]dq
&=A+2\nu\int_s^t\mathsf G_r(q)dq\\
&\ge
A+\frac{2\nu A^2}{r}
\int_s^t\frac{dq}{\mathsf E_r(q)}.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.185}
\]

Since every \(\mathsf E_r\le8\mathcal E_0/(3\pi)\),

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{t-s}\int_s^t
\bigl[\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(r,q)
-\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(2r,q)\bigr]dq
\ge
\frac{A}{t-s}
+c\frac{\nu A^2}{r\mathcal E_0}.
}
\tag{TL.186}
\]

Equations (TL.181), (TL.183), and (TL.185) preserve the two concepts that a
single power ceiling would flatten.  The receiver's lifetime shortens according
to its own participating energy, while the complete signed throughput needed
to strengthen it is measured across the same all-intermediate carrier and is
charged after the simultaneous irreversible loss.  The remaining temporal
step is no longer to invent a finite layer: (TL.181) is that layer.  It is to
prove from the evolution of this same layer that a moving critical receiver
cannot successively occupy its dyadic components faster than (TL.185) depletes
or transports their participating energy.

### 13.3 The participating energy obeys its own non-recounting VPI current

The finite layer in (TL.178) is itself an evolving receiver, not a static
budget assigned separately from (TL.168).  Let \(A_\rho\) be the positive
self-adjoint multiplier defined by

\[
\mathfrak E(\rho,t)=\langle u(t),A_\rho u(t)\rangle.
\tag{TL.187}
\]

Because \(A_\rho\) is the all-intermediate affine-defect multiplier divided
by \(\Lambda^2\), it commutes with \(\Lambda\) and

\[
\langle\Lambda u,A_\rho\Lambda u\rangle
=\mathfrak M(\rho,t).
\tag{TL.188}
\]

Differentiate (TL.187) with the one Navier--Stokes history and retain the full
Leray-projected nonlinear contribution to this readout:

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\mathfrak E(\rho,t)
=\mathfrak T_E(\rho,t)-2\nu\mathfrak M(\rho,t),
\qquad
\mathfrak T_E
:=-2\langle\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u),A_\rho u\rangle.
}
\tag{TL.189}
\]

The logarithmic resolution of the identity behind (TL.178) is

\[
\int_0^\infty A_\rho\frac{d\rho}{\rho}
=\frac{2}{3\pi}I
\tag{TL.190}
\]

as a quadratic-form identity.  Since the complete projected transport does
zero global kinetic-energy work,

\[
\int_0^\infty\mathfrak T_E(\rho,t)\frac{d\rho}{\rho}=0.
\tag{TL.191}
\]

Therefore the signed log-chord energy current

\[
\boxed{
\Pi_E(\rho,t)
:=\int_\rho^\infty
\mathfrak T_E(s,t)\frac{ds}{s}
}
\tag{TL.192}
\]

absorbs the entire nonlinear redistribution.  Since
\(\rho\partial_\rho\Pi_E=-\mathfrak T_E\), (TL.189) becomes

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\mathfrak E
+\rho\partial_\rho\Pi_E
=-2\nu\mathfrak M.
}
\tag{TL.193}
\]

This is the kinetic-energy receiver law on the same all-intermediate VPI
carrier.  Pressure has not been separated: it remains inside the Leray-
projected current in (TL.189).

For a fixed dyadic chord band define the exact log-scale energy and critical
loss stock

\[
\mathbf E_r(t)
:=\int_r^{2r}\mathfrak E(\rho,t)\frac{d\rho}{\rho},
\qquad
\mathbf M_r(t)
:=\int_r^{2r}\mathfrak M(\rho,t)\frac{d\rho}{\rho}.
\tag{TL.194}
\]

Integration of (TL.193) gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathbf E_r'(t)+2\nu\mathbf M_r(t)
=\Pi_E(r,t)-\Pi_E(2r,t).
}
\tag{TL.195}
\]

For adjacent dyadic bands every internal \(\Pi_E\) cancels.  Moreover,

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}\mathbf E_{2^jR}(t)
=\frac{4}{3\pi}\mathcal E(t)
\le\frac{4}{3\pi}\mathcal E_0.
}
\tag{TL.196}
\]

Thus the initial datum supplies not only a finite instantaneous layer but its
exact signed transport law: a successor scale can acquire participating
energy only through the current leaving its neighbor, and that current is
counted once.

The log stock and the averaged stock in (TL.179) are equivalent on one dyadic
band:

\[
\mathbf E_r\le\mathsf E_r\le2\mathbf E_r.
\tag{TL.197}
\]

Consequently (TL.180) sharpens to

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf G_r
\ge\frac{\mathsf S_r^2}{2r\mathbf E_r}.
}
\tag{TL.198}
\]

Define the dimensionless log-stock concentration of this same carrier band by

\[
\boxed{
\Gamma_r^{\log}(t)
:=\frac{r\mathsf S_r(t)}{\mathbf E_r(t)}
}
\tag{TL.199}
\]

when \(\mathbf E_r>0\); (TL.177) makes \(\mathsf S_r=0\) when
\(\mathbf E_r=0\).  Since \(\rho\le2r\) on the band,

\[
\mathbf M_r\ge\frac{\mathsf S_r}{2r}.
\tag{TL.200}
\]

Equations (TL.198)--(TL.200) show that the critical stock and its participating
energy leak on the same clock:

\[
\boxed{
\frac{2\nu\mathsf G_r}{\mathsf S_r}
\ge\frac{\nu\Gamma_r^{\log}}{r^2},
\qquad
\frac{2\nu\mathbf M_r}{\mathbf E_r}
\ge\frac{\nu\Gamma_r^{\log}}{r^2}.
}
\tag{TL.201}
\]

Accordingly, the two exact receiver balances imply

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf S_r'
+\frac{\nu\Gamma_r^{\log}}{r^2}\mathsf S_r
\le\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(r)-\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(2r),
}
\tag{TL.202}
\]

\[
\boxed{
\mathbf E_r'
+\frac{\nu\Gamma_r^{\log}}{r^2}\mathbf E_r
\le\Pi_E(r)-\Pi_E(2r).
}
\tag{TL.203}
\]

This is the exact VPI version of the \(r^2/\nu\) diffusion clock.  If
\(\Gamma_r^{\log}\) is order one, both the critical organization and the kinetic
energy participating in it have lifetime at most a constant multiple of
\(r^2/\nu\).  If \(\Gamma_r^{\log}\) is larger, both lifetimes are shorter.  If it
is smaller, the receiver is not an isolated fine structure: its critical
stock is backed by proportionally more of the surrounding field's actual
participating energy, and that energy remains explicitly present in
(TL.195)--(TL.196).

On a last-passage doubling interval (TL.184), (TL.202) gives the refined
throughput floor

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t
\bigl[\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(r,q)
-\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(2r,q)\bigr]dq
\ge
A+\frac{\nu A}{r^2}
\int_s^t\Gamma_r^{\log}(q)dq.
}
\tag{TL.204}
\]

Equations (TL.195), (TL.202), and (TL.203) are the joined two-level
throughput--lifetime law.  They preserve all of the physical content at once:
the finer organization is a receiver, the energy enabling it is another
stock of the same carrier rather than a new source, every internal transfer
cancels under concatenation, and viscosity produces simultaneous loss of both
stocks on one concentration-adjusted clock.  Nonlinear deformation remains in
the shared signed currents.

### 13.4 Exact concentration evolution: current mismatch minus irreversible dispersion

The averaged-stock concentration comparable to the common clock in (TL.201)
has an exact signed evolution.  Rewrite
(TL.193) in conservative \(d\rho\) form.  Define

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal Q_E(\rho,t)
:=\rho\Pi_E(\rho,t)
+\int_\rho^\infty\Pi_E(s,t)\,ds.
}
\tag{TL.205}
\]

Then
\(\partial_\rho\mathcal Q_E=\rho\partial_\rho\Pi_E\), so

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\mathfrak E
+\partial_\rho\mathcal Q_E
=-2\nu\mathfrak M.
}
\tag{TL.206}
\]

For a fixed dyadic band, using the averaged participating energy from
(TL.179),

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf E_r'
+\frac{2\nu}{r}\mathsf S_r
=\frac{\mathcal Q_E(r)-\mathcal Q_E(2r)}r.
}
\tag{TL.207}
\]

This is the second non-recounting law in exactly the same \(d\rho\) geometry
as the critical receiver law.  Internal \(\mathcal Q_E\) currents cancel
when adjacent chord bands are concatenated.

Assume \(\mathsf S_r,\mathsf E_r>0\), and define the single-carrier Rayleigh
concentration

\[
\boxed{
\Gamma_r:=\frac{r\mathsf S_r}{\mathsf E_r}.
}
\tag{TL.207a}
\]

By (TL.197), it is comparable to the log-stock clock without being identified
with it:

\[
\boxed{
\frac12\Gamma_r^{\log}\le\Gamma_r\le\Gamma_r^{\log}.
}
\tag{TL.207b}
\]

Differentiate this exact Rayleigh quotient using the
equalities (TL.173) and (TL.207).  The result is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
(\log\Gamma_r)'
&+2\nu\left(
\frac{\mathsf G_r}{\mathsf S_r}
-\frac{\mathsf S_r}{r\mathsf E_r}
\right)\\
&=
\frac{\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(r)-\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(2r)}
{\mathsf S_r}
-
\frac{\mathcal Q_E(r)-\mathcal Q_E(2r)}
{r\mathsf E_r}.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.208}
\]

The simultaneous weakening term is nonnegative by (TL.180):

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak V_r
:=\frac{\mathsf G_r}{\mathsf S_r}
-\frac{\mathsf S_r}{r\mathsf E_r}
=\frac{
r\mathsf E_r\mathsf G_r-\mathsf S_r^2
}{r\mathsf E_r\mathsf S_r}
\ge0.
}
\tag{TL.209}
\]

Define the normalized complete-current mismatch of this one carrier by

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak R_r
:=
\frac{\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(r)-\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(2r)}
{\mathsf S_r}
-
\frac{\mathcal Q_E(r)-\mathcal Q_E(2r)}
{r\mathsf E_r}.
}
\tag{TL.210}
\]

Then the concentration law is simply

\[
\boxed{
(\log\Gamma_r)'+2\nu\mathfrak V_r=\mathfrak R_r,
\qquad
(\log\Gamma_r)'\le\mathfrak R_r.
}
\tag{TL.211}
\]

This does not split the fluid into an energy mechanism and a critical
mechanism.  The two currents in \(\mathfrak R_r\) are consecutive moments of
the same all-intermediate VPI current, and \(\Gamma_r\) is their single
scale-shape quotient.  Equation (TL.211) says that critical concentration can
increase only through positive shape work by that complete current; the
simultaneous viscous row contributes the nonnegative dispersion
\(2\nu\mathfrak V_r\) in the opposite direction.

The dispersion is strictly positive for every nonzero decaying
\(\mathbb R^3\) carrier band.  Equality in (TL.180) would require equality in
the Hilbert interpolation (TL.177) across almost every active
\((\rho,z,n,\theta)\), hence

\[
\Lambda^2C_{\theta,\rho,n}=\lambda C_{\theta,\rho,n}
\tag{TL.212}
\]

with one constant \(\lambda\) on the combined active carrier.  Its Fourier
transform would be supported on the sphere \(|\xi|^2=\lambda\), a null set
for an \(L^2(\mathbb R^3)\) function.  Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf S_r>0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\mathfrak V_r>0.
}
\tag{TL.213}
\]

This proves strict positivity of the fixed-time viscous weakening coefficient
for every nonzero reconstructed chord band.  The nonlinear current mismatch
\(\mathfrak R_r\) may offset it, and no persistent material-cohort loss follows.
The remaining quantitative no-Zeno step is to
show that successive record-strength transfers cannot make the positive
current mismatch in (TL.211) approach the zero-dispersion equality surface
fast enough to keep the accumulated \(\mathfrak V_r\) summable.  Unlike a
generic alignment slogan, (TL.209)--(TL.213) identify that equality surface
and the exact VPI current that would have to maintain it.

### 13.5 Quantitative alternative: lifetime weakening or scale-shape spreading

The strictness in (TL.213) has an exact uncertainty lower bound.  Assemble the
single combined carrier vector

\[
g_r(\rho,z,n,\theta)
:=\frac{\Lambda^{-1}C_{\theta,\rho,n}(z)}
{\pi\sqrt{\theta(1-\theta)}\rho},
\qquad r<\rho<2r,
\tag{TL.214}
\]

in its natural product \(L^2\) space.  With \(L_z=-\Delta_z\), the three
carrier moments are exactly

\[
\|g_r\|_2^2=r\mathsf E_r,
\qquad
\langle g_r,L_zg_r\rangle=\mathsf S_r,
\qquad
\|L_zg_r\|_2^2=\mathsf G_r.
\tag{TL.215}
\]

For any center \(z_0\), let

\[
\mathcal A_{z_0}
:=-i\left((z-z_0)\cdot\nabla_z+\frac32\right)
\tag{TL.216}
\]

be the self-adjoint generator of center dilations.  Define the normalized
scale-shape spread of this same carrier by

\[
\boxed{
\sigma_{{\rm dil},r}^2
:=\inf_{z_0\in\mathbb R^3,\ c\in\mathbb R}
\frac{\|(\mathcal A_{z_0}-c)g_r\|_2^2}
{\|g_r\|_2^2}
\in[0,\infty].
}
\tag{TL.217}
\]

The commutator identity

\[
i[L_z,\mathcal A_{z_0}]=2L_z
\tag{TL.218}
\]

and the Robertson inequality give

\[
\operatorname{Var}_{g_r}(L_z)
\,\sigma_{{\rm dil},r}^2
\ge
\left(\frac{\mathsf S_r}{r\mathsf E_r}\right)^2.
\tag{TL.219}
\]

But

\[
\operatorname{Var}_{g_r}(L_z)
=\frac{\mathsf G_r}{r\mathsf E_r}
