-\left(\frac{\mathsf S_r}{r\mathsf E_r}\right)^2
=\frac{\Gamma_r}{r^2}\mathfrak V_r.
\tag{TL.220}
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak V_r\,\sigma_{{\rm dil},r}^2
\ge\frac{\Gamma_r}{r^2}.
}
\tag{TL.221}
\]

Inserted into (TL.211), this yields the quantitative joined alternative

\[
\boxed{
(\log\Gamma_r)'
+\frac{2\nu\Gamma_r}
{r^2\sigma_{{\rm dil},r}^2}
\le\mathfrak R_r,
}
\tag{TL.222}
\]

with the convention that the displayed weakening lower bound is zero when the
dilation variance is infinite.

Equation (TL.221) is a pointwise uncertainty alternative for the fixed-time
reconstruction.  Bounded center-dilation variance forces the displayed
\(r^{-2}\) viscous coefficient; making that coefficient small entails a
delocalized reconstructed band at that same time.  It does not track one
population through time or prove loss of future coherent delivery.

### 13.6 The productive current vanishes at the zero-dispersion surface

The current mismatch in (TL.210) is itself transverse to the equality surface
of (TL.177).  Let \(\mathcal C_{\theta,\rho,n}[f]\) denote the affine
residual (TL.164) with \(u\) replaced by \(f\), and put

\[
\mathcal N_r
:=-\frac{
\Lambda^{-1}\mathcal C_{\theta,\rho,n}
[\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)]
}{\pi\sqrt{\theta(1-\theta)}\rho}
\tag{TL.223}
\]

on the same combined carrier space as \(g_r\).  This is the nonlinear
contribution to the tangent of that fixed-coordinate affine readout.  At fixed
chord coordinates,

\[
\boxed{
\partial_tg_r=\mathcal N_r-\nu L_zg_r.
}
\tag{TL.224}
\]

Let

\[
U_r:=\|g_r\|_2^2=r\mathsf E_r,
\qquad
\lambda_r:=\frac{\mathsf S_r}{U_r}
=\frac{\Gamma_r}{r^2}.
\tag{TL.225}
\]

Differentiating the first two moments in (TL.215) gives

\[
U_r'
=2\operatorname{Re}\langle g_r,\mathcal N_r\rangle
-2\nu\mathsf S_r,
\tag{TL.226}
\]

\[
\mathsf S_r'
=2\operatorname{Re}\langle L_zg_r,\mathcal N_r\rangle
-2\nu\mathsf G_r.
\tag{TL.227}
\]

Comparison with (TL.173) and (TL.207) shows that these two pairings are
exactly the two boundary-current differences already present there.  Hence
the normalized current mismatch has the covariance form

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak R_r
=\frac{2\operatorname{Re}
\langle(L_z-\lambda_r)g_r,\mathcal N_r\rangle}
{\mathsf S_r}.
}
\tag{TL.228}
\]

Moreover,

\[
\|(L_z-\lambda_r)g_r\|_2^2
=\mathsf G_r-\frac{\mathsf S_r^2}{U_r}
=\mathsf S_r\mathfrak V_r.
\tag{TL.229}
\]

Define the actual normalized tangent severity

\[
\mathfrak A_r
:=\frac{\|\mathcal N_r\|_2^2}{\mathsf S_r}.
\tag{TL.230}
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz in (TL.228) gives

\[
\boxed{
|\mathfrak R_r|
\le2\sqrt{\mathfrak V_r\mathfrak A_r}.
}
\tag{TL.231}
\]

Combining this with the exact concentration law (TL.211) yields

\[
\boxed{
(\log\Gamma_r)'
+2\nu\mathfrak V_r
\le2\sqrt{\mathfrak V_r\mathfrak A_r},
}
\tag{TL.232}
\]

and therefore the two useful absorptions

\[
\boxed{
(\log\Gamma_r)'
+\nu\mathfrak V_r
\le\frac{\mathfrak A_r}{\nu},
\qquad
(\log\Gamma_r)'
\le\frac{\mathfrak A_r}{2\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.233}
\]

In particular, \(\mathfrak R_r=0\) at exact zero dispersion: a perfectly
single-frequency carrier cannot increase its critical concentration at first
order.  The fixed-coordinate readout tangent may move the state away from that surface, but
that motion first creates the dispersion that appears with a negative sign in
(TL.211).  This is the precise same-instant content of the physical
stretchability loss; it is not an assumed monotone alignment factor.

The quantity \(\mathfrak A_r\) is not declared datum-finite here.  It is the
explicit squared norm of the same pressure-completed nonlinear readout
contribution whose
pairing generates both receiver currents.  The ultraviolet closure now has a
sharply typed form: prove that its scale--time contribution on successive
record bands is controlled by the finite participating-energy current
(TL.195), rather than bounding an unrelated source norm.

### 13.7 The datum-finite nonlinear reach and the exact temporal-compression fork

The tangent in (TL.223) does possess a datum-finite all-scale action, but its
natural time exponent is one rather than two.  This distinction can be derived
on the same carrier without estimating a separately prescribed source.

For an arbitrary divergence-free field \(f\), the all-intermediate resolution
behind (TL.170) gives

\[
\int_0^\infty \mathfrak M[f](\rho)\,d\rho
=2\|f\|_{\dot H^{1/2}}^2.
\tag{TL.234}
\]

Apply this identity to

\[
f=\Lambda^{-1}\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u).
\tag{TL.235}
\]

For every base chord length \(R>0\), the dyadic carrier bands are disjoint in
\(\rho\), so (TL.223)--(TL.235) give the exact resolution

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}
\|\mathcal N_{2^jR}(t)\|_2^2
=2\|\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)(t)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}^2.
}
\tag{TL.236}
\]

The critical negative-order norm on the right is controlled at the kinetic-
energy level.  Sobolev duality, boundedness of the Leray projection on
\(L^{3/2}\), and \(\dot H^1\hookrightarrow L^6\) yield

\[
\boxed{
\|\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}
\le C\|u\cdot\nabla u\|_{3/2}
\le C\|u\|_6\|\nabla u\|_2
\le C\|\nabla u\|_2^2.
}
\tag{TL.237}
\]

Consequently the one continuing VPI history has finite total nonlinear reach
in the all-scale affine-readout metric:

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T
\left(
\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}
\|\mathcal N_{2^jR}(t)\|_2^2
\right)^{1/2}dt
\le \frac{C\mathcal E_0}{\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.238}
\]

This is the formal version of finite VPI reach generated by the datum.  It is
not a viscosity-only comparison: the vector being measured is the
Leray-projected nonlinear readout contribution, and the upper bound follows from the kinetic
energy loss of the same history.

The concentration equation identifies exactly what one octave of productive
shape change requires.  Let \(J=[s,t]\) be an interval on which
\(\mathsf S_r>0\) and

\[
\log\frac{\Gamma_r(t)}{\Gamma_r(s)}\ge\delta>0.
\tag{TL.239}
\]

Put

\[
V_J:=\int_J\mathfrak V_r(q)\,dq,
\qquad
A_J:=\int_J\mathfrak A_r(q)\,dq.
\tag{TL.240}
\]

Integration of (TL.232) and Cauchy--Schwarz in time give

\[
\delta+2\nu V_J
\le2\sqrt{V_JA_J}.
\tag{TL.241}
\]

Maximizing the right side minus \(2\nu V_J\) over \(V_J\ge0\) proves the
fixed normalized tangent quantum

\[
\boxed{
A_J\ge2\nu\delta.
}
\tag{TL.242}
\]

Thus a concentration octave cannot be produced by vanishing nonlinear-readout
action, even when the active scale changes.  Equations (TL.238) and (TL.242)
also expose the only possible mismatch between finite datum reach and an
infinite successor history.  If \(J_m\) are disjoint concentration-octave
intervals and \(r_m\) their carrier scales, then

\[
\sum_m\int_{J_m}\|\mathcal N_{r_m}\|_2dt
\le
\int_0^T
\left(\sum_j\|\mathcal N_{2^jR}\|_2^2\right)^{1/2}dt
\le\frac{C\mathcal E_0}{\nu}
\tag{TL.243}
\]

whenever the selected scales lie in one dyadic lattice.  An arbitrary band
\([r,2r]\) is covered by at most two adjacent bands of a fixed dyadic lattice,
so the same estimate holds with one universal overlap constant.  On the other
hand, (TL.242) requires

\[
\int_{J_m}
\frac{\|\mathcal N_{r_m}\|_2^2}{\mathsf S_{r_m}}dt
\ge2\nu\delta.
\tag{TL.244}
\]

Therefore an infinite concentration succession would have to compress a
finite total amount of nonlinear readout displacement into increasingly
large instantaneous tangent severity relative to the stock being moved.  It
cannot evade by switching scales or receivers: (TL.236) places every band in
one all-scale tangent, and disjoint time intervals are counted once in
(TL.243).  The ultraviolet contraction is now reduced to the constitutive
VPI assertion that this temporal compression cannot persist while the same
tangent simultaneously creates the dispersion in (TL.241).  This is narrower
than a free \(L_t^2\) source estimate: only the record-selected ratio in
(TL.244), coupled to its own dispersion and finite reach, remains.

The compression has an immediate quantitative consequence.  Write

\[
\ell_m:=\int_{J_m}\|\mathcal N_{r_m}(t)\|_2dt
\tag{TL.245}
\]

and suppose the concentration octave is carried while
\(\mathsf S_{r_m}\ge s_m>0\).  By (TL.244),

\[
2\nu\delta s_m
\le\int_{J_m}\|\mathcal N_{r_m}\|_2^2dt
\le
\left(\sup_{J_m}\|\mathcal N_{r_m}\|_2\right)\ell_m.
\tag{TL.246}
\]

The disjoint-interval bound (TL.243) gives \(\sum_m\ell_m<\infty\), hence
\(\ell_m\to0\).  Therefore every infinite concentration succession would
force

\[
\boxed{
\sup_{t\in J_m}
\frac{\|\mathcal N_{r_m}(t)\|_2}{s_m}
\ge\frac{2\nu\delta}{\ell_m}
\longrightarrow\infty.
}
\tag{TL.247}
\]

By (TL.236)--(TL.237), this also implies

\[
\boxed{
\sup_{t\in J_m}
\frac{\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2}{s_m}
\longrightarrow\infty.
}
\tag{TL.248}
\]

Thus the temporal-compression branch is not a free sequence of short pulses.
It is an exact same-history claim that the kinetic-energy dissipation rate per
unit active critical stock must become unbounded on the successor intervals.
The next carrier calculation must combine this forced rate escape with the
common participating-energy loss (TL.195)--(TL.204); treating the high rate as
an independently available source would discard the non-recounting law that
produced (TL.247).

### 13.8 Only one transverse component of the affine-readout tangent can strengthen the receiver

The nonlinear norm in (TL.230) counts more readout motion than the Rayleigh
quotient uses.
It counts carriage of the carrier, radial amplitude change and turning that is
orthogonal to the concentration gradient.  These motions remain part of the
one VPI continuation, but none can increase the Rayleigh quotient
\(\lambda_r=\Gamma_r/r^2\) at first order.

Put

\[
h_r:=(L_z-\lambda_r)g_r.
\tag{TL.249}
\]

By (TL.225),

\[
\langle g_r,h_r\rangle=0,
\qquad
\|h_r\|_2^2=\mathsf S_r\mathfrak V_r.
\tag{TL.250}
\]

Thus \(h_r\) is the Rayleigh-gradient direction inside the same readout Hilbert
space: motion along it changes mean squared frequency without changing
amplitude to first order.  Whenever \(\mathsf S_r>0\), (TL.213) gives
\(h_r\ne0\).  Define
the signed effective VPI transfer coefficient

\[
\boxed{
\alpha_r
:=
\frac{\operatorname{Re}\langle h_r,\mathcal N_r\rangle}
{\|h_r\|_2^2}.
}
\tag{TL.251}
\]

This is not an independently prescribed alignment factor.  It is the exact
coefficient of the nonlinear affine-readout contribution in the unique direction
that can raise the receiver's concentration.  Equations (TL.228) and (TL.250)
give

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak R_r=2\alpha_r\mathfrak V_r,
\qquad
(\log\Gamma_r)'=2(\alpha_r-\nu)\mathfrak V_r.
}
\tag{TL.252}
\]

The second identity is an exact transverse comparison on the fixed-coordinate
affine readout.  Its full readout tangent (TL.224) contains the two collinear
transverse contributions

\[
\operatorname{proj}_{h_r}\mathcal N_r=\alpha_rh_r,
\qquad
\operatorname{proj}_{h_r}(-\nu L_zg_r)=-\nu h_r.
\tag{TL.253}
\]

They are not two fluids or two independently acting mechanisms: they are two
rows of the same instantaneous readout tangent, resolved in its own concentration
direction.  Consequently

\[
\boxed{
(\log\Gamma_r)' >0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\alpha_r>\nu.
}
\tag{TL.254}
\]

All tangent motion orthogonal to \(h_r\), including motion parallel to
\(g_r\), has disappeared from this exact test.  This deletes the overcount in
(TL.244): a large source norm elsewhere in the field or a rapid translation of
the receiver cannot masquerade as delivered critical strengthening.

For later summation define only the uphill component already present in the
nonlinear readout contribution,

\[
\mathcal N_r^\uparrow:=[\alpha_r]_+h_r,
\qquad
\mathfrak A_r^\uparrow
:=\frac{\|\mathcal N_r^\uparrow\|_2^2}{\mathsf S_r}
=[\alpha_r]_+^2\mathfrak V_r.
\tag{TL.255}
\]

Then \(\|\mathcal N_r^\uparrow\|_2\le\|\mathcal N_r\|_2\), and the positive
current mismatch is represented without a Cauchy--Schwarz loss:

\[
\boxed{
[\mathfrak R_r]_+
=2\sqrt{\mathfrak V_r\mathfrak A_r^\uparrow}.
}
\tag{TL.256}
\]

Hence the datum-finite all-scale reach (TL.238) survives the projection:

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T
\left(
\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}
\|\mathcal N_{2^jR}^\uparrow(t)\|_2^2
\right)^{1/2}dt
\le\frac{C\mathcal E_0}{\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.257}
\]

If an interval \(J=[s,t]\) satisfies (TL.239), (TL.252) gives the exact
uphill requirement

\[
\delta
\le
2\int_J(\alpha_r-\nu)\mathfrak V_r\,dq
\le2\int_J([\alpha_r]_+-\nu)_{+}\mathfrak V_r\,dq.
\tag{TL.258}
\]

The pointwise square completion

\[
2(\alpha-\nu)_+\le\frac{[\alpha]_+^2}{2\nu}
\tag{TL.259}
\]

therefore proves the smaller, exact productive quantum

\[
\boxed{
\int_J\mathfrak A_r^\uparrow\,dq
=\int_J[\alpha_r]_+^2\mathfrak V_r\,dq
\ge2\nu\delta.
}
\tag{TL.260}
\]

Thus (TL.242) did not need the full source norm.  Every positive concentration
octave requires a fixed amount of the nonlinear readout contribution specifically in the
receiver's own uphill direction.  Together with the uncertainty law (TL.221),
the projected productive work obeys

\[
\operatorname{Re}\langle h_r,\mathcal N_r\rangle
=\alpha_r\mathsf S_r\mathfrak V_r
\ge
\frac{\nu\Gamma_r\mathsf S_r}
{r^2\sigma_{{\rm dil},r}^2}
\tag{TL.261}
\]

at every instant of strengthening.  This is a rising local service requirement
on the fixed-time affine readout: unless the reconstructed band has large
center--dilation variance, the projected uphill work needed to overcome its
simultaneous viscous coefficient grows like \(r^{-2}\).

This is an exact local transverse identity and lower service requirement, not
a proved contraction half.  Proportional growth of the readout's critical
stock and participating-energy stock leaves \(\Gamma_r\) unchanged and is
therefore invisible to \(\alpha_r\).  Section 13.12 restores that common
throughput mode and states the two-component record action that remains to be
bounded without recounting.

### 13.9 The productive coefficient is an exact deformation commutator

The coefficient in (TL.251) is not merely an orthogonal projection chosen
after the fact.  It is the commutator by which the same velocity field deforms
its own carrier scale.  Let

\[
\mathcal T_r u:=g_r,
\qquad
B_r:=\mathcal T_r^*\mathcal T_r.
\tag{TL.262}
\]

The integrations over all centers, directions, intermediate points and
chord lengths make \(B_r\) a positive self-adjoint scalar radial Fourier
multiplier.  In particular it commutes with \(\Lambda\), derivatives and the
Leray projection.  The three moments in (TL.215) can therefore be written

\[
U_r=\langle u,B_ru\rangle,
\quad
\mathsf S_r=\langle u,B_r\Lambda^2u\rangle,
\quad
\mathsf G_r=\langle u,B_r\Lambda^4u\rangle.
\tag{TL.263}
\]

Moreover,

\[
h_r=\mathcal T_r(\Lambda^2-\lambda_r)u,
\qquad
\mathcal N_r=-\mathcal T_r\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u).
\tag{TL.264}
\]

Set

\[
M_{r,\lambda_r}:=B_r(\Lambda^2-\lambda_r),
\qquad
D_u f:=u\cdot\nabla f.
\tag{TL.265}
\]

Because \(M_{r,\lambda_r}u\) is divergence free, the pressure projection may
be removed only inside this exact pairing.  Since \(D_u\) is skew-adjoint in
\(L^2\), (TL.264) gives

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{Re}\langle h_r,\mathcal N_r\rangle
=-\langle D_uu,M_{r,\lambda_r}u\rangle
=\frac12\langle u,[D_u,M_{r,\lambda_r}]u\rangle.
}
\tag{TL.266}
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
\alpha_r
=
\frac{\langle u,[D_u,M_{r,\lambda_r}]u\rangle}
{2\langle u,B_r(\Lambda^2-\lambda_r)^2u\rangle}.
}
\tag{TL.267}
\]

This formula keeps the complete pressure response in the divergence-free
state and removes only its zero direct pairing with the divergence-free test.
It also identifies the exact meaning of coherent delivery.  If \(b_r(|\xi|)\)
is the symbol of \(B_r\), the commutator carries the triadic difference

\[
m_{r,\lambda_r}(\xi)-m_{r,\lambda_r}(\zeta),
\qquad
m_{r,\lambda_r}(\xi)
=b_r(|\xi|)(|\xi|^2-\lambda_r).
\tag{TL.268}
\]

Uniform carriage has \(\xi=\zeta\) and contributes exactly zero.  Productive
work therefore requires the one field to change the centered scale-shape of
its own active carrier.  Equations (TL.252), (TL.266), and (TL.268) are the
formal version of the physical statement that a smaller receiver is not fed
by motion past it: only deformation that changes its relative whole-field
geometry can overcome its simultaneous loss.

### 13.10 A scale-critical estimate from the affine symbol

A direct Fourier expansion of (TL.164)--(TL.175), rather than (TL.175) alone,
gives the radial-symbol lemma

\[
b_r(k)=C_0\int_r^{2r}\Phi(k\rho)\,d\rho,
\]

where \(C_0>0\) depends only on the unitary Fourier and spherical-measure
conventions.  Writing \(z=rk\), this yields the two asymptotic regimes

\[
\boxed{
b_r(k)\asymp r\Phi(rk),
\qquad
\Phi(z)\asymp
\begin{cases}
z^2,&0<z\le1,\\[1mm]
(1+\log z)z^{-2},&z\ge1.
\end{cases}
}
\tag{TL.269}
\]

Indeed the spherical pair-increment symbol for \(\Lambda^{-1}u\) is a
constant multiple of \(f(z)=(1-\sin z/z)/z^2\), while integration over all
intermediate affine points replaces it by

\[
\Phi(z)=\frac2{z^2}\int_0^zqf(q)\,dq-f(z).
\]

Taylor expansion gives \(\Phi(z)\asymp z^2\) at zero, and the logarithmic
integral gives \(\Phi(z)\asymp(1+\log z)z^{-2}\) at infinity.

Only the universal two-sided dyadic comparison in (TL.269) is used below.
For a homogeneous Littlewood--Paley decomposition set

\[
\beta(t):=\sup_{j\in\mathbb Z}
2^{j/2}\|\Delta_ju(t)\|_2.
\tag{TL.270}
\]

The same low--high, high--low and high--high paraproduct calculation as in
(TL.72), now with the positive weight \(b_r^{1/2}\), gives uniformly in
\(r>0\)

\[
\boxed{
\|B_r^{1/2}\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)\|_2
\le C\beta(t)\|B_r^{1/2}\Lambda^2u\|_2.
}
\tag{TL.271}
\]

For completeness, on an output octave \(2^j\), a low--high row is bounded
by

\[
b_r(2^j)^{1/2}\beta\,2^{2j}\|\Delta_ju\|_2.
\]

For high--high input at \(2^k\) and output \(2^j\), \(j\le k+O(1)\),
the divergence form gives the additional factor \(2^{5(j-k)/2}\).  Combining
it with the worst square-root weight ratio gives the Schur kernel

\[
K_{jk}\le C(1+k-j)^{1/2}2^{-3(k-j)/2},
\qquad j\le k+O(1),
\]

which is summable.  Low--high and high--low use the local dyadic comparability
in (TL.269).  Squaring and summing gives (TL.271), with \(C\) depending only
on the Littlewood--Paley partition and the universal symbol bounds, uniformly
in \(r\).  This proof retains every interaction
row of the same field; it does not replace the complete current by a shell
source.

Equations (TL.263)--(TL.264) turn (TL.271) into

\[
\boxed{
\|\mathcal N_r\|_2
\le C\beta\sqrt{\mathsf G_r}.
}
\tag{TL.272}
\]

Since \(\mathsf G_r=\mathsf S_r(\lambda_r+\mathfrak V_r)\), the productive
coefficient satisfies

\[
\boxed{
|\alpha_r|
\le
C\beta
\sqrt{1+\frac{\lambda_r}{\mathfrak V_r}},
\qquad
|\alpha_r|\mathfrak V_r
\le
C\beta\sqrt{\mathfrak V_r(\lambda_r+\mathfrak V_r)}.
}
\tag{TL.273}
\]

Combining the first inequality with the strengthening condition (TL.254)
gives the scale-uniform instantaneous estimate

\[
\boxed{
(\log\Gamma_r)'>0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\frac{\mathfrak V_r}{\lambda_r+\mathfrak V_r}
<\frac{C^2\beta(t)^2}{\nu^2}.
}
\tag{TL.274}
\]

This has a direct physical reading.  Fix \(0<\varepsilon<1\).  If the same
field has no critical population larger than

\[
\beta(t)\le\frac{\varepsilon\nu}{C},
\tag{TL.275}
\]

then every strengthening receiver must obey

\[
\mathfrak V_r
<\frac{\varepsilon^2}{1-\varepsilon^2}\lambda_r.
\tag{TL.276}
\]

The dilation uncertainty (TL.221), with \(\lambda_r=\Gamma_r/r^2\), then
forces

\[
\boxed{
\sigma_{{\rm dil},r}^2
>\frac{1-\varepsilon^2}{\varepsilon^2}.
}
\tag{TL.277}
\]

Thus, during strengthening, either the *global* critical Besov amplitude
\(\beta\) is viscosity-sized or the selected fixed-time readout has
quantitatively large dilation-generator variance.  This estimate does not
localize the large dyadic population near the receiver, identify it as a
supplier, or prove degradation of future nonlinear delivery.  The first branch
recovers (TL.79) only under that theorem's additional ultraviolet,
fixed-strength and \(>L(K)\) hypotheses.  Temporal concatenation and no-reset
remain missing.  The proportional common-delivery mode, which can raise
critical stock while leaving \(\Gamma_r\) fixed, is restored in Section 13.12.

### 13.11 Critical scaling audits the claimed contraction

The rising power in (TL.261) and the shortening lifetime in (TL.201) are
simultaneous exact facts.  They are not yet a strict contraction, because the
fixed-viscosity Navier--Stokes scaling preserves their normalized ratio.

For \(\lambda>0\), let

\[
u^{[\lambda]}(x,t)
=\lambda u(\lambda x,\lambda^2t),
\qquad
p^{[\lambda]}(x,t)
=\lambda^2p(\lambda x,\lambda^2t).
\tag{TL.278}
\]

This is another solution with the same viscosity.  Compare the original
carrier band \([r,2r]\) at time \(t\) with the scaled band
\([r/\lambda,2r/\lambda]\) at time \(t/\lambda^2\).  Under
\((\rho,z)\mapsto(\lambda\rho,\lambda z)\), the affine-readout fields scale as

\[
g_{r/\lambda}[u^{[\lambda]}]
=\lambda g_r[u],
\qquad
\mathcal N_{r/\lambda}[u^{[\lambda]}]
=\lambda^3\mathcal N_r[u],
\qquad
L_zg_{r/\lambda}[u^{[\lambda]}]
=\lambda^3L_zg_r[u].
\tag{TL.279}
\]

The product carrier measure \(d\rho\,dz\,dn\,d\theta\) scales by
\(\lambda^{-4}\).  If

\[
U_r=\|g_r\|_2^2,
\qquad
S_r=\langle g_r,L_zg_r\rangle,
\qquad
G_r=\|L_zg_r\|_2^2,
\tag{TL.280}
\]

then

\[
U_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}=\lambda^{-2}U_r,
\qquad
S_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}=S_r,
\qquad
G_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}=\lambda^2G_r.
\tag{TL.281}
\]

Writing \(\mu_r=S_r/U_r=\Gamma_r/r^2\), equations
(TL.209), (TL.217), and (TL.251) therefore give

\[
\boxed{
\begin{gathered}
\mu_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}=\lambda^2\mu_r,
\qquad
\Gamma_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}=\Gamma_r,
\qquad
\mathfrak V_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}=\lambda^2\mathfrak V_r,\\
\sigma_{{\rm dil},r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}
=\sigma_{{\rm dil},r},
\qquad
\alpha_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}=\alpha_r.
\end{gathered}
}
\tag{TL.282}
\]

In particular, the productive and viscous transverse powers scale in exactly
the same way:

\[
\operatorname{Re}\langle h_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]},
\mathcal N_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}\rangle
=\lambda^2\operatorname{Re}\langle h_r,\mathcal N_r\rangle,
\qquad
\nu S_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}
\mathfrak V_{r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}
=\lambda^2\nu S_r\mathfrak V_r.
\tag{TL.283}
\]

The corresponding concentration clock scales oppositely:

\[
\tau_{{\rm conc},r}
:=\frac1{\nu\mathfrak V_r}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\tau_{{\rm conc},r/\lambda}^{[\lambda]}
=\lambda^{-2}\tau_{{\rm conc},r}.
\tag{TL.284}
\]

Thus the \(r^{-2}\) instantaneous work increase in (TL.261) is exactly
cancelled by the \(r^2\) lifetime when work is integrated over one normalized
successor event.  Neither \(\alpha_r/\nu\), \(\Gamma_r\), nor the normalized
dilation geometry is weakened merely because the same shape is viewed at a
finer scale.

The strict inequality (TL.213) also has no hidden uniform gap.  For a Schwartz
velocity whose Fourier support lies in

\[
\{\xi: ||\xi|-k|\le\varepsilon k\},
\tag{TL.285}
\]

every nonzero carrier band generated from it has

\[
\mathfrak V_r
=\frac{\operatorname{Var}_{g_r}(L_z)}
{\langle L_z\rangle_{g_r}}
\lesssim\varepsilon^2k^2.
\tag{TL.286}
\]

Hence \(\mathfrak V_r>0\) for a nonzero decaying field, but it can approach
zero.  The uncertainty law (TL.221) records the corresponding loss as
unbounded dilation spread; it does not create a scale-dependent numerical
deficit for a rescaled coherent shape.

This scaling audit rules out the local inference

\[
\text{higher fine-scale power plus shorter lifetime}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\text{strict successor contraction}
\tag{TL.287}
\]

without an additional same-history theorem.  The surviving statement must
compare consecutive populations in the one original continuation and prove
that exact rescaling cannot be renewed without either irreversible loss of
their participating energy, growth of their scale-shape spread, or a
non-recounted positive uphill action bounded by the datum.  Critical scaling
then fixes the form of the remaining law: its strictness must come from
history and geometry, not from an extra bare power of \(r\).

### 13.12 Common delivery and transverse coherence are two rows of one tangent

The projection in Section 13.8 deliberately removed amplitude motion parallel
to \(g_r\).  That is correct for the Rayleigh quotient but not sufficient for
fixed critical-strength delivery.  Restore its signed coefficient by defining

\[
\boxed{
c_r
:=\frac{\operatorname{Re}\langle g_r,\mathcal N_r\rangle}{U_r},
\qquad
\lambda_r:=\frac{\mathsf S_r}{U_r}.
}
\tag{TL.288}
\]

The nonlinear affine-readout contribution has the orthogonal rows

\[
\mathcal N_r
=c_rg_r+\alpha_rh_r+\mathcal N_r^\circ,
\qquad
\operatorname{Re}\langle g_r,\mathcal N_r^\circ\rangle
=\operatorname{Re}\langle h_r,\mathcal N_r^\circ\rangle=0,
\tag{TL.289}
\]

where only the displayed real pairings are needed.  Since
\(L_zg_r=\lambda_rg_r+h_r\), (TL.224), (TL.226), and (TL.227) give the two
exact signed laws

\[
\boxed{
(\log U_r)'=2(c_r-\nu\lambda_r),
}
\tag{TL.290}
\]

\[
\boxed{
(\log\mathsf S_r)'
=2(c_r-\nu\lambda_r)
+2(\alpha_r-\nu)\mathfrak V_r.
}
\tag{TL.291}
\]

Their difference is (TL.252).  Equation (TL.291) is the complete two-component
local law for this fixed-coordinate affine readout:
\(c_r-\nu\lambda_r\) is the common delivery of
participating energy and critical stock, while
\((\alpha_r-\nu)\mathfrak V_r\) is the transverse change of scale shape.
They are orthogonal readings of the same pressure-completed tangent, not
independent suppliers.

A fixed-band critical-stock doubling therefore obeys the signed event identity

\[
\log2
=2\int_J(c_r-\nu\lambda_r)\,dt
+2\int_J(\alpha_r-\nu)\mathfrak V_r\,dt,
\tag{TL.292}
\]

and hence the exact positive-action lower bound

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\log2}{2}
\le
\int_J
\left(
[c_r-\nu\lambda_r]_+
+[(\alpha_r-\nu)\mathfrak V_r]_+
\right)dt.
}
\tag{TL.293}
\]

This repairs the amplitude omission.  On a fixed-band doubling interval,
common amplitude growth and transverse concentration growth jointly register
at least \((\log2)/2\) of positive variation.  This eventwise registration is
not yet an irreversible or non-recounted charge: taking positive parts loses
the cancellation of the underlying signed boundary currents.

There is also an exact joined geometric term for two candidate coarse/fine
readout bands.  Put their vectors in the orthogonal direct sum provided by two
disjoint chord bands, and write

\[
U_i=\|g_i\|_2^2,
\qquad
\lambda_i=\frac{\langle g_i,L_zg_i\rangle}{U_i},
\qquad
h_i=(L_z-\lambda_i)g_i,
\qquad i\in\{p,c\}.
\tag{TL.294}
\]

For

\[
\bar\lambda
:=\frac{U_p\lambda_p+U_c\lambda_c}{U_p+U_c},
\tag{TL.295}
\]

the Hilbert variance decomposition is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\|(L_z-\bar\lambda)(g_p\oplus g_c)\|_2^2
={}&\|h_p\|_2^2+\|h_c\|_2^2\\
&+\frac{U_pU_c}{U_p+U_c}
(\lambda_p-\lambda_c)^2.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.296}
\]

The last term is compulsory between-band dispersion.  It is present precisely
when two fixed disjoint readout bands coexist with different mean squared
frequencies.  For adjacent carrier bands, the internal
critical and participating-energy boundary currents cancel in the union by
(TL.173) and (TL.207), while the positive term in (TL.296) remains inside the
union's simultaneous viscous shape loss.  Thus coexistence of two such bands
has an exact instantaneous union-variance cost.  This is a local kinematic
readout; it does not establish predecessor--successor ancestry or a causal
time-integrated contraction.

What is not yet proved is a datum-finite time integral of that joint term.
Critical scaling permits the coexistence window and participating stock to
shrink together.  A candidate successor theorem must first define a causal
family of fixed-band intervals, differentiate the joined identity, group and
cancel shared signed interface currents before taking positive parts, and
derive every residual as an explicit outer current.  A schematic upper target
for such a theorem is

\[
\boxed{
\sum_m\int_{J_m}
\left(
[c_{r_m}-\nu\lambda_{r_m}]_+
+[(\alpha_{r_m}-\nu)\mathfrak V_{r_m}]_+
\right)dt
\le C(u_0,\nu).
}
\tag{TL.297}
\]

Equation (TL.297) is not asserted here.  As written, its admissible interval
family, overlap rule and predecessor--successor relation are not yet defined,
so the formula does not itself encode causality or non-recounting.  A proved
family-level version, together with (TL.293), would bound the number of its
fixed-band doubling events.  A further carrier-event extraction theorem is
still required to
show that every first-record doubling of the global \(H\) either contains one
of those fixed-strength events or pays the diffuse majority-width alternative;
without that bridge, (TL.297) alone does not yet bound \(H\) or continue the
solution.

### 13.13 A conditional two-band relative-population identity

The last term in (TL.296) also enters the viscous contribution to the relative
population law for two fixed disjoint chord-band readouts.  The labels \(p\)
and \(c\) below are mnemonic only: an increase of the \(c\)-fraction does not
show that its stock came from \(p\), because either band may exchange current
through its outer boundary.  No predecessor--child ancestry is asserted.

Let

\[
U:=U_p+U_c,
\qquad
x:=\frac{U_c}{U}\in(0,1),
\qquad
e_i:=\frac{g_i}{\sqrt{U_i}},
\qquad i\in\{p,c\}.
\tag{TL.298}
\]

Inside the orthogonal direct sum of the two carrier populations define

\[
e:=\sqrt{1-x}\,e_p+\sqrt{x}\,e_c=\frac{g_p\oplus g_c}{\sqrt U},
\qquad
k:=-\sqrt{x}\,e_p+\sqrt{1-x}\,e_c.
\tag{TL.299}
\]

Then \(e\) and \(k\) are orthonormal.  The first is common amplitude motion;
the second changes the relative two-band population fraction without
changing the union amplitude to first order.  Put

\[
\mathcal T_i:=\mathcal N_i-\nu L_zg_i,
\qquad
q_i:=c_i-\nu\lambda_i.
\tag{TL.300}
\]

Equations (TL.290) and (TL.298) give the exact population equation

\[
\boxed{
x'=2x(1-x)(q_c-q_p)
=2\sqrt{x(1-x)}\,
\frac{\operatorname{Re}\langle k,\mathcal T_p\oplus\mathcal T_c\rangle}
{\sqrt U}.
}
\tag{TL.301}
\]

Thus the relative fixed-band fraction law is one orthogonal projection of the
joined readout tangent.  It is not an ancestry statement.  If

\[
\Delta:=\lambda_c-\lambda_p,
\qquad
\beta:=c_c-c_p,
\tag{TL.302}
\]

then (TL.301) is equivalently

\[
\boxed{
\left(\log\frac{x}{1-x}\right)'
=2(\beta-\nu\Delta).
}
\tag{TL.303}
\]

The nonlinear and viscous rows appearing here are the two collinear pieces
of this same relative-band readout tangent:

\[
\frac{\operatorname{Re}\langle k,
\mathcal N_p\oplus\mathcal N_c\rangle}{\sqrt U}
=\sqrt{x(1-x)}\,\beta,
\qquad
\frac{\operatorname{Re}\langle k,-\nu L_z(g_p\oplus g_c)\rangle}{\sqrt U}
=-\nu\sqrt{x(1-x)}\,\Delta.
\tag{TL.304}
\]

Moreover, the compulsory between-band term in (TL.296) is exactly the square
of the unscaled viscous population direction:

\[
\boxed{
\left|\langle k,L_z(g_p\oplus g_c)\rangle\right|^2
=Ux(1-x)\Delta^2
=\frac{U_pU_c}{U_p+U_c}(\lambda_c-\lambda_p)^2.
}
\tag{TL.305}
\]

This identifies the fixed-coordinate scale gap seen by relative population
change.  It is not an added dissipation proxy: it is already part of the union
variance and of the readout tangent that changes the ratio.  It does not say
which boundary current caused that change.

Fix \(0<a<1/2\), and let \(J=[s,t]\) be a relative-fraction first passage satisfying

\[
x(s)=a,
\qquad
x(t)=1-a,
\qquad
a\le x(q)\le1-a\quad(q\in J).
\tag{TL.306}
\]

When the mean-frequency ordering obeys \(\Delta\ge0\) on \(J\), integration
of (TL.303) yields the exact signed relative-population identity

\[
\boxed{
\int_J\beta(q)\,dq
=\log\frac{1-a}{a}
+\nu\int_J\Delta(q)\,dq.
}
\tag{TL.307}
\]

The first term is the fixed log-odds change.  The second is the excess
nonlinear differential delivery required to overcome the finer band's larger
viscous rate during that change.  Neither term proves internal transfer from
\(p\) to \(c\).

There is an exact quantitative fast/slow decomposition.  Write
\(w=x(1-x)\) and let \(J_+:=\{q\in J:x'(q)>0\}\).  On \(J_+\), (TL.301)
gives \(\beta=\nu\Delta+x'/(2w)>0\), and hence

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\left|
\operatorname{Re}\langle k,\mathcal N_p\oplus\mathcal N_c\rangle
\right|^2}{U}
=w\beta^2
=\nu^2w\Delta^2+\nu\Delta x'+\frac{(x')^2}{4w}.
}
\tag{TL.308}
\]

All three terms belong to the same positive relative-fraction instant.  The first is
long-residence exposure to the between-band viscous direction, the last is
the readout-tangent severity required for rapid fraction change, and the middle
term is their signed same-instant coupling.

Suppose in addition that \(\Delta\ge\Delta_0>0\) on \(J\).  Put
\(w_0=a(1-a)\), \(V_+:=\int_{J_+}x'\,dq\), and
\(\tau_+:=|J_+|\).  First passage gives \(V_+\ge1-2a\).  From (TL.308),
Cauchy--Schwarz and \(w\le1/4\),

\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_{J_+}
\frac{\left|
\operatorname{Re}\langle k,\mathcal N_p\oplus\mathcal N_c\rangle
\right|^2}{U}\,dq
&\ge
\nu^2w_0\Delta_0^2\tau_+
+\nu\Delta_0V_+
+\frac{V_+^2}{\tau_+}\\
&\ge
\nu\Delta_0(1-2a)
\bigl(1+2\sqrt{a(1-a)}\bigr).
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.309}
\]

Thus shortening this conditional first passage cannot make its joined readout
action vanish:
the residence term decreases, but the population-speed term increases by the
reciprocal amount.  This is the precise throughput--lifetime conversion that
the bare \(r^{-2}\) scaling audit could not supply.

There is a conditional scale-independent critical-stock corollary.  Assume
throughout the first passage that

\[
\mathsf S_p+\mathsf S_c
=U_p\lambda_p+U_c\lambda_c\ge A_*,
\qquad
\max(\lambda_p,\lambda_c)\le\Lambda_*,
\qquad
\Delta_0\ge\kappa\Lambda_*
\tag{TL.310}
\]

for some \(A_*>0\) and \(\kappa>0\).  Then \(U\ge A_*/\Lambda_*\), and
(TL.309) gives

\[
\boxed{
\int_{J_+}
\left|
\operatorname{Re}\langle k,\mathcal N_p\oplus\mathcal N_c\rangle
\right|^2dq
\ge
\nu\kappa A_*(1-2a)
\bigl(1+2\sqrt{a(1-a)}\bigr).
}
\tag{TL.311}
\]

Equation (TL.311) is a conditional lower quantum for the nonlinear
relative-band projection during a fixed-critical-stock fraction passage across
a mean-frequency gap.  Its hypotheses have not been extracted from a global
first-record doubling.

This does not tighten the missing upper law (TL.297) by itself.  The projected
action may reflect internal transfer, outer-boundary influx, or differential
depletion of the two fixed bands, and it cannot simply be summed as a free
nonlinear-source norm.  Before this identity can enter a successor proof, a
shared-interface/outer-current decomposition and a same-carrier event
extraction must prove that the selected fraction change represents an
ancestry-preserving transfer.  Those results are not asserted here.

## 14. The exact whole-field record law and this route's upper-bound burden

The zero datum gives the identically zero solution, so assume below that
\(H(0)>0\).  The global critical record admits a direct normal form that does
not require a shell or carrier-event extraction.  This is a readout of the complete same
solution, not a replacement carrier.  Let

\[
d\eta_t(\xi):=\frac12|\widehat u(\xi,t)|^2\,d\xi,
\qquad
\mu_j(t):=\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\xi|^j\,d\eta_t(\xi).
\tag{TL.312}
\]

Then

\[
\mathcal E=\mu_0,
\qquad
H=\mu_1,
\qquad
\mathcal D=2\mu_3.
\tag{TL.313}
\]

Put

\[
N(u):=-\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u),
\qquad
P_H(t):=\langle\Lambda u,N(u)\rangle.
\tag{TL.314}
\]

The pressure response and incompressibility are still present in the Leray
projection and in the one evolving velocity field.  Their zero direct pairing
with a divergence-free test is used only after the complete quadratic response
has been formed.  The exact whole-field balances are

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E'=-2\nu\mu_2,
\qquad
H'=P_H-2\nu\mu_3.
}
\tag{TL.315}
\]

Define the critical mean frequency and its reciprocal effective scale by

\[
\varkappa:=\frac{H}{\mathcal E}=\frac{\mu_1}{\mu_0},
\qquad
\ell_{\rm eff}:=\frac1{\varkappa}=\frac{\mathcal E}{H}.
\tag{TL.316}
\]

This \(\varkappa\) is distinct from the terminal mean
\(\kappa=\mu_2/\mu_1\) used in Section 12.  Jensen's inequality for the
positive spectral measure gives

\[
\frac{\mu_3}{\mu_1}\ge
\left(\frac{\mu_1}{\mu_0}\right)^2
=\varkappa^2.
\tag{TL.317}
\]

Consequently, at the same instant at which fixed critical strength is placed
at a larger mean frequency, its instantaneous viscous decay time decreases and
the productive throughput required merely not to decay increases:

\[
\boxed{
\tau_H^{\rm visc}:=\frac{H}{2\nu\mu_3}
\le\frac1{2\nu\varkappa^2},
\qquad
H'\ge0\ \Longrightarrow\
P_H\ge2\nu\mu_3\ge2\nu\varkappa^2H.
}
\tag{TL.318}
\]

Thus the lifetime and power halves are simultaneous consequences of the one
whole-field balance.  Their product is scale critical, so (TL.318) is a lower
service requirement and not a contraction theorem.

The same balance also exposes an exact radial scale-shape covariance.  Set

\[
\mathcal W
:=\frac{\mu_3}{\mu_1}-\frac{\mu_2}{\mu_0}\ge0,
\qquad
h:=(\Lambda-\varkappa)u.
\tag{TL.319}
\]

If \(\bar\eta_t\) is the radial pushforward of \(\eta_t\), then

\[
\mu_0\mu_3-\mu_1\mu_2
=\frac12\iint_{(0,\infty)^2}
(k+q)(k-q)^2\,d\bar\eta_t(k)d\bar\eta_t(q),
\tag{TL.320}
\]

which proves the sign in (TL.319).  Equality would require the absolutely
continuous spectral measure of a nonzero \(L^2(\mathbb R^3)\) field to be
supported on one sphere, so \(\mathcal W>0\) here.  Moreover kinetic-energy
cancellation of the nonlinearity and direct calculation give

\[
\langle h,N(u)\rangle=P_H,
\qquad
\langle h,\Lambda^2u\rangle=2H\mathcal W.
\tag{TL.321}
\]

For \(\mathcal W>0\), define the productive whole-field coefficient

\[
\alpha_H:=\frac{\langle h,N(u)\rangle}{2H\mathcal W}
=\frac{P_H}{2H\mathcal W}.
\tag{TL.322}
\]

Equations (TL.315) and (TL.321) yield the signed concentration law

\[
\boxed{
\frac d{dt}\log\varkappa
=2(\alpha_H-\nu)\mathcal W.
}
\tag{TL.323}
\]

Uniform amplification proportional to \(u\) cancels from \(h\); uniform
spectral carriage has no centered work; and only the same field's nonlinear
work along its centered scale geometry can raise \(\varkappa\).  Viscosity
acts through exactly the same nonnegative spread \(\mathcal W\).  Equation
(TL.323) is therefore a radial whole-field scale readout analogous to
(TL.252), with common critical amplitude already divided out.  It is not the
complete material coherence geometry: phase, direction, ancestry and localized
surrounding-field incidence have been marginalized.

Let

\[
\varkappa_*(t):=\max_{0\le s\le t}\varkappa(s).
\tag{TL.324}
\]

The running maximum of an absolutely continuous function is absolutely
continuous.  Its derivative is supported on new record times, so (TL.323)
gives the exact signed, causal, non-recounting record identity

\[
\boxed{
\log\frac{\varkappa_*(T)}{\varkappa(0)}
=2\int_0^T
\mathbf 1_{\{\varkappa=\varkappa_*\}}
(\alpha_H-\nu)_+\mathcal W\,dt.
}
\tag{TL.325}
\]

No interval is counted twice in (TL.325), and negative or sub-viscous work is
not silently replaced by unsigned flux.  Define the record action

\[
\mathscr A_{\rm rec}(T)
:=\int_0^T
\mathbf 1_{\{\varkappa=\varkappa_*\}}
(\alpha_H-\nu)_+\mathcal W\,dt.
\tag{TL.326}
\]

If \(t_n\) is the first time at which
\(H(t_n)=2^nH(0)\), then energy monotonicity gives

\[
\varkappa(t_n)
=\frac{H(t_n)}{\mathcal E(t_n)}
\ge2\frac{H(t_{n-1})}{\mathcal E(t_{n-1})}
=2\varkappa(t_{n-1}).
\tag{TL.327}
\]

Because \(H(s)<H(t_n)\) and \(\mathcal E(s)\ge\mathcal E(t_n)\) for every
\(s<t_n\), each \(t_n\) is also a new \(\varkappa\)-record.  Therefore every
first-record \(H\)-doubling consumes at least \((\log2)/2\) of the single
record action.  If \(N_H(T)\) denotes the number of completed levels
\(t_n\le T\), then

\[
\boxed{
N_H(T)\le\frac{2}{\log2}\,\mathscr A_{\rm rec}(T).
}
\tag{TL.328}
\]

This bypasses localized event extraction for the whole-field effective
scale.  It does not identify those record times with the localized carrier
successors in (TL.297).

The exact consequence of a datum-finite action estimate is now transparent.
If one could prove independently that

\[
\boxed{
\sup_{T<T_*}\mathscr A_{\rm rec}(T)\le C(u_0,\nu),
}
\tag{TL.329}
\]

then (TL.325) would imply

\[
\boxed{
\ell_{\rm eff}(t)
\ge\ell_{\rm eff}(0)e^{-2C(u_0,\nu)},
\qquad
H(t)\le H(0)e^{2C(u_0,\nu)}.
}
\tag{TL.330}
\]

Equations (TL.328)--(TL.330) would exclude an infinite descent of the global
effective scale at fixed critical strength, exclude infinitely many
first-record \(H\)-doublings, and give the critical-norm continuation bound
through \(\dot H^{1/2}\hookrightarrow L^3\) and the endpoint
\(L_t^\infty L_x^3\) regularity theorem.  The lower bound in (TL.330) is a
minimum *effective mean scale*; it is
not a hard smallest Fourier or material scale.  A literal minimum scale for
every critical-strength carrier additionally requires uniform critical-tail
tightness or a carrier-to-record localization theorem.

The unresolved fact is that (TL.329) is not presently an estimate.  By
(TL.325)--(TL.326), it is exactly one half of the logarithmic record bound it
is meant to prove:

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr A_{\rm rec}(T)
=\frac12\log\frac{\varkappa_*(T)}{\varkappa(0)}.
}
\tag{TL.331}
\]

Thus (TL.325) is an exact signed and non-recounting whole-field record normal
form; the requested datum-finite causal contraction is still missing.
Equation (TL.329) would be circular unless it is paid by an independent
datum-bounded additive carrier action, trace or capacity estimate, coercive
signed identity, or other noncircular payer.  The kinetic-energy identity controls
\(\int\mu_2dt\), not the positive record action in (TL.326).  Under the
fixed-viscosity critical scaling, \(\varkappa\) scales like inverse length,
\(\mathcal W\) like inverse length squared, and time like length squared, so
the action per octave is invariant.  This is the precise obstruction: the
required faster delivery and the shorter lifetime are exactly coupled, but
their integrated scale factors cancel; no degradation of coherent delivery
has yet been converted into a datum-finite no-return payer.

## 15. The exact labelled material-pair carrier

The preceding record law can be placed on persistent labels of the actual
fluid.  On the smooth interval assume the decay needed for the fractional pair
integrals and integrations by parts below, as holds for a Schwartz datum.  Let
\(X(a,t)\) be the incompressible flow map,

\[
\partial_tX(a,t)=u(X(a,t),t),
\qquad
X(a,0)=a,
\qquad
\det\nabla_aX(a,t)=1.
\tag{TL.332}
\]

For every ordered pair of initial labels \((a,b)\), set

\[
R_{ab}:=X(a,t)-X(b,t),
\qquad
V_{ab}:=u(X(a,t),t)-u(X(b,t),t)=\dot R_{ab},
\qquad
Y_{ab}:=\frac{R_{ab}}{|R_{ab}|^2}.
\tag{TL.333}
\]

The derivative of the inverted separation is

\[
\dot Y_{ab}
=\frac1{|R_{ab}|^2}
\left(I-2\widehat R_{ab}\otimes\widehat R_{ab}\right)V_{ab},
\qquad
|\dot Y_{ab}|^2=\frac{|V_{ab}|^2}{|R_{ab}|^4}.
\tag{TL.334}
\]

Incompressibility preserves the label volume, and the exact fractional
Sobolev identity in three dimensions therefore gives

\[
\boxed{
H(t)
=\frac1{4\pi^2}
\iint_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3}
\frac{|V_{ab}(t)|^2}{|R_{ab}(t)|^4}\,da\,db
=\frac1{4\pi^2}\iint|\dot Y_{ab}(t)|^2\,da\,db.
}
\tag{TL.335}
\]

This is a literal same-fluid carrier: the labels are fixed at the datum and
are never replaced by shell names, successor packets, or independently chosen
vortices.  Define

\[
q_{ab}:=\frac{|V_{ab}|^2}{|R_{ab}|^4},
\qquad
\sigma_{ab}:=\frac{R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab}}{|R_{ab}|^2}
=\frac d{dt}\log|R_{ab}|.
\tag{TL.336}
\]

Along the same labels the material acceleration is

\[
\dot V_{ab}
=-\delta_{ab}\nabla p+\nu\,\delta_{ab}\Delta u,
\qquad
-\Delta p=\partial_i u_j\,\partial_j u_i,
\tag{TL.337}
\]

where \(\delta_{ab}f=f(X(a,t),t)-f(X(b,t),t)\).  Thus the exact pointwise
carrier law is

\[
\boxed{
\dot q_{ab}
=\frac{2V_{ab}\cdot
(-\delta_{ab}\nabla p+\nu\,\delta_{ab}\Delta u)}{|R_{ab}|^4}
-4\sigma_{ab}q_{ab}.
}
\tag{TL.338}
\]

Transport is encoded by the moving labels and by \(\dot R=V\);
incompressibility is the preserved label measure; pressure is the simultaneous
whole-field response in (TL.337); viscosity acts through the simultaneous
Laplacian difference; and deformation is the signed logarithmic separation
rate \(\sigma\).  None of these terms belongs to an independent mechanism.
In particular, surrounding-field participation enters every pair through the
global pressure solution and through the velocity and Laplacian at both
endpoints.

At a fixed time, the fractional pairing identities give

\[
\iint\frac{V_{ab}\cdot\delta_{ab}\nabla p}{|R_{ab}|^4}\,da\,db=0,
\qquad
\frac1{2\pi^2}\iint
\frac{V_{ab}\cdot\delta_{ab}\Delta u}{|R_{ab}|^4}\,da\,db
=-\|\Lambda^{3/2}u\|_2^2.
\tag{TL.339}
\]

The first equality is the exact global cancellation of pressure work against
the divergence-free fractional test; it does not remove pressure from the
causal evolution (TL.337)--(TL.338).  Integrating (TL.338) now recovers
(TL.315) and identifies its nonlinear production directly on the labelled
carrier:

\[
\boxed{
P_H(t)
=-\frac1{\pi^2}\iint\sigma_{ab}(t)q_{ab}(t)\,da\,db,
\qquad
H'=P_H-\nu\mathcal D.
}
\tag{TL.340}
\]

Normalize the positive pair weight by

\[
d\mathbb Q_t(a,b)
:=\frac{q_{ab}(t)}{4\pi^2H(t)}\,da\,db.
\tag{TL.341}
\]

It is a probability measure whenever \(H>0\).  Equations
(TL.317)--(TL.318) and (TL.340) become the exact material-pair service law

\[
\boxed{
\frac{H'}H
=-4\,\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}[\sigma]
-\nu\frac{\mathcal D}{H},
\qquad
\frac{\mathcal D}{H}\ge2\varkappa^2,
\qquad
H'\ge0\ \Longrightarrow\
-\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}[\sigma]\ge\frac\nu2\varkappa^2.
}
\tag{TL.342}
\]

This is the exact simultaneous material-pair service identity on the complete
same-fluid carrier, not yet the throughput--coherence contraction.  A larger
whole-field effective mean frequency
\(\varkappa\) at fixed critical height shortens the instantaneous viscous
clock, and sustaining it demands proportionally faster weighted compression
of the material-pair geometry carrying the critical norm.  For the nonlinear
change of global \(H\), that weighted compression and critical production are
the same signed \(\sigma q\) term in (TL.338)--(TL.342), with pressure,
viscosity and the surrounding field still determining its future.

This does not yet prove that increasing the spectral mean \(\varkappa\) comes
from one particular material cohort reaching smaller \(|R_{ab}|\), nor that
compression degrades the cohort's future ability to deliver.

What (TL.342) does not prove is that the weighted compression must lose its
ability to recur.  The labels persist, but \(\mathbb Q_t\) is an instantaneous
reweighting that can migrate among them and amplify on previously negligible
pairs; critical scaling leaves the integrated requirement invariant.  A
complete contraction theorem must control that migration by a datum-bounded
additive carrier action, stopping-time trace or capacity inequality, or another
signed coercive estimate on the same labels.  Without that new upper law,
(TL.342) is an exact causal carrier identity and service floor,
not a proof of a minimum physical scale, no-Zeno descent, bounded \(H\), or
continuation.

## 16. The complete participation rate and its fixed-label tangent

The last paragraph of Section 15 does not license reselection of a new fluid
population.  The labels remain fixed; only the density with which the same
all-pair carrier contributes to \(H\) changes.  That change has an exact
evolution and an exact nonnegative metric action.

Write

\[
A_{ab}:=-\delta_{ab}\nabla p+\nu\,\delta_{ab}\Delta u
\tag{TL.343}
\]

and, on the set \(q_{ab}>0\), define

\[
\alpha_{ab}
:=\frac{R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab}}{|R_{ab}|^2}
=\sigma_{ab},
\qquad
\chi_{ab}
:=\frac{V_{ab}\cdot A_{ab}}{|V_{ab}|^2}.
\]

The complete logarithmic pair-participation rate is

\[
\boxed{
g_{ab}
:=-4\alpha_{ab}+2\chi_{ab}
=2\frac{V_{ab}\cdot A_{ab}}{|V_{ab}|^2}-4\sigma_{ab}
=:\gamma_{ab}.
}
\tag{TL.344}
\]

Here \(-4\alpha\) is the logarithmic change of the inverse fourth power of
the same pair separation, while \(2\chi\) is the logarithmic change of its
squared relative velocity under the complete material acceleration
\(A=-\delta\nabla p+\nu\delta\Delta u\).  They are not independent
mechanisms or separately payable actions.  They are the two simultaneous
contributions to one exact rate,

\[
g_{ab}
=\frac d{dt}\log\frac{|V_{ab}|^2}{|R_{ab}|^4}
=\frac d{dt}\log q_{ab}.
\]

The strain row is indispensable: by the fundamental theorem of calculus,
\(\alpha_{ab}\) is the directional strain integrated along the current
segment joining the two material endpoints.  Transport is already in the
moving labels, incompressibility preserves label measure, and the whole-field
pressure and viscous responses occur together in \(A_{ab}\).  Thus \(g\) is
a readout of the same complete participation history, not a replacement
carrier.

Equation (TL.338) is precisely \(\dot q_{ab}=g_{ab}q_{ab}\) where
\(q_{ab}>0\).  At zeros, read \(gq\) as \(\dot q\); the Fisher--Rao
formula below holds when the displayed action is finite and otherwise by
lower-semicontinuous extension.  Since \(4\pi^2H=\iint q\),

\[
\boxed{
\bar g(t):=\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}[g]
=\frac{H'(t)}{H(t)},
\qquad
\partial_t d\mathbb Q_t
=(g-\bar g)d\mathbb Q_t.
}
\tag{TL.345}
\]

Consequently, if one interval \(J=[s,t]\) completes a genuine
\(q_*\)-fold whole-field strengthening,
\(H(t)=q_*H(s)\), then the exact participation delivered on that interval is

\[
\boxed{
\int_J\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_r}[g(r)]\,dr
=\int_J\frac{H'(r)}{H(r)}\,dr
=\log q_*.
}
\tag{TL.345a}
\]

This is not a new cost assigned to the event.  It is the endpoint identity
for the same complete pair service already represented by the source-free
critical current.  Indeed, (TL.169), (TL.340), and (TL.345) give pointwise

\[
\frac{\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(0,t)}{4H(t)}
=\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}[g]
+\nu\frac{\mathcal D}{H},
\]

and hence on the same \(q_*\)-record interval

\[
\boxed{
\int_J\frac{\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(0,r)}{4H(r)}\,dr
=\log q_*
+\nu\int_J\frac{\mathcal D(r)}{H(r)}\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.345b}
\]

Thus \(g\) is the fixed-label material-pair readout of what the existing
completed current delivers.  The term \(\log q_*\) is its net whole-field
record production, and the second term is the simultaneous critical loss
during that same delivery.  Neither term defines another route or another
fluid object.

For successive first-hit record intervals \(J_0,\ldots,J_{N-1}\), each with
\(H(t_{j+1})=q_*H(t_j)\), the same identity adds without resetting any
carrier:

\[
\boxed{
N\log q_*
+\nu\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{J_j}\frac{\mathcal D}{H}\,dr
=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{J_j}
\frac{\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(0,r)}{4H(r)}\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.345c}
\]

The trajectory's upper theorem is therefore precise: derive from the
source-free completed-current law and the initial datum a uniform bound for
the right side of (TL.345c), with common internal transfer cancelled in the
stopped carrier rather than reintroduced as fresh delivery.  Such a bound
immediately limits \(N\), bounds \(H\), and yields continuation.  The pair
rate \(g\) supplies the exact record readout inside that theorem; it is not a
separate payer for it.

Thus apparent movement of the active population is one replicator tangent on
the original label space; no pair is replaced and no new source is inserted.
If \(m=da\,db\) and \(\varrho_t=d\mathbb Q_t/dm\), then

\[
\boxed{
4\int\left|\partial_t\sqrt{\varrho_t}\right|^2dm
=\operatorname{Var}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\gamma).
}
\tag{TL.346}
\]

The right side is the exact squared Fisher--Rao speed, or action density, of the active all-pair
population.  It vanishes under proportional evolution of every pair weight, so it
does not charge common proportional evolution as migration.  Conversely, any change of
which fixed labels carry the critical norm has positive action unless the
change is null.

This action controls the mass change of each fixed coarse label set.  Let
\(B\) be any fixed measurable set of material-pair labels and put

\[
x_B(t):=\mathbb Q_t(B).
\tag{TL.347}
\]

From (TL.345),

\[
x_B'
=\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\mathbf1_B,\gamma).
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz and
\(\operatorname{Var}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\mathbf1_B)=x_B(1-x_B)\) give the exact
data-processing bound for \(0<x_B<1\), with the endpoint cases read by a
limiting convention,

\[
\boxed{
\frac{|x_B'|^2}{x_B(1-x_B)}
\le\operatorname{Var}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\gamma).
}
\tag{TL.348}
\]

Hence, when two candidate populations are realized by persistent fixed subsets
of the labelled carrier, their fraction speed is a coarse projection of the
single migration action (TL.346).  Equation (TL.348) does not control an
adaptive receiver set \(B(t)\), whose motion creates an additional selection
boundary term, nor does it yet sum a changing family without recounting.

The fixed-label compression row can also be separated exactly from reweighting
without splitting the VPI evolution.  Assuming the logarithmic moment and
variance below are finite, choose one fixed reference length \(r_*>0\) and set

\[
\ell_{ab}:=\log\frac{|R_{ab}|}{r_*},
\qquad
\mathscr L(t):=\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}[\ell].
\tag{TL.349}
\]

Because \(\dot\ell=\sigma\), differentiation using (TL.345) gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr L'
=\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}[\sigma]
+\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\ell,\gamma).
}
\tag{TL.350}
\]

The first term follows the same material pairs to smaller or larger
separation.  The covariance is exactly the change caused by shifting critical
weight among those persistent pairs.  It obeys

\[
\boxed{
\left|\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\ell,\gamma)\right|
\le
\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\ell)}
\sqrt{\operatorname{Var}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\gamma)}.
}
\tag{TL.351}
\]

Finally, (TL.315), (TL.319), and (TL.340) give the whole-field record rate
directly on these labels:

\[
\boxed{
\frac d{dt}\log\varkappa
=-4\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}[\sigma]-2\nu\mathcal W
=-4\mathscr L'
+4\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\ell,\gamma)
-2\nu\mathcal W.
}
\tag{TL.352}
\]

Equation (TL.352) is an exact fixed-label compression--reweighting
decomposition.  A new effective-scale record is the signed sum of material
compression, covariance from migration of critical weight across the fixed
label field, and the subtracted spectral dispersion \(2\nu\mathcal W\).
These terms come from differentiating one expectation on one label carrier,
but the identity is not yet a non-recounting family estimate.

For this route, the remaining candidate estimate is now explicit: combine the
material evolution of \(\ell\) with the Fisher--Rao action density in
(TL.344)--(TL.346) and seek a datum-finite bound on the record-selected positive
compression--migration covariance.  The pointwise Cauchy bound (TL.351) does
not absorb or time-integrate that covariance, and no datum bound for the full
right side is asserted.  In particular, kinetic-energy dissipation controls
neither \(\int\operatorname{Var}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\gamma)dt\) nor the negative
variation of \(\mathscr L\); Fisher--Rao geometry supplies an activity measure,
not a finite payer.  Material log-separation spread and the radial spectral
covariance \(\mathcal W\) are also distinct readouts, so coercivity between
them would itself be a new theorem.

## 17. The shared-interface law turns relative strengthening into current curvature

The conditional two-band identity becomes causal only after its nonlinear
coefficients are returned to the participating-energy current that generated
them.  For every fixed dyadic chord band \([s,2s]\), (TL.207), (TL.225),
(TL.226), and (TL.288) give

\[
\boxed{
U_s'
=\mathcal Q_E(s)-\mathcal Q_E(2s)-2\nu\lambda_sU_s,
\qquad
2c_sU_s=\mathcal Q_E(s)-\mathcal Q_E(2s).
}
\tag{TL.353}
\]

Now take the fine band \(c=[r,2r]\) and the adjacent coarse band
\(p=[2r,4r]\).  Their labels describe fixed readouts of the same carrier; no
ancestry is inferred.  Abbreviate

\[
Q_f:=\mathcal Q_E(r),
\qquad
Q_m:=\mathcal Q_E(2r),
\qquad
Q_c:=\mathcal Q_E(4r),
\tag{TL.354}
\]

where \(Q_m\) is the identical shared-interface current in both band
balances.  Equation (TL.353) gives

\[
2c_cU_c=Q_f-Q_m,
\qquad
2c_pU_p=Q_m-Q_c.
\tag{TL.355}
\]

With \(U=U_p+U_c\), \(x=U_c/U\),
\(W:=U_pU_c/U=Ux(1-x)\), and the actual mean-frequency gap
\(\Delta=\lambda_c-\lambda_p\), substitution into (TL.301) yields

\[
\boxed{
Ux'+2\nu W\Delta
=(1-x)Q_f+xQ_c-Q_m.
}
\tag{TL.356}
\]

The right side is the affine defect of the one current at the shared
boundary.  Denote it by

\[
\mathfrak C_{pc}
:=(1-x)Q_f+xQ_c-Q_m.
\tag{TL.357}
\]

This is the exact throughput statement that the normalized coefficients
alone could not provide.  If the participating-energy current is affine
across the three boundaries, then \(\mathfrak C_{pc}=0\): the relative
fine-band fraction cannot increase against a positive frequency gap.
Relative strengthening requires positive curvature of the actual current,
and the simultaneous term \(2\nu W\Delta\) is subtracted before the
population can move.

On the positive-fraction set \(J_+=\{x'>0\}\), if \(\Delta\ge0\), (TL.356)
implies \(\mathfrak C_{pc}>0\) and

\[
\boxed{
\int_{J_+}\Delta\,\mathfrak C_{pc}\,dt
=\int_{J_+}U\Delta x'\,dt
+2\nu\int_{J_+}W\Delta^2\,dt.
}
\tag{TL.358}
\]

The second integral is twice the viscous between-band variance from
(TL.305).  The first is the critical content moved in relative population.
Under the event hypotheses (TL.306) and (TL.310),
\(U\Delta\ge\kappa A_*\), and therefore

\[
\boxed{
\int_{J_+}\Delta\,\mathfrak C_{pc}\,dt
\ge\kappa A_*(1-2a)
+2\nu\int_{J_+}W\Delta^2\,dt.
}
\tag{TL.359}
\]

Unlike the squared nonlinear projection in (TL.311), (TL.359) is a signed
lower quantum written entirely in the shared participating-energy current and
the loss produced during that same relative change.  Outer-boundary influx,
shared-interface passage, stored stock and viscous leakage have not been
assigned to separate mechanisms.

Because \(\partial_\rho\mathcal Q_E=-\mathfrak T_E\) by
(TL.189), (TL.192), and (TL.205), the same affine defect has the exact
complete-source representation

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak C_{pc}
=(1-x)\int_r^{2r}\mathfrak T_E(\rho,t)\,d\rho
-x\int_{2r}^{4r}\mathfrak T_E(\rho,t)\,d\rho.
}
\tag{TL.360}
\]

Thus \(\mathfrak C_{pc}\) is not a new current inserted between the bands.  It
is the signed difference between the complete VPI work already occurring in
the two adjacent parts of the same carrier.  A uniform through-current
cancels from the relative-population law; only its non-affine scale variation
can move the fraction against the frequency gap.

For a fixed dyadic lattice \(r_j=2^jR\), set

\[
\Theta_j(t):=\int_{r_j}^{2r_j}\mathfrak T_E(\rho,t)\,d\rho.
\tag{TL.361}
\]

Then (TL.360) is
\(\mathfrak C_j=(1-x_j)\Theta_j-x_j\Theta_{j+1}\).  Consequently every
source band enters at most two adjacent defects and

\[
\boxed{
\sum_j|\mathfrak C_j(t)|
\le2\sum_j|\Theta_j(t)|
\le2\int_0^\infty|\mathfrak T_E(\rho,t)|\,d\rho.
}
\tag{TL.362}
\]

This is the fixed-time bounded-overlap form of shared-interface
non-recounting.  It remains valid for any subfamily of adjacent pairs; changing
the fractions \(x_j\) does not increase the overlap constant.

The critical weighting of this source has an exact whole-field moment.  The
radial-symbol formula used in (TL.269) gives

\[
\int_0^\infty\rho^{-2}A_\rho\,d\rho
=c_\Phi\Lambda,
\qquad
c_\Phi:=C_0\int_0^\infty z^{-2}\Phi(z)\,dz\in(0,\infty).
\tag{TL.363}
\]

Therefore, with \(P_H=-\langle\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u),\Lambda u\rangle\),

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^\infty\rho^{-2}\mathfrak T_E(\rho,t)\,d\rho
=2c_\Phi P_H(t).
}
\tag{TL.364}
\]

Equations (TL.362)--(TL.364) locate the exact scale of the upper problem.
The signed critical moment is the already existing whole-field production,
whereas replacing it by
\(\int\rho^{-2}|\mathfrak T_E|\,d\rho\) would discard the cancellations and
ask for a stronger absolute-current estimate.  Hence the moving-chain proof
must preserve the sign of the affine defects through the shared boundaries;
an absolute-value summation is not the desired contraction.

The exact signed chain algebra shows what such preservation requires.  Let
\(Q_j=\mathcal Q_E(2^jR)\), and for the adjacent pair of bands \(j,j+1\)
write

\[
\mathfrak C_j=(1-x_j)Q_j-Q_{j+1}+x_jQ_{j+2}.
\tag{TL.365}
\]

For every finitely supported scalar sequence \(w_j\), index shifting gives

\[
\boxed{
\sum_jw_j\mathfrak C_j
=\sum_j
\left[w_j(1-x_j)-w_{j-1}+w_{j-2}x_{j-2}\right]Q_j.
}
\tag{TL.366}
\]

Thus all internal currents cancel exactly only when the selected weights obey
the adjoint recurrence

\[
\boxed{
w_j(1-x_j)-w_{j-1}+w_{j-2}x_{j-2}=0
}
\tag{TL.367}
\]

away from the outer endpoints.  This makes the changing-weight issue
explicit rather than hiding it in a moving-front slogan.

It also audits the critical weight.  If the relative fractions are constant,
\(x_j\equiv1/2\), then (TL.367) has only affine-in-\(j\) solutions
\(w_j=A+Bj\).  The critical frequency-gap weights of comparable dyadic
populations are exponential in \(j\), not affine.  Therefore ordinary
participating-energy current cancellation cannot by itself pay the
scale-critical sum in (TL.359).  This is an algebraic statement about the
readout identities, not admission of a self-similar fluid cascade.  It proves
that the strict contraction must additionally use the same-history loss of
scale shape or coherence already isolated in (TL.252), (TL.296), and
(TL.352); the energy-current telescope alone is structurally insufficient.

The current and coherence rows can be recombined before any inequality, but
the common change of total participating stock must remain.  Define the
child's absolute and relative excess critical contents above the parent mean
by

\[
\mathscr X_{pc}:=U_c(\lambda_c-\lambda_p)=U_c\Delta,
\qquad
\mathscr Y_{pc}:=\frac{\mathscr X_{pc}}U=x\Delta.
\tag{TL.368}
\]

Since \(U_c=Ux\), multiplying (TL.356) by \(\Delta\) and applying the product
rule gives the exact absolute law

\[
\boxed{
\Delta\mathfrak C_{pc}
=\mathscr X_{pc}'-U_c\Delta'-xU'\Delta
+2\nu W\Delta^2.
}
\tag{TL.369}
\]

Equivalently, division by \(U>0\) gives the exact relative law

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\Delta\mathfrak C_{pc}}U
=\mathscr Y_{pc}'-x\Delta'
+2\nu x(1-x)\Delta^2.
}
\tag{TL.369a}
\]

The last term in each formula is the between-band variance already present in
(TL.296).  The term \(-xU'\Delta\) in (TL.369) is compulsory: it is the
common participating-stock change removed by relative normalization.  Omitting
it would falsely treat common delivery as if it were transverse transfer.
The gap derivative is not a free scale motion: (TL.252) gives

\[
\boxed{
\Delta'
=2\lambda_c(\alpha_c-\nu)\mathfrak V_c
-2\lambda_p(\alpha_p-\nu)\mathfrak V_p.
}
\tag{TL.370}
\]

Therefore (TL.369) is a single signed critical receiver identity.  Its left
side is actual non-affine participating-energy throughput weighted by the
frequency gain; \(\mathscr X_{pc}\) is one-time stored excess critical
content; \(-xU'\Delta\) retains common delivery; \(-U_c\Delta'\) is the
simultaneous transverse change of the same two populations; and
\(2\nu W\Delta^2\) is the irreversible loss generated while they coexist.
No term has been assigned to an independently evolving shell, donor, or
damping process.

On any interval \([s,t]\),

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t\Delta\mathfrak C_{pc}\,dq
=\mathscr X_{pc}(t)-\mathscr X_{pc}(s)
-\int_s^tU_c\Delta'\,dq
-\int_s^t xU'\Delta\,dq
+2\nu\int_s^tW\Delta^2\,dq.
}
\tag{TL.371}
\]

The corresponding relative integral is

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t\frac{\Delta\mathfrak C_{pc}}U\,dq
=\mathscr Y_{pc}(t)-\mathscr Y_{pc}(s)
-\int_s^t x\Delta'\,dq
+2\nu\int_s^t x(1-x)\Delta^2\,dq.
}
\tag{TL.371a}
\]

These are the precise local forms of the objective's
throughput--storage--geometry--loss law.  They identify two coupled terms that
must be carried through chaining: common stock change in the absolute law and
signed transverse gap work in both laws.  The next no-return step must show,
on one ancestry-preserving moving family, how both pass into successor storage,
external current, or positive union loss rather than disappearing when the
receiver coordinates change.

The family-level no-recounting problem is correspondingly sharpened.  It is
not to bound arbitrary positive shell flux or the free norm of
\(\mathcal N\).  It is to sum the record-selected positive affine defects
\(\Delta\mathfrak C_{pc}\) through a moving adjacent-band chain while using
the exact cancellation of every shared \(Q_m\).  If that chain sum reduces to
the datum-generated outer boundary and the already finite loss, then
(TL.359) supplies the required fixed critical quantum.  Establishing that
moving-chain cancellation, including the changing interpolation weights
\(x\), is the next calculation; it is not asserted by the two-band identity.

## 18. The affine receiver is a marginal of one labelled all-incidence history

The fixed chord coordinates in Section 13 do not by themselves identify a
material predecessor.  They can, however, be lifted without approximation to
one persistent labelled carrier containing every endpoint pair and every
intermediate fluid point.

For three fixed initial labels \((a,b,c)\) and \(0<\theta<1\), write

\[
X_a:=X(a,t),
\quad
U_a:=u(X_a,t),
\quad
R_{ab}:=X_a-X_b,
\quad
Z_{ab}^{\theta}:=(1-\theta)X_b+\theta X_a,
\quad
W_{ab}^{\theta}:=(1-\theta)U_b+\theta U_a,
\tag{TL.372}
\]

and define the complete incidence defect and its velocity by

\[
\boxed{
\Xi_{abc}^{\theta}
:=X_c-Z_{ab}^{\theta},
\qquad
C_{abc}^{\theta}
:=\dot\Xi_{abc}^{\theta}
=U_c-W_{ab}^{\theta}.
}
\tag{TL.373}
\]

The labels never change.  The hypersurface \(\Xi=0\) records which labelled
material point currently occupies the affine intermediate position of the
two labelled endpoints; changing incidence is motion of this one triple field,
not substitution of a new point.

Volume preservation gives the exact labelled representation of the carrier
density in (TL.165):

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathfrak M(\rho,t)
=\int_{0}^{1}\!\iiint
&\frac{|C_{abc}^{\theta}(t)|^2}
{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)\rho^4}\,
\delta^{(3)}\!\left(\Xi_{abc}^{\theta}(t)\right)\\
&\times\delta\!\left(|R_{ab}(t)|-\rho\right)
\,da\,db\,dc\,d\theta .
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.374}
\]

Indeed, for fixed endpoints the map \(c\mapsto X_c\) has unit Jacobian, so
the incidence delta evaluates the unique label occupying the current
intermediate point.  The change from endpoint positions \((X_a,X_b)\) to
center, direction and length has Jacobian \(\rho^2\); it converts the
\(\rho^{-4}\) factor in (TL.374) into the
\(\rho^{-2}\) carrier weight in (TL.165).  Thus (TL.374) is exactly the same
all-intermediate object, now with its full label history retained.

The center and the full chord vector must remain in the carrier.  Projecting
bare label measure only onto \((\Xi,|R|)\) would integrate out translations,
would not be locally finite, and would discard the direction needed by the
VPI current.  Define instead

\[
\Gamma_t
:=\bigl(Z_{ab}^{\theta},R_{ab},\Xi_{abc}^{\theta},\theta\bigr)_{\#}
\bigl(da\,db\,dc\,d\theta\bigr).
\]

For every compactly supported smooth
\(\varphi(z,R,\xi,\theta)\), the exact kinematics give

\[
\boxed{
\frac d{dt}\int\varphi\,d\Gamma_t
=\int
\left[
W_{ab}^{\theta}\cdot\nabla_z\varphi
+V_{ab}\cdot\nabla_R\varphi
+C_{abc}^{\theta}\cdot\nabla_\xi\varphi
\right]d\Gamma_t,
}
\tag{TL.375}
\]

or, in weak conservation form,

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\Gamma_t
+\nabla_z\cdot(W\Gamma_t)
+\nabla_R\cdot(V\Gamma_t)
+\nabla_\xi\cdot(C\Gamma_t)=0.
}
\tag{TL.376}
\]

This is locally finite and retains center, direction, length, incidence and
label fraction.  It is the scalable all-field-point relation: every endpoint
pair and every intermediate label are coordinates of one continuing
configuration measure, and any pair or chord law is obtained only after
marginalization.

The velocity defect also evolves on those same labels.  If

\[
A_a:=-\nabla p(X_a,t)+\nu\Delta u(X_a,t),
\qquad
D_{abc}^{\theta}
:=A_c-\bigl[(1-\theta)A_b+\theta A_a\bigr],
\qquad
\sigma_{ab}:=\frac{R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab}}{|R_{ab}|^2},
\tag{TL.377}
\]

then

\[
\boxed{
\dot C_{abc}^{\theta}
=D_{abc}^{\theta}.
}
\tag{TL.378}
\]

The receiver is not the bare configuration measure.  It is the weighted
incidence trace of that measure, so the weight must evolve on the same labels.
Put

\[
\boxed{
q_{abc}^{\theta}
:=\frac{|C_{abc}^{\theta}|^2}
{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)|R_{ab}|^4},
\qquad
d\Omega_t:=q_{abc}^{\theta}\,d\Gamma_t.
}
\tag{TL.379}
\]

This weight changes the carrier domain.  Let

\[
\mathcal C^\circ
:=\mathbb R_z^3\times
(\mathbb R_R^3\setminus\{0\})\times
\mathbb R_\xi^3\times(0,1)_\theta .
\]

\(\Omega_t\) is a Radon measure on \(\mathcal C^\circ\), but it is not
locally finite on the closed configuration space containing \(R=0\) or
\(\theta=0,1\).  For a nonconstant field, an off-incidence configuration can
have \(C\to C_0\ne0\) as \(R\to0\), so \(q\sim |C_0|^2|R|^{-4}\) and the
three-dimensional \(R\)-integral diverges.  At fixed off-incidence
\(\xi\), the same issue gives \(q\sim c/\theta\) or
\(c/(1-\theta)\) at the fraction endpoints.  All weighted weak laws below
therefore use tests compactly supported in \(\mathcal C^\circ\).

The product rule on each fixed label gives

\[
\boxed{
\dot q_{abc}^{\theta}
=\frac{2C_{abc}^{\theta}\cdot D_{abc}^{\theta}
-4\sigma_{ab}|C_{abc}^{\theta}|^2}
{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)|R_{ab}|^4}.
}
\tag{TL.380}
\]

Consequently, for every \(\varphi\in C_c^\infty(\mathcal C^\circ)\), the
regular part of the weighted all-incidence carrier obeys the signed causal law

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\frac d{dt}\int\varphi\,d\Omega_t
=\int&\left[
W_{ab}^{\theta}\cdot\nabla_z\varphi
+V_{ab}\cdot\nabla_R\varphi
+C_{abc}^{\theta}\cdot\nabla_\xi\varphi
\right]d\Omega_t\\
&+\int\varphi\,
\frac{2C_{abc}^{\theta}\cdot D_{abc}^{\theta}
-4\sigma_{ab}|C_{abc}^{\theta}|^2}
{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)|R_{ab}|^4}
\,d\Gamma_t.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.381}
\]

The fixed-time carrier density is the material incidence-zero, fixed-length
restriction already defined by the convergent labelled formula (TL.374).
It is useful to denote that restriction by

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak M(\rho,t)
:=\operatorname{Tr}^{\rm mat}_{\xi=0,\ |R|=\rho}\Omega_t
\ \overset{\rm formal}{=}\
\int\delta^{(3)}(\xi)\,\delta(|R|-\rho)\,d\Omega_t.
}
\tag{TL.382}
\]

The last delta expression is mnemonic, not an ordinary Radon-measure trace.
At exact incidence \(C=O(\theta(1-\theta))\), so (TL.374) is finite under
the stated hypotheses; an ordinary smooth \(\xi\)-mollification has
off-incidence mass and can recover the endpoint divergence just identified.
Section 32 supplies the lawful definition: truncate
\(\theta\in[\delta,1-\delta]\), recenter a tube on the actual material
incidence label, take its width to zero, and only then let
\(\delta\downarrow0\).

Equation (TL.381) is the full configuration-space
throughput--storage--geometry law.  The three transport terms are actual
crossing of center, chord and incidence boundaries by the same labelled
configuration.  The final signed term is its simultaneous acceleration and
geometric-weight change.  Transport is carried by the material map;
incompressibility is the unit product Jacobian; pressure and viscosity occur
inside the one acceleration residual \(D_{abc}^{\theta}\); and
strain/rotation geometry is encoded by the joint motion of \(R\), \(\Xi\),
and \(C\).  Nothing is assigned to a separately evolving donor, receiver or
damping process.

One may not derive the trace law by applying (TL.381) to arbitrary smooth
approximations of both deltas.  The lawful sequential trace in Section 32
must keep the full \(R\)-flux, incidence flux and acceleration source
*together*; only their joined contraction obtains the Eulerian radial current
and viscous term in (TL.166).  No one of those pieces is separately equal to
\(\mathfrak F\), and the bare transport law (TL.375) alone does not imply
(TL.166).

Equations (TL.374)--(TL.382) remove label loss from the representation, but
they do not yet select or concatenate the successor cells required by the
no-Zeno theorem.  Regular cells compactly contained in
\(\mathcal C^\circ\) may be stopped as in Section 19.  A record-selected
family approaching incidence, collapsed separation or fraction endpoints
requires the sequential trace and uniform integrability before it is
licensed.  Only after that full stopped balance is formed may (TL.371) be
used as its adjacent-scale marginal.

## 19. Exact regular-cell balance and internal-interface cancellation

The lift above makes internal-interface single-counting a direct divergence
theorem, not an ancestry slogan.  For fixed \(\theta\), the transformation

\[
(X_a,X_b,X_c)\longmapsto
(Z_{ab}^{\theta},R_{ab},\Xi_{abc}^{\theta})
\]

has determinant of absolute value one.  The product material map also has
unit Jacobian.  Hence

\[
d\Gamma_t=dz\,dR\,d\xi\,d\theta
\tag{TL.383}
\]

at every smooth time.  In particular, (TL.381) is an ordinary local balance
on the regular configuration domain \(\mathcal C^\circ\).

Let \(B(t)\) be a bounded Lipschitz region with
\(\overline{B(t)}\Subset\mathcal C^\circ\), whose moving boundary has
configuration velocity \(b=(b_z,b_R,b_\xi,0)\).  More generally the same
formulas hold when all displayed bulk and trace integrals are finite.  Put

\[
\mathscr S_B(t):=\int_{B(t)}q\,d\Gamma_t,
\qquad
\mathscr R_B(t):=\int_{B(t)}
\frac{2C\cdot D-4\sigma|C|^2}
{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)|R|^4}\,d\Gamma_t,
\tag{TL.384}
\]

and, with \(v=(W,V,C,0)\), define the actual outward and inward boundary
throughputs

\[
\mathscr J_B^{\rm out}
:=\int_{\partial B(t)}[q(v-b)\cdot n]_+\,d\Sigma,
\qquad
\mathscr J_B^{\rm in}
:=\int_{\partial B(t)}[-q(v-b)\cdot n]_+\,d\Sigma.
\tag{TL.385}
\]

Reynolds transport applied to (TL.381) gives the exact signed law

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr S_B'
+\mathscr J_B^{\rm out}
-\mathscr J_B^{\rm in}
=\mathscr R_B.
}
\tag{TL.386}
\]

Therefore, on every interval \([s,t]\),

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t\mathscr J_B^{\rm out}\,dq
=\int_s^t\mathscr J_B^{\rm in}\,dq
+\mathscr S_B(s)-\mathscr S_B(t)
+\int_s^t\mathscr R_B\,dq.
}
\tag{TL.387}
\]

If a finite or countable locally finite admissible family of such cells
shares the same moving interfaces, and the union bulk and boundary integrals
are finite, the same trace \(q(v-b)\cdot n\) occurs with opposite normal on
the two sides.  Every internal face therefore cancels exactly when (TL.386)
is summed; the stored quantity is integrated over the union once, and only
the external boundary current and the source over the union remain.

This is algebraic interface single-counting, not yet dynamical
non-recounting.  A configuration can cross the same moving face more than
once, and the signed source \(\mathscr R_B\) has neither a one-way sign nor a
datum-finite upper bound.  Label persistence and internal cancellation alone
therefore do not make a record cell a no-return region.

The source \(\mathscr R_B\) is deliberately still signed.  Splitting it into
independently owned pressure, geometric and viscous ledgers would destroy the
VPI object.  On the complete incidence-zero radial marginal, their joined
contraction is exactly the current divergence and the negative bulk loss in
(TL.166), giving (TL.174).  For a record-selected stopped family, the remaining
proof task is stricter: first justify the singular sequential trace and its
boundary limits, then show that the union contraction of \(\mathscr R_B\)
has the same external-current-minus-bulk-loss form, and finally prevent
repeated crossings from being charged as new degradation.  Until all three
are proved, (TL.387) is only the exact regular-cell Reynolds law on persistent
labels, not the requested causal no-return contraction.

## 20. The complete three-point carrier has no internal energy source

The signed source in (TL.381) is created by differentiating the critical
weight and by restricting independent points to the affine-incidence trace.
Before either operation, the same affine defect obeys a stronger local law
with no positive bulk source.

Let \((x_a,x_b,x_c)\in(\mathbb R^3)^3\) be independent current positions and
put

\[
\gamma_c:=1,
\qquad
\gamma_b:=-(1-\theta),
\qquad
\gamma_a:=-\theta,
\qquad
C:=\sum_{m\in\{a,b,c\}}\gamma_m u(x_m,t),
\qquad
e_C:=\frac12|C|^2.
\tag{TL.388}
\]

The coefficients sum to zero and recover (TL.373) on the incidence surface
\(x_c=(1-\theta)x_b+\theta x_a\).  For each point coordinate,

\[
\nabla_{x_m}C=\gamma_m\nabla u(x_m,t),
\qquad
\nabla_{x_m}\cdot C=0.
\tag{TL.389}
\]

Write \(u_m=u(x_m,t)\) and \(p_m=p(x_m,t)\).  Multiplying the three copies of
the Navier--Stokes equation by the one combined residual, using (TL.389), and
applying the product rule gives

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t e_C
+\sum_{m\in\{a,b,c\}}
\nabla_{x_m}\cdot
\left(
u_m e_C+\gamma_m p_m C-\nu\nabla_{x_m}e_C
\right)
=-\nu\sum_{m\in\{a,b,c\}}\gamma_m^2|\nabla u_m|^2.
}
\tag{TL.390}
\]

This identity contains the physical receiver statement without metaphor.
The defect energy can change only by the complete boundary flux through the
three current-point coordinates, while the only interior term is the
nonnegative loss

\[
\mathcal L_C
:=\nu\sum_m\gamma_m^2|\nabla u_m|^2.
\tag{TL.391}
\]

The pressure contribution is part of the same boundary flux as transport and
viscous exchange.  It is neither an external source nor a separately priced
mechanism.

The stopped form is immediate.  Let \(\mathcal B(t)\) be a bounded Lipschitz
region in \((x_a,x_b,x_c,\theta)\)-space, with boundary velocity
\(b=(b_a,b_b,b_c,0)\), and set

\[
F_m:=u_m e_C+\gamma_m p_mC-\nu\nabla_{x_m}e_C,
\qquad
\mathcal E_{\mathcal B}(t):=\int_{\mathcal B(t)}e_C,
\qquad
\mathcal L_{\mathcal B}(t):=\int_{\mathcal B(t)}\mathcal L_C.
\tag{TL.392}
\]

With \(n_m\) the three point-coordinate components of the outward normal,
define the relative outward and inward currents by

\[
\mathcal J_{\mathcal B}^{\rm out}
:=\int_{\partial\mathcal B(t)}
\left[\sum_m(F_m-b_me_C)\cdot n_m\right]_+d\Sigma,
\qquad
\mathcal J_{\mathcal B}^{\rm in}
:=\int_{\partial\mathcal B(t)}
\left[-\sum_m(F_m-b_me_C)\cdot n_m\right]_+d\Sigma.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E_{\mathcal B}'
+\mathcal J_{\mathcal B}^{\rm out}
-\mathcal J_{\mathcal B}^{\rm in}
=-\mathcal L_{\mathcal B},
}
\tag{TL.393}
\]

and hence

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t\mathcal J_{\mathcal B}^{\rm out}\,dq
=\int_s^t\mathcal J_{\mathcal B}^{\rm in}\,dq
+\mathcal E_{\mathcal B}(s)-\mathcal E_{\mathcal B}(t)
-\int_s^t\mathcal L_{\mathcal B}\,dq.
}
\tag{TL.394}
\]

This is an exact signed causal law at the unweighted independent-three-point
carrier level: outgoing throughput is exactly incoming throughput plus
one-time stored energy minus remaining stock and irreversible loss.  It is not
yet the requested critical throughput--coherence contraction, because the
incidence trace and the radial critical weight have not been applied.  For a
locally finite partition of a moving configuration region, every shared face
cancels with the opposite normal, the initial stock is counted once, and
\(\mathcal L_C\) is additive.  Thus (TL.394) proves native unweighted
non-recounting without positive parts of shell flux and without choosing
independent donor and receiver objects.

Pulling (TL.390) back by the product material map keeps the initial labels
fixed because its product Jacobian is one.  Restricting to the affine-incidence
surface and disintegrating by \(|x_a-x_b|=\rho\) recovers the carrier used in
Sections 13 and 18, with the incidence-crossing flux retained.  The remaining
critical step is not another energy law.  It is the effect of the homogeneous
weight

\[
w_\theta(R):=
\frac1{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)|R|^4}.
\tag{TL.395}
\]

Multiplying (TL.390) by \(w_\theta\) shows exactly where apparent critical
gain enters:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\partial_t(w_\theta e_C)
+\sum_m\nabla_{x_m}\cdot(w_\theta F_m)
=-w_\theta\mathcal L_C
+(F_a-F_b)\cdot\nabla_R w_\theta,
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.396}
\]

because \(R=x_a-x_b\), \(\nabla_{x_a}w_\theta=\nabla_Rw_\theta\), and
\(\nabla_{x_b}w_\theta=-\nabla_Rw_\theta\).  The last term is not new fluid
energy.  It is the exact conversion produced when the same physical
