throughput crosses the scale-dependent critical weight.  After angular and
radial contraction it is the current correction already encoded in
\(\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}\) in (TL.167).

Equations (TL.394) and (TL.396) isolate the central inference without
flattening it: physical defect energy has strict leaky-receiver
non-amplification, whereas critical height can rise only through the signed
radial-weight conversion of that same boundary flux.  The ultraviolet gain
theorem is therefore exactly the assertion that, on a record-selected stopped
union, the positive radial-weight conversion is no larger than the external
incoming defect-energy throughput plus its one-time initial stock, after the
bulk loss in (TL.394) is retained.  This is the single term the next
contraction must control; common delivery and transverse deformation are its
two marginals in (TL.369)--(TL.371a).

## 21. Incompressibility converts radial gain into angular reorganization

The transport part of the critical conversion in (TL.396) has an additional
exact all-direction constraint.  In the endpoint coordinates

\[
x_a=Z+(1-\theta)R,
\qquad
x_b=Z-\theta R,
\]

the pair velocity and affine center velocity are

\[
V(Z,R)=u(x_a)-u(x_b),
\qquad
W(Z,R)=(1-\theta)u(x_b)+\theta u(x_a).
\tag{TL.397}
\]

Incompressibility at the two endpoints gives the separate configuration
divergences

\[
\boxed{
\nabla_R\cdot V=0,
\qquad
\nabla_Z\cdot W=0.
}
\tag{TL.398}
\]

Write \(R=\rho n\) and decompose the actual chord motion as

\[
V=\rho\sigma n+\rho g,
\qquad
\sigma:=\frac{R\cdot V}{|R|^2},
\qquad
g:=\frac{(I-n\otimes n)V}{\rho},
\qquad
g\cdot n=0.
\tag{TL.399}
\]

The polar-coordinate form of the first identity in (TL.398) is

\[
\boxed{
\operatorname{div}_{\mathbb S^2}g
=-3\sigma-\rho\,\partial_\rho\sigma.
}
\tag{TL.400}
\]

The divergence theorem in the \(R\)-ball also gives, at every fixed
\((Z,\rho,\theta,t)\),

\[
\boxed{
\int_{\mathbb S^2}\sigma(Z,\rho,n,\theta,t)\,dn=0.
}
\tag{TL.401}
\]

Thus there is no all-direction radial compression available to feed a finer
receiver.  Contraction in some chord directions occurs simultaneously with
expansion and directional transport in the rest of the same pair field.

This statement couples directly to the active affine defect.  Let

\[
A(n):=|C_{\theta,\rho,n}(Z,t)|^2,
\qquad
\bar A:=\frac1{4\pi}\int_{\mathbb S^2}A(n)\,dn.
\]

Then (TL.401) and the sharp first spherical Poincare eigenvalue give

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\left|\int_{\mathbb S^2}\sigma A\,dn\right|
&=\left|\int_{\mathbb S^2}\sigma(A-\bar A)\,dn\right|\\
&\le \frac1{\sqrt2}
\|\sigma\|_{L^2(\mathbb S^2)}
\|\nabla_{\mathbb S^2}A\|_{L^2(\mathbb S^2)}.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.402}
\]

The transport contribution to the last term of (TL.396) is exactly

\[
(u_a-u_b)e_C\cdot\nabla_Rw_\theta
=-4w_\theta\sigma e_C.
\tag{TL.403}
\]

Therefore positive critical gain from chord contraction is necessarily a
covariance between contraction and the angular distribution of the same
affine-defect energy.  It vanishes for an angularly impartial receiver, and
(TL.402) forces every nonzero value to carry angular reorganization of that
receiver.  Equation (TL.400) supplies the simultaneous directional motion;
there is no radial contraction channel independent of it.

This is the exact geometric part of the stretchability-collapse inference.
It does not yet absorb the pressure and viscous pieces of the joined flux in
(TL.396), and (TL.402) alone is not a datum-finite estimate.  The remaining
gain calculation must retain those pieces and show that their stopped-union
sum converts the angular derivative in (TL.402) into the bulk loss already
present in (TL.394), rather than estimating \(\sigma\) and \(A\) as unrelated
factors.

There is already an exact part of that conversion.  On the incidence surface,

\[
\nabla_{\mathbb S^2}C
=\theta(1-\theta)\rho
(\nabla u_b-\nabla u_a)P_{n^\perp},
\]

and hence the bulk loss in (TL.391) satisfies

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal L_C
\ge \frac{\nu}{2\rho^2}
|\nabla_{\mathbb S^2}C|^2.
}
\tag{TL.404}
\]

Since \(A=|C|^2\), this also gives

\[
|\nabla_{\mathbb S^2}A|^2
\le \frac{8\rho^2}{\nu}A\mathcal L_C.
\tag{TL.405}
\]

Combining (TL.402) and (TL.405) yields the joined estimate

\[
\boxed{
\left|\int_{\mathbb S^2}\sigma A\,dn\right|
\le
\frac{2\rho}{\sqrt\nu}
\|\sigma\|_{L^2(\mathbb S^2)}
\left(\int_{\mathbb S^2}A\mathcal L_C\,dn\right)^{1/2}.
}
\tag{TL.406}
\]

Thus contraction-biased critical gain and angular viscous loss are not merely
simultaneous qualitative effects: they occur in one inequality on the same
incidence population.  What remains in (TL.406) is the complete-field
contraction factor \(\sigma\) weighted by the active loss.  The next step is
to use its all-center/all-chord representation, rather than a pointwise
\(L^\infty\) estimate, so that finite initial energy can pay it without
reintroducing an active-weighted assumption.

The contraction factor itself has a scale-uniform datum-finite all-field
bound.  Let \(S=(\nabla u+\nabla u^{\mathsf T})/2\) be the rate-of-strain
tensor.  The fundamental theorem of calculus along the same endpoint chord
gives

\[
\boxed{
\sigma(Z,\rho,n,\theta,t)
=\int_{-\theta}^{1-\theta}
n\cdot S(Z+s\rho n,t)n\,ds.
}
\tag{TL.407}
\]

Jensen's inequality, translation invariance in \(Z\), and the energy identity
therefore yield, for every \((\rho,\theta)\),

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\int_0^T\!\int_{\mathbb R^3}\!\int_{\mathbb S^2}
|\sigma|^2\,dn\,dZ\,dt
&\le4\pi\int_0^T\|S(t)\|_2^2dt\\
&\le\frac{2\pi}{\nu}\,\mathcal E_0.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.408}
\]

This pays the unweighted whole-field contraction history directly from the
datum.  Combining it with (TL.406) shows precisely why the remaining step is
not another estimate of strain: the only unabsorbed factor is the active
weight \(A\) multiplying the simultaneous loss.  The strict-gain proof must
eliminate that weight through the same stopped throughput law, not assume its
integrability.

## 22. Subcritical covariance and the critical-weight audit

The active factor in (TL.406) can be removed before the scale-critical radial
weight is applied.  For \(M>0\), set

\[
A_M:=\min(A,M).
\tag{TL.409}
\]

Since \(\int_{\mathbb S^2}\sigma=0\), the argument of (TL.402) applies to
\(A_M\).  On \(\{A<M\}\), (TL.405) gives

\[
|\nabla_{\mathbb S^2}A_M|^2
\le\frac{8\rho^2M}{\nu}\mathcal L_C.
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
\left|\int_{\mathbb S^2}\sigma A_M\,dn\right|
\le
2\rho\sqrt{\frac M\nu}\,
\|\sigma\|_{L^2(\mathbb S^2)}
\left(\int_{\mathbb S^2}\mathcal L_C\,dn\right)^{1/2}.
}
\tag{TL.410}
\]

Let \(\rho(t)>0\) and \(\theta(t)\in(0,1)\) be arbitrary measurable choices
on a union \(J\) of pairwise disjoint time intervals.  The pointwise-in-time
proof of (TL.408) remains valid after these choices, so Cauchy--Schwarz and
Young's inequality give, for every \(\varepsilon>0\),

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\int_J\!\int_{\mathbb R^3}
\left|\int_{\mathbb S^2}\sigma A_M\,dn\right|dZ\,dt\\
&\qquad\le
\varepsilon
\int_J\!\int_{\mathbb R^3}\!\int_{\mathbb S^2}
\mathcal L_C\,dn\,dZ\,dt
+\frac{2\pi\rho_*^2M_*}{\varepsilon\nu^2}\mathcal E_0,
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.411}
\]

whenever \(\rho(t)^2M(t)\le\rho_*^2M_*\) on \(J\); in (TL.411), \(A_M\)
uses the corresponding time-dependent threshold \(M(t)\).
This is one measurable \((\rho(t),\theta(t),M(t))\) selection at each time;
simultaneous overlapping cell families require the stopped-union
multiplicity and trace construction and are not covered by (TL.411) alone.

Choose the scale-covariant threshold

\[
M(t)=\kappa\frac{\nu^2}{\rho(t)^2}
\qquad(\kappa>0).
\tag{TL.412}
\]

Then the datum term in (TL.411) is simply
\(2\pi\kappa\mathcal E_0/\varepsilon\), independently of how far the selected
scale descends and independently of the number of disjoint record intervals.
This proves datum-finite absorption of the *unweighted* subcritical angular
covariance.

It does not yet absorb the critical conversion in (TL.396).  After the
incidence trace and the endpoint polar Jacobian, the effective weight is

\[
\widetilde w_\theta(\rho)
:=\frac1{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)\rho^2}.
\tag{TL.413}
\]

Applying Young's inequality to (TL.410) with this weight gives

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\widetilde w_\theta
\left|\int_{\mathbb S^2}\sigma A_M\,dn\right|
&\le
\varepsilon\widetilde w_\theta
\int_{\mathbb S^2}\mathcal L_C\,dn\\
&\quad+
\frac{M}{\varepsilon\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)\nu}
\int_{\mathbb S^2}|\sigma|^2\,dn.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.414}
\]

At the scale-covariant threshold (TL.412), the final coefficient is
\(\kappa\nu/[\varepsilon\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)\rho^2]\).  The factor
\(\rho^{-2}\) is exactly the critical throughput--lifetime balance; (TL.408)
does not sum it over a descending record family.  Hence the earlier unweighted
datum payment cannot be promoted to critical absorption.

This failure is sharp at the level of the available measure bound.  For
dyadic \(\rho_j=2^{-j}\), disjoint time intervals can carry nonnegative
numbers \(a_j=\int_{J_j}\|\sigma_{\rho_j}\|_2^2dt=\rho_j^2\).  Then

\[
\sum_j a_j<\infty,
\qquad
\sum_j\rho_j^{-2}a_j=\infty.
\]

This is an audit sequence for (TL.408), not an asserted Navier--Stokes
history.  It proves that no summation argument using only the unweighted
datum measure can establish the critical stopped-family bound; a PDE
no-return relation between successive cells is indispensable.

The complementary supercritical incidence set remains

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal U_\kappa
:=\{(Z,\rho,n,\theta,t):\rho|C|>\sqrt\kappa\,\nu\}.
}
\tag{TL.415}
\]

Thus truncation identifies the physical circulation threshold but does not
close either side by itself.  A valid contraction has to combine the
subcritical \(\rho^{-2}\)-weighted contraction history and the supercritical
set \(\mathcal U_\kappa\) inside the single stopped telescope (TL.394), so
that the same physical energy crossing successive scales is not charged once
per radial weight.

## 23. The full vector pair tangent forces deformation but not yet degradation

The scalar weight law in Section 16 sees which fixed labels carry critical
strength, but it discards how their delivery vectors turn.  Both rows can be
kept on the same material-pair carrier.  Write

\[
F_{ab}:=\dot Y_{ab},
\qquad
K_R:=\frac{I-2\widehat R\otimes\widehat R}{|R|^2},
\qquad
\mathscr B_{ab}:=\dot F_{ab},
\qquad
M:=\iint|F|^2\,da\,db=4\pi^2H.
\tag{TL.416}
\]

Thus \(F=K_RV\).  With
\(A=-\delta\nabla p+\nu\delta\Delta u\), differentiation on the same
labels gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr B
=K_RA+(D_RK_R[V])V.
}
\tag{TL.417}
\]

More explicitly,

\[
(D_RK_R[V])V
=-2\sigma F
-\frac{2}{|R|^2}
\left[
(\dot{\widehat R}\otimes\widehat R
+\widehat R\otimes\dot{\widehat R})V
\right],
\qquad
\dot{\widehat R}
=\frac{(I-\widehat R\otimes\widehat R)V}{|R|}.
\]

Equation (TL.417) is one material acceleration: pressure and viscosity occur
inside \(A\), while transport, compression, strain and rotation occur through
the simultaneous motion of \(R\) and \(K_R\).  The rows are displayed only
to identify the geometry; they are not assigned independent budgets.

On \(q=|F|^2>0\), put

\[
e_{ab}:=\frac{F_{ab}}{|F_{ab}|},
\qquad
\bar\gamma:=\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}\gamma=\frac{H'}H.
\]

Since \(2F\cdot\mathscr B=\gamma|F|^2\), the actual vector tangent has the
orthogonal decomposition

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr B
=\frac{\bar\gamma}{2}F
+\frac{\gamma-\bar\gamma}{2}F
+|F|\dot e.
}
\tag{TL.418}
\]

The first row is common signed critical-strength change.  The second is
centered magnitude migration among the original labels.  The third is
transverse turning of the same delivery vectors.  The first two are globally
orthogonal because

\[
\iint(\gamma-\bar\gamma)|F|^2\,da\,db=0,
\]

and the turning row is pointwise orthogonal to \(F\).  For a zero-safe
statement set

\[
Z_F(t):=\{(a,b):F_{ab}(t)=0\},
\qquad
\mathfrak z_F(t):=\frac1M\iint_{Z_F(t)}|\mathscr B_{ab}|^2\,da\,db.
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
4\frac{\|\mathscr B\|_{L^2(da\,db)}^2}{M}
=\bar\gamma^2
+\operatorname{Var}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\gamma)
+4\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}|\dot e|^2
+4\mathfrak z_F.
}
\tag{TL.419}
\]

Equivalently, for the normalized vector carrier

\[
\Phi:=\frac{F}{\sqrt M},
\qquad \|\Phi\|_2=1,
\]

one has

\[
\boxed{
4\|\dot\Phi\|_2^2
=\operatorname{Var}_{\mathbb Q_t}(\gamma)
+4\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q_t}|\dot e|^2
+4\mathfrak z_F.
}
\tag{TL.420}
\]

The defect records acceleration born on a zero-increment pair.  At every
positive analytic time of a nonconstant solution \(Z_F(t)\) has pair measure
zero, so \(\mathfrak z_F=0\); retaining it makes the identities valid at a
general smooth initial surface.  Thus (TL.346) is exactly the
centered-magnitude projection of (TL.420).  Fisher--Rao sees migration but is
blind to transverse turning and is not a complete coherence payer.

The common row also gives an exact speed cost.  If
\(H(t)=2H(s)>0\), then Cauchy--Schwarz and (TL.419) imply

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t
\frac{\|\mathscr B(q)\|_2^2}{4\pi^2H(q)}\,dq
\ge
\frac{(\log2)^2}{4(t-s)}.
}
\tag{TL.421}
\]

A faster doubling therefore requires a larger normalized pair-acceleration
action.  Together with the shorter viscous clock and the simultaneous service
floor in (TL.318) and (TL.342), this is the faster-delivery half of the
objective on persistent labels.  It is not a datum-finite upper bound on that
action.

There is nevertheless a genuine qualitative coupling.  If
\(\dot\Phi=0\) at a positive time, then the direct normalized-vector identity
gives

\[
\mathscr B=cF
\quad\hbox{for every pair},
\qquad
c=\frac{H'}{2H},
\tag{TL.422}
\]

where equality almost everywhere extends by continuity.  Fix one current
endpoint \(x\) and send the other endpoint \(y\) to infinity.  Because \(u\)
and \(D_tu\) decay, while

\[
(D_RK_R[V])V=O(|R|^{-3}|V|^2),
\]

multiplication of (TL.422) by \(|R|^2\) and passage to the limit gives

\[
D_tu(x)=c\,u(x).
\]

The Navier--Stokes equation then yields

\[
\nu\Delta u-\nabla p=cu,
\qquad
\nu\Delta\omega=c\omega.
\]

An \(L^2\) vorticity satisfying the second identity has Fourier support only
on the zero-measure set \(\{\nu|\xi|^2=-c\}\), and hence vanishes.  A
divergence-free finite-energy curl-free field on \(\mathbb R^3\) is zero.
Therefore

\[
\boxed{
H>0\ \hbox{and}\ H'>0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\|\dot\Phi\|_2>0.
}
\tag{TL.423}
\]

Positive critical amplification cannot be an exactly unchanged common ray of
the complete all-pair geometry.  The same flow that increases \(H\) must at
that instant migrate some normalized pair weight, turn some pair-delivery
vectors, or both.  This proves deformation.  It does not prove that the
deformation is adverse, irreversible, or bounded below by a record-independent
quantum.

The reason the exact rigidity is noncoercive is itself visible on the carrier.
For every \(L>0\), volume preservation and
\(|u(x)-u(y)|^2\le2(|u(x)|^2+|u(y)|^2)\) give

\[
\boxed{
\mathbb Q_t\{|R|>L\}
\le\frac{8}{\pi}\frac{\mathcal E(t)}{L H(t)}
=\frac{8}{\pi L\varkappa(t)}.
}
\tag{TL.424}
\]

Hence at an effective-scale record \(\varkappa\to\infty\), the remote pairs
used to force \(D_tu=cu\) have vanishing normalized critical weight.  More
precisely, for \(g_c:=D_tu-cu\), the surrounding-field row is recovered by the
renormalized far-pair shell, not by the ordinary critical probability:

\[
\boxed{
\lim_{L\to\infty}
L\iint_{L<|R_{ab}|<2L}
|\mathscr B_{ab}-cF_{ab}|^2\,da\,db
=4\pi\|g_c\|_2^2.
}
\tag{TL.425}
\]

For Schwartz data, (TL.425) follows from (TL.417), orthogonality of the
Householder factor in \(K_R\), decay of its
\(O(|R|^{-3}|V|^2)\) derivative row, and the vanishing at infinity of the
\(L^2\) correlation of \(g_c\).  The unrenormalized shell is only
\(O(L^{-1})\).  Thus the exact finite-energy rigidity lives in the far
surrounding collar that the active critical probability suppresses.

The corresponding compactness attempt loses the same row.  At hypothetical
records with \(H_n\to\infty\), let
\(r_n=\varkappa(t_n)^{-1}\) and use the fixed-viscosity normalization

\[
v_n(s,y)
:=r_nu(t_n+r_n^2s,x_n+r_ny).
\]

Then

\[
\varkappa(v_n)=1,
\qquad
\mathcal E(v_n)=H(v_n)=H_n.
\tag{TL.426}
\]

Thus the normalization that preserves \(\nu\) has no bounded full-field
energy.  Dividing by \(a_n=\sqrt{H_n}\) restores bounded energy and critical
height, but after the time change \(\tau=a_ns\) the exact equation is

\[
\partial_\tau w_n+\mathbb P(w_n\cdot\nabla w_n)
=\frac{\nu}{a_n}\Delta w_n,
\qquad
w_n:=\frac{v_n}{a_n}.
\tag{TL.427}
\]

The limit is Euler/vanishing-viscosity and can acquire loss of compactness or
Reynolds stress.  No normalization therefore makes the all-pair record class
compact while retaining the fixed-viscosity throughput--coherence coupling.
This is why the exact zero-defect rigidity in (TL.423) has no automatic
positive modulus.

There is no local substitute for that collar.  As a comparison test, a steady
trace-free symmetric affine strain \(u(x)=Ax\),
\(p=-\tfrac12x\cdot A^2x\), is an exact unforced fixed-viscosity
Navier--Stokes solution with \(\Delta u=0\).  Along a compressive eigenline
\(AR=\mu R\), \(\mu<0\), one has

\[
F=-\mu Y,
\qquad
\dot F=-\mu F,
\]

so that cohort amplifies with no centered migration or turning.  This affine
history has infinite energy and is not a Gold counterexample; it proves only
that a pointwise or isolated-cohort contraction cannot replace whole-field
participation.

Equations (TL.423)--(TL.427) locate the remaining central inference.  A
closing law must turn the qualitative inequality \(\|\dot\Phi\|_2>0\) into a
record-uniform signed loss of delivery coherence, join the active core to the
far surrounding collar, and place that loss inside the stopped telescope
(TL.394).  It must then bound the cumulative positive record charge from the
original datum.  Fisher--Rao distance, the acceleration norm, exact ray
rigidity and critical scaling do not supply those steps: they can cycle or
lack a datum-finite time integral.  Accordingly the minimum-scale, no-Zeno,
bounded-\(H\), and continuation conclusions remain open.

## 24. The supercritical circulation set is not a no-return region

The threshold set in (TL.415) does not itself supply the missing direction of
time.  Put

\[
h:=\frac{\rho^2|C|^2}{\nu^2}.
\]

Along one fixed material triple, \(\dot\rho/\rho=\sigma\) and
\(\dot C=D\), with the joined material acceleration residual from (TL.377).
Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\frac d{dt}\log h
=2\sigma+2\frac{C\cdot D}{|C|^2},
\qquad
D=-\delta\nabla p+\nu\,\delta\Delta u.
}
\tag{TL.428}
\]

Incompressibility makes the angular mean of \(\sigma\) vanish, but it gives no
sign to the joined pressure--viscosity term in (TL.428).  Crossing
\(\partial\mathcal U_\kappa=\{h=\kappa\}\) is therefore not one-way: a
component may be entered, exited and re-entered.  The supercritical set is not
a monovariant and cannot by itself turn (TL.394) into a no-relay theorem.

Critical scaling makes the obstruction quantitative.  Under

\[
u_\lambda(x,t)=\lambda u(\lambda x,\lambda^2t),
\qquad
\rho_\lambda=\frac{\rho}{\lambda},
\qquad
C_\lambda=\lambda C,
\]

one has

\[
\boxed{
\rho_\lambda|C_\lambda|=\rho|C|,
\qquad
\mathcal U_{\kappa,\lambda}=\mathcal U_\kappa
\quad\hbox{in normalized coordinates}.
}
\tag{TL.429}
\]

The affine critical carrier scales by

\[
\mathfrak M_\lambda(\rho/\lambda,t/\lambda^2)
=\lambda\mathfrak M(\rho,t),
\]

so every corresponding radial band retains the same critical mass:

\[
\boxed{
\int_{I/\lambda}\mathfrak M_\lambda(r,t/\lambda^2)\,dr
=\int_I\mathfrak M(\rho,t)\,d\rho.
}
\tag{TL.430}
\]

The kinetic energy and the corresponding unweighted defect stock and
time-integrated physical loss instead scale like \(\lambda^{-1}\) (or faster
for a full configuration cell).  Thus a scale family can retain fixed
critical strength and the same supercritical status while its successive
unweighted payments form a geometric, summable sequence.  This is a scaling
countertest for estimates, not a constructed same-fluid Zeno history; it
proves that the unweighted telescope alone has the wrong homogeneity for the
Gold conclusion.

Finite energy gives only an upper size estimate.  At fixed
\((\rho,\theta,t)\), translation invariance and the triangle inequality give

\[
\int_{\mathbb R^3}\int_{\mathbb S^2}|C|^2\,dn\,dZ
\le32\pi\mathcal E(t).
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
|\mathcal U_\kappa(\rho,\theta,t)|
\le
\frac{32\pi\mathcal E_0}{\kappa\nu^2}\rho^2.
}
\tag{TL.431}
\]

Here the left side is the \(dZ\,dn\) measure of the displayed fixed
\((\rho,\theta,t)\) slice.  There is no lower component volume, residence
time, or physical-loss quantum.
Components may become arbitrarily thin in configuration space unless a new
derivative/trace capacity estimate forbids it.

The accounting fork is exact.  If (TL.394) is summed only over separated
connected components of \(\mathcal U_\kappa\), exit through a subcritical
corridor and later re-entry are both external faces, so the same physical
throughput can be recounted.  If the corridors are included to cancel those
faces, their conversion is precisely the unresolved
\(\rho^{-2}\)-weighted term in (TL.414).  Hence the supercritical and
subcritical branches cannot be closed separately.

The remaining bridge is a record-stopped weighted flux/capacity/no-reentry
estimate on the complete union: it must count the subcritical corridors once,
control re-entry across \(\partial\mathcal U_\kappa\), and bound the
cumulative positive radial conversion from the datum.  No such estimate is
proved here, and the threshold decomposition is not promoted to a strict
Gold reduction.

## 25. Critical radial gain is a completed current of the full carrier

The factor \(\rho^{-2}\) left by the separated estimate (TL.414) is not a
second physical bill.  It appears because the critical radial weight was
differentiated and its derivative was then estimated apart from the complete
configuration current.  Before any positive part or incidence contraction is
taken, that derivative can be restored exactly to the radial current that
generated it.

For fixed \(0<\theta<1\), change the independent three-point coordinates in
(TL.390) to

\[
Z=\theta x_a+(1-\theta)x_b,
\qquad
R=x_a-x_b,
\qquad
\Xi=x_c-Z.
\tag{TL.432}
\]

The inverse map is
\(x_a=Z+(1-\theta)R\),
\(x_b=Z-\theta R\), and \(x_c=Z+\Xi\); its absolute Jacobian is one.  The
complete currents corresponding to these coordinates are therefore

\[
\boxed{
F_Z=\theta F_a+(1-\theta)F_b,
\qquad
F_R=F_a-F_b,
\qquad
F_\Xi=F_c-\theta F_a-(1-\theta)F_b.
}
\tag{TL.433}
\]

Direct substitution of the transformed gradients gives the exact joined law

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t e_C
+\nabla_Z\!\cdot F_Z
+\nabla_R\!\cdot F_R
+\nabla_\Xi\!\cdot F_\Xi
=-\mathcal L_C.
}
\tag{TL.434}
\]

Nothing has been discarded in (TL.434): transport, pressure exchange and
viscous exchange are all inside the same three components of \(F_R\), while
the irreversible interior loss remains \(\mathcal L_C\).

Write \(R=\rho n\) and decompose the *full* radial-coordinate current as

\[
f_\rho:=n\cdot F_R,
\qquad
f_\angle:=(I-n\otimes n)F_R.
\tag{TL.435}
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\partial_t e_C
&+\nabla_Z\cdot F_Z+\nabla_\Xi\cdot F_\Xi
+\frac1{\rho^2}\partial_\rho(\rho^2f_\rho)
+\frac1\rho\operatorname{div}_{\mathbb S^2}f_\angle
=-\mathcal L_C.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.436}
\]

Put

\[
c_\theta:=\frac1{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)},
\qquad
w_\theta(\rho)=c_\theta\rho^{-4}.
\]

Multiplying (TL.436) by \(w_\theta\) first gives the raw weighted identity

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\partial_t(w_\theta e_C)
&+\nabla_Z\cdot(w_\theta F_Z)
+\nabla_\Xi\cdot(w_\theta F_\Xi)
+\frac1{\rho^2}\partial_\rho(\rho^2w_\theta f_\rho)
+\frac1\rho\operatorname{div}_{\mathbb S^2}(w_\theta f_\angle)\\
&=-w_\theta\mathcal L_C-\frac{4w_\theta}{\rho}f_\rho.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.437}
\]

The last term is the full version of the conversion in (TL.396).  In
particular, (TL.403) is only its transport marginal; \(f_\rho\) in (TL.437)
already contains the simultaneous pressure and viscous exchange of the same
three-point residual.

For a finite outer radius \(R_*>\rho\), define the completed radial current

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal Q_\rho^{R_*}(Z,\rho,n,\Xi,\theta,t)
&:=w_\theta(\rho)f_\rho(\rho)
+\frac1{\rho^2}\int_\rho^{R_*}
w_\theta'(s)s^2f_s(s)\,ds\\
&=w_\theta(\rho)f_\rho(\rho)
-\frac{4c_\theta}{\rho^2}
\int_\rho^{R_*}\frac{f_s(s)}{s^3}\,ds.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.438}
\]

Here every unshown coordinate is held fixed while \(s\) varies.  A direct
differentiation, with no inequality, gives

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{\rho^2}\partial_\rho
\bigl(\rho^2\mathcal Q_\rho^{R_*}\bigr)
=w_\theta(\rho)
\frac1{\rho^2}\partial_\rho(\rho^2f_\rho),
\qquad 0<\rho<R_*.
}
\tag{TL.439}
\]

Assume explicitly that
\(\int_\rho^\infty |f_s|s^{-3}ds<\infty\) and that the outer radial flux
vanishes.  These conditions hold on the stated Schwartz solution slices and
make the limit \(R_*\to\infty\) define \(\mathcal Q_\rho\).  Combining
(TL.436) and (TL.439), equivalently moving
the final term of (TL.437) back into its parent current, yields

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\partial_t(w_\theta e_C)
&+\nabla_Z\cdot(w_\theta F_Z)
+\nabla_\Xi\cdot(w_\theta F_\Xi)
+\frac1{\rho^2}\partial_\rho(\rho^2\mathcal Q_\rho)
+\frac1\rho\operatorname{div}_{\mathbb S^2}(w_\theta f_\angle)
=-w_\theta\mathcal L_C.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.440}
\]

Thus the complete critical carrier has no positive interior source.  Critical
radial strengthening is passage through \(\mathcal Q_\rho\); it is not a
quantity created inside a scale cell.  The nonlocal integral in (TL.438) does
not introduce a second history: it accumulates, at the same time and on the
same \((Z,n,\Xi,\theta)\) incidence ray, the larger-radius part of the one
current already present in (TL.434).

The corresponding stopped law is now exact.  Multiply (TL.440) by the polar
Jacobian \(\rho^2\) and set

\[
\mu:=\rho^2w_\theta e_C,
\qquad
\lambda:=\rho^2w_\theta\mathcal L_C,
\qquad
\mathbf K:=
\bigl(
\rho^2w_\theta F_Z,
\rho^2w_\theta F_\Xi,
\rho w_\theta f_\angle,
\rho^2\mathcal Q_\rho,
0
\bigr).
\tag{TL.441}
\]

The last component corresponds to the fixed parameter \(\theta\).  On the
product configuration manifold,

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\mu+\operatorname{Div}\mathbf K=-\lambda.
}
\tag{TL.442}
\]

Let \(\mathcal A(t)\) be a bounded Lipschitz configuration cell compactly
contained in \(\rho>0\) and \(0<\theta<1\), whose boundary has coordinate
velocity \(b\).  Cells meeting \(\rho=0\) or \(\theta=0,1\) require separate
finiteness and trace hypotheses because \(\mu\), \(\lambda\), and the
completed current are singular there.  With \(N\) its outward product normal,
define

\[
J_{\mathcal A}
:=\int_{\partial\mathcal A(t)}
(\mathbf K-\mu b)\cdot N\,d\Sigma.
\]

Reynolds transport gives

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^tJ_{\mathcal A}\,dq
=\int_{\mathcal A(s)}\mu
-\int_{\mathcal A(t)}\mu
-\int_s^t\!\int_{\mathcal A(q)}\lambda\,dq.
}
\tag{TL.443}
\]

Every common face of a locally finite moving partition has the identical
completed current and the opposite normal, so its signed contribution cancels
exactly.  Initial stock and irreversible loss are each counted once.
Equation (TL.443) is therefore the exact pre-incidence-trace critical
signed-throughput--one-time-storage--loss law for the displayed bounded product
cells.  It is one required carrier law from the objective, not yet the
terminal trace exclusion or the resulting contraction theorem.

The same cancellation survives record normalization when it is performed
once on the whole carrier rather than separately on successive events.  Let

\[
H_*(t):=\sup_{0\le r\le t}H(r),
\qquad
\beta_*(t):=\frac{H_*'(t)}{H_*(t)}\ge0,
\]

and set

\[
\widetilde\mu:=\frac\mu{H_*},
\qquad
\widetilde{\mathbf K}:=\frac{\mathbf K}{H_*},
\qquad
\widetilde\lambda:=\frac\lambda{H_*}.
\]

Since the same scalar \(H_*(t)\) multiplies every configuration point and
every common face, (TL.442) gives the exact joined law

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\widetilde\mu
+\operatorname{Div}\widetilde{\mathbf K}
=-\widetilde\lambda-\beta_*\widetilde\mu.
}
\tag{TL.443a}
\]

Thus formation of a new record appears as a one-way normalization term when
the same carrier is measured against its running record.  This is not an
additional physical loss or source; the physical loss remains
\(\widetilde\lambda\).  The normalization does not alter any internal current
cancellation.  With the signed relative boundary current

\[
\widetilde J_{\mathcal A}
:=\int_{\partial\mathcal A(t)}
(\widetilde{\mathbf K}-\widetilde\mu b)\cdot N\,d\Sigma,
\]

Reynolds transport gives

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\int_s^t\widetilde J_{\mathcal A}\,dq
={}&\int_{\mathcal A(s)}\widetilde\mu
-\int_{\mathcal A(t)}\widetilde\mu\\
&-\int_s^t\!\int_{\mathcal A(q)}\widetilde\lambda\,dq
-\int_s^t\beta_*(q)
\int_{\mathcal A(q)}\widetilde\mu\,dq.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.443b}
\]

After the lawful incidence trace and radial contraction,
\(\int_0^\infty\mathfrak M/H_*\,d\rho=4H/H_*\).  On new-record times
\(H=H_*\), so the last term in (TL.443b), exhausted through the traced
carrier, registers exactly \(4\,d\log H_*\).  This is the complete-current
version of the material-pair \(\log q_*\) identity (TL.345a).  The remaining
upper step is now confined to the incidence boundary: prove that its incoming
normalized completed current is datum-finite.  No interior transfer can be
used to pay that boundary more than once because it has already cancelled in
(TL.443b).

That upper step has an exact finite-layer formulation on the same current.
For every smooth scalar test \(\Phi\) compactly supported in a regular part of
the product carrier, multiplication of (TL.443a) and integration by parts give

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\int \Phi(t)\widetilde\mu(t)
&+\int_s^t\!\int
\Phi\bigl(\widetilde\lambda+\beta_*\widetilde\mu\bigr)
\\
&=\int \Phi(s)\widetilde\mu(s)
+\int_s^t\!\int
\left(widetilde\mu\,\partial_q\Phi
+\widetilde{\mathbf K}\cdot\nabla\Phi\right).
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.443c}
\]

Thus no new action has to be postulated.  A finite initial layer is precisely
a family of tests \(0\le\Phi\le1\), equal to one on the material incidence
tubes carrying the successive records, for which

\[
\boxed{
\widetilde\mu\,\partial_t\Phi
+\widetilde{\mathbf K}\cdot\nabla\Phi\le0,
\qquad
\sup\int\Phi(0)\frac{\mu(0)}{H(0)}<\infty.
}
\tag{TL.443d}
\]

The first inequality says that the boundary of the layer is carried by the
complete joined current, so no service enters through an artificially fixed
wall.  The second measures only the part of the original carrier from which
that current can reach the record incidence trace.  Passing (TL.443c) through
the fraction-truncated sequential material trace then bounds
\(4\log(H_*(T)/H(0))\) by this initial layer mass.  Conversely, any failure of
the desired datum bound must occur as loss of uniform finiteness of this
backward-reach layer at the terminal \((\rho,\Xi)=(0,0)\) boundary; regular
interior faces have already cancelled in (TL.443b).

Equation (TL.443d) is a calibration of the existing completed current, not a
second carrier or a separately accumulated payment.  The construction task is
to obtain this calibration directly from the initial material map and the
fixed-viscosity product diffusion, with a bound uniform as the incidence tube
and active radius tend to zero.

There is one compulsory scope distinction.  The incidence set \(\Xi=0\) is
not a material hypersurface of the product carrier.  Passing from (TL.440) to
the all-intermediate trace that represents \(H\) must retain the normal
\(F_\Xi\) current; it cannot silently turn trace stock into material stock.
After the complete \(\Xi\), angular and center contraction, the radial part of
(TL.440) is the labelled parent of \(\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}\) in (TL.167), and
the traced moving-band identity is (TL.174).  The remaining no-Zeno step is
therefore not critical bulk absorption: (TL.440) proves there is no critical
bulk source to absorb.  It is to show, using the same incidence current and
the smooth initial datum, that a record-selected trace cannot transport
nonzero completed critical throughput into the terminal boundary
\((t,\rho)=(T_*,0)\).  That trace statement is the next same-carrier
calculation; it is not supplied merely by (TL.443).

## 26. The incidence trace is motion of one inverse material map

The normal incidence current in Section 25 can be written without changing
the participating labels.  Let \(X_t\) be the volume-preserving material map
and \(A_t=X_t^{-1}\) its inverse.  For every fixed endpoint pair
\((a,b)\) and \(0<\theta<1\), define the unique current incidence label

\[
\boxed{
\iota_{ab}^{\theta}(t)
:=A_t\!\left(Z_{ab}^{\theta}(t)\right),
\qquad
Z_{ab}^{\theta}
=(1-\theta)X_b+\theta X_a.
}
\tag{TL.444}
\]

Thus \(X(\iota_{ab}^{\theta}(t),t)=Z_{ab}^{\theta}(t)\).  The endpoints
remain the same material labels; \(\iota\) records which label of that same
fluid presently occupies their affine incidence point.

Let

\[
\mathsf F(a,t):=D_aX(a,t),
\qquad
\mathsf G(a,t):=\mathsf F(a,t)^{\mathsf T}\mathsf F(a,t),
\]

and let

\[
C_{ab}^{\theta}(t)
:=u(Z_{ab}^{\theta}(t),t)
-\bigl[(1-\theta)U_b(t)+\theta U_a(t)\bigr].
\]

Differentiating the defining incidence relation gives the exact kinematic
identity

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf F(\iota,t)\dot\iota=-C_{ab}^{\theta},
\qquad
\dot\iota=-\mathsf F(\iota,t)^{-1}C_{ab}^{\theta}.
}
\tag{TL.445}
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
|C_{ab}^{\theta}|^2
=\dot\iota^{\mathsf T}\mathsf G(\iota,t)\dot\iota.
}
\tag{TL.446}
\]

This is the exact physical content of the incidence row: it is the squared
speed of the changing incidence label measured in the current material metric.
The metric is not an external ruler.  It is generated by the same deformation
history and has determinant one by incompressibility.

The delta trace in (TL.374) now has an exact one-label evaluation.  Since
\(\det D_cX(c,t)=1\),

\[
\int_{\mathbb R^3}
\delta^{(3)}\!\left(X(c,t)-Z_{ab}^{\theta}(t)\right)\psi(c)\,dc
=\psi(\iota_{ab}^{\theta}(t)).
\]

Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathfrak M(\rho,t)
=\int_0^1\!\iint
&\frac{
\dot\iota_{ab}^{\theta\,\mathsf T}
\mathsf G(\iota_{ab}^{\theta},t)
\dot\iota_{ab}^{\theta}}
{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)\rho^4}\\
&\times\delta\!\left(|R_{ab}(t)|-\rho\right)
\,da\,db\,d\theta.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.447}
\]

Integrating in \(\rho\) and using (TL.170) yields

\[
\boxed{
4H(t)
=\int_0^1\!\iint
\frac{
\dot\iota_{ab}^{\theta\,\mathsf T}
\mathsf G(\iota_{ab}^{\theta},t)
\dot\iota_{ab}^{\theta}}
{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)|R_{ab}(t)|^4}
\,da\,db\,d\theta.
}
\tag{TL.448}
\]

Thus an \(H\)-record is literally an all-field incidence-speed record of the
one material map.  A change of the label occupying an affine point is not
reselection of a new fluid object; it is motion of \(\iota\), and its complete
physical speed is already registered by (TL.448).

The initial datum supplies one useful time-global bound on this trace.  With
the spectral moments of (TL.312), Cauchy--Schwarz and the kinetic-energy
identity give

\[
H^2=\mu_1^2\le\mu_0\mu_2=\mathcal E\mu_2,
\qquad
\mathcal E'=-2\nu\mu_2.
\]

Hence, for every smooth \(T<T_*\),

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T H(t)^2\,dt
\le
\frac{\mathcal E_0[\mathcal E_0-\mathcal E(T)]}{2\nu}
\le\frac{\mathcal E_0^2}{2\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.449}
\]

Equivalently, the square of the total instantaneous incidence speed in
(TL.448) has a datum-finite time integral:

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T
\left[
\int_0^1\!\iint
\frac{
\dot\iota^{\mathsf T}\mathsf G\dot\iota}
{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)|R|^4}
\,da\,db\,d\theta
\right]^2dt
\le\frac{8\mathcal E_0^2}{\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.450}
\]

This does not by itself bound the supremum of that speed, but it determines
exactly what a finite-time Zeno record history would have to do.  Put
\(h_j=2^jH(0)\), let \(t_{j+1}\) be the first time
\(H(t_{j+1})=2h_j\), and let \(s_j\) be the last preceding time at which
\(H(s_j)=h_j\).  Then the intervals
\(J_j=[s_j,t_{j+1}]\) are disjoint and
\(h_j\le H<2h_j\) on their interiors.  Consequently

\[
\boxed{
\sum_j h_j^2|J_j|
\le\int_0^{T_*}H(t)^2dt
\le\frac{\mathcal E_0^2}{2\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.451}
\]

If infinitely many records accumulated before \(T_*\), then
\(h_j^2|J_j|\to0\).  The exact critical balance in (TL.315) simultaneously
gives

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{|J_j|}\int_{J_j}P_H(t)\,dt
=\frac{h_j}{|J_j|}
+\frac{2\nu}{|J_j|}\int_{J_j}\mu_3(t)\,dt,
\qquad
\frac{\displaystyle |J_j|^{-1}\int_{J_j}P_Hdt}{h_j^3}
\longrightarrow\infty.
}
\tag{TL.452}
\]

The second conclusion follows from the first term and (TL.451).  It is a
strict necessary signature, not an abstract schedule promoted to a fluid
scenario: a Zeno history would require the one incidence map in (TL.444) to
raise its aggregate metric speed through successive records on intervals so
short that the average complete critical throughput grows supercubically in
the record height.  The remaining causal contraction can therefore be aimed
at this precise trace: the joined VPI current must preclude supercubic
record-interval throughput generated by the same datum while (TL.443) counts
its stock and loss only once.

## 27. Every nested receiver belongs to one conjugated-dilation cocycle

The incidence maps in (TL.444) do more than identify the current intermediate
label.  For each fixed base label \(b\), time \(t\), and \(0<\theta<\infty\),
put

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}(a)
:=A_t\!\left(
X_b(t)+\theta[X_a(t)-X_b(t)]
\right).
}
\tag{TL.453}
\]

For \(0<\theta<1\), this is exactly
\(\iota_{ab}^{\theta}\).  The extension to \(\theta>1\) is useful only for
the group identity below.  It introduces no second flow: \(\mathcal T\) is
the physical dilation about the current point \(X_b(t)\), conjugated back to
the one initial-label space by the same material map.

Differentiation in \(a\) gives

\[
\boxed{
D_a\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}(a)
=\theta
\mathsf F(\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}(a),t)^{-1}
\mathsf F(a,t),
\qquad
\det D_a\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}=\theta^3.
}
\tag{TL.454}
\]

Thus \(\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}\) is a global diffeomorphism and

\[
(\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t})_\#da=\theta^{-3}dc.
\]

In particular, the incidence labels cannot be created or compressed into a
new base population.  If

\[
v_{b,\theta,t}(c)
:=\partial_t\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}
\bigl(\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}^{-1}(c)\bigr),
\]

then differentiating the time-independent pushed-forward density gives

\[
\boxed{
\nabla_c\cdot v_{b,\theta,t}=0.
}
\tag{TL.455}
\]

This is the label-space form of the all-field tether: changing which point
occupies an intermediate position is an incompressible rearrangement of the
same complete label field.

The maps also continue exactly through every intermediate point:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal T_{b,\theta\eta,t}
=\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}\circ\mathcal T_{b,\eta,t},
\qquad
\mathcal T_{b,1,t}=\operatorname{Id}.
}
\tag{TL.456}
\]

Taking the time derivative gives the corresponding tangent cocycle

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\mathcal T_{b,\theta\eta,t}(a)
=\partial_t\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}
\bigl(\mathcal T_{b,\eta,t}(a)\bigr)
+D\mathcal T_{b,\theta,t}
\bigl(\mathcal T_{b,\eta,t}(a)\bigr)
\partial_t\mathcal T_{b,\eta,t}(a).
}
\tag{TL.457}
\]

Define the physical affine residual at fraction \(\theta\) by

\[
C_\theta(a,b,t)
:=u\!\left(
X_b+\theta R_{ab},t
\right)
-\bigl[(1-\theta)U_b+\theta U_a\bigr].
\]

Using (TL.445) in (TL.457), or expanding the three velocities directly,
gives the exact nested receiver law

\[
\boxed{
C_{\theta\eta}(a,b,t)
=C_\theta\!\left(
\mathcal T_{b,\eta,t}(a),b,t
\right)
+\theta C_\eta(a,b,t).
}
\tag{TL.458}
\]

Equation (TL.458) is the all-field-point continuation that a pair-only
description lacks.  A smaller residual is the joined continuation of the
outer residual through the actual intermediate label plus the scaled residual
already present on the parent segment.  There is no third term that can act as
a new source.

At each fixed time, place these residual fields in the complete pair Hilbert
space

\[
\|f\|_{\mathscr H_t}^2
:=\frac1{\pi^2}\iint
\frac{|f(a,b)|^2}{|R_{ab}(t)|^4}\,da\,db.
\tag{TL.459}
\]

The pullback

\[
(\mathcal U_{\eta,t}f)(a,b)
:=\eta^{-1/2}
f\!\left(\mathcal T_{b,\eta,t}(a),b\right)
\]

is an isometry of \(\mathscr H_t\).  Indeed, (TL.454) gives
\(da=\eta^{-3}dc\), while
\(|R_{cb}|=\eta|R_{ab}|\).  Moreover
\(\mathcal U_{\theta,t}\mathcal U_{\eta,t}
=\mathcal U_{\theta\eta,t}\).  Thus (TL.458) becomes one Hilbert-space
relay identity,

\[
\boxed{
C_{\theta\eta}
=\sqrt\eta\,\mathcal U_{\eta,t}C_\theta
+\theta C_\eta.
}
\tag{TL.460}
\]

Writing \(N_\theta:=\|C_\theta\|_{\mathscr H_t}^2\), its exact squared form is

\[
\boxed{
N_{\theta\eta}
=\eta N_\theta+\theta^2N_\eta
+2\theta\sqrt\eta\,
\langle\mathcal U_{\eta,t}C_\theta,C_\eta\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}.
}
\tag{TL.461}
\]

The last pairing is not an independent alignment hypothesis.  It is the only
coherent cross-term permitted by the exact complete-carrier cocycle.  Its
distance from maximal relay is the nonnegative identity

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathfrak d_{\theta,\eta}(t)
&:=
\bigl(\sqrt{\eta N_\theta}+\theta\sqrt{N_\eta}\bigr)^2
-N_{\theta\eta}\\
&=\theta\sqrt\eta\sqrt{N_\theta N_\eta}
\left\|
\frac{\mathcal U_{\eta,t}C_\theta}{\sqrt{N_\theta}}
-\frac{C_\eta}{\sqrt{N_\eta}}
\right\|_{\mathscr H_t}^2
\ge0,
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.462}
\]

with the evident zero convention.  Hence a nested relay can approach its
maximal critical gain only by making the two actual continuing residuals
approach the same ray after the lawful all-field dilation pullback.

There is no nonzero finite-energy exact dilation ray.  For fixed
\(0<\eta<1\), if
\(\mathcal U_{\eta,t}f=\zeta f\) with \(|\zeta|=1\), then the
\(\mathscr H_t\)-mass of \(f\) in one fundamental separation annulus is
repeated with the same value in every disjoint annulus obtained by
\(\eta^j\).  Finite total \(\mathscr H_t\)-mass forces every such annular
mass, and therefore \(f\), to vanish.  Thus

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal U_{\eta,t}f=\zeta f,\qquad
f\in\mathscr H_t,\qquad |\zeta|=1
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad f=0.
}
\tag{TL.463}
\]

Equations (TL.462)--(TL.463) are exact fixed-time scale geometry, but they are
not yet a temporal upper--lower split.  The Fourier audit in Section 29 shows
that the whole fixed-time Gram matrix of these affine residuals is a universal
constant matrix multiplied by \(H(t)\).  Its defect is therefore a
reconstructive fractional-norm identity, not flow-specific coherence loss.
It cannot distinguish diffuse occupation from a concentrated migrating
profile and cannot be charged anew at each successor.  The live comparison
must instead place residuals at different times in the one metric-compatible
VPI tangent derived next.

## 28. The temporal dilation connection keeps migration inside one VPI tangent

The Hilbert space in (TL.459) changes with the same material pairs.  Put

\[
\sigma_{ab}(t):=\frac{R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab}}{|R_{ab}|^2},
\qquad
\mathscr D_t f:=\partial_tf-2\sigma_{ab}f.
\tag{TL.464}
\]

Since
\(\partial_t|R_{ab}|^{-4}=-4\sigma_{ab}|R_{ab}|^{-4}\), this derivative is
metric compatible:

\[
\boxed{
\frac d{dt}\langle f,g\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}
=\langle\mathscr D_tf,g\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}
+\langle f,\mathscr D_tg\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}.
}
\tag{TL.465}
\]

The time dependence of the all-field dilation pullback is therefore measured
by the exact connection

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr C_{\eta,t}f
:=\mathscr D_t(\mathcal U_{\eta,t}f)
-\mathcal U_{\eta,t}(\mathscr D_tf).
}
\tag{TL.466}
\]

Writing \(c=\mathcal T_{b,\eta,t}(a)\), direct differentiation of
(TL.459) gives

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
(\mathscr C_{\eta,t}f)(a,b)
=\eta^{-1/2}\bigl[&
D_af(c,b)\,\partial_t\mathcal T_{b,\eta,t}(a)\\
&+2(\sigma_{cb}-\sigma_{ab})f(c,b)
\bigr],
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.467}
\]

where
\(\partial_t\mathcal T_{b,\eta,t}
=-\mathsf F(c,t)^{-1}C_\eta(a,b,t)\) by (TL.445).  Hence the connection is
generated by the same incidence residual whose critical strength it
transports.  It is not a freely selected scale velocity.

Differentiating the isometry
\(\langle\mathcal U_\eta f,\mathcal U_\eta g\rangle
=\langle f,g\rangle\) with (TL.465) gives

\[
\boxed{
\langle\mathscr C_{\eta,t}f,\mathcal U_{\eta,t}g\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}
+\langle\mathcal U_{\eta,t}f,\mathscr C_{\eta,t}g\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}
=0.
}
\tag{TL.468}
\]

Thus \(\mathcal U_{\eta,t}^{-1}\mathscr C_{\eta,t}\) is skew-adjoint.  The
motion of the scale frame can turn a residual but cannot supply its norm.
Any increase of residual strength must remain in the complete VPI tangent of
the residual itself.

That tangent is also explicit without freezing the intermediate point.  Put

\[
\mathcal A(x,t):=D_tu(x,t)=-\nabla p(x,t)+\nu\Delta u(x,t).
\]

For fixed endpoint labels \((a,b)\), the affine point moves with
\(W_\theta=(1-\theta)U_b+\theta U_a\), while
\(W_\theta-u(Z_\theta)=-C_\theta\).  Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\partial_tC_\theta
=\;&\mathcal A(Z_\theta,t)
-(1-\theta)\mathcal A(X_b,t)
-\theta\mathcal A(X_a,t)\\
&-(C_\theta\cdot\nabla)u(Z_\theta,t).
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.469}
\]

Pressure and viscosity are inside the one material acceleration
\(\mathcal A\); endpoint transport, incidence motion and deformation occur
simultaneously in the last row.  Equation (TL.469), not an independently
prescribed alignment rate, is the tangent that controls future relay.

Applying \(\mathscr D_t\) to the exact scale cocycle (TL.460) now gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr D_tC_{\theta\eta}
=\sqrt\eta\,
\mathcal U_{\eta,t}(\mathscr D_tC_\theta)
+\sqrt\eta\,\mathscr C_{\eta,t}C_\theta
+\theta\mathscr D_tC_\eta.
}
\tag{TL.470}
\]

This is the complete temporal relay law.  The first and third terms are the
same residual tangent continued through the outer and parent incidences.  The
middle term is the compulsory turning caused by motion of their common scale
frame.  A concentrated profile can migrate without a fixed-time defect in
(TL.462), but it cannot do so without satisfying (TL.470) at every material
instant.

For the actual coherence pairing in (TL.461), (TL.465)--(TL.468) give

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\frac d{dt}
\langle\mathcal U_{\eta,t}C_\theta,C_\eta\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}
=\;&
\langle\mathcal U_{\eta,t}\mathscr D_tC_\theta,C_\eta\rangle
+\langle\mathscr C_{\eta,t}C_\theta,C_\eta\rangle\\
&+\langle\mathcal U_{\eta,t}C_\theta,
\mathscr D_tC_\eta\rangle.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.471}
\]

Equation (TL.471) is the exact joined tangent of the fixed-time coherence
pairing.  Its value as a no-return quantity still depends on whether that
pairing contains history beyond the instantaneous critical norm.  The audit
in Section 29 shows that it does not: its three signed rows recombine to a
constant multiple of \(H'\).  Estimating them separately would both discard
their cancellation and falsely promote a reconstructive identity into new
entropy production.

## 29. Fixed-time dilation coherence is a universal fractional-norm frame

The coherence pairing in (TL.471) must be audited before it is used as a
history variable.  Volume preservation permits the change from labels
\((a,b)\) to current endpoints \((x,y)\).  With \(R=x-y\),

\[
C_\alpha(x,y)
=u(y+\alpha R)-(1-\alpha)u(y)-\alpha u(x).
\]

For

\[
m_\alpha(s):=e^{i\alpha s}-(1-\alpha)-\alpha e^{is},
\]

Plancherel in \(y\), followed by rotation and the change
\(z=|\xi|R\), shows that there is a universal positive number

\[
b_\alpha
:=\frac2{\pi^2}
\int_{\mathbb R^3}
\frac{|m_\alpha(e_1\cdot z)|^2}{|z|^4}\,dz
\tag{TL.472}
\]

such that

\[
\boxed{
N_\alpha(t)=\|C_\alpha(t)\|_{\mathscr H_t}^2
=b_\alpha H(t).
}
\tag{TL.473}
\]

The integral in (TL.472) is finite because
\(m_\alpha(s)=O(s^2)\) at zero and is bounded at infinity.  Its positivity
follows because \(m_\alpha\) is not identically zero for
\(0<\alpha<1\).

The cross pairing is equally rigid.  Define

\[
g_{\theta,\eta}
:=\frac2{\pi^2}
\int_{\mathbb R^3}
\frac{
\operatorname{Re}\!\left[
\eta^{-1/2}m_\theta(\eta e_1\cdot z)
\overline{m_\eta(e_1\cdot z)}
\right]}
{|z|^4}\,dz.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\left\langle
\mathcal U_{\eta,t}C_\theta(t),C_\eta(t)
\right\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}
=g_{\theta,\eta}H(t).
}
\tag{TL.474}
\]

Consequently the cocycle itself forces

\[
b_{\theta\eta}
=\eta b_\theta+\theta^2b_\eta
+2\theta\sqrt\eta\,g_{\theta,\eta},
\]

and the defect in (TL.462) is simply

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak d_{\theta,\eta}(t)
=d_{\theta,\eta}H(t),
\qquad
d_{\theta,\eta}
:=\bigl(\sqrt{\eta b_\theta}
+\theta\sqrt{b_\eta}\bigr)^2-b_{\theta\eta}\ge0.
}
\tag{TL.475}
\]

Thus the fixed-time angle is independent of the spectrum, phases,
localization and material history of \(u\).  It is a universal Gram angle
between two reconstructive embeddings of the same \(\dot H^{1/2}\) field.
It cannot measure whether productive coupling has weakened, and counting
\(\mathfrak d_{\theta,\eta}\) at successive records would count the same
instantaneous critical norm again.

Differentiating (TL.474) also audits (TL.471):

\[
\boxed{
\frac d{dt}
\left\langle
\mathcal U_{\eta,t}C_\theta,C_\eta
\right\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}
=g_{\theta,\eta}H'(t).
}
\tag{TL.476}
\]

Therefore the three right-hand pairings in (TL.471), kept with their signs,
recombine to a constant multiple of the critical-height balance.  They do
not furnish an additional entropy production.  The genuine no-return object
must be cross-*time*: it must compare a later residual with the image of the
earlier residual under a complete history propagator before the fixed-time
Gram contraction is taken.  This audit prevents the instantaneous dilation
frame from being mistaken for the missing temporal VPI tether.

## 30. Cross-time material transport isolates genuine coherence change

The metric-compatible derivative (TL.464) has an explicit parallel
transport.  For \(0\le s<t<T_*\), define

\[
\boxed{
(\mathscr P_{s,t}f)(a,b)
:=\frac{|R_{ab}(t)|^2}{|R_{ab}(s)|^2}f(a,b).
}
\tag{TL.477}
\]

Then \(\mathscr D_t(\mathscr P_{s,t}f)=0\),
\(\mathscr P_{r,t}\mathscr P_{s,r}=\mathscr P_{s,t}\), and

\[
\|\mathscr P_{s,t}f\|_{\mathscr H_t}
=\|f\|_{\mathscr H_s}.
\]

This is not a second fluid continuation.  It is the parallel transport
generated by the declared scalar connection \(\mathscr D_t\) on the pair
metric carried by the same material separations; metric compatibility by
itself would still permit skew-adjoint additions.  All physical change
relative to this declared transport remains in
\(\mathscr D_tC_\alpha\), whose exact VPI formula is (TL.469).

Normalize one nontrivial affine residual by

\[
\boxed{
\Psi_\alpha(t)
:=\frac{C_\alpha(t)}{\sqrt{b_\alpha H(t)}},
\qquad
\|\Psi_\alpha(t)\|_{\mathscr H_t}=1,
}
\tag{TL.478}
\]

where (TL.473) was used.  Its same-history cross-time coherence is

\[
\mathscr K_\alpha(s,t)
:=\left\langle
\mathscr P_{s,t}\Psi_\alpha(s),\Psi_\alpha(t)
\right\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}.
\]

The exact distance identity is

\[
\boxed{
1-\mathscr K_\alpha(s,t)
=\frac12
\left\|
\Psi_\alpha(t)-\mathscr P_{s,t}\Psi_\alpha(s)
\right\|_{\mathscr H_t}^2.
}
\tag{TL.479}
\]

Unlike the fixed-time Gram angle, \(\mathscr K_\alpha(s,t)\) depends on the
actual intervening history.  Metric compatibility gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha
=\frac{\mathscr D_tC_\alpha}{\sqrt{b_\alpha H}}
-\frac{H'}{2H}\Psi_\alpha,
\qquad
\langle\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha,\Psi_\alpha\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}=0.
}
\tag{TL.480}
\]

Consequently the amplitude and shape rows are exactly orthogonal:

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\|\mathscr D_tC_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_t}^2}{b_\alpha H}
=\frac14\left(\frac{H'}H\right)^2
+\|\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_t}^2.
}
\tag{TL.481}
\]

Path length also gives the cross-time bound

\[
\boxed{
1-\mathscr K_\alpha(s,t)
\le\frac{t-s}{2}
\int_s^t
\|\mathscr D_q\Psi_\alpha(q)\|_{\mathscr H_q}^2\,dq.
}
\tag{TL.482}
\]

The shape row cannot vanish for a nonzero finite-energy decaying field.  If
\(\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha=0\) at one smooth time, (TL.480) gives

\[
\mathscr D_tC_\alpha=cC_\alpha,
\qquad c=\frac{H'}{2H}.
\]

Fix one current endpoint \(x\) and send the other endpoint \(y\) to infinity.
Then the affine point \((1-\alpha)y+\alpha x\) also tends to infinity,
\(C_\alpha\to-\alpha u(x)\), \(\sigma_{xy}\to0\), and (TL.469) gives
\(\partial_tC_\alpha\to-\alpha D_tu(x)\).  Hence
\(D_tu=cu\).  Taking curl yields

\[
\nu\Delta\omega=c\omega.
\]

As in (TL.423), an \(L^2\) vorticity satisfying this equation has Fourier
support on a zero-measure sphere and must vanish; finite energy then gives
\(u=0\).  Therefore

\[
\boxed{
H(t)>0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\|\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha(t)\|_{\mathscr H_t}>0,
\qquad 0<\alpha<1.
}
\tag{TL.483}
\]

The pointwise far-endpoint passage uses the stated decay of both \(u\) and
\(D_tu\).  At finite-energy regularity it must instead be read through an
\(L^2\) far-collar limit, and that collar has only \(O(L^{-1})\) mass.
Consequently (TL.483) has no record-uniform coercive modulus.

Every strengthening and every concentrated scale migration therefore carries
real cross-time deformation of the same all-field incidence shape.  The
statement is qualitative: remote pairs again enforce rigidity without yet
providing a record-uniform modulus.  The live quantitative step is now exact:
place the shape action in (TL.482), with its signed VPI tangent (TL.469),
inside the stopped completed-current law (TL.443) and prove that the
supercubic record concentration in (TL.452) would require datum-infinite
cross-time deformation.  The squared shape speed is reversible path action:
without a signed stopping law it can recount loops and is not itself
irreversible degradation.

## 31. Incidence stock is datum-paid; the completed cone-wall current is not

The incidence trace itself is not the missing capacity.  Its affine
cancellation gives a datum estimate that a generic codimension-three trace
would not have.  Put \(a_\theta:=\theta(1-\theta)\) and use the multiplier
\(m_\theta\) from Section 29.  The two global interpolation-error bounds are

\[
\boxed{
|m_\theta(s)|
\le 2a_\theta|s|,
\qquad
|m_\theta(s)|
\le \frac{a_\theta}{2}|s|^2.
}
\tag{TL.484}
\]

The first follows by splitting the residual into the two endpoint
increments; the linear terms cancel.  The second is the integral remainder
for linear interpolation of \(e^{is}\).  Plancherel in the center, followed
by
\[
\int_{\mathbb S^2}|n\cdot\xi|^2\,dn=\frac{4\pi}{3}|\xi|^2,
\qquad
\int_{\mathbb S^2}|n\cdot\xi|^4\,dn=\frac{4\pi}{5}|\xi|^4,
\]
therefore gives, with the normalization of (TL.165),

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak M(\rho,t)
\le
\min\left\{
\frac{8}{9\pi}\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2,\,
\frac{\rho^2}{30\pi}\|\Delta u(t)\|_2^2
\right\}.
}
\tag{TL.485}
\]

The first bound is uniform in scale and is paid by the kinetic-energy
identity.  If
\(\mathcal E_*:=\lim_{t\uparrow T_*}\mathcal E(t)\), then for every measurable
scale path \(\varrho(t)>0\),

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\sup_{\varrho}
\int_s^T\mathfrak M(\varrho(t),t)\,dt
&\le
\frac{8}{9\pi\nu}\,[\mathcal E(s)-\mathcal E(T)],\\
\lim_{s\uparrow T_*}\;
\sup_{\varrho}
\int_s^{T_*}\mathfrak M(\varrho(t),t)\,dt
&=0,\\
\lim_{\rho\downarrow0}
\int_0^T\mathfrak M(\rho,t)\,dt
&=0
\qquad (T<T_*).
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.486}
\]

The last line uses dominated convergence and the second-order cancellation
in (TL.484).  Thus no fixed or adaptively selected terminal scale can retain
nonzero time-integrated *stock* on a shrinking terminal time tail.

This does not make the completed endpoint current vanish.  In fact
(TL.169) and the record intervals of Section 26 give the opposite necessary
signature:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(0,t)=4P_H(t),
\qquad
\frac{1}{h_j^3|J_j|}
\int_{J_j}\mathcal Q_{\rm ch}(0,t)\,dt
\longrightarrow\infty
}
\tag{TL.487}
\]

under a hypothetical infinite first-record history.  Ordinary smooth
solutions may have nonzero \(P_H\).  The terminal theorem must therefore
exclude unbounded record-normalized apex concentration, not all nonzero
zero-scale current.

The exact scale-adapted trace balance shows where that distinction enters.
On a compact subinterval of \(0<\theta<1\), set

\[
\psi_{\theta,\rho}(\Xi)
:=(2\pi a_\theta\rho^2)^{-3/2}
\exp\!\left(-\frac{|\Xi|^2}{2a_\theta\rho^2}\right).
\]

Contract (TL.442) in \(Z,n,\Xi,\theta\), retaining \(\rho\), and denote the
\(\psi\)-weighted density, completed radial current and loss by
\(\mathcal M_\psi,\mathcal Q_\psi,\mathcal L_\psi\).  Direct integration by
parts, with no estimate and no separation of physical mechanisms, gives

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\partial_t\mathcal M_\psi+\partial_\rho\mathcal Q_\psi
&=-\mathcal L_\psi+\mathcal R_{\rm cone},\\
\mathcal R_{\rm cone}
&=\int\psi_{\theta,\rho}
\left[
\frac{|\Xi|^2/(a_\theta\rho^2)-3}{\rho}\,\mathbf K_\rho
-\frac{\Xi\cdot\mathbf K_\Xi}{a_\theta\rho^2}
\right].
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.488}
\]

All suppressed carrier variables are integrated in the last row.
\(\mathbf K_\rho=\rho^2\mathcal Q_\rho\) is the completed radial current and
\(\mathbf K_\Xi=\rho^2w_\theta F_\Xi\) is the complete incidence current.
Consequently \(\mathcal R_{\rm cone}\) contains transport, pressure response,
deformation and viscosity at the same time.  It is the signed current through
the wall of the \(|\Xi|\simeq\rho\sqrt{a_\theta}\) incidence cone; it has no
sign supplied by (TL.442).

The positive independent-point loss also cannot be carried through the trace
as a separate degradation payment.  At \(\Xi=0\), write

\[
A_c:=\nabla u(Z),\qquad
A_a:=\nabla u(Z+(1-\theta)R),\qquad
A_b:=\nabla u(Z-\theta R),
\qquad
G:=A_c-\theta A_a-(1-\theta)A_b.
\]

If \(F_R^\nu,F_\Xi^\nu\) are the viscous rows of the unweighted currents in
(TL.433), comparison with the direct traced diffusion identity gives

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\nu\int C(\cdot,0)\cdot\Delta_ZC(\cdot,0)\,dZ
&=-\nu\int|G|^2\,dZ,\\
\int
\left[
\mathcal L_C+\nabla_R\cdot F_R^\nu
+\nabla_\Xi\cdot F_\Xi^\nu
\right]_{\Xi=0}dZ
&=\nu\int|G|^2\,dZ.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.489}
\]

This is the compulsory viscous cross-term cancellation.  For example, as
\(\theta\downarrow0\) at fixed smooth \((Z,R,t)\),

\[
\boxed{
\frac{\mathcal L_C|_{\Xi=0}}{a_\theta}
=\frac{2\nu|\nabla u(Z)|^2}{\theta}+O(1),
\qquad
\frac{\nu|G|^2}{a_\theta}=O(\theta).
}
\tag{TL.490}
\]

The first expression is not trace-integrable; the second is.  The signed
radial and incidence currents in (TL.489) supply exactly the cross terms
that remove the artificial divergence.  Retaining
\(\mathcal L_C\) as an independent irreversible payer while discarding those
currents would therefore overcharge the same fluid.

Finally, the stock estimate has the wrong scale size for a no-Zeno payment.
For
\(\mathsf S_r(t):=\int_r^{2r}\mathfrak M(\rho,t)\,d\rho\), (TL.485) gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf S_r(t)\ge A
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2\ge\frac{9\pi A}{8r}.
}
\tag{TL.491}
\]

If such stock persists for its natural \(r^2/\nu\) service time, the forced
kinetic-energy expenditure has scale only \(A r\), which is summable along a
geometric descent.  Equivalently, an abstract conservation-law packet with
band stock \(A\), density \(A/r\), radial speed \(1/r\), and residence time
\(r^2\) has vanishing occupancy \(Ar\) while transporting order-\(A\)
current across every scale.  This is an estimate countertest, not a
Navier--Stokes history.

The trace calculation therefore closes the stock branch and leaves one exact
burden.  A proof must bound the positive record-selected contribution of
\(\mathcal R_{\rm cone}\), equivalently the joined completed apex current in
(TL.487), by a datum-finite cross-time deformation payment derived from
(TL.469)--(TL.483).  Neither (TL.443), (TL.486), nor qualitative
shape change supplies that bound.  No minimum-scale, no-Zeno, \(H\)-bound or
continuation conclusion is promoted from this section.

## 32. A material incidence tube removes apparent transport rebuilding

The fixed physical cone in (TL.488) does not follow the label that actually
occupies the affine incidence point.  Its transport contribution can be
removed exactly by recentering on that persistent label, without changing the
fluid history.

For fixed endpoint labels \((a,b)\) and fraction \(\theta\), retain the
incidence label \(\iota=\iota_{ab}^{\theta}(t)\) from (TL.444).  For an
independent material label \(c\), put

\[
\widetilde C_{abc}^{\theta}
:=U_c-[(1-\theta)U_b+\theta U_a],
\qquad
\widetilde D_{abc}^{\theta}
:=\mathcal A(X_c)-[(1-\theta)\mathcal A(X_b)
+\theta\mathcal A(X_a)].
\tag{TL.492}
\]

On fixed labels,
\(\partial_t\widetilde C=\widetilde D\).  Let
\(\chi\ge0\) be a smooth compactly supported unit-mass mollifier and
\(\chi_\varepsilon(\zeta)=\varepsilon^{-3}\chi(\zeta/\varepsilon)\).
A fixed off-incidence tube is not integrable against the critical
\([\theta(1-\theta)]^{-1}\) weight at the two fraction endpoints.  Therefore
fix \(0<\delta<1/2\) first and define the truncated material-tube stock

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal I_{\varepsilon,\delta}(t)
:=\int_\delta^{1-\delta}\!\iiint
\frac{|\widetilde C_{abc}^{\theta}(t)|^2}
{\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)|R_{ab}(t)|^4}
\chi_\varepsilon(c-\iota_{ab}^{\theta}(t))
\,da\,db\,dc\,d\theta.
}
\tag{TL.493}
\]

Every coordinate in (TL.493) is an original material label.  For fixed
\(\delta\), differentiating it and using
\(\dot w_\theta=-4\sigma_{ab}w_\theta\) gives, exactly,

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal I_{\varepsilon,\delta}'
=\int_\delta^{1-\delta}\!\iiint w_\theta
\Bigl[&
2\widetilde C\cdot\widetilde D
-4\sigma_{ab}|\widetilde C|^2
\Bigr]\chi_\varepsilon(c-\iota)\\
&-w_\theta|\widetilde C|^2
\dot\iota\cdot\nabla_c\chi_\varepsilon(c-\iota)
\,da\,db\,dc\,d\theta.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.494}
\]

Here \(w_\theta=[\pi^2\theta(1-\theta)|R|^4]^{-1}\).
The last row is not an external influx.  Integration by parts in the one
material label \(c\) turns it into

\[
\int w_\theta\chi_\varepsilon(c-\iota)
\dot\iota\cdot\nabla_c|\widetilde C|^2,
\]

so it is precisely the sweep of the same incidence population.

Because \(X_t\) is a smooth volume-preserving diffeomorphism and the datum is
decaying, the tube first converges to the truncated exact trace.  Only after
that trace has restored the affine endpoint cancellation may
\(\delta\downarrow0\):

\[
\boxed{
\lim_{\delta\downarrow0}\;
\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow0}
\mathcal I_{\varepsilon,\delta}(t)=4H(t).
}
\tag{TL.495}
\]

At \(c=\iota\), (TL.445) gives
\(\mathsf F(\iota,t)\dot\iota=-C_\theta\), while

\[
\dot\iota\cdot\nabla_c|\widetilde C|^2\big|_{c=\iota}
=-2C_\theta\cdot(C_\theta\cdot\nabla)u(Z_\theta).
\]

Thus the sequential limit of (TL.494), first
\(\varepsilon\downarrow0\) and then \(\delta\downarrow0\), is the exact
scalar trace law

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
4H'(t)
=\int_0^1\!\iint w_\theta
\Bigl{&
2C_\theta\cdot
\bigl[
\mathcal A(Z_\theta)
-(1-\theta)\mathcal A(X_b)
-\theta\mathcal A(X_a)
-(C_\theta\cdot\nabla)u(Z_\theta)
\bigr]\\
&-4\sigma_{ab}|C_\theta|^2
\Bigr\}\,da\,db\,d\theta.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.496}
\]

The bracket is exactly the complete incidence tangent (TL.469).  Hence the
transport portion of \(\mathcal R_{\rm cone}\) was motion of a fixed physical
tube relative to the continuing incidence labels; it is not a separate
rebuilding mechanism.  After lawful recentering, the entire apex source is
the single signed pairing in (TL.496): material acceleration, affine-scale
deformation and incidence sweep occur together on the original labels.

Equation (TL.496) does not yet have a sign, but it removes one false degree of
freedom from the terminal problem.  It also proves why a scalar trace law
cannot finish the contraction.  For each fixed \(\theta\), (TL.480) gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr D_tC_\theta
=\frac{H'}{2H}C_\theta
+\sqrt{b_\theta H}\,\mathscr D_t\Psi_\theta,
\qquad
\langle C_\theta,\mathscr D_t\Psi_\theta\rangle_{\mathscr H_t}=0.
}
\tag{TL.497}
\]

The pairing in (TL.496) therefore removes the transverse row and recombines
to \(H'\), exactly as the fixed-time audit in Section 29 predicts.  Using its
positive part as a new payment would be circular.  The required contraction
must retain the *vector* tangent in (TL.497) before this radial projection and
derive a signed relation by which supercubic radial growth forces cumulative
cross-time deformation inside the same non-recounting material tube.

## 33. Full cone contraction returns to the ordinary critical current

The remaining cone algebra can be contracted exactly, and it supplies no
hidden nonnegative loss.  Fix a compact fraction interval
\(I\Subset(0,1)\) and perform the complete \(Z,n,\Xi,\theta\) contraction of
the Gaussian tube in (TL.488).  Plancherel gives a positive scalar radial
multiplier

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal M_I(\rho,t)
=\int_{\mathbb R^3}
B_I(\rho,|\xi|)|\widehat u(\xi,t)|^2\,d\xi,
\qquad
B_I(\rho,k)=\rho^{-2}\beta_I(\rho k),
}
\tag{TL.498}
\]

where
\(\beta_I\ge0\), \(\beta_I(s)=O(s^2)\) at zero and
\(\beta_I(s)=O(1)\) at infinity.  Hence there is a finite
\(c_I>0\) such that

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^\infty B_I(\rho,k)\,d\rho=c_Ik.
}
\tag{TL.499}
\]

Let \(B_{I,\rho}=B_I(\rho,\Lambda)\) and
\(N(u)=-\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)\).  Since \(B_{I,\rho}\) is scalar and
radial, it preserves divergence-free fields.  Therefore the complete
pressure contribution cancels only after the joined contraction:

\[
\langle B_{I,\rho}u,\nabla p\rangle=0.
\]

The exact tube evolution is consequently

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\mathcal M_I
=2\langle B_{I,\rho}u,N(u)\rangle
-2\nu\mathcal G_I,
\qquad
\mathcal G_I
:=\int |\xi|^2B_I(\rho,|\xi|)
|\widehat u(\xi)|^2\,d\xi.
}
\tag{TL.500}
\]

Completing the radial current gives

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\widehat{\mathcal Q}_I(\rho,t)
&:=2\int_\rho^\infty
\langle B_{I,s}u,N(u)\rangle\,ds,\\
\partial_t\mathcal M_I+\partial_\rho\widehat{\mathcal Q}_I
&=-2\nu\mathcal G_I,\\
\int_0^\infty\mathcal M_I\,d\rho&=2c_IH,
\qquad
\int_0^\infty\mathcal G_I\,d\rho=2c_I\mu_3,
\qquad
\widehat{\mathcal Q}_I(0)=2c_IP_H.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.501}
\]

Thus scale integration of the lawful incidence tube is exactly
\(H'=P_H-2\nu\mu_3\), up to the harmless positive factor \(2c_I\).
The incidence and radial pressure rows redistribute internally and cancel;
they leave no separate payer.  Pressure response has not been removed from
the fluid equation--its cancellation is licensed only after the complete
divergence-free contraction.

The apparent positive pretrace loss also cancels at the required order.  For
the affine incidence jet
\[
C(Z,\sqrt{a_\theta}\rho\,\zeta)
=\sqrt{a_\theta}\rho\,\nabla u(Z)\zeta+O(\rho^2),
\]
both \(\mathcal L_\psi\) and the incidence-viscous part of
\(\mathcal R_{\rm cone}\) have the same nonzero
\(\rho^{-2}\|\nabla u\|_2^2\) leading coefficient and opposite signs.  Their
finite joined remainder is precisely \(-2\nu\mathcal G_I\) in (TL.501).
Taking a positive part before that cancellation would diverge even for a
smooth nonzero field and would overcharge viscosity.

The temporal shape decomposition does not replace the lost payer.  On an
\(H\)-doubling interval \(J=[s,t]\), (TL.481) gives exactly

\[
\boxed{
\int_J
\frac{\|\mathscr D_qC_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_q}^2}
{b_\alpha H(q)}\,dq
=\frac14\int_J\left(\frac{H'}H\right)^2dq
+\int_J\|\mathscr D_q\Psi_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_q}^2dq
\ge
\frac{(\log2)^2}{4|J|}
+\int_J\|\mathscr D_q\Psi_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_q}^2dq.
}
\tag{TL.502}
\]

The universal reciprocal-time floor is wholly the radial amplitude row.
Doubling supplies no positive lower quantum for the transverse shape row.
The Stokes linearization makes this lack of a modulus explicit.  With

\[
dQ_t(k):=\frac{k\,d\eta_t(k)}{\mu_1(t)},
\]

the leading normalized shape speed is

\[
\boxed{
\|\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_t,\mathrm{lin}}^2
=\nu^2\operatorname{Var}_{Q_t}(k^2),
}
\tag{TL.503}
\]

independently of \(\alpha\).  Smooth narrow-annulus data make (TL.503)
arbitrarily small at fixed nonzero \(H\).  This is a small-amplitude
linearization countertest, not a rising Navier--Stokes record, but it proves
that the qualitative rigidity in (TL.483) has no state-independent modulus.

Nor does record normalization recover a fixed-viscosity compact family.  At
a record \(H=h\), put \(r=\mathcal E/H\) and first make the
Navier--Stokes rescaling

\[
v(s,y):=r\,u(t+r^2s,x+ry).
\]

Then
\(\mathcal E(v)=H(v)=h\) and the mean frequency of \(v\) is one.
Writing \(w=v/\sqrt h\) and \(\tau=\sqrt h\,s\) gives

\[
\boxed{
\partial_\tau w+\mathbb P(w\cdot\nabla w)
=\frac{\nu}{\sqrt h}\Delta w.
}
\tag{TL.504}
\]

Hence a normalization that keeps both energy and critical height bounded
sends the effective viscosity to zero as the records diverge.  The efficient
compactness branch is an Euler-limit problem, not a fixed-viscosity
coherence contraction.

Equations (TL.498)--(TL.504) close this attempted payer route.  The complete
cone, material tube, pressure response and viscous cross terms recombine to
the already known signed apex current \(P_H\); the cross-time shape action is
transverse, reversible, and neither lower-forced by a record nor upper-paid
by the datum.  A successful proof still requires a new fixed-viscosity
dynamical coercivity theorem coupling the radial apex row to transverse
shape change and an independent datum-finite payment for that change.  The
present identities do not prove it, so the minimum-scale, no-Zeno,
\(H\)-bound and continuation conclusions remain open.

## 34. Rising critical throughput forces an instantaneous dual-geometry alternative

The missing contraction is not a failure to prove any coupling at all.  A
derived whole-field Hilbert factorization of the Eulerian critical balance
gives an exact simultaneous production--source alternative.  It is recorded
only after the complete carrier laws above and is a subordinate readout of
them.  It is not the material tangent in (TL.469)--(TL.481).

Put

\[
\mathcal B(u):=\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)=-N(u),
\qquad
\mathfrak f:=\Lambda^{-1/2}\mathcal B(u),
\qquad
\mathfrak g:=\Lambda^{3/2}u,
\qquad
\mathfrak z:=\Lambda^{1/2}u
\]

and define

\[
\boxed{
R_H:=\|\mathfrak z\|_2^2=2H,
\qquad
K_H:=\langle\mathfrak g,\mathfrak z\rangle
=\|\Lambda u\|_2^2=2\mu_2,
\qquad
D_H:=\|\mathfrak g\|_2^2=2\mu_3.
}
\tag{TL.505}
\]

The complete pressure response is inside \(\mathcal B\).  Kinetic-energy
tangency and critical production read

\[
\boxed{
\langle\mathfrak f,\mathfrak z\rangle
=\langle\mathcal B(u),u\rangle=0,
\qquad
P_H=-\langle\mathfrak f,\mathfrak g\rangle.
}
\tag{TL.506}
\]

Remove from the critical viscous direction the component forbidden by
kinetic-energy tangency:

\[
\mathfrak g_{\rm sh}
:=\mathfrak g-\frac{K_H}{R_H}\mathfrak z,
\qquad
\mathcal S_H
:=\|\mathfrak g_{\rm sh}\|_2^2
=D_H-\frac{K_H^2}{R_H}.
\]

Also remove from the \(H^{-1/2}\) nonlinear source its component parallel to
the critical viscous test direction:

\[
\mathfrak f_\perp
:=\mathfrak f+\frac{P_H}{D_H}\mathfrak g,
\qquad
\mathcal T_H:=\|\mathfrak f_\perp\|_2^2
\]

when \(D_H>0\), with the zero convention otherwise.  Then

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
P_H^2
&\le \|\mathfrak f\|_2^2\,\mathcal S_H,\\
\mathcal T_H
&\ge
\frac{P_H^2K_H^2}
{D_H(R_HD_H-K_H^2)}
\qquad (R_HD_H>K_H^2).
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.507}
\]

The first row is Cauchy--Schwarz after (TL.506).  For the second,
\(\mathfrak f_\perp\perp\mathfrak g\) while energy tangency forces
\(\langle\mathfrak f_\perp,\mathfrak z\rangle=P_HK_H/D_H\).
Thus critical production cannot avoid a simultaneous pressure-completed
nonlinear-source component orthogonal to \(\mathfrak g\), unless the spectral
shape term supplies the projection.

At every rising time, (TL.315) gives \(P_H\ge\nu D_H\).  Substitution in
(TL.507) yields the exact coupled dichotomy

\[
\boxed{
H'(t)\ge0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\mathcal S_H(t)+\nu^{-2}\mathcal T_H(t)\ge D_H(t).
}
\tag{TL.508}
\]

If \(R_HD_H=K_H^2\), then \(\mathfrak g\) is collinear with
\(\mathfrak z\), and (TL.506) forces \(P_H=0\).  Thus no nonzero rising state
is lost at the degenerate endpoint of (TL.507).

This is an exact instantaneous Eulerian dual-geometry alternative: the same
state that supplies critical production must have either broad spectral
scale-shape, a large orthogonal component of its pressure-completed
\(H^{-1/2}\) nonlinear source, or both.  Neither term can be deleted from
this Hilbert factorization.

It is not yet the central same-material inference requested in the goal.
The actual nonlinear tangent of \(\Lambda^{1/2}u\) contains
\(\Lambda^{1/2}\mathcal B(u)\), whereas \(\mathfrak f\) is
\(\Lambda^{-1/2}\mathcal B(u)\), chosen because its pairing with
\(\mathfrak g\) is \(P_H\).  Therefore \(\mathcal T_H\) is not temporal
deformation of the material carrier and cannot be called degradation of
coherent delivery without a new bridge.

The standard critical bilinear estimate

\[
\|\mathfrak f\|_2
\le C_{\rm bil}\sqrt{R_HD_H}
\]

also gives

\[
\boxed{
H'
\le\frac{C_{\rm bil}^2}{2\nu}H\mathcal S_H,
\qquad
H(t)=2H(s)
\Longrightarrow
\int_s^t\mathcal S_H(q)\,dq
\ge\frac{2\nu\log2}{C_{\rm bil}^2}.
}
\tag{TL.509}
\]

Thus infinitely many first-record doublings would force infinite cumulative
critical scale-shape action on their disjoint last-passage intervals.

What (TL.508)--(TL.509) do **not** prove is the required contraction.
\(\mathcal S_H\) is spectral variance and \(\mathcal T_H\) is an
instantaneous dual-source norm; neither is signed no-return degradation.
Energy pays
\(\int K_H\,dt\), while \(\mathcal S_H\) lives at the one-derivative-higher
\(D_H\) level; fixed-viscosity Navier--Stokes scaling leaves
\(\int\mathcal S_Hdt\) invariant on a rescaled upcrossing.  A finite datum
bound for that critical action would already give the missing \(H\) bound
through (TL.509), and no such independent bound has been derived.
There is also no licensed norm bridge from the instantaneous Eulerian
\(\mathcal T_H\) source row to the material cross-time action in (TL.481):
\(\int\mathcal T_Hdt\) is invariant under Navier--Stokes scaling, whereas
\(\int\|\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_t}^2dt\) gains a factor
\(\lambda^2\).  Any comparison needs an inverse-frequency capacity that is
absent in the low-frequency and narrow-annulus sectors.  The exact dichotomy
therefore proves only simultaneous Eulerian source geometry, not deformation
or irreversible coherence contraction through an infinite descent.

## 35. The datum-paid rising occupancy is subcritical for record counting

There is one datum-finite consequence of the same Hilbert geometry.  For a
nonzero state put

\[
\mathcal A_H
:=\frac{P_H^2}{\|\mathfrak f\|_2^2},
\]

with the zero convention when \(\mathfrak f=0\).  This is the squared norm of
the projection of the viscous critical direction \(\mathfrak g\) onto the
active nonlinear row.  Cauchy--Schwarz and the bilinear bound in Section 34
give \(\mathcal A_H\le D_H\), while on the rising set
\(\mathcal R_T:=\{t\in[0,T]:H'(t)\ge0\}\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal A_H
\ge\frac{\nu^2D_H}{C_{\rm bil}^2R_H},
\qquad
\frac{K_H}{R_H}
\le\frac{C_{\rm bil}^2}{\nu^2}
\frac{K_H}{D_H}\mathcal A_H .
}
\tag{TL.510}
\]

The kinetic-energy identity pays the right side:

\[
\int_0^T K_H\,dt
=\frac{\mathcal E(0)-\mathcal E(T)}{\nu}
\le\frac{\mathcal E_0}{\nu}.
\]

The same active projection gives the sharper scale-critical doubling action.
Indeed

\[
\boxed{
H'
\le C_{\rm bil}\sqrt{\mathcal A_HR_HD_H}-\nu D_H
\le\frac{C_{\rm bil}^2}{2\nu}H\mathcal A_H,
\qquad
H(t)=2H(s)
\Longrightarrow
\int_s^t\mathcal A_H(q)\,dq
\ge\frac{2\nu\log2}{C_{\rm bil}^2}.
}
\tag{TL.511}
\]

This is stronger than the \(\mathcal S_H\) charge in (TL.509), because
\(\mathcal A_H\le\mathcal S_H\).  A datum-finite unweighted
\(\int\mathcal A_Hdt\) estimate would therefore give the Gold bound directly;
none is proved.

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
\int_{\mathcal R_T}\frac{K_H(t)}{R_H(t)}\,dt
\le
\frac{C_{\rm bil}^2\mathcal E_0}{\nu^3}.
}
\tag{TL.512}
\]

Thus the initial datum pays a causal time--frequency occupancy of every
rising episode.  It does not pay a record count.  The integrand can become
large on intervals shorter than its reciprocal, and neither (TL.510) nor the
complete-current law gives one positive occupancy quantum per H doubling.
Likewise, multiplying (TL.508) by \(K_H/D_H\) only moves the unpaid burden to
\((K_H/(\nu^2D_H))\mathcal T_H\); it does not produce an upper bound for that
row.  The paid occupancy is therefore a strict support statement, not the
minimum-scale or no-Zeno theorem.

## 36. Material coercivity reduces to an approximate positive-growth eigenray

The missing Eulerian-to-material bridge has an exact fixed-profile
countertest.  Fix a smooth divergence-free Schwartz profile \(\phi\), one
\(0<\alpha<1\), and the initial material metric.  Write

\[
c_\alpha:=C_\alpha[\phi],
\qquad
H_\phi:=H(\phi),
\qquad
P_\phi:=P_H(\phi),
\qquad
D_\phi:=D_H(\phi).
\]

The full tangent (TL.469) is polynomial in the amplitude.  Denote its joined
linear viscous row by \(L_\alpha[\phi]\) and its joined quadratic
transport--pressure--deformation--incidence row by \(Q_\alpha[\phi]\).  Then

\[
\boxed{
\left.\mathscr D_tC_\alpha[a\phi]\right|_{t=0}
=a\nu L_\alpha[\phi]+a^2Q_\alpha[\phi],
\qquad
\left.\frac{H'}H\right|_{a\phi}
=a\frac{P_\phi}{H_\phi}
-\nu\frac{D_\phi}{H_\phi}.
}
\tag{TL.513}
\]

Let \(\Pi_{c_\alpha}^{\perp}\) be orthogonal projection in
\(\mathscr H_0\) off the ray generated by \(c_\alpha\).  The orthogonal
amplitude--shape splitting (TL.481) gives the exact initial material shape
rate

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal A_\alpha(a\phi)
:=\left\|\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha[a\phi]\right\|_{\mathscr H_0}^2
=
\frac{\left\|
\nu\Pi_{c_\alpha}^{\perp}L_\alpha[\phi]
+a\Pi_{c_\alpha}^{\perp}Q_\alpha[\phi]
\right\|_{\mathscr H_0}^2}
{b_\alpha H_\phi}.
}
\tag{TL.514}
\]

If \(P_\phi>0\), large amplitude makes the datum initially rising and

\[
\boxed{
\lim_{a\to\infty}
\frac{\mathcal A_\alpha(a\phi)}
{\bigl((H'/H)|_{a\phi}\bigr)^2}
=
\frac{H_\phi
\left\|\Pi_{c_\alpha}^{\perp}Q_\alpha[\phi]\right\|_{\mathscr H_0}^2}
{b_\alpha P_\phi^2}.
}
\tag{TL.515}
\]

This amplitude family tests a putative universal instantaneous coercivity; it
is not being interpreted as successive events in one fluid history.
Therefore amplitude does not manufacture or destroy a material
shape-to-radial gap; the issue is entirely profile dependent.  The ordinary
fixed-viscosity Navier--Stokes scaling also multiplies both the numerator
\(\mathcal A_\alpha\) and \((H'/H)^2\) by \(\lambda^4\), so it leaves the
ratio in (TL.515) invariant.

Let \(\mathcal S_\sigma(\mathbb R^3)\) denote the divergence-free Schwartz
fields.  The exact unresolved instantaneous fork is now:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{gathered}
\text{either find }\phi_n\in\mathcal S_\sigma,\quad P_{\phi_n}>0,\quad
\frac{\sqrt{H_{\phi_n}}\,
\|\Pi_{c_{\alpha,n}}^\perp Q_\alpha[\phi_n]\|_{\mathscr H_0}}
{\sqrt{b_\alpha}\,P_{\phi_n}}\longrightarrow0,\\
\text{or prove that no such approximate positive-growth material eigenray
exists.}
\end{gathered}
}
\tag{TL.516}
\]

The tests by spatial rescaling, asymptotically disjoint copies,
near-equilateral rising triads, and localized affine strain do not settle
this fork.  Scaling preserves the ratio; disjoint unequal radial rates add
shape variance; the tested triads have small radial production but a large
complementary Fourier acceleration for which no coercive map to the exact
material-pair tangent is proved; and exact affine strain has
\(C_\alpha=0\) and infinite energy, while its natural finite-energy cutoffs
carry critical norm and cutoff tangent at the same order.

The qualitative rigidity (TL.483) excludes vanishing of the full
finite-amplitude material shape row datum by datum, but its remote-collar
proof supplies no uniform modulus and does not decide (TL.516).  Moreover,
even exclusion of (TL.516) would prove only an instantaneous unsigned
coercivity.  The original goal would still require a causal signed no-return
quantity and an independent datum-finite payment preventing the material
shape action from recounting loops.

## 37. The square-root receiver law pays the nonvanishing common-delivery branch

The finite nonlinear reach in Section 13.7 yields a stronger causal statement
than the unsigned source norm used there.  It directly controls positive
