motion of the participating-energy stock, with the loss of that same stock
retained in the identity.  This is the precise part of the leaky-receiver
picture that can be paid from the datum without a critical active weight.

Retain one fixed affine carrier band and the notation

\[
U_r=\|g_r\|_2^2=r\mathsf E_r,
\qquad
S_r=\langle g_r,L_zg_r\rangle=\mathsf S_r,
\qquad
\partial_tg_r=\mathcal N_r-\nu L_zg_r
\]

from (TL.215), (TL.224), and (TL.225).  Whenever \(U_r>0\), its exact
square-root balance is

\[
\boxed{
\frac d{dt}\sqrt{U_r}
+\nu\frac{S_r}{\sqrt{U_r}}
=\frac{\operatorname{Re}\langle g_r,\mathcal N_r\rangle}
{\sqrt{U_r}}
\le \|\mathcal N_r\|_2.
}
\tag{TL.517}
\]

The zero-stock case follows by replacing \(U_r\) with
\(U_r+\varepsilon\) and taking \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\); if \(U_r=0\),
then \(g_r=0\) and hence \(S_r=0\).  Thus every interval \([s,t]\) obeys

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\sqrt{U_r(t)}
+\nu\int_s^t\frac{S_r(q)}{\sqrt{U_r(q)}}\,dq
&\le
\sqrt{U_r(s)}+\int_s^t\|\mathcal N_r(q)\|_2\,dq,\\
\bigl[\sqrt{U_r(t)}-\sqrt{U_r(s)}\bigr]_+
&\le\int_s^t\|\mathcal N_r(q)\|_2\,dq.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.518}
\]

This is a signed causal receiver inequality.  The stock at the right endpoint
plus its actual intervening loss cannot exceed the one-time entering stock
plus the complete nonlinear VPI displacement delivered on that interval.
No positive shell flux has been summed, and no loss is counted twice.

The estimate remains non-recounting when the selected band changes between
pairwise disjoint time intervals.  Let \(J_m=[s_m,t_m]\) be disjoint and let
\(r_m>0\) be fixed on \(J_m\).  The full all-scale resolution (TL.234)--
(TL.238) gives pointwise

\[
\|\mathcal N_{r_m}(q)\|_2
\le \sqrt2\,
\|\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)(q)\|_{\dot H^{-1/2}}.
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
\sum_m
\bigl[\sqrt{U_{r_m}(t_m)}-
\sqrt{U_{r_m}(s_m)}\bigr]_+
\le \frac{C\mathcal E_0}{\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.519}
\]

The estimate uses the actual band chosen on each disjoint interval; it does
not require all choices to belong to one dyadic lattice.  Disjointness in
time and the full \(\rho\)-resolution count the same nonlinear displacement
only once.

The stock also has a scale-dependent datum ceiling.  By (TL.196)--(TL.197),

\[
\boxed{
0\le U_r(t)=r\mathsf E_r(t)
\le2r\mathbf E_r(t)
\le\frac{8}{3\pi}\,r\mathcal E_0.
}
\tag{TL.520}
\]

Thus the absolute participating-energy amplitude of every receiver tends to
zero with its physical chord scale, uniformly in time.  This does not make
its *relative* rate small; (TL.519) is deliberately an absolute, rather than
logarithmic, statement.

The exact consequence for a fixed-band critical-stock doubling is an
exhaustive two-row fork.  Put

\[
\lambda_r:=\frac{S_r}{U_r}=\frac{\Gamma_r}{r^2}.
\]

If \(S_r(t)=2S_r(s)>0\), then

\[
\frac{U_r(t)}{U_r(s)}
\frac{\lambda_r(t)}{\lambda_r(s)}=2,
\]

and hence

\[
\boxed{
\frac{U_r(t)}{U_r(s)}\ge\sqrt2
\quad\text{or}\quad
\frac{\lambda_r(t)}{\lambda_r(s)}\ge\sqrt2.
}
\tag{TL.521}
\]

On the first branch,

\[
\sqrt{U_r(t)}-\sqrt{U_r(s)}
\ge(2^{1/4}-1)\sqrt{U_r(s)}.
\]

Therefore (TL.519) proves, for all common-delivery branches in a disjoint
family,

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{m\in\mathcal C}\sqrt{U_{r_m}(s_m)}
\le
\frac{C\mathcal E_0}
{(2^{1/4}-1)\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.522}
\]

Infinitely many such branches are possible only if their entering
participating-energy amplitudes form a summable sequence and tend to zero.
They cannot repeatedly amplify a receiver carrying a fixed positive amount
of participating stock.

On the second branch the band is fixed, so
\(\lambda_r(t)/\lambda_r(s)=\Gamma_r(t)/\Gamma_r(s)\).  Applying the exact
productive quantum (TL.260) with
\(\delta=\tfrac12\log2\) gives

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t\mathfrak A_r^\uparrow(q)\,dq
\ge\nu\log2.
}
\tag{TL.523}
\]

Equations (TL.519)--(TL.523) remove a genuine ambiguity from the central
inference.  On disjoint fixed-band critical-stock doubling intervals, the
positive square-root increments of nonvanishing common stock have
datum-finite total path length.  Any infinite disjoint family of those
fixed-band doublings must therefore alternate between only two precise
behaviours:

1. common amplification of receivers whose participating stock tends to
   zero fast enough to satisfy (TL.522); or
2. infinitely much normalized uphill scale-shape action as in (TL.523).

These are not separate fluid mechanisms.  They are the radial and transverse
rows of the same tangent (TL.289)--(TL.291), now joined to the datum-finite
absolute receiver law.  The unresolved self-interruption statement is
therefore narrower than a generic throughput ceiling: it must show, on the
record-stopped complete material history, that vanishing-stock common
amplification necessarily enters the normalized shape row before it can be
repeated.  The present section proves the common-delivery half of that
statement; it does not yet supply the record extraction or the signed
cross-row implication needed for the minimum effective scale and continuation.

## 38. The datum pays material-metric action, but not critical velocity variation

The actual deformation of the one material carrier has datum-paid quadratic
action and, on every finite horizon, finite same-history length.  Let

\[
\mathsf F(a,t):=D_aX(a,t),
\qquad
\mathsf G(a,t):=\mathsf F(a,t)^T\mathsf F(a,t),
\qquad
S:=\frac12\bigl(\nabla u+\nabla u^T\bigr).
\]

Incompressibility gives \(\det\mathsf G=1\), and the same material evolution
gives

\[
\boxed{
\dot{\mathsf F}=(\nabla u)(X,t)\mathsf F,
\qquad
\dot{\mathsf G}=2\mathsf F^TS(X,t)\mathsf F,
\qquad
\operatorname{tr}
\bigl(
\mathsf G^{-1}\dot{\mathsf G}
\mathsf G^{-1}\dot{\mathsf G}
\bigr)
=4|S(X,t)|^2.
}
\tag{TL.524}
\]

The last row is the squared speed in the affine-invariant metric on the
determinant-one positive matrices.  Since
\(\int|S|^2dx=\frac12\|\nabla u\|_2^2\), its complete spacetime action is

\[
\int_0^T\!\int
\operatorname{tr}
\bigl(
\mathsf G^{-1}\dot{\mathsf G}
\mathsf G^{-1}\dot{\mathsf G}
\bigr)\,da\,dt
=\frac2\nu\bigl[\mathcal E(0)-\mathcal E(T)\bigr]
\le\frac{2\mathcal E_0}{\nu}.
\]

For positive determinant-one matrices put

\[
d_{\rm aff}(P,Q)
:=
\left\|
\log\!\left(P^{-1/2}QP^{-1/2}\right)
\right\|_{\rm HS},
\]

and define the complete material distance

\[
\mathbf d(s,t)^2
:=\int_{\mathbb R^3}
d_{\rm aff}\bigl(\mathsf G(a,s),\mathsf G(a,t)\bigr)^2\,da.
\]

Path length and Cauchy--Schwarz give the exact datum bound

\[
\boxed{
\mathbf d(s,t)^2
\le
4(t-s)\int_s^t\|S(q)\|_2^2\,dq
=\frac{2(t-s)}{\nu}
\bigl[\mathcal E(s)-\mathcal E(t)\bigr].
}
\tag{TL.525}
\]

The full affine-metric length is also finite:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\operatorname{Len}_{[0,T]}(\mathsf G)
&=
2\int_0^T\|S(t)\|_2\,dt\\
&\le
\left[
\frac{2T}{\nu}
\bigl(\mathcal E(0)-\mathcal E(T)\bigr)
\right]^{1/2}.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.526}
\]

The affine symmetric space is complete, as is the component of its
\(L^2(da)\) map space at finite distance from the identity map.
Thus if \(T_*<\infty\), \(\mathsf G(t)\) has a terminal limit in this
complete material metric.  More sharply, for any disjoint first-record
doubling intervals \(J_j=[t_j,t_{j+1}]\),

\[
\boxed{
\sum_j\mathbf d(t_j,t_{j+1})
\le
\operatorname{Len}_{[0,T_*)}(\mathsf G)
\le
\left(\frac{2T_*\mathcal E_0}{\nu}\right)^{1/2},
\qquad
\mathbf d(t_j,t_{j+1})\longrightarrow0.
}
\tag{TL.527}
\]

This is a genuine same-fluid, causal finite-action statement.  The sum counts
each disjoint interval once, although the unsigned affine length itself also
charges loops.  Material deformation cannot undergo a fixed affine-metric
renewal at every record; any hypothetical finite-time infinite ladder must
double critical height across material-metric steps tending to zero.

It is not yet the contraction theorem.  \(H\) is a squared velocity of the
reciprocal-separation carrier, not the affine-metric position
\(\mathsf G\).  A finite-length curve can have unbounded positive variation
of its velocity.  The missing same-material observability theorem must give
record doubling a scale-covariant quantitative image in the datum-paid metric,
or place it in another independently finite deformation capacity, strongly
enough to preclude summable vanishing steps.  The local
fine-incidence gap of Cycle 155 does not give that global image: present
estimates do not prevent its active weight from concentrating on label sets
whose unweighted affine-metric action tends to zero.

## 39. Projective transport is exact and ordered, but supplies no real no-return holonomy

The normalized affine residual does retain cross-time order, but its
connection is geometrically reversible.  Trivialize the moving pair Hilbert
space by

\[
(\mathscr J_tf)(a,b)
:=\frac{f(a,b)}{|R_{ab}(t)|^2}.
\]

Then \(\mathscr J_t\) is an isometry into the fixed space
\(L^2(da\,db/\pi^2)\), and

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr J_t\mathscr D_tf
=\partial_t(\mathscr J_tf),
\qquad
\mathscr J_t\mathscr P_{s,t}=\mathscr J_s.
}
\tag{TL.528}
\]

Thus the scalar metric connection is pure gauge and has identity temporal
holonomy.  Put

\[
\psi_\alpha:=\mathscr J_t\Psi_\alpha,
\qquad
v_\alpha:=\partial_t\psi_\alpha,
\qquad
\Pi_\alpha:=\psi_\alpha\otimes\psi_\alpha.
\]

Since \(\|\psi_\alpha\|=1\), the canonical Kato generator

\[
\mathsf A_\alpha
:=v_\alpha\otimes\psi_\alpha-\psi_\alpha\otimes v_\alpha
\]

obeys

\[
\boxed{
\dot\Pi_\alpha=[\mathsf A_\alpha,\Pi_\alpha],
\qquad
\mathsf A_\alpha^*=-\mathsf A_\alpha,
\qquad
\operatorname{tr}\mathsf A_\alpha=0,
\qquad
\|\mathsf A_\alpha\|_{\rm HS}^2
=2\|\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_t}^2.
}
\tag{TL.529}
\]

Its ordered exponential transports the actual rank-one projector, but it is
orthogonal and reversible.  The cross-time coherence has only the ordinary
unit-sphere normal curvature:

\[
\boxed{
\left.\partial_t\mathscr K_\alpha(s,t)\right|_{t=s}=0,
\qquad
\left.\partial_t^2\mathscr K_\alpha(s,t)\right|_{t=s}
=-\|\mathscr D_s\Psi_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_s}^2
=-\frac12\|\mathsf A_\alpha(s)\|_{\rm HS}^2.
}
\tag{TL.530}
\]

After the same trivialization, if \(q_\alpha=\partial_t^2\psi_\alpha\),

\[
\langle q_\alpha,\psi_\alpha\rangle=-\|v_\alpha\|^2,
\qquad
\langle q_\alpha,v_\alpha\rangle
=\frac12\frac d{dt}\|v_\alpha\|^2.
\]

The first scalar is the time-reversal-even shape action; the second is a
sign-indefinite boundary derivative.  Neither is irreversible degradation.
There is no real Berry form on the normalized line because
\(\langle\psi_\alpha,d\psi_\alpha\rangle=0\), and in the actual one-time
parameter problem the curvature two-form vanishes identically.  In an
auxiliary multiparameter Kato family any nontrivial curvature is
operator-valued, skew and traceless; taking its norm returns an unsigned action.
A spatial cross product or triple product adds an orientation or axis, so
reflection changes its sign while leaving \(H\), viscosity and record growth
unchanged.

The recounting obstruction is exact at the Hilbert-geometry level.  In any
fixed two-plane let

\[
\psi_n(t)
=\cos(2\pi nt/T)e_1+\sin(2\pi nt/T)e_2.
\]

Let

\[
\mathsf A_n:=\dot\psi_n\otimes\psi_n-
\psi_n\otimes\dot\psi_n,
\qquad
\dot{\mathsf U}_n=\mathsf A_n\mathsf U_n,
\qquad
\mathsf U_n(0)=I.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\Pi_n(T)=\Pi_n(0),
\qquad
\mathscr K_n(0,T)=1,
\qquad
\mathsf U_n(T)=I,
\qquad
\int_0^T\|\dot\psi_n\|^2dt
=\frac{4\pi^2n^2}{T}.
}
\tag{TL.531}
\]

This is an algebraic countermodel to extracting a non-recounting payment
from projective geometry alone, not a claimed Navier--Stokes history.
Excluding analogous recurrence for the actual tangent requires a new
fixed-viscosity dynamical theorem.

Cycle 155's local fine-incidence polynomial gap does not supply that theorem.
It controls a denominator-cleared infinitesimal polynomial in
\((\nabla u,\nabla D_tu)\) with a near-null active weight and a
radial-collar alternative.  Equations (TL.513)--(TL.516) instead concern the
global finite-fraction projection
\(\Pi_{C_\alpha}^\perp Q_\alpha[u]\).  There is no identity or coercive map
between those objects.  Exact affine strain makes the distinction visible:
its finite-fraction affine residual vanishes although its local gradient
geometry need not; finite-energy cutoffs do not provide a uniform bridge.

The ordered projective history is therefore necessary for carrier fidelity
but insufficient by itself as a payer.  Within this route, closure still
requires either a PDE-derived irreversible stock coupling record-positive
critical work to the datum-paid material deformation in Section 38, or a
dynamical no-ray/no-recurrence theorem together with another datum-paid bridge.
Neither flat metric transport, Kato holonomy, local normal curvature,
chirality, nor positive projective path action alone supplies that result.

## 40. A Zeno ladder has only a ray-or-unpaid-action dichotomy

The preceding obstruction can be stated directly on hypothetical record
intervals without prescribing a cascade.  Let the levels \(h_j\) be
successive, let \(t_j\) be the first time the corresponding upper level
\(2h_j\) is reached, and let \(s_j<t_j\) be the last preceding time at which
\(H=h_j\).  Then \(J_j=[s_j,t_j]\) are disjoint and
\(h_j\le H\le2h_j\) on them, with

\[
H(s_j)=h_j,
\qquad
H(t_j)=2h_j,
\qquad
h_j\longrightarrow\infty,
\qquad
t_j\uparrow T_*<\infty.
\]

Put

\[
g(t):=\left(\frac{H'}H\right)_+,
\qquad
a_\alpha(t):=
\|\mathscr D_t\Psi_\alpha(t)\|_{\mathscr H_t},
\qquad
\delta_j:=
\inf_{\substack{t\in J_j\\g(t)>0}}
\frac{2a_\alpha(t)}{g(t)}.
\tag{TL.532}
\]

Since \(\int_{J_j}g\,dt\ge\log2\), exactly one of the following alternatives
occurs after passage to a subsequence.

If \(\delta_j\to0\), there are rising times \(q_j\in J_j\) for which

\[
\boxed{
\frac{
\left\|
\mathscr D_tC_\alpha-\frac{H'}{2H}C_\alpha
\right\|_{\mathscr H_t}}
{
\left|\frac{H'}{2H}\right|
\|C_\alpha\|_{\mathscr H_t}}
(q_j)
\longrightarrow0.
}
\tag{TL.533}
\]

Thus the complete material tangent approaches a positive-growth radial ray
at those instants.  This is a same-history statement and is stronger than
small change of a shell or scalar moment.

Otherwise \(\delta_j\ge\delta>0\) eventually, and every such record consumes

\[
\boxed{
\int_{J_j}a_\alpha\,dt
\ge\frac{\delta\log2}{2},
\qquad
\int_{J_j}a_\alpha^2\,dt
\ge
\frac{\delta^2(\log2)^2}{4|J_j|}
\longrightarrow\infty.
}
\tag{TL.534}
\]

The second branch is not contradictory.  The projective shape action is
unsigned and can recount loops; no datum-finite upper bound for it has been
derived from these identities.  Therefore these identities do not force a
Zeno ladder into the approximate-ray branch.  If one added the unproved estimate
\(\int_0^{T_*}a_\alpha^2dt<\infty\), terminal-tail Cauchy--Schwarz would
force \(\delta_j\to0\); that hypothesis is itself the missing material-action
payment.

Record rescaling does not repair the dichotomy.  Let
\(e_j:=\mathcal E(s_j)\), \(r_j:=e_j/h_j\), choose any centers \(x_j\), and
set

\[
\boxed{
w_j(\tau,y)
:=
\frac{r_j}{\sqrt{h_j}}\,
u\!\left(
s_j+\frac{r_j^2}{\sqrt{h_j}}\tau,\,
x_j+r_jy
\right).
}
\tag{TL.535}
\]

On the normalized last-passage interval,

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E(w_j)\le1,
\qquad
1\le H(w_j)\le2,
\qquad
\partial_\tau w_j+\mathbb P(w_j\cdot\nabla w_j)
=\frac{\nu}{\sqrt{h_j}}\Delta w_j,
\qquad
L_j=\frac{h_j^{5/2}|J_j|}{e_j^2}.
}
\tag{TL.536}
\]

The record law \(h_j^2|J_j|\to0\) does not determine \(L_j\), which may tend
to zero, a positive number, or infinity.  Bounded snapshots have only weak
\(L^2\cap\dot H^{1/2}\) subsequences after the usual symmetry choices; the
limit may be zero.  High-frequency critical tails, spatial splitting and
translation remain, while \(P_H\), the material quadratic row in (TL.513),
and the endpoint doubling are not weakly continuous.  Most decisively, the
effective viscosity is \(\nu/\sqrt{h_j}\to0\).

Record normalization alone therefore produces no time-dependent limiting
history.  If additional local compactness and nonlinear defect control gave a
nontrivial limit, a positive finite limit of \(L_j\) would yield a
vanishing-viscosity Euler arc, while \(L_j\to\infty\), with the present
anchoring at \(s_j\), would yield a forward-global Euler candidate.  Neither
is a fixed-viscosity VPI limit, and neither follows from the weak snapshot
compactness above or forces the fixed-profile eigenray alternative (TL.516).
The exact surviving burden is a fixed-viscosity observability theorem stable
under active-weight concentration, profile splitting, remote-collar loss and
the terminal current defect, together with an independently datum-finite
non-recounting payment.

## 41. Fixed-viscosity scaling rules out a datum-independent affine record charge

The finite material action in Section 38 cannot by itself be upgraded to a
universal positive charge for one critical record.  This is ruled out by the
exact fixed-viscosity Navier--Stokes scaling, not by a proxy schedule.  For
\(\lambda>0\), put

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
u_\lambda(x,\tau)&:=\lambda u(\lambda x,\lambda^2\tau),
&p_\lambda(x,\tau)&:=\lambda^2p(\lambda x,\lambda^2\tau),\\
X_\lambda(a,\tau)&:=\lambda^{-1}X(\lambda a,\lambda^2\tau),
&\mathsf F_\lambda(a,\tau)&=\mathsf F(\lambda a,\lambda^2\tau),\\
&&\mathsf G_\lambda(a,\tau)&=\mathsf G(\lambda a,\lambda^2\tau).
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.537}
\]

This preserves the same viscosity \(\nu\), the pressure-completed equation,
incompressibility, smoothness and the complete material-label construction.
It also gives

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E_\lambda(\tau)=\lambda^{-1}\mathcal E(\lambda^2\tau),
\qquad
H_\lambda(\tau)=H(\lambda^2\tau).
}
\tag{TL.538}
\]

Let

\[
\mathcal A_{\mathsf G}([s,t])
:=
\int_s^t\!\int
\operatorname{tr}
\bigl(\mathsf G^{-1}\dot{\mathsf G}
\mathsf G^{-1}\dot{\mathsf G}\bigr)\,da\,dq.
\]

If \(J=[s,t]\) is an actual last-passage record from \(h\) to \(2h\), then
\(J_\lambda=[s/\lambda^2,t/\lambda^2]\) is the same critical record for
\(u_\lambda\), whereas

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathbf d_\lambda(s/\lambda^2,t/\lambda^2)
&=\lambda^{-3/2}\mathbf d(s,t),\\
\operatorname{Len}_{J_\lambda}(\mathsf G_\lambda)
&=\lambda^{-3/2}\operatorname{Len}_{J}(\mathsf G),\\
\mathcal A_{\mathsf G_\lambda}(J_\lambda)
&=\lambda^{-1}\mathcal A_{\mathsf G}(J)
=\frac{2}{\nu}\lambda^{-1}
[\mathcal E(s)-\mathcal E(t)].
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.539}
\]

Consequently, if the class of smooth fixed-viscosity \(h\)-to-\(2h\)
records is nonempty, the infimum of each affine distance, length and action in
that class is zero.  No positive lower record charge depending only on
\((h,\nu)\) can be true.  If the class is empty, the stronger no-doubling
statement is already true.

This does not rule out a floor depending on the complete root datum; such a
floor would have to transform covariantly under (TL.537).  It does prove that
the observability burden at the end of Section 38 cannot be discharged by the
raw unweighted affine metric alone.  The bridge must retain a full-datum
concentration modulus or place every record in a genuinely scale-invariant,
irreversible capacity.  Present identities provide neither, so (TL.539) is a
strict obstruction, not the contraction theorem.

## 42. Every record forces a strain--incidence concentration product

The complete pair service law (TL.342) has an exact spatial incidence form
which keeps its material origin visible.  This is a pushforward readout of the
fixed-label carrier, not a new independently evolving object.  Put

\[
n_{ab}:=\frac{R_{ab}}{|R_{ab}|},
\qquad
Z_{ab}^{\theta}:=X_b+\theta R_{ab},
\]

and define the positive matrix-valued incidence measure \(\mathsf M_t\) by

\[
\boxed{
\int \varphi(z):d\mathsf M_t(z)
:=
\int d\mathbb Q_t(a,b)
\int_0^1
\varphi(Z_{ab}^{\theta}):
(n_{ab}\otimes n_{ab})\,d\theta .
}
\tag{TL.540}
\]

Its trace measure has total mass one; \(\mathsf M_t\) is not asserted to have
pointwise trace one.  Volume preservation and the change
\(x=z+(1-\theta)R\), \(y=z-\theta R\) give its current-coordinate density

\[
\mathsf m(z,t)
=\frac1{4\pi^2H(t)}
\int_0^1\!\int_{\mathbb R^3}
\frac{
|u(z+(1-\theta)R,t)-u(z-\theta R,t)|^2
}{|R|^4}
(\widehat R\otimes\widehat R)\,dR\,d\theta,
\]

with \(\mathsf m\in L^1\), \(\mathsf m\ge0\), and
\(\int\operatorname{tr}\mathsf m\,dz=1\).  Define its directional,
incompressible part by

\[
\mathsf m^\circ
:=\mathsf m-\frac{1}{3}(\operatorname{tr}\mathsf m)I.
\]

The fundamental theorem of calculus along the actual segment gives

\[
\sigma_{ab}
=\int_0^1 n_{ab}^TS(Z_{ab}^{\theta},t)n_{ab}\,d\theta.
\]

Consequently incompressibility removes only the isotropic incidence row, and
(TL.342) becomes the exact signed complete-carrier readout

\[
\boxed{
\frac{H'}H+\nu\frac{\mathcal D}{H}
=-4\int_{\mathbb R^3}S(z,t):\mathsf m^\circ(z,t)\,dz.
}
\tag{TL.541}
\]

In particular, at every rising time,

\[
\boxed{
H'\ge0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\|S\|_2\|\mathsf m^\circ\|_2
\ge\frac{\nu\mathcal D}{4H}
\ge\frac{\nu\varkappa^2}{2}.
}
\tag{TL.541a}
\]

Thus a smaller whole-field effective scale raises, at the same instant, the
required product of actual strain and directionally anisotropic incidence.

Pressure and viscosity have not been assigned separate carriers: they remain
in the fixed-label evolution (TL.337)--(TL.345) which determines the next
\(\mathbb Q_t\) and hence the next \(\mathsf m\).  Equation (TL.541) is formed
only after that complete material acceleration and the global pressure
cancellation have been retained.

For an interval \(J=[s,t]\), set

\[
\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}(J)
:=\int_J\|\mathsf m^\circ(q)\|_{L^2_z}^2\,dq,
\]

with value \(+\infty\) when the density is not in spacetime \(L^2\).  Integrating
(TL.541), using its sign, and then applying spacetime Cauchy--Schwarz together
with the energy identity gives

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\log\frac{H(t)}{H(s)}
+\nu\int_s^t\frac{\mathcal D}{H}\,dq
&=-4\int_s^t\!\int S:\mathsf m^\circ\,dz\,dq\\
&\le
4\left(\frac{\mathcal E(s)-\mathcal E(t)}{2\nu}\right)^{1/2}
\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}(J)^{1/2}.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.542}
\]

Thus every last-passage doubling interval obeys the simultaneous product law

\[
\boxed{
[\mathcal E(s)-\mathcal E(t)]\,
\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}(J)
\ge
\frac{\nu}{8}
\left(
\log2+\nu\int_J\frac{\mathcal D}{H}\,dq
\right)^2
\ge\frac{\nu(\log2)^2}{8}.
}
\tag{TL.543}
\]

The two requested halves now occur at the same causal instant and on the same
fluid history.  Viscosity shortens the critical lifetime through
\(\nu\mathcal D/H\); productive throughput must oppose it through the strain
paired with the actual directionally coherent incidence; and a small remaining
kinetic-energy payment is possible only if that incidence becomes strongly
concentrated.  Isotropic surrounding incidence cannot help because
\(S:I=0\).

For disjoint first-record doubling intervals \(J_j\), the energy drops are
not recounted.  Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\sum_j\frac1{\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}(J_j)}
\le
\frac{8\mathcal E_0}{\nu(\log2)^2}.
}
\tag{TL.544}
\]

In particular, an infinite finite-time record ladder would require
\(\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}(J_j)\to\infty\).  If

\[
\mathcal V_{\rm inc}(J)
:=\frac{|J|}{\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}(J)},
\]

then \(\mathcal V_{\rm inc}\) has the scaling of a spatial volume and
(TL.543) gives

\[
\mathcal V_{\rm inc}(J_j)
\le
\frac{8[\mathcal E(s_j)-\mathcal E(t_j)]|J_j|}
{\nu(\log2)^2}
\longrightarrow0.
\]

This is a strict same-history throughput--incidence concentration law, but it
is not yet contraction.  The norm of \(\mathsf m^\circ\) combines directional
anisotropy with spatial concentration; it is not by itself the complete
temporal coherence.  The incidence density marginalizes labels, endpoint
velocities, pressure and Laplacian rows, and temporal order; at one time it is
reconstructible from \(u(t)\).  No datum-finite upper bound for
\(\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}\), no positive lower bound for
\(\mathcal V_{\rm inc}\), and no signed no-return law for their repeated
concentration has been proved.  The remaining theorem must lift (TL.543) back
to the unmarginalized fixed-label evolution and show that the very
concentration which preserves delivery consumes a finite irreversible
capacity or forces the approximate material-ray branch of Section 40.

## 43. The incidence hierarchy is exact, but its entropy has no sign

The natural evolution of the concentration in Section 42 can also be written
without leaving the same carrier.  In current endpoint coordinates let

\[
\varrho(x,y,t)
:=
\frac{|u(x,t)-u(y,t)|^2}
{4\pi^2H(t)|x-y|^4},
\qquad
\bar\gamma:=\frac{H'}H,
\]

and use the complete logarithmic pair-growth rate \(\gamma\) from (TL.344).
Incompressibility and the fixed-label law (TL.345) give

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\varrho
+\operatorname{div}_x\!\bigl(u(x)\varrho\bigr)
+\operatorname{div}_y\!\bigl(u(y)\varrho\bigr)
=(\gamma-\bar\gamma)\varrho.
}
\tag{TL.545}
\]

Thus the active pair density is transported by the divergence-free
six-dimensional endpoint flow, but its relative weights follow a replicator;
there is no diffusion on label space.  Pressure has zero contribution to the
\(\varrho\)-mean of \(\gamma\), and viscosity has mean
\(-\nu\mathcal D/H\), but neither statement fixes their conditional action on
the concentrating pairs.

The loss of sign survives the spatial incidence pushforward.  Put

\[
W^\theta:=(1-\theta)u(y)+\theta u(x),
\qquad
\beta:=\frac{(I-n\otimes n)[u(x)-u(y)]}{|x-y|},
\qquad n:=\frac{x-y}{|x-y|},
\]

and define

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathsf J_{ijk}(z)
&:=
\iint\varrho
\int_0^1n_in_jW_k^\theta
\delta(z-y-\theta[x-y])\,d\theta\,dx\,dy,\\
\mathsf O_{ij}(z)
&:=
\iint\varrho
\int_0^1(\beta_in_j+n_i\beta_j)
\delta(z-y-\theta[x-y])\,d\theta\,dx\,dy,\\
\mathsf G_{ij}(z)
&:=
\iint(\gamma-\bar\gamma)\varrho
\int_0^1n_in_j
\delta(z-y-\theta[x-y])\,d\theta\,dx\,dy .
\end{aligned}
\]

Differentiating the same segment pushforward gives, distributionally,

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\mathsf m_{ij}
+\partial_{z_k}\mathsf J_{ijk}
=\mathsf O_{ij}+\mathsf G_{ij}.
}
\tag{TL.546}
\]

The three rows are the projected chord-point transport, orientation turning,
and centered pressure--viscosity--geometry replicator.  They are one evolution,
not separate mechanisms.  For a smooth density,

\[
\boxed{
\frac12\frac d{dt}\|\mathsf m\|_2^2
=
\int \partial_{z_k}\mathsf m_{ij}\,\mathsf J_{ijk}\,dz
+\int\mathsf m:(\mathsf O+\mathsf G)\,dz,
}
\tag{TL.547}
\]

which is neither closed in \(\mathsf m\) nor sign definite.

The two most natural entropy attempts fail for the same exact reason.  Let
\(\mu:=\operatorname{tr}\mathsf m\),
\(\mathbf j_k:=\operatorname{tr}\mathsf J_{\cdot\cdot k}\), and
\(g:=\operatorname{tr}\mathsf G\).  The orientation source is traceless, so
\(\partial_t\mu+\nabla\cdot\mathbf j=g\).  Where
\(\mathbf j=\mu b\) and \(g=\mu\zeta\),

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\frac d{dt}\int\mu\log\mu\,dz
&=-\int\mu\,\nabla\cdot b\,dz
+\int\mu\zeta\log\mu\,dz,\\
\frac d{dt}\iint\varrho\log\varrho\,dx\,dy
&=\operatorname{Cov}_{\varrho}(\gamma,\log\varrho),\\
\frac d{dt}\iint\varrho^2\,dx\,dy
&=2\iint(\gamma-\bar\gamma)\varrho^2\,dx\,dy.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.548}
\]

The endpoint transport drops from the pair entropy because it is
incompressible.  It does not drop after projection: generally
\(W^\theta\ne u(y+\theta[x-y])\), so the chord-point flux is compressible.
The pressure row can have nonzero entropy and Rényi covariance despite its
zero global mean, and the viscous row is not conditionally negative.  Hence
none of (TL.547)--(TL.548) is the requested irreversible stock.

The obstruction is scaling exact.  Under (TL.537),

\[
\mathsf m_\lambda(z,\tau)
=\lambda^3\mathsf m(\lambda z,\lambda^2\tau),
\qquad
\mathfrak C_{{\rm inc},\lambda}(J_\lambda)
=\lambda\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}(J),
\]

while the pair and incidence entropies shift by \(6\log\lambda\) and
\(3\log\lambda\), respectively.  The energy drop shrinks by
\(\lambda^{-1}\), so its product with the incidence concentration in
(TL.543) is invariant.  A Shannon bound would in any case be too weak to
dualize against the datum-paid \(S\in L_t^2L_x^2\) row.

The exact hierarchy defect is now localized: the unmarginalized pair
transport is volume preserving, but the chord-point projection is
compressible and the centered material acceleration can refocus its weight
without a signed entropy cost presently visible in these identities.  Closing
the goal through this lane requires a new \(L^2\)-dual, ancestry-preserving
capacity bound on that refocusing, or a PDE sign/cancellation theorem for the
conditional pressure--viscosity replicator.  Deriving (TL.546) does not supply
either one.

## 44. Endpoint viscosity diffuses chord location, not relative scale

The viscous row in Section 43 can be reorganized as a genuine diffusion, but
only in the common spatial location of a chord.  Fix \(0<\theta<1\) and use

\[
R:=x-y,
\qquad
z:=\theta x+(1-\theta)y,
\qquad
x=z+(1-\theta)R,
\qquad
y=z-\theta R.
\]

Put

\[
V:=u(x)-u(y),
\qquad
W^\theta:=\theta u(x)+(1-\theta)u(y),
\qquad
\zeta:=\frac{V}{|R|^2},
\qquad
\sigma:=\frac{R\cdot V}{|R|^2},
\]

and

\[
\mathfrak p_\theta
:=\frac{\nabla p(x)-\nabla p(y)}{|R|^2}.
\]

The drift \((W^\theta,V)\) is divergence free in \((z,R)\), and
\(\Delta_zV=\Delta u(x)-\Delta u(y)\).  Therefore the complete endpoint
equation is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\left(
\partial_t+W^\theta\cdot\nabla_z+V\cdot\nabla_R-\nu\Delta_z
\right)\zeta
&=-\mathfrak p_\theta-2\sigma\zeta,\\
\left(
\partial_t+W^\theta\cdot\nabla_z+V\cdot\nabla_R-\nu\Delta_z
\right)|\zeta|^2
&=-2\zeta\cdot\mathfrak p_\theta
-4\sigma|\zeta|^2
-2\nu|\nabla_z\zeta|^2.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.549}
\]

Thus viscosity supplies common-location diffusion and the exact negative
center-gradient term, but no diffusion in the relative separation \(R\).
The latter is moved only by the actual endpoint velocity difference \(V\).
This is equivalent to the full \((x,y)\)-Laplacian expansion: its apparently
separate Hardy potential and kernel-gradient terms cancel into the structured
common-center equation (TL.549).

Let \(Z:=4\pi^2H\), \(\varrho_\theta:=|\zeta|^2/Z\), and, where
\(|\zeta|>0\), put

\[
g_\theta
:=
-2\frac{\zeta\cdot\mathfrak p_\theta}{|\zeta|^2}
-4\sigma
-2\nu\frac{|\nabla_z\zeta|^2}{|\zeta|^2},
\qquad
\bar g:=\int\varrho_\theta g_\theta\,dz\,dR=\frac{H'}H.
\]

The zero set is read through the product
\(\varrho_\theta(g_\theta-\bar g)\).  The normalized density satisfies the
diffusion--replicator law, and its Shannon identity is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\left(
\partial_t+W^\theta\cdot\nabla_z+V\cdot\nabla_R-\nu\Delta_z
\right)\varrho_\theta
&=(g_\theta-\bar g)\varrho_\theta,\\
\frac d{dt}\int\varrho_\theta\log\varrho_\theta\,dz\,dR
+\nu I_z(\varrho_\theta)
&=\operatorname{Cov}_{\varrho_\theta}
(g_\theta,\log\varrho_\theta),
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.550}
\]

where

\[
I_z(\varrho):=\int\frac{|\nabla_z\varrho|^2}{\varrho}\,dz\,dR.
\]

The covariance in (TL.550) has no sign: the pressure row has zero global
critical mean but not zero conditional covariance, and the viscous killing is
not pointwise or conditionally negative after normalization.  Nor can a
relative-coordinate Fisher term be added for free.  Generically,

\[
\varrho_\theta(z,rn)
\sim
\frac{|\nabla u(z)n|^2}{4\pi^2H\,r^2}
\qquad(r\downarrow0),
\]

so both \(\int\varrho_\theta^2\) and
\(\int\varrho_\theta|\nabla_R\log\varrho_\theta|^2\) diverge like
\(\int_0^\varepsilon r^{-2}dr\).  The critical pair diagonal therefore
prevents a finite relative-scale Rényi or Fisher payer.

The finite center Fisher information still yields a sharp concentration
estimate.  Since

\[
\int|\nabla_z\zeta|^2\,dz\,dR=2\pi^2\mathcal D,
\]

the pointwise inequality
\(|\nabla|\zeta|^2|^2/|\zeta|^2\le4|\nabla\zeta|^2\) gives
\(I_z(\varrho_\theta)\le2\mathcal D/H\).  The trace density
\(\mu=\operatorname{tr}\mathsf m\) is a marginal of
\(d\theta\,\varrho_\theta dz\,dR\), so Fisher data processing, positivity of
\(\mathsf m\), and the three-dimensional Gagliardo--Nirenberg inequality for
\(\sqrt\mu\) give

\[
\boxed{
\|\mathsf m^\circ\|_2^2
\le\frac23\|\mu\|_2^2
\le C I(\mu)^{3/2}
\le C\left(\frac{\mathcal D}{H}\right)^{3/2}.
}
\tag{TL.551}
\]

There is a stronger snapshot form.  If

\[
A(z):=\int_{\mathbb R^3}
\frac{|u(z+R)-u(z)|^2}{|R|^4}\,dR,
\]

splitting each \(\theta\)-chord through \(u(z)\) gives
\(\mu\le C A/H\).  The difference characterization and the critical Sobolev
embedding yield

\[
\|A\|_2
\le C\|u\|_{\dot B^{1/2}_{4,2}}^2
\le C\|u\|_{\dot H^{5/4}}^2.
\]

Spectral interpolation then proves

\[
\boxed{
\|\mathsf m^\circ\|_2^2
\le
C\frac{\|u\|_{\dot H^{5/4}}^4}{H^2}
\le
C\frac{K_H\mathcal D}{H^2}
\le
C\left(\frac{\mathcal D}{H}\right)^{3/2}.
}
\tag{TL.552}
\]

Every exponent in (TL.551)--(TL.552) is fixed by Navier--Stokes scaling.
Inserting the strongest row into (TL.541) returns

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
P_H=H'+\nu\mathcal D
&\le C K_H\sqrt{\mathcal D},\\
H'
&\le\frac{C_*}{\nu}K_H^2,\\
H(t)=2H(s)=2h
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\int_s^tK_H(q)^2\,dq
&\ge c_*\nu h.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.553}
\]

This is a genuine record consequence, but not a new datum payment.  The
kinetic-energy identity controls \(\int K_Hdt\), not
\(\int K_H^2dt\).  By Sobolev,
\(\int K_H^2dt\) controls the critical \(L_t^4L_x^6\) Serrin norm of
\(u\); a datum-finite bound for it is already a continuation criterion.
Hence the endpoint-diffusion/Hardy route does not make the Gold
burden smaller: after the exact carrier coupling is retained, it lands on an
unpaid critical regularity action.

The failure is already visible in the linear support problem.  A rescaled
anisotropic Schwartz Stokes solution can keep its kinetic-energy and Stokes
dissipation budgets fixed while
\(\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}\) grows linearly with its frequency.  This is not a
Navier--Stokes record history, but it proves that smoothness and the energy
identity alone cannot bound the incidence capacity.  Nonlinear ancestry or a
new conditional pressure--viscosity sign is indispensable.

## 45. Record normalization is causal and non-recounting, but not itself contraction

There is one exact way to put the running record directly into the completed
carrier without counting a later oscillation as a new event.  The zero datum
gives \(u\equiv0\), so assume \(H(0)>0\), and set

\[
H^*(t):=\max_{0\le q\le t}H(q),
\qquad
dr(t):=d\log H^*(t).
\]

Since \(H\) is absolutely continuous on every smooth preterminal interval,
the running-maximum lemma gives, as an equality of absolutely continuous
measures,

\[
\boxed{
dr
=\mathbf 1_{\{H=H^*\}}
\left[\frac{H'}{H}\right]_+dt .
}
\tag{TL.554}
\]

Thus \(dr\) is causal, signed in the forward-record direction, and supported
only where the same history makes a new critical-height record.  It has no
atoms at isolated first-passage times.  If
\(\tau_h:=\inf\{t:H(t)=h\}\), then for every nonnegative Borel function
\(\phi\),

\[
\boxed{
\int_{[0,T]}\phi(t)\,dr(t)
=\int_{H(0)}^{H^*(T)}\phi(\tau_h)\,\frac{dh}{h}.
}
\tag{TL.555}
\]

Consequently every nonoverlapping first-record ladder
\(H(0),qH(0),q^2H(0),\ldots\) spends exactly \(\log q\) of this measure per
completed step.  This is a pushforward of \(dh/h\), not an atomic charge at
\(\tau_h\).

Apply this clock to the complete contracted incidence carrier (TL.501).  Put

\[
\mathcal M_I^*:=\frac{\mathcal M_I}{H^*},
\qquad
\mathcal Q_I^*:=\frac{\widehat{\mathcal Q}_I}{H^*},
\qquad
\mathcal G_I^*:=\frac{\mathcal G_I}{H^*},
\]

where \(\widehat{\mathcal Q}_I\) is the completed current in (TL.501).  The
quotient rule and (TL.501) give the distributional identity

\[
\boxed{
d\mathcal M_I^*
+\partial_\rho\mathcal Q_I^*\,dt
=-2\nu\mathcal G_I^*\,dt
-\mathcal M_I^*\,dr .
}
\tag{TL.556}
\]

The last term is the exact non-recounting record-normalization loss.  It is a
quotient-rule sink created by division by the target record \(H^*\); it is
not an independently derived viscous or geometric degradation.

For a causally prescribed finite union \(\mathcal A(t)\Subset(0,\infty)\)
of compatible regular moving cells, combine signed shared-face currents
before taking their positive parts.  If

\[
S_{\mathcal A}^*(t)
:=\int_{\mathcal A(t)}\mathcal M_I^*(\rho,t)\,d\rho,
\qquad
L_{\mathcal A}^*(t)
:=2\nu\int_{\mathcal A(t)}\mathcal G_I^*(\rho,t)\,d\rho,
\]

Reynolds transport gives

\[
\boxed{
dS_{\mathcal A}^*
+J_{\mathcal A}^{\rm out}dt
+L_{\mathcal A}^*dt
+S_{\mathcal A}^*\,dr
=J_{\mathcal A}^{\rm in}dt .
}
\tag{TL.557}
\]

This formula assumes that the cell union has no unrecorded temporal selection
jumps; any change of components contributes an additional temporal face.
Integrating (TL.557) and discarding only nonnegative terms yields

\[
\boxed{
\int_{[0,T]}S_{\mathcal A}^*\,dr
\le
S_{\mathcal A}^*(0)
+\int_0^T J_{\mathcal A}^{\rm in}(t)\,dt .
}
\tag{TL.558}
\]

This is the exact conditional no-Zeno mechanism.  If one specified a single
causal, \(T\)-independent regular union for which

\[
S_{\mathcal A}^*\ge c(u_0,\nu)>0
\quad dr\hbox{-a.e.},
\qquad
\sup_{T<T_*}\int_0^T J_{\mathcal A}^{\rm in}dt
\le C(u_0,\nu),
\tag{TL.559}
\]

with the second bound paid by a known datum-finite quantity containing no
\(H^*\), \(P_H\), positive critical variation or continuation norm, then
(TL.558) would bound \(\log(H^*(T)/H(0))\), exclude infinitely many
first-record doublings and continue the solution.

The occupancy half is not automatic.  On the entire radial contraction,
(TL.501) gives

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^\infty\mathcal M_I^*(\rho,t)\,d\rho
=2c_I\frac{H(t)}{H^*(t)},
\qquad
\int_{[0,T]}\!\int_0^\infty
\mathcal M_I^*\,d\rho\,dr
=2c_I\log\frac{H^*(T)}{H(0)}.
}
\tag{TL.560}
\]

But \((0,\infty)\) is not a regular pretrace cell: it meets the singular
apex and infinity.  Its apex influx is

\[
\mathcal Q_I^*(0,t)
=2c_I\frac{P_H(t)}{H^*(t)},
\tag{TL.561}
\]

which is exactly the critical production being counted.  Therefore
(TL.560) inserted into (TL.558) is circular.  Record normalization has now
made causality and non-recounting exact, but it has not supplied the missing
contraction.  A genuine strict reduction requires the actual union in
(TL.559), a proved transfer of positive record occupancy from the singular
full contraction to that regular pretrace union, and an external-inflow bound
derived from an independent datum-paid deformation or capacity law.

## 46. Zeno normalization has a local Euler limit, but loses the record

The normalized histories in (TL.535)--(TL.536) admit a sharper compactness
audit than weak snapshots alone.  Write

\[
\nu_j:=\frac{\nu}{\sqrt{h_j}},
\qquad 0\le\tau\le L_j.
\]

At the normalized last lower hit and throughout its record interval one has
exactly

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E(w_j(0))=H(w_j(0))=1,
\qquad
\mathcal E(w_j(\tau))\le1,
\qquad
1\le H(w_j(\tau))\le2,
}
\tag{TL.562}
\]

and

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal E(w_j(\tau))
+\nu_j\int_0^\tau\|\nabla w_j(\sigma)\|_2^2\,d\sigma
=1.
}
\tag{TL.563}
\]

Thus \(w_j\) is bounded in
\(L_\tau^\infty(L^2\cap\dot H^{1/2})\), hence in
\(L_\tau^\infty L^3\), while only
\(\sqrt{\nu_j}\nabla w_j\) is bounded in spacetime.  Suppose first that,
after passage to a subsequence, \(L_j\to L<\infty\), and put
\(\widetilde w_j(\sigma)=w_j(L_j\sigma)\) on \([0,1]\).  Then

\[
\partial_\sigma\widetilde w_j
+L_j\mathbb P\operatorname{div}
(\widetilde w_j\otimes\widetilde w_j)
=\nu_jL_j\Delta\widetilde w_j,
\tag{TL.564}
\]

with

\[
\|\nu_jL_j\Delta\widetilde w_j\|_{L^2_\sigma H^{-1}}^2
\le\nu_jL_j\longrightarrow0.
\tag{TL.565}
\]

Local Rellich compactness from \(\dot H^{1/2}\), together with the
\(W^{-1,3/2}\) bound for the quadratic time derivative, yields a subsequence
converging strongly in \(L^2([0,1]\times K)\) for every compact spatial set
\(K\).  The local quadratic term therefore has no Reynolds defect, and the
limit solves

\[
\boxed{
\partial_\sigma w
+L\mathbb P\operatorname{div}(w\otimes w)=0
}
\tag{TL.566}
\]

distributionally.  For \(L>0\) this is a weak Euler arc after a time change;
for \(L=0\) it is static.  If \(L_j\to\infty\), the same argument on each
fixed forward \(\tau\)-interval gives a forward-global weak Euler candidate;
recentering at the upper record time gives an ancient candidate.

None of these limits retains the critical record.  The equality
\(H(w_j(0))=1\), the upper endpoint value \(H=2\), the material incidence
current, and the pressure-completed critical throughput are not continuous
under the available convergence.  The failure is concrete.  A very
low-frequency divergence-free component can carry order-one kinetic energy
and vanishing \(H\), while a critically rescaled high-frequency component
can carry order-one \(H\) and vanishing kinetic energy.  With disjoint Fourier
supports their sum keeps both normalized bounds but both components may
vanish locally after every single translation choice.  The viscous force can
also vanish distributionally while
\(\nu_j|\nabla w_j|^2\) leaves a nonzero dissipation defect.

Therefore a Zeno contradiction by compactness requires all of the following,
not merely an Euler subsequence:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{gathered}
\text{phase-space tightness modulo one lawful symmetry choice},\\
\text{a nonzero profile and strong endpoint }\dot H^{1/2}\text{ convergence},\\
\text{defect-free convergence of the complete material VPI current},\\
\text{and rigidity of the resulting explicitly characterized Euler class}.
\end{gathered}
}
\tag{TL.567}
\]

With these properties, the \(L=0\) case would contradict a static limit whose
endpoint critical heights are one and two.  For \(0<L<\infty\), however, they
would only produce a nontrivial Euler arc that transfers critical strength;
the equations and the present bounds do not forbid such an arc.  The
forward-global or ancient cases likewise have no Liouville theorem under only
the properties above.  Hence “every Zeno ladder has an Euler limit” is a
proved support statement, not no-Zeno.  A strict reduction must first force a
smaller rigid class--for example an exact material eigenray or an
almost-periodic orbit with independently proved compactness--and then exclude
that class without assuming the desired critical bound.

## 47. Datum-paid strain action alone cannot control incidence capacity

The material labels in Section 38 preserve ancestry, but their paid
\(L^2\) strain action cannot by itself bound the concentration forced by
(TL.543).  Integrating (TL.552) gives the strongest direct comparison
available from that route:

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}(J)
\le C\int_J\frac{K_H\mathcal D}{H^2}\,dt
=2C\int_J\frac{\mathcal D}{H^2}\|S\|_2^2\,dt .
}
\tag{TL.568}
\]

Here \(K_H=\|\Lambda u\|_2^2=2\|S\|_2^2\), and the energy identity pays
only

\[
\int_0^T\|S\|_2^2dt
=\frac{\mathcal E(0)-\mathcal E(T)}{2\nu}.
\tag{TL.569}
\]

The missing factor \(\mathcal D/H^2\) has exactly the inverse-length-squared
size of critical refocusing.  Persistent labels do not remove it: the
normalized pair weight obeys a replicator equation with the complete
pressure--deformation--viscosity row, rather than passive volume-preserving
transport.

The fixed-viscosity scaling makes the mismatch exact.  For

\[
u_\lambda(x,t)=\lambda u(\lambda x,\lambda^2t),
\qquad
X_\lambda(a,t)=\lambda^{-1}X(\lambda a,\lambda^2t),
\]

one has

\[
\mathsf m_\lambda(z,t)=\lambda^3
\mathsf m(\lambda z,\lambda^2t).
\]

Consequently, on \(J_\lambda=\lambda^{-2}J\),

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak C_{{\rm inc},\lambda}(J_\lambda)
=\lambda\mathfrak C_{\rm inc}(J),
\qquad
\int_{J_\lambda}\|S_\lambda\|_2^2dt
=\lambda^{-1}\int_J\|S\|_2^2dt .
}
\tag{TL.570}
\]

Thus no universal coefficient can bound the first quantity by the second.
A root-datum coefficient is not ruled out, but it must retain an independent
inverse-length-squared capacity and cannot be manufactured from the paid
strain action.

There is also a one-history kinematic countertest.  Choose an anisotropic
divergence-free Schwartz field \(\phi\), with annular Fourier support and
\(M_\phi:=\|\mathsf m_\phi^\circ\|_2^2>0\).  For
\(\lambda_j=4^j\), put

\[
v_j(x):=\lambda_j\phi(\lambda_jx).
\tag{TL.571}
\]

Then

\[
H(v_j)=H(\phi),
\qquad
\|S(v_j)\|_2^2=\lambda_j\|S(\phi)\|_2^2,
\qquad
\|\mathsf m_{v_j}^\circ\|_2^2=\lambda_j^3M_\phi.
\]

Join plateaus of length \(\varepsilon\lambda_j^{-2}\) by flat smooth
transitions of length \(\varepsilon\lambda_{j+1}^{-3}\).  Because adjacent
Fourier supports are disjoint, a transition
\((1-\eta)v_j+\eta v_{j+1}\) satisfies

\[
H\bigl((1-\eta)v_j+\eta v_{j+1}\bigr)
=\bigl((1-\eta)^2+\eta^2\bigr)H(\phi)
\ge\frac12H(\phi).
\]

The resulting divergence-free history begins from one fixed datum, is smooth
on every preterminal interval, and has one volume-preserving material map,
but

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^{T_*}\|S\|_2^2dt<\infty,
\qquad
\int_0^{T_*}\|\mathsf m^\circ\|_2^2dt=\infty.
}
\tag{TL.572}
\]

This history is not an unforced Navier--Stokes solution; its defect force is
nonzero.  It proves exactly that incompressibility, smoothness, persistent
ancestry and finite material strain action contain no hidden incidence
coercivity.  The surviving theorem is therefore Navier--Stokes-specific:

\[
\boxed{
\text{a conditional pressure--viscosity no-refocusing sign, or a
record-stopped ancestry-current capacity paid independently by the datum.}
}
\tag{TL.573}
\]

Without such a theorem, (TL.543) forces concentration along a hypothetical
record ladder but cannot forbid it.

## 48. Causal mass quantiles move the record bill into their incoming faces

The regular union in (TL.559) cannot be obtained for free by selecting a
fixed fraction of the positive radial stock.  Abbreviate the unnormalized
law (TL.501) by

\[
M_t+Q_\rho=-L,
\qquad
m(t):=\int_0^\infty M\,d\rho=2c_IH(t),
\qquad
m'=Q(0)-L_{\rm tot}.
\tag{TL.574}
\]

Let \(F(\rho,t)=\int_0^\rho M(s,t)ds\), and suppose a regular quantile
boundary satisfies

\[
F(\rho_\alpha(t),t)=\alpha(t)m(t).
\]

If

\[
j_\alpha:=Q(\rho_\alpha)-M(\rho_\alpha)\dot\rho_\alpha,
\qquad
L_\alpha^{\rm tail}:=\int_{\rho_\alpha}^\infty L\,d\rho,
\]

then differentiation of the quantile equation gives the exact relative
current

\[
\boxed{
j_\alpha
=(1-\alpha)m'+L_\alpha^{\rm tail}-m\dot\alpha .
}
\tag{TL.575}
\]

For fixed \(\alpha\), the record set satisfies
\(m'dt=2c_IH^*dr\).  Hence

\[
\boxed{
\frac{j_\alpha}{H^*}dt
=2c_I(1-\alpha)dr
+\frac{L_\alpha^{\rm tail}}{H^*}dt
\ge0 .
}
\tag{TL.576}
\]

Now take a fixed quantile band
\(A_{\alpha,\beta}=(\rho_\alpha,\rho_\beta)\), with
\(0\le\alpha<\beta\le1\), interpreting a tail endpoint by regular
truncation.  At a record its normalized stock is
\(S_A^*=2c_I(\beta-\alpha)\), and both relative currents point toward
increasing \(\rho\).  Therefore

\[
\boxed{
\frac{j_\alpha-j_\beta}{H^*}dt
=2c_I(\beta-\alpha)dr
+L_A^*dt,
\qquad
dJ_A^{\rm in}
=dJ_A^{\rm out}+L_A^*dt+S_A^*dr .
}
\tag{TL.577}
\]

Thus the incoming face already carries the entire proposed record charge.
A finite union of disjoint quantile bands obeys the same formula with
\(2c_I\sum_k(\beta_k-\alpha_k)dr\); fragmentation merely adds incoming
faces.  The least-inflow fixed choice is a tail, and even there incoming
current equals its full record sink plus its loss.

Allowing the quantile itself to move cannot preserve both low inflow and
positive occupancy.  To cancel the record part of (TL.575), one needs

\[
\dot\alpha
\ge(1-\alpha)\frac{m'}m
=(1-\alpha)\frac{dr}{dt},
\]

and therefore

\[
\boxed{
d[-\log(1-\alpha)]\ge dr,
\qquad
1-\alpha(t)
\le(1-\alpha(0))\frac{H(0)}{H^*(t)}.
}
\tag{TL.578}
\]

The retained tail fraction then vanishes precisely as the record grows.
Resetting \(\alpha\) downward restores occupancy through the positive term
\(-m\dot\alpha\), which is exactly new incoming current and recounts the
charge it was meant to avoid.  In particular, on the full radial carrier,

\[
\boxed{
\frac{Q(0,t)}{H^*(t)}dt
=2c_I,dr
+\frac{L_{\rm tot}(t)}{H^*(t)}dt .
}
\tag{TL.579}
\]

Mass-quantile selection therefore cannot prove (TL.559).  It shows instead
that definite record occupancy and datum-paid inflow are the two inseparable
halves of the missing no-return theorem: conservation alone makes the first
force the second.

## 49. The instantaneous pressure--viscosity replicator has both signs

The alternative in (TL.573) cannot be an unconditional instantaneous sign
already hidden in the pair equation.  Split the complete material score
(TL.344) as

\[
\gamma=\gamma_E+\gamma_\nu,
\qquad
\gamma_E
:=-2\frac{V\cdot\delta\nabla p}{|V|^2}-4\sigma,
\qquad
\gamma_\nu
:=2\nu\frac{V\cdot\delta\Delta u}{|V|^2}.
\tag{TL.580}
\]

For any smooth divergence-free datum \(v\) and nonzero real amplitude \(A\),
put \(u_A=Av\).  At the initial time,

\[
\mathbb Q_{u_A}=\mathbb Q_v,
\qquad
p[u_A]=A^2p[v].
\]

Consequently the centered scores scale with opposite parity:

\[
\boxed{
(\gamma_E-\mathbb E\gamma_E)[u_A]
=A(\gamma_E-\mathbb E\gamma_E)[v],
\qquad
(\gamma_\nu-\mathbb E\gamma_\nu)[u_A]
=(\gamma_\nu-\mathbb E\gamma_\nu)[v].
}
\tag{TL.581}
\]

For every fixed material-pair set \(B\), (TL.345) therefore gives

\[
\boxed{
\left.\frac d{dt}\mathbb Q_{u_A,t}(B)\right|_{t=0}
=A\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathbb Q_v}
(\mathbf1_B,\gamma_E[v])
+\operatorname{Cov}_{\mathbb Q_v}
(\mathbf1_B,\gamma_\nu[v]).
}
\tag{TL.582}
\]

Whenever the Euler score is nonconstant, choosing
\(B=\{\gamma_E>\mathbb E\gamma_E\}\) makes its covariance strictly
positive.  The two lawful fixed-viscosity data \(Av\) with \(A\to+\infty\)
and \(A\to-\infty\) then give opposite conditional refocusing directions;
the even viscous row cannot mask the odd pressure--deformation row.

The sign freedom is not formal.  On the periodic algebraic countertest

\[
v=(\sin x\cos y,-\cos x\sin y,0),
\qquad
p=\frac14(\cos2x+\cos2y),
\]

the pair \(X=(\pi/4,0)\), \(Y=(0,0)\) has

\[
-2\frac{V\cdot\delta\nabla p}{|V|^2}=\sqrt2,
\qquad
\sigma=\frac{2\sqrt2}{\pi},
\qquad
\gamma_E=\sqrt2\left(1-\frac8\pi\right)\ne0.
\tag{TL.583}
\]

This is a steady Euler field, so the \(\mathbb Q\)-mean of \(\gamma_E\) is
zero and the score has both signs.  Moreover \(\Delta v=-2v\), whence
\(\gamma_\nu=-4\nu\) is constant and its centered contribution vanishes.
Solenoidal Fourier packets localized around the same finite-mode
interactions give smooth decaying finite-energy countertests; the sign is
stable under sufficiently narrow localization.  The periodic formula is
used only to expose the exact local algebra, not as the primary fluid object.

Viscosity is also conditionally two-sided after normalization.  For a
subordinate two-annulus heat readout with \(k_1\ll k_2\) and low-frequency
critical fraction \(w\),

\[
\boxed{
w'=2\nu(k_2^2-k_1^2)w(1-w)>0.
}
\tag{TL.584}
\]

Thus the low-killing population has positive centered viscous score and the
high-killing population negative score.  Smooth decaying divergence-free
packets realize the same initial sign, and small amplitude makes the nonlinear
correction negligible.  The only unconditional viscous signs that survive
the complete contraction are the global mean
\(\mathbb E_{\mathbb Q}\gamma_\nu=-\nu\mathcal D/H\) and the finite
common-center Fisher sink.  Neither is a conditional no-refocusing law.

Therefore the remaining sign theorem, if true, must be genuinely causal and
history-dependent--for example record-stopped ancestry capacity with a
time-odd no-return mechanism.  No instantaneous conditional sign depending
only on the current complete geometry can close the record law.

## 50. The exact dual target is retained ancestry inflow

The regular-pretrace problem has a sharper dual form that discards inflow
which is dissipated or diluted before it can support a later record.  In this
section abbreviate the normalized variables of Section 45 by

\[
M:=\mathcal M_I^*,
\qquad
Q:=\mathcal Q_I^*,
\qquad
L:=2\nu\mathcal G_I^*.
\]

Then

\[
dM+\partial_\rho Q\,dt=-L\,dt-M\,dr.
\tag{TL.585}
\]

For every nonnegative regular test \(\varphi\) compactly supported in
\(0<\rho<\infty\), integration by parts gives

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\int_{(0,T]}\!\int\varphi M\,d\rho\,dr
+\int\varphi(T)M(T)\,d\rho
+\int_0^T\!\int\varphi L\,d\rho\,dt\\
&\qquad=
\int\varphi(0)M(0)\,d\rho
+\int_0^T\!\int
\bigl(M\partial_t\varphi+Q\partial_\rho\varphi\bigr)
\,d\rho\,dt .
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.586}
\]

On a regular moving union \(\Omega(t)\), put \(b=Q/M\) and
\(\ell=L/M\) where \(M>0\), using the weak formulation across its zero set.
For a causal record sampler \(0\le f\le1\), let \(\varphi_T\) solve the
killed backward adjoint

\[
\boxed{
-d\varphi_T
-b\partial_\rho\varphi_T\,dt
+\ell\varphi_T\,dt
+\varphi_T\,dr
=f\,dr,
\qquad
\varphi_T(T)=0.
}
\tag{TL.587}
\]

Along a radial characteristic, up to its first regular face,

\[
\varphi_T(t,\rho)
=\int_{(t,T]}f(s,\rho_s)
\exp\!\left[-\int_t^s\ell(q,\rho_q)dq-r(s)+r(t)\right]dr(s),
\]

so \(0\le\varphi_T\le1\).  Pairing (TL.585) with (TL.587) yields

\[
\boxed{
\int_{(0,T]}\!\int_{\Omega(t)}fM\,d\rho\,dr
=\int_{\Omega(0)}M(0)\varphi_T(0)\,d\rho
+\mathcal B_T(\varphi_T),
}
\tag{TL.588}
\]

where \(\mathcal B_T\) is the signed relative face current weighted by the
survival factor \(\varphi_T\).  Since \(\int M(0)d\rho=2c_I\),

\[
\int_{(0,T]}\!\int_{\Omega(t)}fM\,d\rho\,dr
\le2c_I+\int_0^T
\bigl[\mathcal J_{\partial\Omega}^{\varphi_T}(t)\bigr]_+dt.
\]

The minimal sufficient certificate is now explicit: construct one causal,
\(T\)-independent regular union and sampler for which

\[
\boxed{
\begin{gathered}
\int_{(0,T]}\!\int_{\Omega(t)}fM\,d\rho\,dr
\ge c_0\log\frac{H^*(T)}{H(0)}-C_{\rm occ},\\
\sup_{T<T_*}\int_0^T
\bigl[\mathcal J_{\partial\Omega}^{\varphi_T}(t)\bigr]_+dt
\le C_{\rm in}(u_0,\nu),
\end{gathered}
}
\tag{TL.589}
\]

with the second line derived from a datum-finite quantity containing no
\(H^*\), \(P_H\), positive critical variation or continuation norm.  Then

\[
\log\frac{H^*(T)}{H(0)}
\le\frac{2c_I+C_{\rm occ}+C_{\rm in}(u_0,\nu)}{c_0}.
\]

This retained-inflow target is smaller than bounding every positive face
current, because \(\varphi_T\) deletes everything that dies before serving a
record.  It is not yet a strict reduction of bounded \(H^*\), however.  The
obvious causal sampler proves the circularity exactly.  Let

\[
F(\rho,t)
:=\frac{\int_0^\rho\mathcal M_I(s,t)ds}{2c_IH(t)},
\qquad
f_\chi:=\chi(F),
\qquad
q_\chi:=\int_0^1\chi(p)dp>0,
\]

with \(\chi\in C_c^1((0,1))\), \(\chi\ge0\).  This is a regular
positive-quantile sampler and

\[
\int Mf_\chi d\rho
=2c_Iq_\chi\frac{H}{H^*},
\qquad
\int\!\int Mf_\chi d\rho\,dr
=2c_Iq_\chi\log\frac{H^*(T)}{H(0)}.
\]

But direct differentiation gives, with
\(Q_0=2c_IP_H\),
\(L_{\rm tot}=\int_0^\infty2\nu\mathcal G_I\,d\rho\), and
\(L_\chi=\int_0^\infty2\nu\mathcal G_I\chi(F)d\rho\),

\[
\boxed{
\int
\bigl(M\partial_tf_\chi+Q\partial_\rho f_\chi\bigr)d\rho
=\frac{q_\chi Q_0+L_\chi-q_\chi L_{\rm tot}}{H^*}.
}
\tag{TL.590}
\]

At \(dr\)-almost every record time the right side is

\[
\frac{L_\chi}{H^*}
+2c_Iq_\chi\frac{dr}{dt}.
\]

Thus every natural positive-occupancy quantile sampler retains the apex
production with exactly the coefficient of its record charge; taking
\(q_\chi=0\) destroys occupancy.  The backward adjoint moves that term into
surviving face inflow but does not pay it.  The genuine remaining theorem is
the construction of a different fixed ancestry sampler whose retained inflow
is controlled by an independent Navier--Stokes capacity.  Until that object
is built, (TL.589) is an exact closure certificate, not a proof of closure.

## 51. Critical size and strain alignment are two readouts of one material tensor

The isotropic critical height and the directional participation geometry do
not require two independently evolved norms.  Let

\[
(\mathscr U_t f)(a):=f(X(a,t)),
\qquad
L_C:=-\operatorname{div}_a(C^{-1}\nabla_a),
\qquad C=F^TF.
\]

Volume preservation makes \(\mathscr U_t\) unitary on \(L^2\), and the exact
conjugacy of the Eulerian Laplacian is

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr U_t(-\Delta_x)\mathscr U_t^{-1}=L_C,
\qquad
\mathscr U_t\Lambda_x\mathscr U_t^{-1}=L_C^{1/2}.
}
\tag{TL.591}
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
H(t)
=\frac12\langle U,L_C^{1/2}U\rangle_{L^2_a}
=\frac12\|L_C^{1/4}U\|_2^2.
}
\tag{TL.592}
\]

Thus Eulerian isotropy and material anisotropy are the same readout in two
coordinate systems.  The full tensor \(C^{-1}\) remains smooth through
repeated eigenvalues, so no chosen strain eigenvector, spectral projector or
eigenvalue-gap convention is needed.

Retain the complete all-chord tensor \(\mathsf T_u\) before taking its trace
and put

\[
\Theta=F^{-1}\mathsf T_u(X)F^{-T},
\qquad
A=C^{1/2}\Theta C^{1/2},
\qquad
B=C^{-1/2}\dot C\,C^{-1/2}.
\]

If \(F=\mathcal RC^{1/2}\) is the polar decomposition, then

\[
A=\mathcal R^T\mathsf T_u(X)\mathcal R,
\qquad
B=2\mathcal R^TS(X)\mathcal R.
\]

Hence \(A\) is positive semidefinite, \(B\) is trace free, and, with
\(\tau=\operatorname{tr}A\) and
\(A^\circ=A-\tfrac13\tau I\),

\[
\boxed{
4H=\int\tau\,da,
\qquad
P_H=-\frac12\int B:A^\circ\,da,
\qquad
\int_0^T\!\int|B|^2\,da\,dt
=\frac2\nu[\mathcal E(0)-\mathcal E(T)].
}
\tag{TL.593}
\]

This is the one-fluid form of the proposed two readouts.  The trace of \(A\)
records total critical concentration; its trace-free part records the
directional distribution of that same concentration; and the simultaneous
strain reads that distribution through the signed contraction with \(B\).

Normalize without selecting a sign:

\[
d\pi_t(a):=\frac{\tau(a,t)}{4H(t)}\,da,
\qquad
N(a,t):=
\begin{cases}
A^\circ/\tau,&\tau>0,\\
0,&\tau=0.
\end{cases}
\]

Since \(A\ge0\),

\[
|A^\circ|^2
=|A|^2-\frac13\tau^2
\le\frac23\tau^2,
\qquad |N|\le\sqrt{\frac23}.
\]

The complete critical balance is therefore

\[
\boxed{
\frac{H'}H+\nu\frac{\mathcal D}{H}
=-2\int B:N\,d\pi_t.
}
\tag{TL.594}
\]

On any interval \(J=[s,t]\) with \(H(t)=qH(s)\), this gives the exact signed
record law

\[
\boxed{
\log q+\nu\int_J\frac{\mathcal D}{H}\,dr
=-2\int_J\!\int B:N\,d\pi_r\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.595}
\]

There is now no separate anisotropic proof object and no mode-blind product
derivative.  Every record is the signed work of the finite-action material
metric against the normalized directional distribution of the same all-chord
critical content.

This formulation also isolates the concentration defect without any sign
selection.  Set

\[
\mathfrak a(J):=\int_J\!\int|B|^2\,da\,dr,
\qquad
\mathfrak c(J):=\int_J\!\int
\left(\frac{\tau}{4H}\right)^2 da\,dr.
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz and \(|N|^2\le2/3\) applied directly to (TL.595) give

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak a(J)\mathfrak c(J)
\ge
\frac38
\left(
\log q+\nu\int_J\frac{\mathcal D}{H}\,dr
\right)^2
\ge\frac38(\log q)^2.
}
\tag{TL.596}
\]

For \(N\) disjoint record intervals, (TL.593) and another
Cauchy--Schwarz inequality consequently imply

\[
\boxed{
\sum_{j=1}^N\mathfrak c(J_j)
\ge
\frac{3\nu(\log q)^2}{16\mathcal E_0}N^2.
}
\tag{TL.597}
\]

The same tensor density also carries the exact lifetime penalty.  The
all-chord square-function identity and Sobolev inequality give

\[
|\nabla\tau|^2\le4\tau g,
\qquad
\int g\,da=2\mathcal D,
\qquad
\|\tau\|_3\le C_F\mathcal D.
\]

Since \(d\pi_t=(\tau/(4H))da\) is a probability measure, interpolation
between \(L^1\) and \(L^3\) yields

\[
\boxed{
\mathfrak c(t)^{2/3}
\le\left\|\frac{\tau}{4H}\right\|_3
\le C_F\frac{\mathcal D}{H},
\qquad
\mathfrak c(t):=\int\left(\frac{\tau}{4H}\right)^2da.
}
\tag{TL.598}
\]

Combining (TL.595) with (TL.598) gives the joined throughput--lifetime
inequality

\[
\boxed{
\log q+\frac{\nu}{C_F}\int_J\mathfrak c(r)^{2/3}dr
\le
2\sqrt{\frac23}\int_J
\|B(r)\|_{L^2_a}\,\mathfrak c(r)^{1/2}dr.
}
\tag{TL.599}
\]

The right side is the actual strain-aligned delivery by the same material
history.  The second term on the left is the shortening lifetime of the same
concentrated all-chord content.  These are not competing mechanisms or two
norms: both are consequences of the one tensor law.  In particular, spatial
concentration cannot become arbitrarily sharp at fixed delivery rate; a
hypothetical terminal ladder must concentrate the delivery itself into
shorter temporal bursts while preserving the signed tensor alignment.

That alignment must not be discarded after deriving the concentration
necessity.  Define the signed coherence

\[
\zeta(a,t):=
\begin{cases}
-\dfrac{B:N}{|B|\,|N|},&|B|\,|N|>0,\\
0,&|B|\,|N|=0.
\end{cases}
\qquad -1\le\zeta\le1.
\]

Then (TL.595) is equivalently

\[
\boxed{
\log q+\nu\int_J\frac{\mathcal D}{H}\,dr
=2\int_J\!\int |B|\,|N|\,\zeta\,d\pi_r\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.600}
\]

Thus a terminal record sequence would require both increasing spatial
concentration of \(d\pi_t\) and recurrent positive signed coherence
\(\zeta\) between that concentrated directional tensor and the instantaneous
material strain.  Concentration without this coherence is harmless for
critical growth; tensor turning without the contraction is likewise
harmless.  The physical self-limitation question is whether the same
deformation that shortens the receiver lifetime can preserve this productive
coherence through an infinite succession.

Equations (TL.596)--(TL.600) therefore give two simultaneous necessary
signatures: the normalized trace density of the complete all-chord tensor
must acquire infinite spacetime \(L^2\) concentration while the same material
metric has finite quadratic action, and the bounded tensor \(N\) must keep
renewing its productive signed relation with that metric rate.

The completed-current finite layer from (TL.443c)--(TL.443d) must therefore
be constructed for this matrix-valued material readout: its scalar thickness
is \(d\pi_t\), while \(N\) and \(\zeta\) retain the directional work
relation.  It may close either by making its initial bound dominate
\(\sum_j\mathfrak c(J_j)\), or more sharply by bounding the retained signed
coherent influx in (TL.600) directly.  In either form, the internal current
must cancel before the datum is charged.  This is the exact point at which
the isotropic critical readout, strain geometry, finite material action and
non-recounting completed current meet.

## 52. The derivative tower is a centered scale governor, not a list of norms

The whole Sobolev family must not be read as independently evolving energy
accounts.  Proportional growth of two adjacent moments changes their size but
does not move the scale carried by the same field.  Actual refinement is the
relative change of adjacent moments.

For every real \(s\) for which the smooth history has the indicated moments,
put

\[
E_s(t):=\frac12\|\Lambda^s u(t)\|_2^2,
\qquad
P_s(t):=-\langle\Lambda^sB(u,u),\Lambda^su\rangle,
\]

where \(B(u,u)=\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)\) is only the Eulerian contraction
of the same joined participation tangent.  Then

\[
\boxed{
E_s'+2\nu E_{s+1}=P_s.
}
\tag{TL.601}
\]

The temporal derivative tower of the critical height and the spatial tower
are the same differentiated graph.  Repeatedly using (TL.601) gives, for
\(H=E_{1/2}\),

\[
\boxed{
H^{(n)}
=(-2\nu)^nE_{n+1/2}
+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}(-2\nu)^j
\frac{d^{\,n-1-j}}{dt^{\,n-1-j}}P_{j+1/2}.
}
\tag{TL.601a}
\]

Thus the highest spatial moment exposed by the \(n\)-th temporal derivative
is exactly \(E_{n+1/2}\); all other terms are differentiated branches of the
same participation graph.  For the pure heat history they vanish and

\[
(-1)^nH^{(n)}=(2\nu)^nE_{n+1/2}.
\]

For the full history the relation is triangular, not diagonal, but no spatial
rung has been lost.  The embeddings and interpolation

\[
\boxed{
\|\nabla^n u\|_3^2\lesssim E_{n+1/2},
\qquad
E_{n+1}^2\le E_{n+1/2}E_{n+3/2}
}
\tag{TL.601b}
\]

show why increasing temporal depth carries increasing spatial and amplitude
resolution.  The issue is therefore the organization of the complete family
of participation terms in (TL.601a), not a missing relation between time and
space derivatives.

Define the effective squared frequency and normalized signed participation
readout

\[
\kappa_s^2:=\frac{E_{s+1}}{E_s},
\qquad
\Gamma_s:=\frac{P_s}{E_s}.
\]

Dividing two adjacent copies of (TL.601) and subtracting gives

\[
\boxed{
(\log\kappa_s^2)'
+2\nu(\kappa_{s+1}^2-\kappa_s^2)
=\Gamma_{s+1}-\Gamma_s.
}
\tag{TL.602}
\]

This is the centered tower law.  Every component of the joined evolution
which amplifies adjacent rungs proportionally cancels.  Only a mismatch of
the same signed participation under the two native moment weights can move
the effective frequency.

The viscous term in (TL.602) is not a formal difference of two losses.  Moment
log-convexity gives the exact nonnegative covariance

\[
\boxed{
\kappa_{s+1}^2-\kappa_s^2
=\frac{E_sE_{s+2}-E_{s+1}^2}{E_sE_{s+1}}
\ge0.
}
\tag{TL.603}
\]

Indeed, for the probability measure

\[
d\mu_s(\xi)
:=\frac{|\xi|^{2s}|\widehat u(\xi)|^2}{2E_s}\,d\xi,
\]

one has

\[
\kappa_s^2=\mathbb E_{\mu_s}|\xi|^2,
\qquad
\kappa_{s+1}^2-\kappa_s^2
=\frac{\operatorname{Var}_{\mu_s}(|\xi|^2)}{\kappa_s^2}.
\]

Thus viscosity supplies a strict scale-shape penalty whenever the current
moment population has nonzero frequency breadth.  Equality means that this
particular readout is supported on one frequency sphere; it does not mean
that the full fluid is undeformed or stationary.

The right side of (TL.602) is equally exact:

\[
\boxed{
\Gamma_{s+1}-\Gamma_s
=\frac{E_sP_{s+1}-E_{s+1}P_s}{E_sE_{s+1}}.
}
\tag{TL.604}
\]

It is a signed centered current, not a positive selection.  Where a radial
transfer density is divided by the current energy density for interpretation,
(TL.604) is the covariance of that relative transfer with \(|\xi|^2\), divided
by \(\kappa_s^2\).  The determinant form (TL.604), which makes no such
division, is the native statement.

Consequently, on any interval \(J=[a,b]\) for which
\(\kappa_s^2(b)=q\kappa_s^2(a)\),

\[
\boxed{
\log q
+2\nu\int_J(\kappa_{s+1}^2-\kappa_s^2)\,dt
=\int_J(\Gamma_{s+1}-\Gamma_s)\,dt.
}
\tag{TL.605}
\]

This is the continuous scale-accessibility law lying underneath any dyadic
sampler.  It says that finer scale becomes dynamically occupied only through
a scale-selective signed reorganization of the complete participation
history; common amplification cannot do it.

There is no separate shell-ancestry bridge between this law and the critical
height.  Since \(H=E_{1/2}\), moment interpolation gives

\[
\boxed{
H^2\le E_0E_1,
\qquad
\kappa_0^2=\frac{E_1}{E_0}
\ge\left(\frac{H}{E_0}\right)^2.
}
\tag{TL.606}
\]

The kinetic energy \(E_0\) is nonincreasing.  Hence unbounded \(H\) in this
one history forces unbounded \(\kappa_0\) in that same history.  Named Fourier
packets and independently tracked vortex labels are unnecessary for this
implication.

Summing (TL.602) through derivative depth makes the internal cancellation
literal.  For every integer \(M\ge1\),

\[
\boxed{
\frac d{dt}\log\frac{E_{s+M}}{E_s}
+2\nu(\kappa_{s+M}^2-\kappa_s^2)
=\Gamma_{s+M}-\Gamma_s.
}
\tag{TL.607}
\]

All intermediate normalized productions and all intermediate covariance
gaps telescope.  Therefore the tower is one through-current: it forbids
counting each derivative rung as a new source, and it locates the only lawful
forward transfer at the changing boundary between the currently resolved
moments and the still finer continuation of the same field.

### 52.1 The finite viscous-lifetime realization of the tower

The positive moment generating polynomial

\[
\mathfrak E_M(z,t)
:=\sum_{m=0}^M\frac{z^m}{m!}E_{m+1/2}(t)
\]

obeys the exact finite identity

\[
\boxed{
(\partial_t+2\nu\partial_z)\mathfrak E_M
=\mathfrak P_M
-2\nu\frac{z^M}{M!}E_{M+3/2},
\qquad
\mathfrak P_M:=\sum_{m=0}^M\frac{z^m}{m!}P_{m+1/2}.
}
\tag{TL.608}
\]

The top remainder is favourable for \(z\ge0\), but an infinite positive-\(z\)
stock presupposes the corresponding analytic radius.  It is therefore not a
datum-finite reserve for arbitrary smooth data.

The lawful finite-datum realization is the heat-survival transform of the
same tower.  For \(\sigma>0\), set

\[
S_\sigma:=e^{-\sigma\Lambda^2/2},
\qquad
\mathscr H(\sigma,t)
:=\frac12\|\Lambda^{1/2}S_\sigma u(t)\|_2^2.
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr H(\sigma,t)
=\int_0^\infty k e^{-\sigma k^2}e(k,t)\,dk,
\qquad
(-\partial_\sigma)^m\mathscr H(0,t)=E_{m+1/2}(t).
}
\tag{TL.609}
\]

The alternating moment series is the Taylor representation of this object
only on a radius where that series converges; the semigroup expression is the
global definition and requires no analytic datum.  In particular,

\[
\boxed{
0\le\mathscr H(\sigma,t)
\le \frac{1}{\sqrt{2e\,\sigma}}E_0(t)
\le \frac{1}{\sqrt{2e\,\sigma}}E_0(0).
}
\tag{TL.610}
\]

Define the complete heat-surviving signed throughput

\[
\mathscr P(\sigma,t)
:=-\langle
\Lambda^{1/2}S_\sigma u,
\Lambda^{1/2}S_\sigma B(u,u)
\rangle.
\]

Direct differentiation, with no separation of the fluid evolution into
independent mechanisms, gives

\[
\boxed{
(\partial_t-2\nu\partial_\sigma)\mathscr H
=\mathscr P.
}
\tag{TL.611}
\]

Along the actual remaining viscous lifetime

\[
\sigma_T(t)=2\nu(T-t),
\]

the whole pure-successor tower cancels and

\[
\boxed{
H(T)
=\mathscr H(2\nu(T-s),s)
+\int_s^T\mathscr P(2\nu(T-t),t)\,dt.
}
\tag{TL.612}
\]

Thus \(\mathscr P\) does not count activity merely created and then erased
before \(T\).  It is the signed portion of the complete participation
throughput which survives the remaining fixed-viscosity continuation into
the terminal critical readout.

If \(H(T)=qH(s)\), semigroup contraction gives the exact net requirement

\[
\boxed{
\frac1{H(T)}
\int_s^T\mathscr P(2\nu(T-t),t)\,dt
\ge1-\frac1q.
}
\tag{TL.613}
\]

No positive part and no selected pair cohort appears.  Every temporal profile
that completes the same multiplicative rise is included.

The same law is a completed scale current.  With \(T(k,t)\) and \(\Pi(k,t)\)
from (TL.3)--(TL.4),

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathscr P(\sigma,t)
&=\int_0^\infty k e^{-\sigma k^2}T(k,t)\,dk\\
&=\int_0^\infty e^{-\sigma k^2}(1-2\sigma k^2)\Pi(k,t)\,dk.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.614}
\]

The sign change in the completed weight is essential: lower-scale outflow,
higher-scale inflow and the intervening redistribution remain one signed
current.  At \(\sigma=0\), (TL.614) reduces to

\[
\mathscr P(0,t)=P_H(t)
=-\frac12\int B:A^\circ\,da,
\]

so the base of the lifetime tower is exactly the material tensor law
(TL.593), not a second proof object.

For \(\sigma>0\), the source contains both the strain contraction of the
heat-resolved all-chord tensor and the exact commutator between heat
resolution and material transport.  Suppressing that commutator would again
split the one participation graph.  Equations (TL.602), (TL.611) and
(TL.614) therefore give three inseparable readings of one event:

\[
\begin{array}{ccl}
\Gamma_{s+1}-\Gamma_s
&:&\text{which adjacent moments are being strengthened relatively},\\
\mathscr P(2\nu(T-t),t)
&:&\text{which part of that strengthening survives its remaining lifetime},\\
-\frac12B:A^\circ
&:&\text{the signed material strain--incidence geometry at the critical face}.
\end{array}
\]

This is the merged governor suggested by the full tower.  A carrier-level
completion must retain all three readings in one matrix-valued completed
current: internal derivative transfer cancels by (TL.607), pure viscous
successors cancel along (TL.611), the initial heat face is datum-finite by
(TL.610), and every completed critical rise has the surviving signed floor
(TL.613).  The remaining construction is therefore not another scalar norm
or a per-rung action name; it is the all-field tensor current whose trace is
(TL.611) and whose trace-free material contraction retains the coherence in
(TL.600) throughout the heat-lifetime characteristic.

## 53. The heat-surviving tower has an exact same-label participation carrier

The throughput \(\mathscr P\) in (TL.611) need not remain an Eulerian scalar.
Fix its terminal time \(T\), write

\[
\tau(t):=\nu(T-t),
\qquad
G_\tau:=e^{\tau\Delta},
\qquad
v_T(t):=G_{\tau(t)}u(t),
\qquad
p_T(t):=G_{\tau(t)}p(t),
\]

and define the same-field heat covariance

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal R_T
:=G_\tau(u\otimes u)-v_T\otimes v_T.
}
\tag{TL.615}
\]

This is not an independently evolved subgrid model.  It is the covariance of
the actual velocity under one Gaussian all-field averaging.  For every vector
\(c\),

\[
c^T\mathcal R_Tc
=G_\tau[(c\cdot u)^2]-[G_\tau(c\cdot u)]^2\ge0,
\]

and the exact carré-du-champ representation is

\[
\boxed{
(\mathcal R_T)_{ij}
=2\int_0^\tau
G_{\tau-r}
\bigl(\nabla G_ru_i\cdot\nabla G_ru_j\bigr)\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.616}
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal R_T\ge0,
\qquad
\int\operatorname{tr}\mathcal R_T\,dx
=\|u\|_2^2-\|v_T\|_2^2
\le2E_0(0).
}
\tag{TL.617}
\]

At fixed physical time its scale evolution is also exact:

\[
\boxed{
\partial_\tau\mathcal R_T-\Delta\mathcal R_T
=2\sum_{k=1}^3
(\partial_kv_T)\otimes(\partial_kv_T),
\qquad
\mathcal R_T|_{\tau=0}=0.
}
\tag{TL.618}
\]

Thus the tower's scale layer is matrix positive before its critical trace or
directional contraction is taken.

Because \(\tau'=-\nu\), differentiating \(v_T=G_\tau u\) along this same
terminal heat characteristic cancels the pure viscous successor and gives

\[
\boxed{
\partial_tv_T
+\nabla\cdot(v_T\otimes v_T+\mathcal R_T)
+\nabla p_T=0,
\qquad
\nabla\cdot v_T=0.
}
\tag{TL.619}
\]

Equation (TL.619) does not remove viscosity from the fluid.  The fixed
viscosity is exactly what determines the decreasing covariance depth
\(\tau(t)=\nu(T-t)\); (TL.619) is the one-history equation seen along that
viscous-lifetime coordinate.

Now return it to the actual material labels.  The particles continue to obey
\(\dot X=u(X,t)\), not \(v_T\).  For every pair \((a,b)\), set

\[
R_{ab}=X_a-X_b,
\qquad
V_{ab}=u(X_a)-u(X_b),
\qquad
W^T_{ab}=v_T(X_a)-v_T(X_b).
\]

Then

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\dot R_{ab}&=V_{ab},\\
\dot W^T_{ab}
&=\delta_{ab}\!\left[
(u-v_T)\cdot\nabla v_T
-\nabla p_T
-\nabla\cdot\mathcal R_T
\right].
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.620}
\]

Equivalently, the second line is

\[
\dot W^T_{ab}
=-\delta_{ab}\nabla p_T
-\delta_{ab}
\bigl[G_\tau(u\cdot\nabla u)-u\cdot\nabla G_\tau u\bigr].
\]

The bracket is the exact heat--material commutator.  It contains the
scale-changing transport which would be lost if the heat-resolved field were
treated as a second fluid.

Define the terminal-surviving critical pair density on those same labels by

\[
e^T_{ab}(t)
:=\frac{|W^T_{ab}(t)|^2}{4\pi^2|R_{ab}(t)|^4}.
\]

Its complete material derivative is

\[
\boxed{
\frac d{dt}\log e^T_{ab}
=-4\alpha_{ab}+2\chi^T_{ab},
\quad
\alpha_{ab}:=\frac{R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab}}{|R_{ab}|^2},
\quad
\chi^T_{ab}:=
\frac{W^T_{ab}\cdot\dot W^T_{ab}}{|W^T_{ab}|^2}.
}
\tag{TL.621}
\]

Here \(-4\alpha\) is the present chord-averaged strain of the actual fluid,
while \(2\chi^T\) contains its simultaneous pressure, covariance and
heat--transport response.  Neither row exists independently of the other.

Volume preservation and the fractional pair identity now give

\[
\boxed{
\iint e^T_{ab}(t)\,da\,db
=\frac12\|\Lambda^{1/2}v_T(t)\|_2^2
=\mathscr H(2\nu(T-t),t),
}
\tag{TL.622}
\]

and therefore

\[
\boxed{
\mathscr P(2\nu(T-t),t)
=\iint
(-4\alpha_{ab}+2\chi^T_{ab})e^T_{ab}\,da\,db.
}
\tag{TL.623}
\]

This proves the carrier bridge that (TL.611) alone did not display.  The
surviving tower throughput is the full signed participation rate of all
actual material pairs, with no selected cohort, evaluated through the one
heat-resolved relative velocity that can still reach \(T\).

The same construction has a source-free finite-energy row.  Put

\[
\overline e_T
:=G_\tau\frac{|u|^2}{2}
=\frac{|v_T|^2}{2}+\frac12\operatorname{tr}\mathcal R_T,
\qquad
\overline J_T
:=G_\tau\left[\left(\frac{|u|^2}{2}+p\right)u\right].
\]

Applying the terminal heat characteristic to the exact kinetic-energy law
gives

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\overline e_T
+\nabla\cdot\overline J_T
=-\nu G_\tau|\nabla u|^2.
}
\tag{TL.624}
\]

Thus the covariance depth, the resolved velocity, the pressure current and
the irreversible loss are already one leaky receiver.  Equations
(TL.618)--(TL.624) identify the concrete matrix layer which the
non-recounting proof must carry through the critical fractional trace: its
total unweighted stock is datum-finite, its scale production is positive,
its physical-time law is source free after the current is completed, and its
critical terminal marginal is exactly the signed same-label throughput in
(TL.623).  No placeholder action has been introduced.

## 54. The surrounding rows already pay the height; the tower must prevent temporal concentration

The direction of the interpolation is from the already controlled surrounding
rows toward the critical height.  With

\[
E_s(t):=\frac12\|\Lambda^su(t)\|_2^2,
\qquad
H(t):=E_{1/2}(t),
\]

Fourier Cauchy--Schwarz gives

\[
\boxed{
H(t)^2\le E_0(t)E_1(t).
}
\tag{TL.625}
\]

The ordinary energy identity therefore pays the complete temporal mass of the
critical height:

\[
E_0(t)+2\nu\int_0^tE_1(r)\,dr=E_0(0),
\]

and hence, on every interval of smooth existence,

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T H(t)^2\,dt
\le \frac{E_0(0)^2}{2\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.626}
\]

Thus the unresolved behavior is not unlimited total critical height.  It is
concentration of that already finite temporal mass into successively narrower
time intervals.  A time-regularity row closes this gap from above.  Indeed, if

\[
B_0:=\int_0^T H^2\,dt,
\qquad
B_1:=\int_0^T|H'|^2\,dt,
\]

then choosing \(s\in[0,T]\) with \(H(s)^2\le B_0/T\) and integrating
\((H^2)'=2HH'\) gives

\[
\boxed{
\|H\|_{L^\infty(0,T)}^2
\le \frac{B_0}{T}+2\sqrt{B_0B_1}.
}
\tag{TL.627}
\]

Consequently the tower is not being asked to produce another magnitude budget
for \(H\).  The lower spatial row and the first dissipative row have already
produced (TL.626).  The tower's remaining job is to place a datum-finite
temporal modulus on the same critical readout.  The convenient sufficient form
is \(H'\in L_t^2\), although (TL.627) can be replaced by any datum-uniform
time modulus strong enough to prevent narrowing spikes.

The covariance carrier of Section 53 makes this reverse architecture exact.
At an arbitrary heat depth \(\tau>0\), rather than only on a terminal
characteristic, put

\[
v_\tau:=G_\tau u,
\qquad
\mathcal R_\tau:=G_\tau(u\otimes u)-v_\tau\otimes v_\tau,
\]

and define its unresolved kinetic stock, scale density, scale loss and joined
transfer by

\[
\begin{aligned}
K(\tau,t)
&:=\frac12\int\operatorname{tr}\mathcal R_\tau\,dx
=E_0(t)-\frac12\|v_\tau(t)\|_2^2,\\
Y(\tau,t)&:=\partial_\tau K(\tau,t)
=\|\nabla v_\tau(t)\|_2^2,\\
L(\tau,t)&:=Y(0,t)-Y(\tau,t)
=\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2-\|\nabla v_\tau(t)\|_2^2\ge0,\\
\Phi(\tau,t)&:=\int\mathcal R_\tau:S(v_\tau)\,dx.
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.628}
\]

Here \(K\) is not a new fluid energy.  It is the exact part of the kinetic
energy of \(u(t)\) removed by the same-field heat observation at depth
\(\tau\).  Applying \(\partial_t-\nu\partial_\tau\) to the resolved equation
and only then taking the whole-field energy trace gives

\[
(\partial_t-\nu\partial_\tau)
\frac12\|v_\tau\|_2^2=\Phi.
\]

Together with \(E_0'=-\nu\|\nabla u\|_2^2\), this yields the fixed-scale signed
receiver law

\[
\boxed{
\partial_tK(\tau,t)+\Phi(\tau,t)+\nu L(\tau,t)=0.
}
\tag{TL.629}
\]

This is the exact one-time stock plus incoming throughput minus outgoing
throughput minus irreversible loss relation.  No positive part is selected:
the sign of \(\Phi\) itself distinguishes which way the complete transfer is
running.  Pressure remains in the complete resolved spatial current and
cancels in (TL.629) only after the divergence-free whole-field trace; it has
not been removed from the VPI evolution.

The scale density is positive and has datum-finite total mass:

\[
\boxed{
Y\ge0,
\qquad
-\partial_\tau Y
=2\|\Delta G_\tau u\|_2^2\ge0,
\qquad
\int_0^\infty Y(\tau,t)\,d\tau=E_0(t)\le E_0(0).
}
\tag{TL.630}
\]

Moreover \(\partial_\tau L=-\partial_\tau Y\ge0\).  Differentiating
(TL.629) in scale therefore produces the completed scale-current law

\[
\boxed{
\partial_tY+\partial_\tau J=0,
\qquad
J(\tau,t):=\Phi(\tau,t)+\nu L(\tau,t).
}
\tag{TL.631}
\]

For every \(0<a<b<\infty\),

\[
\boxed{
\int_a^bY(\tau,t)\,d\tau
+\int_s^tJ(b,r)\,dr
=
\int_a^bY(\tau,s)\,d\tau
+\int_s^tJ(a,r)\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.632}
\]

Thus adjacent scale bands share one boundary current.  A transfer leaving one
band is the same transfer entering its neighbor and cancels when the bands are
