joined; it cannot be registered again as a fresh source.  The finite layer
whose thickness was sought in the no-Zeno picture is precisely the positive
scale density \(Y(\tau,t)d\tau\), whose total mass is the one kinetic energy
of the initial datum.

The critical height is the negative half-moment of this finite layer.  The
equivalent exact trace formulas are

\[
\boxed{
H(t)
=\frac1{2\sqrt{2\pi}}
\int_0^\infty\tau^{-3/2}K(\tau,t)\,d\tau
=\frac1{\sqrt{2\pi}}
\int_0^\infty\tau^{-1/2}Y(\tau,t)\,d\tau.
}
\tag{TL.633}
\]

The same trace sends the two terms in the completed current to the ordinary
critical participation and dissipation rows:

\[
\boxed{
P_H(t)
=-\frac1{2\sqrt{2\pi}}
\int_0^\infty\tau^{-3/2}\Phi(\tau,t)\,d\tau,
\qquad
\mathcal D(t)
=\frac1{2\sqrt{2\pi}}
\int_0^\infty\tau^{-3/2}L(\tau,t)\,d\tau.
}
\tag{TL.634}
\]

Accordingly (TL.629) traces to

\[
H'-P_H+\nu\mathcal D=0,
\]

while the complete tower current itself gives

\[
\boxed{
H'(t)
=-\frac1{2\sqrt{2\pi}}
\int_0^\infty\tau^{-3/2}J(\tau,t)\,d\tau.
}
\tag{TL.635}
\]

This identifies the temporal anti-spike quantity without adding a proxy or
splitting the evolution.  A datum-finite bound for the \(L_t^2\) norm of the
right-hand side of (TL.635) would combine with the already paid (TL.626)
through (TL.627), bound \(H\), and continue the same solution.  It is a
sufficient estimate, but it is not the sharp governor: it would square every
direction of a signed current before using the scale cancellation in
(TL.632).  Section 56 derives the smaller state-aligned signed lift already
anticipated by the repo's source-square/no-waste work.  The proof direction
is therefore fixed without promoting the full current square to the central
theorem:

\[
\boxed{
\text{finite lower--upper spatial interpolation}
+\text{completed-tower temporal regularity}
\Longrightarrow
\text{bounded intermediate critical height}.
}
\tag{TL.636}
\]

## 55. Differentiating the finite layer removes cutoff recounting and exposes the exact viscous lifetime

The cumulative stock \(K(\tau,t)\) records everything removed by heat
resolution at depth \(\tau\), and \(Y=\partial_\tau K\) is already a positive
finite layer.  To localize that layer around the physical length
\(\sqrt{\tau}\), differentiate once more in heat depth and retain the Jacobian
of logarithmic scale.  Define

\[
\boxed{
m(\tau,t):=-\tau\partial_\tau Y(\tau,t)
=2\tau\|\Delta G_\tau u(t)\|_2^2\ge0,
\qquad
\rho(\sigma,t):=\tau m(\tau,t),
\quad \sigma:=\log\tau.
}
\tag{TL.637}
\]

Here \(m\,d\tau=\rho\,d\sigma\) is not a selected shell population.  It is a
positive resolution of the kinetic energy of the same velocity field over all
heat depths.  Integration by parts in \(\tau\), with
\(\tau Y(\tau,t)\to0\) at both endpoints, gives

\[
\boxed{
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\rho(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma
=\int_0^\infty m(\tau,t)\,d\tau
=E_0(t)\le E_0(0).
}
\tag{TL.638}
\]

The critical height is the corresponding negative half-moment:

\[
\boxed{
H(t)
=\sqrt{\frac2\pi}
\int_0^\infty\tau^{-1/2}m(\tau,t)\,d\tau
=\sqrt{\frac2\pi}
\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}e^{-\sigma/2}\rho(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma.
}
\tag{TL.639}
\]

Differentiate the completed current law (TL.631) in heat depth.  The current
carried by the localized layer is

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal J(\tau,t)
:=J(\tau,t)-\tau\partial_\tau J(\tau,t),
}
\tag{TL.640}
\]

and it obeys either of the equivalent conservation laws

\[
\boxed{
\partial_tm+\partial_\tau\mathcal J=0,
\qquad
\partial_t\rho+\partial_\sigma\mathcal J=0.
}
\tag{TL.641}
\]

Consequently every finite logarithmic scale interval is an exact leaky
receiver:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\int_{\sigma_a}^{\sigma_b}\rho(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma
&+\int_s^t\mathcal J(e^{\sigma_b},r)\,dr\\
={}&
\int_{\sigma_a}^{\sigma_b}\rho(\sigma,s)\,d\sigma
+\int_s^t\mathcal J(e^{\sigma_a},r)\,dr .
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.642}
\]

The current at a common boundary occurs once with each sign when adjacent
intervals are joined.  Thus (TL.642) is the exact outgoing equals incoming
plus one-time stock relation before any record, sign selection or scale
normalization is imposed.

The irreversible part of this current is explicit.  With the spectral energy
density \(e(k,t)\), transfer density \(T(k,t)\), and forward flux
\(\Pi(k,t)=\int_k^\infty T(q,t)\,dq\), equations (TL.628)--(TL.631) give

\[
\Phi(\tau,t)
=\int_0^\infty e^{-2\tau k^2}T(k,t)\,dk,
\]

and therefore

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal J(\tau,t)
={}&\mathcal J_{\rm VPI}(\tau,t)
+\mathcal J_\nu(\tau,t),\\
\mathcal J_{\rm VPI}(\tau,t)
={}&-8\tau^2\int_0^\infty
k^3e^{-2\tau k^2}\Pi(k,t)\,dk,\\
\mathcal J_\nu(\tau,t)
={}&2\nu\int_0^\infty k^2
\left[1-(1+2\tau k^2)e^{-2\tau k^2}\right]e(k,t)\,dk
\ge0 .
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.643}
\]

This split is an algebraic reading of one completed current, not two
independent mechanisms.  The first line retains the actual signed transfer of
the full incompressible evolution.  The second is the simultaneous
irreversible part, and its sign follows from

\[
e^{2x}\ge1+2x,\qquad x\ge0.
\]

For one spectral contribution with \(x=\tau k^2\), its logarithmic-layer
stock and irreversible current have the exact ratio

\[
\boxed{
\frac{d\mathcal J_\nu}{d\rho}
=\frac{\nu}{\tau}\,
\frac{e^{2x}-1-2x}{2x}.
}
\tag{TL.644}
\]

On every genuine heat band \(x\in[x_-,x_+]\Subset(0,\infty)\), the last factor
has a strictly positive lower bound depending only on the fixed band.  Hence
the irreversible emptying time of energy actually situated at heat depth
\(\tau\) is at most a fixed multiple of

\[
\boxed{
t_\nu(\tau)=\frac{\tau}{\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.645}
\]

The critical trace is also preserved by this localization.  Using
\(\mathcal J=J-\tau J_\tau\) and the endpoint behavior of \(J\),

\[
\boxed{
H'(t)
=-\frac1{\sqrt{2\pi}}
\int_0^\infty\tau^{-3/2}\mathcal J(\tau,t)\,d\tau .
}
\tag{TL.646}
\]

Equations (TL.638), (TL.642), (TL.643) and (TL.645) are the carrier-native
throughput--lifetime law in localized form:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\text{outgoing VPI throughput}
\ \le\;&
\text{actual incoming VPI throughput}
+\text{one-time band stock}\\
&-\text{irreversible loss accumulated while that stock is present}.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.647}
\]

No energy is created at a finer scale, no internal boundary crossing is
recounted, and the total stock present across every logarithmic scale is
bounded by the initial kinetic energy.  A pointwise crossing-speed estimate is
not required.  The signed current must first be contracted with the critical
stock actually occupying the scale interval.  That contraction simultaneously
registers residence, rapid transit, and the change of the occupied scale
profile, while preserving the cancellation of every shared face.  This is the
signed lifted source-square law derived next.

## 56. The repo's signed lifted source-square law is the temporal governor of the completed scale current

The earlier source-square construction already found the correct operation,
although it expressed it in packet-specific coordinates.  Sections 123--126
of
`mpp-forward-gold-tfe2748-strict-storage-compactness-rigidity-attack-20260701.md`
derive a signed lifted transfer, perform summation by parts across its moving
interfaces, and identify its no-self-financing form.  The carrier-native
content of that construction is recovered directly from (TL.7), (TL.44b),
and (TL.641); none of its packet-specific selection apparatus is needed here.

There is first an exact continuous source-square identity.  Multiply the
kinetic scale law

\[
\partial_te+\partial_k\Pi=-2\nu k^2e
\]

by the critical stock density \(ke\), without changing its sign.  Product
differentiation gives

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\!\left(\frac12ke^2\right)
+2\nu k^3e^2
+\partial_k(ke\Pi)
=\partial_k(ke)\Pi .
}
\tag{TL.648}
\]

The four terms have fixed physical meanings on the same scale history:

1. \(ke^2/2\) is stored scale density paired with itself;
2. \(2\nu k^3e^2\) is irreversible residence at that occupied scale;
3. \(ke\Pi\) is the signed boundary carriage of that stock; and
4. \(\partial_k(ke)\Pi\) is the signed work of the current against the
   actually changing critical-scale profile.

Thus the full square of \(\Pi\), \(J\), or \(\mathcal J\) is not the native
positive quantity.  The positive quantity is the simultaneous residence of
the stock being carried; the nonlinear current remains signed until it is
paired with the gradient of that same stock.

The finite-band version makes non-recounting explicit.  Fix
\(K_{j+1}=2K_j\) and define, from the one spectral measure in (TL.2),

\[
E_j:=\int_{K_j}^{K_{j+1}}e(k,t)\,dk,
\qquad
A_j:=K_jE_j,
\qquad
L_j:=2\nu\int_{K_j}^{K_{j+1}}k^2e(k,t)\,dk,
\qquad
\Pi_j:=\Pi(K_j,t).
\tag{TL.649}
\]

Here \(A_j\) is the critical amount carried by the band, up to the fixed
factor forced by \(K_j\le k\le2K_j\).  Integrating (TL.7) over the band gives

\[
\boxed{
E_j'+L_j=\Pi_j-\Pi_{j+1}.
}
\tag{TL.650}
\]

Multiplication by the same band's \(A_j\), followed by summation over
\(J\le j\le N\), gives the exact lifted identity

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf S_{J,N}'+\mathsf R_{J,N}=\mathsf W_{J,N},
}
\tag{TL.651}
\]

where

\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathsf S_{J,N}
&:=\frac12\sum_{j=J}^{N}K_jE_j^2
=\frac12\sum_{j=J}^{N}\frac{A_j^2}{K_j},\\
\mathsf R_{J,N}
&:=\sum_{j=J}^{N}A_jL_j
\ge2\nu\sum_{j=J}^{N}K_jA_j^2,\\
\mathsf W_{J,N}
&:=\sum_{j=J}^{N}A_j(\Pi_j-\Pi_{j+1}).
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.652}
\]

The lower bound in \(\mathsf R_{J,N}\) is the scale-dependent lifetime
charge.  It becomes stronger with \(K_j\), but only while the same critical
stock \(A_j\) is actually present.  It therefore neither charges an empty
fine scale nor invents a separately evolving dissipative history.

Summation by parts exposes the no-recounting structure of the right side:

\[
\boxed{
\mathsf W_{J,N}
=A_J\Pi_J-A_N\Pi_{N+1}
+\sum_{j=J+1}^{N}(A_j-A_{j-1})\Pi_j.
}
\tag{TL.653}
\]

Every interior \(\Pi_j\) first occurs with both signs.  It survives only
through its signed correlation with the actual change
\(A_j-A_{j-1}\) of occupied critical stock across that interface.  A current
that crosses several interfaces is therefore not billed several times; a
current through a scale carrying no critical stock is not billed at all; and
back-and-forth carriage retains its sign rather than becoming two positive
events.  The only uncompensated terms are the true entrance, true exit,
stored-state change, and irreversible residence of this one history.

The residence term controls the ultraviolet critical tail in exactly the
weight needed for temporal thickness.  Since

\[
\sum_{j\ge J}A_j
\le H_{>K_J}
\le2\sum_{j\ge J}A_j,
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz and \(\sum_{j\ge J}K_j^{-1}=2K_J^{-1}\) give

\[
\boxed{
H_{>K_J}(t)^2
\le\frac8{K_J}
\sum_{j\ge J}K_jA_j(t)^2
\le\frac4{\nu K_J}\,\mathsf R_{J,\infty}(t).
}
\tag{TL.654}
\]

Thus a critical tail cannot remain large at a fine scale without generating
source-square residence.  If it changes too quickly to reside, (TL.651)
forces the same amount into the signed lifted transit \(\mathsf W\); and
(TL.653) follows that transit through shared interfaces without recounting it.
This is the exact residence--or--transit alternative that the raw
\(L_t^2\)-current trace obscures.

The heat-layer and frequency-band statements are two resolutions of the same
positive spectral measure, not two fluid objects.  Indeed (TL.637) has the
explicit representation

\[
\boxed{
\rho(\log\tau,t)
=4\tau^2\int_0^\infty
k^4e^{-2\tau k^2}e(k,t)\,dk.
}
\tag{TL.655}
\]

Consequently \(\tau\simeq K_j^{-2}\) reads the same occupied scale as the
band in (TL.649), while (TL.643) reads the same signed transfer through its
heat kernel.  Equations (TL.648)--(TL.654) are therefore the state-aligned
source-square contraction of the completed current
\(\rho_t+\partial_\sigma\mathcal J=0\), not a replacement for it.

This identification also joins several results which previously appeared as
separate obstructions.  Fixed-strength fine growth already forces migration
of a viscosity-sized critical population by (TL.79); its scale--time
occupancy is datum-finite by (TL.81)--(TL.84); its ultraviolet ordinary-time
tail vanishes by (TL.89); diffuse octave proliferation is exponentially thin
by (TL.101)--(TL.102); nonvanishing common delivery has datum-finite
square-root path length by (TL.519); and the killed adjoint (TL.587)--(TL.590)
retains only inflow that survives to serve a later record.  The signed lift
is the common algebraic carrier of these facts: residence is read by
\(\mathsf R\), actual successor transit by \(\mathsf W\), and dead or
reversed carriage cancels before either is turned into a record charge.

The temporal continuation argument should therefore not demand

\[
\int_0^{T_*}\left|
\int_0^\infty\tau^{-3/2}J(\tau,t)\,d\tau
\right|^2dt<\infty
\]

as its central theorem.  That estimate remains sufficient, but it prices
every current direction.  The carrier-faithful governor is the signed
record-stopped consequence of (TL.651)--(TL.653): positive record growth must
be supplied either by new entrance through the one outer boundary or by
stored critical stock, while every occupied fine-scale residence is charged
by \(\mathsf R\) and every internal transit is carried forward once by
\(\mathsf W\).  This is precisely the source-square/no-self-financing
architecture already present in the repo, now placed on the completed global
scale current rather than on an auxiliary packet history.

## 57. The repo's uniform multiplier theorem governs the completed current at every heat depth

The completed current in (TL.643) is not an arbitrary signed function of
heat depth and time.  The uniform multiplier estimate proved in Cycle 159
applies to it directly and couples its two signed rows before any temporal
norm is taken.

For \(\tau>0\), define the nondecreasing multiplier

\[
\boxed{
q_\tau(k)
:=1-(1+2\tau k^2)e^{-2\tau k^2}.
}
\tag{TL.656}
\]

It obeys

\[
q_\tau(k)\ge0,
\qquad
\partial_kq_\tau(k)
=8\tau^2k^3e^{-2\tau k^2}\ge0,
\tag{TL.657}
\]

with \(q_\tau(k)\simeq(\tau k^2)^2\) for
\(\tau k^2\ll1\) and \(q_\tau(k)\to1\) for
\(\tau k^2\to\infty\).  Integration by parts in (TL.643), using
\(\partial_k\Pi=-T\), gives the exact same-field representations

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
\mathcal J_{\rm VPI}(\tau,t)
&=-\int_0^\infty q_\tau(k)T(k,t)\,dk
=\operatorname{Re}\langle B(u,u),q_\tau(\Lambda)u\rangle,\\
\mathcal J_\nu(\tau,t)
&=2\nu\int_0^\infty k^2q_\tau(k)e(k,t)\,dk
=\nu\|q_\tau(\Lambda)^{1/2}\Lambda u\|_2^2.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.658}
\]

The first row contains the complete projected nonlinear evolution; the
second is its simultaneous irreversible row in the same multiplier.  They
are not independently evolved currents.

Put

\[
\beta(t)
:=\sup_{j\in\mathbb Z}2^{j/2}\|\Delta_ju(t)\|_2.
\tag{TL.659}
\]

The proof of Cycle 159's multiplier estimate uses only positivity,
monotonicity, uniform dyadic comparability and the standard derivative
bounds of the multiplier.  The family \(q_\tau\) has those properties
uniformly in \(\tau\).  The same low--high, high--low and high--high
paraproduct summation therefore yields

\[
\boxed{
|\mathcal J_{\rm VPI}(\tau,t)|
\le C\beta(t)
\|q_\tau(\Lambda)^{1/2}\Lambda u(t)\|_2^2
=\frac{C\beta(t)}\nu\mathcal J_\nu(\tau,t)
}
\tag{TL.660}
\]

for every \(\tau>0\), with one universal constant.  Consequently, if

\[
\vartheta(t):=\frac{C\beta(t)}\nu,
\tag{TL.661}
\]

then the complete signed current satisfies the pointwise cone law

\[
\boxed{
(1-\vartheta(t))\mathcal J_\nu(\tau,t)
\le\mathcal J(\tau,t)
\le(1+\vartheta(t))\mathcal J_\nu(\tau,t)
\quad(\tau>0).
}
\tag{TL.662}
\]

Thus one time-dependent coefficient governs every heat depth.  In
particular, whenever \(\vartheta<1\), the completed current has the
irreversible orientation at all scales simultaneously.  A reversal capable
of critical rebuilding is possible only while the same field carries a
viscosity-sized critical octave; it cannot be prescribed separately near
\(\tau=0\).

That coefficient is already part of the positive scale layer.  The heat
kernel formula (TL.655) and the Littlewood--Paley characterization give

\[
\boxed{
c\beta(t)^2
\le
\sup_{\tau>0}\tau^{-1/2}\rho(\log\tau,t)
\le C\beta(t)^2,
}
\tag{TL.663}
\]

up to the fixed choice of dyadic partition.  Moreover positivity of the
critical decomposition gives \(\beta^2\le C H\).  The already proved
interpolation payment (TL.626) therefore supplies

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T\beta(t)^4\,dt
\le C\int_0^TH(t)^2\,dt
\le\frac{C\mathcal E_0^2}{\nu},
}
\tag{TL.664}
\]

and hence

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T\vartheta(t)^4\,dt
\le\frac{C\mathcal E_0^2}{\nu^5}.
}
\tag{TL.665}
\]

Equations (TL.660)--(TL.665) remove both apparent freedoms left by the raw
trace formulation in (TL.635).  The current cannot concentrate near the
ultraviolet boundary without the positive layer itself acquiring large
critical octave occupancy, and its sign cannot oscillate independently at
different heat depths: all depths share the same VPI coefficient
\(\vartheta(t)\).  The repo's surrounding-row interpolation pays the full
time mass of that coefficient, while (TL.651)--(TL.653) retains the signed
state-aligned cancellation of the current it governs.

The correct temporal governor is therefore the combined law

\[
\boxed{
\begin{gathered}
\rho_t+\partial_\sigma\mathcal J=0,
\qquad
|\mathcal J_{\rm VPI}|
\le\vartheta\mathcal J_\nu,
\qquad
\vartheta\in L_t^4,\\
\mathsf S'+\mathsf R=\mathsf W,
\qquad
\mathsf W\text{ is summed with its signed shared-face cancellation.}
\end{gathered}
}
\tag{TL.666}
\]

This is stronger and more structured than demanding an unqualified
\(L_t^2\) bound on the singular current trace.  The whole scale field, its
common critical-occupancy coefficient, its irreversible current and its
signed state-aligned lift are four readings of one continuing VPI history.

There is an immediate one-sided temporal consequence.  Combining (TL.646)
with the lower cone in (TL.662) gives

\[
\boxed{
[H'(t)]_+
\le
\frac{(\vartheta(t)-1)_+}{\sqrt{2\pi}}
\int_0^\infty\tau^{-3/2}\mathcal J_\nu(\tau,t)\,d\tau .
}
\tag{TL.667}
\]

In particular,

\[
\boxed{
H'(t)>0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\vartheta(t)>1
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\beta(t)>\frac\nu C .
}
\tag{TL.668}
\]

This is not a small-data assumption imposed on the history.  It is the exact
directional statement that follows from the completed current: below one
viscosity-sized critical occupancy, the current at every heat depth has the
irreversible orientation simultaneously, so the critical trace cannot rise.
Every lawful strengthening is therefore confined to the same super-threshold
times, and (TL.665) gives their datum-finite ordinary-time occupancy,

\[
\boxed{
\bigl|\{t\in[0,T]:H'(t)>0\}\bigr|
\le
\int_0^T\vartheta(t)^4\,dt
\le\frac{C\mathcal E_0^2}{\nu^5}.
}
\tag{TL.669}
\]

For record control this one-sided gate must be used before absolute values.
It restricts positive trace work to super-threshold occupancy, while
(TL.651)--(TL.653) cancels shared-interface recounting and
(TL.612)--(TL.613) deletes passage which does not survive to the record.
The continuation must remain on this signed, record-stopped current and derive
its no-self-financing consequence from the same initial history.

## 58. Sobolev restart: the centered interpolation-defect tower

The restart begins with the reverse Sobolev payment, not with a terminal
record object.  The already controlled rows give

\[
H^2\le E_0E_1,
\qquad
\int_0^T H^2\,dt\le \frac{E_0(0)^2}{2\nu}.
\tag{TL.670}
\]

Thus the remaining issue is temporal concentration of this finite mass.  To
see what can actually create such concentration, retain the cancellation of
the kinetic row inside the critical production.  Put

\[
\lambda_H:=\frac{E_1}{H},
\qquad
V_H:=E_{3/2}-\frac{E_1^2}{H}
=\frac12\|\Lambda^{1/2}(\Lambda-\lambda_H)u\|_2^2\ge0.
\tag{TL.671}
\]

Since \(\langle B(u,u),u\rangle=0\), the critical production has the exact
centered form

\[
\boxed{
P_H
=-\langle B(u,u),(\Lambda-\lambda_H)u\rangle .
}
\tag{TL.672}
\]

The Leray projection is bounded on \(L^{3/2}\), and incompressibility,
Sobolev and Holder give

\[
\|B(u,u)\|_{3/2}
\le C\|u\|_6\|\nabla u\|_2
\le C E_1,
\]

while

\[
\|(\Lambda-\lambda_H)u\|_3
\le C\|\Lambda^{1/2}(\Lambda-\lambda_H)u\|_2
=C\sqrt{2V_H}.
\]

Consequently

\[
\boxed{|P_H|\le C E_1\sqrt{V_H}.}
\tag{TL.673}
\]

This is the first exact upper/lower splice.  A field supported on one
frequency sphere has \(V_H=0\), so its energy-conserving nonlinear tangent
cannot raise \(H\).  At the other extreme, the same breadth \(V_H\) occurs
inside the viscous next row,

\[
E_{3/2}=\frac{E_1^2}{H}+V_H.
\]

Therefore the complete critical balance gives

\[
\boxed{
H'
\le C E_1\sqrt{V_H}
-2\nu V_H
-2\nu\frac{E_1^2}{H}.
}
\tag{TL.674}
\]

No mechanism has been split here: (TL.674) is one estimate on the joined
Navier--Stokes tangent after its energy-conserving common-scale component has
cancelled.  If \(H'>0\) and

\[
x_H:=\frac{\sqrt{H V_H}}{E_1},
\]

then necessarily

\[
\boxed{
C\sqrt H\,x_H>2\nu(1+x_H^2).
}
\tag{TL.675}
\]

Hence productive critical strengthening is impossible both at zero spectral
breadth and beyond the upper root of the same quadratic.  More explicitly,

\[
H'>0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
H>\frac{16\nu^2}{C^2},
\qquad
x_-(H)<x_H<x_+(H),
\tag{TL.676}
\]

where

\[
x_\pm(H)
=\frac{C\sqrt H\pm\sqrt{C^2H-16\nu^2}}{4\nu},
\qquad x_-(H)x_+(H)=1.
\]

The centered defect is itself one Sobolev row, not a diagnostic appended to
the equation.  For every real \(s\) for which the moments are finite, define

\[
\lambda_s:=\frac{E_{s+1/2}}{E_s},
\qquad
V_s:=E_{s+1}-\frac{E_{s+1/2}^2}{E_s}
=\frac12\|\Lambda^s(\Lambda-\lambda_s)u\|_2^2.
\tag{TL.677}
\]

Differentiating at the minimizing center \(\lambda_s\) removes every
\(\lambda_s'\) term and yields the exact defect evolution

\[
\boxed{
V_s'+2\nu W_s=Q_s,
}
\tag{TL.678}
\]

with

\[
\begin{aligned}
W_s
&:=E_{s+2}-2\lambda_sE_{s+3/2}+\lambda_s^2E_{s+1}
=\frac12\|\Lambda^{s+1}(\Lambda-\lambda_s)u\|_2^2,\\
Q_s
&:=P_{s+1}-2\lambda_sP_{s+1/2}+\lambda_s^2P_s\\
&=-\langle B(u,u),\Lambda^{2s}(\Lambda-\lambda_s)^2u\rangle.
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.679}
\]

Moreover, because \(\lambda_{s+1}\) minimizes the next defect,

\[
\boxed{W_s\ge V_{s+1}.}
\tag{TL.680}
\]

Equations (TL.670)--(TL.680) are the Sobolev version of the one-fluid physical
picture.  The lower and upper rows already pay the amount of critical height;
common one-scale amplification cancels from its source; any actual rise must
create a nonzero scale-shape defect; and the evolution of that defect exposes
the next irreversible Sobolev row together with its own centered VPI source.
The no-Zeno proof must therefore be carried through the signed family
\((V_s,Q_s,W_s)\) before any positive record selection: an apparent successor
which merely translates or rescales the same one-frequency shape contributes
zero at (TL.672), while every genuine rebuilding appears as a new centered
source in (TL.678) and simultaneously exposes the next viscous row through
(TL.680).

## 59. Effective-frequency passage is paid by the next centered row

The critical height can become unbounded only if its effective frequency also
becomes unbounded.  Indeed, (TL.670) gives

\[
\lambda_H=\frac{E_1}{H}\ge\frac{H}{E_0(0)}.
\tag{TL.681}
\]

The evolution of \(\lambda_H\) retains the same centering as Section 58.
Subtracting the normalized \(H\)- and \(E_1\)-balances gives

\[
\boxed{
(\log\lambda_H)'
+2\nu\Delta_H
=-\frac{\langle B(u,u),\Lambda(\Lambda-\lambda_H)u\rangle}{E_1},
}
\tag{TL.682}
\]

where

\[
\Delta_H
:=\frac{E_2}{E_1}-\frac{E_{3/2}}H\ge0.
\tag{TL.683}
\]

The sign in (TL.683) is moment log-convexity: the ratio
\(E_{s+1}/E_s\) is nondecreasing in \(s\).  The right side of (TL.682) is
not a new source.  It is the difference between the same normalized VPI
production read one half derivative apart; the common amplification cancels.

Let

\[
W_H:=W_{1/2}
=\frac12\|\Lambda^{3/2}(\Lambda-\lambda_H)u\|_2^2.
\tag{TL.684}
\]

The same \(L^{3/2}\)--\(L^3\) estimate used in (TL.673) gives

\[
\boxed{
\left|
\frac{\langle B(u,u),\Lambda(\Lambda-\lambda_H)u\rangle}{E_1}
\right|
\le C\sqrt{W_H}.
}
\tag{TL.685}
\]

Consequently, if \(J=[a,b]\) completes an effective-frequency multiplication
\(\lambda_H(b)=q\lambda_H(a)\), then

\[
\boxed{
\log q+2\nu\int_J\Delta_H\,dt
\le C\int_J\sqrt{W_H}\,dt
\le C|J|^{1/2}
\left(\int_JW_H\,dt\right)^{1/2}.
}
\tag{TL.686}
\]

In particular,

\[
\boxed{
\int_JW_H\,dt
\ge\frac{(\log q)^2}{C^2|J|}.
}
\tag{TL.687}
\]

This is the precise turnover-speed statement supplied by the Sobolev tower.
A passage through a fixed frequency factor may occur, but compressing that
passage into a shorter physical interval increases, rather than decreases,
the required next-row action.

That action is not counted separately on successive intervals.  From
(TL.678) at \(s=1/2\),

\[
V_H'+2\nu W_H=Q_H,
\qquad
Q_H=-\langle B(u,u),\Lambda(\Lambda-\lambda_H)^2u\rangle.
\tag{TL.688}
\]

Therefore, for consecutive intervals \(J_j=[t_j,t_{j+1}]\),

\[
\boxed{
2\nu\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\int_{J_j}W_H\,dt
=V_H(t_0)-V_H(t_N)
+\int_{t_0}^{t_N}Q_H\,dt.
}
\tag{TL.689}
\]

The endpoint defect occurs once, and every shared time face cancels.  Combining
(TL.687)--(TL.689), any sequence of fixed-factor frequency passages with
\(|J_j|\to0\) forces

\[
\int_{t_0}^{T}W_H\,dt=\infty
\quad\text{and hence}\quad
\int_{t_0}^{T}Q_H\,dt=\infty,
\tag{TL.690}
\]

unless the passages stop after finitely many steps.  Thus the Zeno schedule
cannot hide inside vanishing residence: it becomes an infinite signed source
requirement in the next centered equation of the same field.

The entire location law has the same form.  For
\(\lambda_s=E_{s+1/2}/E_s\),

\[
\boxed{
(\log\lambda_s)'
+2\nu\Delta_s
=-\frac{
\langle B(u,u),\Lambda^{2s}(\Lambda-\lambda_s)u\rangle
}{E_{s+1/2}},
}
\tag{TL.691}
\]

where

\[
\Delta_s
:=\frac{E_{s+3/2}}{E_{s+1/2}}
-\frac{E_{s+1}}{E_s}\ge0.
\tag{TL.692}
\]

Equations (TL.678) and (TL.691) are the two coupled readings of every Sobolev
level: (TL.691) records movement of the occupied scale, while (TL.678) records
the creation and destruction of the breadth required to move it.  Neither a
pure rescaling nor an arbitrarily short crossing is free, and neither can be
recounted on the next interval.  The remaining all-row step is to sum these
centered source requirements through the heat-normalized tower so that their
internal derivative faces cancel against the datum-finite mass in (TL.638),
rather than estimating \(Q_H\) as an isolated magnitude.

## 60. The heat-normalized Sobolev tower resolves every scale from the same datum-finite mass

The centered defects in Sections 58--59 are not the tower used from this
point onward.  The canonical tower is obtained by differentiating the same
heat-resolved velocity while normalizing each derivative row by its own heat
scale.  Put \(\sigma=\log\tau\) and, for every integer \(n\ge1\), define

\[
\boxed{
\rho_n(\sigma,t)
:=\frac{2^{n-1}}{\Gamma(n)}\,
\tau^n\|\Lambda^nG_\tau u(t)\|_2^2 .
}
\tag{TL.693}
\]

Every \(\rho_n\) is a positive resolution of the kinetic energy of the same
velocity field.  Tonelli's theorem and

\[
\int_0^\infty \tau^{n-1}e^{-2\tau k^2}\,d\tau
=\frac{\Gamma(n)}{(2k^2)^n}
\]

give the exact common-mass identity

\[
\boxed{
\int_{\mathbb R}\rho_n(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma
=E_0(t)\le E_0(0)
\qquad(n\ge1).
}
\tag{TL.694}
\]

The rows also have a pointwise datum ceiling.  Since
\(\sup_{x\ge0}x^ne^{-2x}=(n/(2e))^n\),

\[
\boxed{
0\le\rho_n(\sigma,t)
\le \kappa_nE_0(t),
\qquad
\kappa_n:=\frac{n^n}{e^n\Gamma(n)}.
}
\tag{TL.695}
\]

Increasing \(n\) therefore sharpens the scale observer without opening a
new energy reserve.  Indeed its spectral kernel is proportional to
\(x^ne^{-2x}\), \(x=\tau k^2\), and is centered at \(x=n/2\) with relative
width of order \(n^{-1/2}\).

Every row reconstructs the same critical height.  A second Gamma integral
gives

\[
\boxed{
H(t)=c_n\int_{\mathbb R}e^{-\sigma/2}\rho_n(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma,
\qquad
c_n:=\frac{\Gamma(n)}{\sqrt2\,\Gamma(n-\frac12)}.
}
\tag{TL.696}
\]

The compatibility between the increasingly sharp observations is exact.
Differentiating (TL.693) in \(\sigma\) gives

\[
\boxed{
\partial_\sigma\rho_n=n(\rho_n-\rho_{n+1}),
\qquad
\rho_{n+1}=\rho_n-\frac1n\partial_\sigma\rho_n.
}
\tag{TL.697}
\]

In particular \(\rho_1=\tau Y\), while the localized finite layer of
Section 55 is \(\rho_2=2\tau^2\|\Delta G_\tau u\|_2^2\).  Thus that layer is
the second row of the full normalized Sobolev tower, not an additional
carrier.

The current tower is forced by the same recurrence.  Start from the completed
same-field current \(J_1:=J=\Phi+\nu L\) in (TL.631) and define

\[
\boxed{
J_{n+1}:=J_n-\frac1n\partial_\sigma J_n.
}
\tag{TL.698}
\]

Applying the same scale operator to
\(\partial_t\rho_n+\partial_\sigma J_n=0\) proves inductively that

\[
\boxed{
\partial_t\rho_n+\partial_\sigma J_n=0,
\qquad
H'(t)=-\frac{c_n}{2}
\int_{\mathbb R}e^{-\sigma/2}J_n(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma.
}
\tag{TL.699}
\]

The second identity follows by integration by parts in \(\sigma\).  Its
endpoint terms vanish first for Schwartz data and then by the standard smooth
approximation on every compact classical interval.  In particular

\[
\boxed{
\partial_\sigma^2\rho_1=\rho_1-3\rho_2+2\rho_3,
\qquad
\partial_\sigma^2J_1=J_1-3J_2+2J_3.
}
\tag{TL.700}
\]

This is the promised second-scale exactness.  It is carrier-faithful: all
rows are heat-scale derivatives of one \(u(t)\), and every \(J_n\) is a scale
derivative of the one completed VPI current.  No derivative row is assigned
an independent source.

## 61. Signed affine descent is an endpoint-stock law before record selection

Let \(I=[a,b]\subset\mathbb R\), \(L=b-a\), and
\(\theta_I(\sigma)=(b-\sigma)/L\).  Two integrations by parts, followed by
the continuity equation in (TL.699), give

\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_a^b\theta_I\,\partial_\sigma^2J_1\,d\sigma
&=-\partial_\sigma J_1(a)
+\frac{J_1(b)-J_1(a)}L\\
&=\partial_t\rho_1(a)
-\frac1L\partial_t\int_a^b\rho_1\,d\sigma.
\end{aligned}
\]

Hence

\[
\boxed{
\int_s^t\!\int_a^b
\theta_I\,\partial_\sigma^2J_1\,d\sigma\,dr
=D_I(t)-D_I(s),
\quad
D_I(r):=\rho_1(a,r)-\frac1L\int_a^b\rho_1(\sigma,r)\,d\sigma.
}
\tag{TL.701}
\]

This is stronger than the earlier first-order affine-quotient descent: the
entire signed interior contribution is an endpoint change of the stock
already present in the same heat layer.  Equations (TL.694)--(TL.695) give
the fixed-band datum bound

\[
\boxed{
|D_I(r)|
\le\left(\frac1e+\frac1L\right)E_0(0),
\qquad
\left|\int_s^t\!\int_I
\theta_I\partial_\sigma^2J_1\right|
\le2\left(\frac1e+\frac1L\right)E_0(0).
}
\tag{TL.702}
\]

No absolute value of the current was taken before (TL.701).  Adjacent time
faces occur once with each sign, and a translated scale grid replaces the
point trace \(\rho_1(a,r)\) by an average of the same finite layer.  Thus the
second descent really does remove the old potential-average defect on every
fixed affine scale interval.

The critical packing must nevertheless retain the exponential scale weight.
Fix \(q>1\), put \(L=2\log q\), and for a translated grid \(r\in[0,L)\) set

\[
s_j(r):=r-jL,
\qquad
I_j(r):=[s_{j+1}(r),s_j(r)],
\qquad
M_j^{(n)}(t;r):=\int_{I_j(r)}\rho_n(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma.
\tag{TL.703}
\]

Because \(e^{-s_j/2}=e^{-r/2}q^j\), (TL.696) gives the uniform comparison

\[
\boxed{
c_ne^{-r/2}\sum_jq^jM_j^{(n)}
\le H(t)
\le qc_ne^{-r/2}\sum_jq^jM_j^{(n)}.
}
\tag{TL.704}
\]

Writing \(J_{n,j}=J_n(s_j(r),t)\), the exact receiver law is

\[
\boxed{
(M_j^{(n)})'=J_{n,j+1}-J_{n,j}.
}
\tag{TL.705}
\]

For a finite ladder \(J\le j\le N\), summation by parts gives

\[
\begin{aligned}
\frac d{dt}\sum_{j=J}^Nq^jM_j^{(n)}
={}&q^NJ_{n,N+1}-q^JJ_{n,J}\\
&-(1-q^{-1})\sum_{j=J+1}^{N}q^jJ_{n,j}.
\end{aligned}
\tag{TL.706}
\]

Thus every unweighted shared face cancels exactly; the surviving interior
coefficient \(1-q^{-1}\) is not recounting, but the real increase in critical
value when the same stock crosses to a finer band.  Correspondingly, for
\(\Delta J_{n,j}:=J_{n,j+1}-J_{n,j}\),

\[
\boxed{
\sum_jq^j\Delta^2J_{n,j}
=(1-q^{-1})^2\sum_jq^jJ_{n,j},
}
\tag{TL.707}
\]

whenever the terminal faces vanish, with the finite-ladder version carrying
only the two true exterior faces.  Equation (TL.707) fixes the role of the
second descent: it converts fast affine motion into signed stock and exterior
faces before record selection, but it does not erase the genuine critical
gain.  The remaining gain must be reduced by the simultaneous irreversible
row during the stock's actual residence.  This is exactly where the slow
loss in (TL.643)--(TL.645), the source-square receiver in
(TL.651)--(TL.654), and first-record stopping must enter the same packing,
rather than being asserted after the scale sum.

## 62. Every Sobolev row carries the same datum-finite irreversible scale measure

The irreversible part of the current tower can be written explicitly.  Put

\[
q_n(x):=1-e^{-2x}\sum_{m=0}^{n-1}\frac{(2x)^m}{m!},
\qquad x\ge0.
\tag{TL.708}
\]

Then \(q_n\ge0\), \(q_n(0)=0\), \(q_n(\infty)=1\), and

\[
q_n'(x)=\frac{2^n}{\Gamma(n)}x^{n-1}e^{-2x}.
\tag{TL.709}
\]

The current in (TL.698) has the exact same-field decomposition

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
J_n^{\rm VPI}(\tau,t)
&=\operatorname{Re}\langle
\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u),q_n(\tau\Lambda^2)u\rangle,\\
J_n^\nu(\tau,t)
&=\nu\|q_n(\tau\Lambda^2)^{1/2}\Lambda u\|_2^2,\\
J_n&=J_n^{\rm VPI}+J_n^\nu.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.710}
\]

For \(n=1\), this is (TL.631); applying the recurrence (TL.698) proves
the formula for every \(n\).  The first row is the complete projected
nonlinear current and the second is its simultaneous fixed-viscosity row in
the same multiplier.

Differentiating the positive quadratic row in logarithmic scale and using
(TL.693), (TL.697), and (TL.709) gives

\[
\boxed{
d\mu_n(\sigma,t)
:=\partial_\sigma J_n^\nu(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma\,dt
=\frac{n\nu}{\tau}\rho_{n+1}(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma\,dt
\ge0.
}
\tag{TL.711}
\]

This is the irreversible scale measure of the \(n\)-th observer.  It is
datum-finite over the complete scale--time plane:

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^T\!\int_{\mathbb R}d\mu_n
=\int_0^T\nu\|\Lambda u(t)\|_2^2\,dt
=E_0(0)-E_0(T)
\le E_0(0).
}
\tag{TL.712}
\]

The value is independent of \(n\): each row redistributes the same physical
energy loss across scale resolution; the rows do not provide multiple
budgets.

For a log-scale band \(I=[a,b]\), (TL.711) yields the exact local lifetime
bound

\[
\boxed{
\mu_n(I\times[s,t])
\ge n\nu e^{-b}
\int_s^t\!\int_I\rho_{n+1}(\sigma,r)\,d\sigma\,dr.
}
\tag{TL.713}
\]

Consequently, if the \((n+1)\)-st row carries band mass at least \(m>0\)
throughout a time interval of length

\[
t-s\ge\gamma\frac{e^b}{\nu},
\]

then

\[
\boxed{
\mu_n(I\times[s,t])\ge n\gamma m.
}
\tag{TL.714}
\]

This is the slow-residence charge in the desired packing.  It is a fixed
fraction of the actual unweighted stock occupying that scale, it is generated
during that stock's own viscous lifetime, and disjoint scale--time regions
cannot recount it because they are all subsets of the single measure
\(\mu_n\) in (TL.712).

There is an exact alternative at the same band.  If a stock level \(m\) does
not remain above \(m/2\) for a fixed fraction of \(e^b/\nu\), then it changes
by at least \(m/2\) before that lifetime expires.  The receiver identity
(TL.705) gives

\[
\boxed{
\frac m2
\le
\left|\int_s^t
\bigl[J_{n}(a,r)-J_{n}(b,r)\bigr]\,dr\right|.
}
\tag{TL.715}
\]

Thus every occupied band is governed by a literal residence--or--transit
dichotomy.  Slow occupation is paid by the datum-finite irreversible measure
(TL.712); fast departure is not declared free, but remains the same signed
current through the two actual faces and is passed to the affine endpoint law
(TL.701) before any record-positive contribution is selected.  The remaining
packing calculation is now precise: first-record assignment of the fast terms
in (TL.715) must be followed through (TL.701), leaving only the two exterior
faces and a \(q\)-weighted portion of (TL.712) small enough to be absorbed by
the slow contraction in (TL.714).

## 63. Translation averaging removes face traces without losing the critical weight

The translated grids in (TL.703) admit exact, rather than merely comparable,
averages.  Write

\[
\mathcal M_n(r,t):=\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}q^jM_j^{(n)}(t;r).
\]

For fixed \(\sigma=mL+s\), \(0\le s<L\), the band containing \(\sigma\)
has weight \(q^{-m-1}\) for \(0\le r<s\) and \(q^{-m}\) for
\(s\le r<L\).  Direct integration therefore gives

\[
\int_0^Le^{-r/2}q^{j(\sigma,r)}\,dr
=2(1-q^{-1})e^{-\sigma/2}.
\]

Tonelli's theorem and (TL.696) now yield

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^Le^{-r/2}\mathcal M_n(r,t)\,dr
=\frac{2(1-q^{-1})}{c_n}H(t).
}
\tag{TL.716}
\]

The corresponding face average is just as simple.  Every
\(\sigma\in\mathbb R\) occurs at exactly one face \(s_j(r)\) while
\(r\) traverses \([0,L)\), and
\(e^{-r/2}q^j=e^{-\sigma/2}\).  Hence

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^Le^{-r/2}
\sum_jq^j\rho_n(s_j(r),t)\,dr
=\frac1{c_n}H(t).
}
\tag{TL.717}
\]

Thus no point-face trace survives translation averaging: it becomes the same
critical moment already represented by the band stocks.  For the lower-face
affine defect

\[
D_{n,j}^-(r,t)
:=\rho_n(s_{j+1}(r),t)-\frac1L M_j^{(n)}(t;r),
\]

(TL.716)--(TL.717) give

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^jD_{n,j}^-\,dr
=\left[q^{-1}-\frac{2(1-q^{-1})}{L}\right]\frac{H(t)}{c_n}.
}
\tag{TL.718}
\]

For the upper-face version \(D_{n,j}^+\), the coefficient is

\[
\boxed{
\int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^jD_{n,j}^+\,dr
=\left[1-\frac{2(1-q^{-1})}{L}\right]\frac{H(t)}{c_n}.
}
\tag{TL.719}
\]

Equations (TL.718)--(TL.719) are the exact translated form of the affine
second descent.  They prove both required facts at once:

1. the pointwise faces are not a separate uncontrolled object; and
2. after the critical weights are restored, their surviving combination is
   exactly proportional to the true critical height.

The second fact prevents a false cancellation argument.  Signed second
descent removes interior recounting, but cannot call genuine finer-scale
transport zero.  On a first-record interval its endpoint contribution is the
actual record increase.  Therefore the strict gain in the final recurrence
must come from (TL.714), while (TL.718)--(TL.719) carry every fast contribution
to that next receiving band exactly once.  This fixes the order of the packing:

\[
\boxed{
\text{translate and descend signed currents}
\;\longrightarrow\;
\text{assign the first receiving record}
\;\longrightarrow\;
\text{extract irreversible residence}.
}
\tag{TL.720}
\]

## 64. The signed second descent cancels finite shared faces but is not itself a contraction

The two translated endpoint coefficients in (TL.718)--(TL.719) have a
strict structure which should be recorded before any first-record positive
part is taken.  Put

\[
 a_q:=\frac{1-q^{-1}}{\log q},
 \qquad
 \varepsilon_q^+:=1-a_q,
 \qquad
 \varepsilon_q^-:=a_q-q^{-1}.
\tag{TL.721}
\]

For \(q>1\), the elementary inequalities
\(1-q^{-1}<\log q<q-1\) imply

\[
 \boxed{
 0<\varepsilon_q^+,\varepsilon_q^-<1-q^{-1},
 \qquad
 \varepsilon_q^++\varepsilon_q^-=1-q^{-1}.
 }
\tag{TL.722}
\]

Consequently (TL.718)--(TL.719) are exactly

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^jD_{n,j}^+(r,t)\,dr
 &=\varepsilon_q^+\frac{H(t)}{c_n},\\
 \int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^jD_{n,j}^-(r,t)\,dr
 &=-\varepsilon_q^-\frac{H(t)}{c_n}.
 \end{aligned}
 }
\tag{TL.723}
\]

Thus the complete signed translated grid, not a cellwise absolute-value
sum, obeys

\[
 \boxed{
 \left|
 \int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^jD_{n,j}^+(r,t)\,dr
 \right|
 +
 \left|
 \int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^jD_{n,j}^-(r,t)\,dr
 \right|
 =(1-q^{-1})\frac{H(t)}{c_n}.
 }
\tag{TL.724}
\]

In particular, both coefficients are asymptotic to
\((q-1)/2\) as \(q\downarrow1\).  The point trace has not been estimated; it
has been combined with the band average into the exact affine defect, and only
the globally signed \(q\)-grid has then been evaluated.

The small coefficient is not, by itself, an absorption gain.  Applying the
row-\(n\) version of (TL.701) to every translated cell and using the lower-face
identity in (TL.723) gives the exact equality

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
&\int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^j
 \int_s^t\!\int_{I_j(r)}
 \theta_{I_j(r)}\,\partial_\sigma^2J_n(\sigma,v)
 \,d\sigma\,dv\,dr\\
&\hspace{35mm}
=-\varepsilon_q^-\frac{H(t)-H(s)}{c_n}.
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.724a}
\]

Thus the same \(\varepsilon_q^-\) multiplies the descended interior and its
endpoint stock.  Dividing (TL.724a) by that coefficient returns the original
critical-height increment.  Equations (TL.723)--(TL.724a) remove point-face
recounting exactly, but they do not make genuine fineward VPI transfer small.
Any strict gain must come from an independently controlled consequence of the
quadratic VPI current before the record-positive part is selected.

Now form a completed finite complex of \(q\)-cells and physical-time slabs:
whenever a selected cell exposes a common scale or time face, retain the
neighbouring complementary cell until that face occurs with both
orientations.  Apply (TL.701) on each cell and sum before selecting a sign.
Every completed intermediate-time defect occurs once as an outgoing value
and once as the next incoming value.  Every completed common scale current
already cancels by (TL.705).  Hence common refill has

\[
 \boxed{B_j^{\rm refill}=0}
\tag{TL.725}
\]

as an independent contribution: changing the receiving cell or derivative
resolution creates no new stock and no new current.  A genuinely exposed
selection face is not covered by (TL.725); it remains a true exterior face
of the selected complex and must be retained until the complement is put
back or the exterior is estimated.  For the complete translated ladder the
only affine endpoint terms are the true initial and final values in
(TL.723).  The initial value is datum-finite because \(H(0)<\infty\).  The
terminal value has the coefficient in (TL.724), but (TL.724a) proves that it
cannot be absorbed merely by choosing \(q\) near one: the same coefficient is
already present on the interior identity that produced it.

There is likewise no fixed-time ultraviolet exterior.  For every fixed
\(T<T_*\), smoothness on \([0,T]\), the small-\(\tau\) behavior of the
Poisson-tail multipliers in (TL.708), and the critical factor
\(q^j=e^{(r-s_j)/2}\) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \lim_{N\to\infty}
 q^N\int_s^tJ_n(s_{N+1}(r),v)\,dv=0
 \qquad(0\le s<t\le T<T_*).
 }
\tag{TL.726}
\]

Indeed, \(q_n(x)=O(x^n)\) as \(x\downarrow0\).  On a compact classical
interval, the multiplier formula (TL.710) therefore gives uniformly
\(J_n(s,t)=O(e^{ns})\) as \(s\to-\infty\); since
\(q^N=e^{(r-s_N)/2}\), the weighted face is
\(O(e^{(n-1/2)s_N})\to0\) for every \(n\ge1\).  At the coarse end,
\(q^J\to0\) while the completed current remains bounded
on compact classical intervals, so

\[
 \boxed{
 \lim_{J\to-\infty}
 q^J\int_s^tJ_n(s_J(r),v)\,dv=0.
 }
\tag{TL.727}
\]

Equations (TL.723)--(TL.727) settle the topology of the finite packing, not
its datum bound.  After signed second descent, all completed shared faces,
common refill, and both fixed scale exteriors are gone.  What remains is the
genuine signed fineward VPI transfer represented equivalently by either side
of (TL.724a), together with a possible joint limit in which the receiving
scale tends to the ultraviolet while physical time tends to \(T_*\).  Neither
is an additional source or proxy: both are limits of the same completed VPI
current in (TL.698).  The next calculation must use the quadratic VPI source
law to force a datum-finite irreversible part of this genuine transfer before
its record-positive part is selected; affine descent alone cannot supply it.

## 65. The surviving VPI current is an exact commutator, so rigid carriage cannot feed the ultraviolet face

The nonlinear face in (TL.710) is not an arbitrary scale source.  Let

\[
 m_{n,\tau}(\xi):=q_n(\tau|\xi|^2),
 \qquad A_u v:=u\cdot\nabla v .
\]

Because \(u\) and \(m_{n,\tau}(D)u\) are divergence free, the Leray
projection in (TL.710) retains the complete pressure-corrected evolution but
drops out of this global pairing.  The transport operator \(A_u\) is
skew-adjoint.  Consequently

\[
\boxed{
 J_n^{\rm VPI}(\tau,t)
 =\frac12\operatorname{Re}
 \left\langle u,
 [m_{n,\tau}(D),A_u]u\right\rangle .
}
\tag{TL.728}
\]

Thus uniform transport, which commutes with every Fourier multiplier, creates
no scale current.  The whole nonlinear face is the failure of the actual
spatially varying velocity to commute with the heat-scale observation.

With \(k=p+q\), the Fourier form of (TL.728) is

\[
\boxed{
\begin{aligned}
 J_n^{\rm VPI}
 =\frac12\operatorname{Re}
 \iint_{k=p+q} i\,
 &\bigl(\widehat u(p)\cdot q\bigr)
 \bigl[m_{n,\tau}(k)-m_{n,\tau}(q)\bigr]\\
 &\times\widehat u(q)\cdot\overline{\widehat u(k)}\,dp\,dq .
\end{aligned}
}
\tag{TL.729}
\]

The multiplier difference contains the missing geometric derivative.  From
(TL.709),

\[
 |\nabla_\xi m_{n,\tau}(\xi)|
 =2\sqrt\tau\,\sqrt x\,q_n'(x),
 \qquad x=\tau|\xi|^2,
\]

and therefore, with
\(C_n:=\sup_{x\ge0}2\sqrt x\,q_n'(x)<\infty\),

\[
\boxed{
 |m_{n,\tau}(p+q)-m_{n,\tau}(q)|
 \le \min\{1,C_n\sqrt\tau\,|p|\}.
}
\tag{TL.730}
\]

Equation (TL.730) is the exact VPI locality split at heat length
\(\sqrt\tau\).  If the advecting leg has
\(|p|\ll\tau^{-1/2}\), only its gradient, not its rigid velocity, can move
critical stock through the face.  Otherwise the advecting leg itself lies at
the observed scale or finer.  Together with \(k=p+q\), every non-negligible
ultraviolet transfer therefore has two lawful forms inside the same triad:

\[
\boxed{
\begin{array}{ll}
\text{coarse--fine deformation:}&
 \text{a differentiated coarse leg acts on a fine leg},\\[1mm]
\text{scale-local transfer:}&
 \text{at least two triad legs occupy the observed scale or finer}.
\end{array}
}
\tag{TL.731}
\]

This removes a freely translating spectral bump from the admitted proof
branches: such a schedule satisfies a scalar continuity equation but is not a
source law for (TL.729).  It does not yet bound the two lawful rows in
(TL.731).  The datum-finite fast estimate must now be proved on their joined
commutator, with the low--high derivative and the high--high donor stock kept
inside the signed q-telescope until their common faces cancel.

## 66. The commutator closes the low-participation branch at every heat scale simultaneously

The two rows in (TL.731) are exactly the paraproduct rows of the standard
critical multiplier estimate; they must be summed before either is assigned
an independent sign.  Put

\[
 \beta(t):=\sup_{k\in\mathbb Z}2^{k/2}\|\Delta_k u(t)\|_2,
 \qquad
 \mathcal D_{n,\tau}(t)
 :=\|q_n(\tau\Lambda^2)^{1/2}\Lambda u(t)\|_2^2.
\]

The symbols \(q_n(\tau|\xi|^2)\) are positive and nondecreasing, and their
dyadic comparability and Mikhlin constants are uniform in \(\tau\), with a
constant depending only on \(n\).  Applying the low--high commutator bound
from (TL.730), then the high--low and high--high Bernstein sums, gives

\[
\boxed{
 |J_n^{\rm VPI}(\tau,t)|
 \le C_n\beta(t)\mathcal D_{n,\tau}(t)
 =\frac{C_n\beta(t)}{\nu}J_n^\nu(\tau,t).
}
\tag{TL.732}
\]

This is one estimate on the complete commutator, not separate ownership of
its paraproduct pieces.  Since \(J_n=J_n^{\rm VPI}+J_n^\nu\), it yields the
all-scale cone

\[
\boxed{
 (\nu-C_n\beta)\mathcal D_{n,\tau}
 \le J_n(\tau,t)
 \le(\nu+C_n\beta)\mathcal D_{n,\tau}
 \qquad(\tau>0).
}
\tag{TL.733}
\]

Consequently, whenever \(\beta(t)\le\nu/C_n\), every heat-scale face has
the nonnegative orientation at the same time.  The critical trace in
(TL.699) then gives

\[
\boxed{
 \beta(t)\le\frac\nu{C_n}
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad H'(t)\le0.
}
\tag{TL.734}
\]

Thus the low-participation part of a first-record packing contributes no
positive strengthening at all; it is already contained in the simultaneous
irreversible row.  Every record-positive time belongs to the same-field set

\[
 \mathcal R_\beta
 :=\{t:\beta(t)>\nu/C_n\}.
\]

Critical integration of (TL.733) also shows exactly what survives on that
set.  Since

\[
 \frac{c_n}{2}\int_{\mathbb R}e^{-\sigma/2}
 \mathcal D_{n,e^\sigma}(t)\,d\sigma
 =\|\Lambda^{3/2}u(t)\|_2^2,
\]

(TL.699) gives

\[
\boxed{
 [H'(t)]_+
 \le(C_n\beta(t)-\nu)_+
 \|\Lambda^{3/2}u(t)\|_2^2.
}
\tag{TL.735}
\]

The surrounding-row interpolation already pays the coefficient:

\[
\boxed{
 \int_0^T\beta(t)^4\,dt
 \le C\int_0^TH(t)^2\,dt
 \le\frac{C E_0(0)^2}{\nu}.
}
\tag{TL.736}
\]

Equations (TL.734)--(TL.736) complete the low-participation branch and
confine every fast record to a datum-finite high-participation time set.  They
do not yet bound its positive current: the unpaired factor
\(\|\Lambda^{3/2}u\|_2^2\) in (TL.735) is the critical next-row action, not
the kinetic-energy dissipation.  Taking absolute values of the two rows in
(TL.731) has therefore returned the standard critical estimate with no
q-adic gain.  The high-participation branch must retain the signed
polarization of the complete commutator across adjacent receiving faces;
another scalar magnitude estimate cannot close the packing.

## 67. The first heat current is exactly the deformation of one complete material-pair layer

The commutator in (TL.728) has a carrier-native physical-space form which
does not use an active-point selection.  For \(a>0\), let

\[
 g_a(R)=(4\pi a)^{-3/2}e^{-|R|^2/(4a)},
 \qquad E_a=e^{-a\Lambda^2},
\]

and define the complete Gaussian relative-velocity layer

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal Q_a(t)
 :=\iint g_a(x-y)|u(x,t)-u(y,t)|^2\,dx\,dy
 =2\langle u,(I-E_a)u\rangle .
 }
 \tag{TL.737}
\]

Let \(X(\alpha,t)\) be the volume-preserving material map and put

\[
 R_{\alpha\beta}=X(\alpha,t)-X(\beta,t),
 \qquad
 V_{\alpha\beta}=u(X(\alpha,t),t)-u(X(\beta,t),t).
\]

Then the same quantity is

\[
 \mathcal Q_a(t)
 =\iint g_a(R_{\alpha\beta})|V_{\alpha\beta}|^2
 \,d\alpha\,d\beta .
 \tag{TL.738}
\]

Writing \(B(u,u)=\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u)\), symmetrization and
incompressibility give the exact increment identity

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak p_a(t)
 &:=\langle B(u,u),E_au\rangle\\
 &=\frac14\iint
 \nabla g_a(R_{\alpha\beta})\cdot V_{\alpha\beta}
 |V_{\alpha\beta}|^2\,d\alpha\,d\beta\\
 &=-\frac1{8a}\iint
 g_a(R_{\alpha\beta})
 (R_{\alpha\beta}\cdot V_{\alpha\beta})
 |V_{\alpha\beta}|^2\,d\alpha\,d\beta .
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.739}
\]

Since \(q_1(a\Lambda^2/2)=I-E_a\) and
\(\langle B(u,u),u\rangle=0\), (TL.710) and (TL.739) yield

\[
 \boxed{
 J_1^{\rm VPI}(\tau,t)
 =-\mathfrak p_{2\tau}(t)
 =\frac1{16\tau}\iint
 g_{2\tau}(R_{\alpha\beta})
 (R_{\alpha\beta}\cdot V_{\alpha\beta})
 |V_{\alpha\beta}|^2\,d\alpha\,d\beta .
 }
 \tag{TL.740}
\]

This is the exact all-field meaning of the surviving signed heat current:
only relative material motion which changes the Gaussian pair incidence can
move stock through a heat face.  Rigid carriage cancels in (TL.739), exactly
as it does in the commutator (TL.728).

The acceleration and loss rows remain in this same pair history.  Put

\[
 \mathcal L_a(t)
 :=\langle\Lambda u,(I-E_a)\Lambda u\rangle\ge0.
\]

Differentiating (TL.738), using
\(\dot R_{\alpha\beta}=V_{\alpha\beta}\) and the complete
Navier--Stokes acceleration of \(V_{\alpha\beta}\), gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\mathcal Q_a
 =4\mathfrak p_a-4\nu\mathcal L_a .
 }
 \tag{TL.741}
\]

The pressure contribution vanishes only after the complete all-pair
contraction, while the viscous contribution is exactly
\(-4\nu\mathcal L_a\).  Consequently, at \(a=2\tau\),

\[
 \boxed{
 J_1^{\rm VPI}=-\frac14\partial_t\mathcal Q_{2\tau}
               -\nu\mathcal L_{2\tau},
 \qquad
 J_1^\nu=\nu\mathcal L_{2\tau},
 \qquad
 J_1=-\frac14\partial_t\mathcal Q_{2\tau}.
 }
 \tag{TL.742}
\]

Thus the completed heat current is literally the signed material-time motion
of one bounded all-pair layer.  This is the carrier-level reason all common
time and scale faces must cancel before record positivity: a shared face is
the same \(\mathcal Q_a\)-change with opposite orientations, not a second
source.

This calculation also fixes the scope of the earlier all-incidence
transversality notes.  Their quantitative ``active'' coercivities use the
deprecated \(r_+\)-selected carrier and cannot enter the present proof.
The unclipped residual of Cycle 157 is lawful as a material-acceleration
identity, but it is not the current in (TL.740): for every radial multiplier
\(m(\Lambda)\),

\[
 \langle D_tu,m(\Lambda)u\rangle
 =-\nu\langle\Lambda u,m(\Lambda)\Lambda u\rangle,
 \tag{TL.743}
\]

whereas \(J_n^{\rm VPI}=\langle B(u,u),m_{n,\tau}(\Lambda)u\rangle\).
Material recombination has already cancelled the transport commutator in
(TL.743).  Therefore a residual norm for \(D_tu\) cannot be used as an upper
bound for the heat-scale current without a new identity restoring precisely
the pair deformation in (TL.739).  Equations (TL.737)--(TL.742), rather than
that imported residual, are the complete carrier used below.

## 68. Fixed heat faces and slow pair residence are datum-finite

The pair layer is bounded and has an exact scale geometry:

\[
 0\le\mathcal Q_a\le4E_0(0),
 \qquad
 \partial_a\mathcal Q_a
 =2\|\Lambda E_{a/2}u\|_2^2\ge0,
 \qquad
 \partial_a^2\mathcal Q_a
 =-2\|\Lambda^2E_{a/2}u\|_2^2\le0.
 \tag{TL.744}
\]

It is also exactly the cumulative first Sobolev row.  With
\(a=2e^\sigma\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal Q_{2e^\sigma}(t)
 =4\int_{-\infty}^{\sigma}\rho_1(s,t)\,ds .
 }
 \tag{TL.745}
\]

Hence the pair picture and the Sobolev tower are two coordinates of the same
heat-resolved field, not objects to be recombined after separate estimates.

Every fixed heat face has finite temporal variation from the initial datum.
For a time interval \(J=[s,t]\), set

\[
 \mathfrak e(J)
 :=\nu\int_J\|\nabla u(r)\|_2^2\,dr
 =E_0(s)-E_0(t).
\]

Holder, Gagliardo--Nirenberg, and the heat estimate give

\[
 |\mathfrak p_a|
 \le C a^{-1/2}\|u\|_2^{3/2}\|\nabla u\|_2^{3/2}.
\]

Using (TL.741) and \(0\le\mathcal L_a\le\|\nabla u\|_2^2\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_J|\partial_t\mathcal Q_a(r)|\,dr
 \le
 C a^{-1/2}E_0(0)^{3/4}|J|^{1/4}
 \nu^{-3/4}\mathfrak e(J)^{3/4}
 +4\mathfrak e(J).
 }
 \tag{TL.746}
\]

For fixed \(a>0\), the right side tends to zero with \(|J|\).  Thus no
nonvanishing terminal chain can repeatedly cross one fixed heat face; any
surviving fast chain must be the genuine joint limit
\(a\downarrow0\), \(t\uparrow T_*\) already isolated in (TL.727).

Put \(q_a=\mathcal Q_a/\sqrt a\).  Monotonicity and concavity in (TL.744)
give the fixed logarithmic collar

\[
 \boxed{
 q_b(t)\ge\frac12q_a(t)
 \qquad\left(\frac a4\le b\le4a\right).
 }
 \tag{TL.747}
\]

The simultaneous pair loss is coercive on that same layer.  Since
\((1-e^{-x})^2\le x(1-e^{-x})\), Cauchy--Schwarz and
\(\|u\|_2^2\le2E_0(0)\) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal L_a(t)
 \ge\frac{\mathcal Q_a(t)^2}{8E_0(0)a}
 =\frac{q_a(t)^2}{8E_0(0)}.
 }
 \tag{TL.748}
\]

Equations (TL.747)--(TL.748) are the pair-layer form of the slow charge in
(TL.713)--(TL.714): concentration occupies a real scale collar, and ordinary
residence of that collar consumes the one datum-finite energy-loss measure.

## 69. Every complete-pair layer doubling has a scale-independent deformation--loss charge

The fast alternative also has an exact complete-carrier coordinate.  Whenever
\(\mathcal Q_a>0\), normalize the whole Gaussian pair layer by

\[
 d\pi_{a,t}(\alpha,\beta)
 :=\frac{g_a(R_{\alpha\beta})|V_{\alpha\beta}|^2}
 {\mathcal Q_a(t)}\,d\alpha\,d\beta
\]

and define its mean longitudinal deformation rate

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak c_a(t)
 :=-\frac1{2a}\int R_{\alpha\beta}\cdot V_{\alpha\beta}\,d\pi_{a,t}
 =\frac{4\mathfrak p_a(t)}{\mathcal Q_a(t)}.
 }
 \tag{TL.749}
\]

No pair is selected and no sign is clipped: (TL.749) is the signed
longitudinal projection of the complete pair population.  Dividing (TL.741)
by \(\mathcal Q_a\) gives the joined law

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\log q_a
 +4\nu\frac{\mathcal L_a}{\mathcal Q_a}
 =\mathfrak c_a.
 }
 \tag{TL.750}
\]

Fix \(a,h>0\) and let \(J=[s,t]\) be a first upcrossing of this same layer:

\[
 q_a(s)=\frac h2,
 \qquad q_a(t)=h,
 \qquad \frac h2\le q_a(r)\le h\quad(r\in J).
 \tag{TL.751}
\]

Writing \(\ell=|J|\), (TL.748)--(TL.750) imply

\[
 \int_J\mathfrak c_a\,dr
 \ge\log2+\frac{\nu h}{4E_0(0)\sqrt a}\,\ell .
 \tag{TL.752}
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz, followed by minimization over \(\ell>0\), yields

\[
 \int_J\mathfrak c_a^2\,dr
 \ge\frac{\nu h\log2}{E_0(0)\sqrt a}.
\]

Since \(\mathcal Q_a\ge h\sqrt a/2\) throughout \(J\), every such
upcrossing therefore carries the scale-independent charge

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_J\mathcal Q_a(r)\mathfrak c_a(r)^2\,dr
 \ge\frac{\nu\log2}{2E_0(0)}h^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.753}
\]

This is the quantitative fast/slow synthesis on the complete carrier.
Lengthening the crossing increases the simultaneous loss term in (TL.752);
shortening it increases the squared deformation rate.  The heat length
cancels between the actual layer stock and its deformation rate, so passage
to finer scales does not make the charge in (TL.753) cheaper.

The remaining upper-bound problem is now on the correct object.  One must
insert the signed identity

\[
 \mathcal Q_a\mathfrak c_a=4\mathfrak p_a
 =-4J_1^{\rm VPI}(a/2,t)
 \tag{TL.754}
\]

into the completed translated \(q\)-complex before selecting the first
upcrossings, and prove that the resulting non-recounted sum of
\(\int\mathcal Q_a\mathfrak c_a^2\) is datum-finite.  Treating (TL.753) as
an independent positive action would lose the signed face cancellation and
replace the desired packing by an unproved square of the nonlinear current.
Equations (TL.742), (TL.745), and (TL.754) show exactly where the next signed
second-scale descent must act: on the material-time derivative of the same
cumulative Sobolev stock, with the irreversible part retained inside
(TL.750).

## 70. The surrounding rows pay diffuse octave proliferation and identify the concentrated pair layer

The passage from a layer upcrossing to a critical-height record must not
assume that one scale carries all of \(H\).  The exact alternative is obtained
from the Littlewood--Paley masses of the same velocity field.  Put

\[
 A_k(t):=2^k\|\Delta_ku(t)\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 H_d:=\sum_kA_k,
 \qquad
 E_d:=\sum_k2^{-k}A_k,
\]

\[
 K_d:=\sum_k2^kA_k,
 \qquad
 M_d:=\sup_kA_k.
 \tag{TL.755}
\]

These are simultaneous spectral readouts of one \(u(t)\), with

\[
 H_d\asymp H,
 \qquad E_d\asymp E_0,
 \qquad K_d\asymp E_1.
\]

There is an elementary breadth-sensitive interpolation which is stronger
than \(H_d^2\le E_dK_d\).  Choose integers \(a<b\) so that

\[
 \sum_{k\le a}A_k\le2^aE_d\le\frac{H_d}{4},
 \qquad
 \sum_{k\ge b}A_k\le2^{-b}K_d\le\frac{H_d}{4},
\]

with the adjacent choices failing.  The middle interval carries at least
\(H_d/2\), so \(b-a-1\ge H_d/(2M_d)\), while the adjacent-choice
inequalities give \(2^{b-a}<64E_dK_d/H_d^2\).  Hence

\[
 \boxed{
 E_dK_d
 \ge\frac{H_d^2}{32}
 \exp\!\left(\frac{\log2}{2}\frac{H_d}{M_d}\right).
 }
 \tag{TL.756}
\]

Finite truncation followed by monotone convergence covers an infinite
octave family.  The ordinary energy identity therefore gives the
datum-finite diffuse-occupancy bound

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T H_d(t)^2
 \exp\!\left(c\frac{H_d(t)}{M_d(t)}\right)dt
 \le\frac{C E_0(0)^2}{\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.757}
\]

In particular,

\[
 \boxed{
 \left|\left\{t: H_d(t)\ge h,
 \ \frac{H_d(t)}{M_d(t)}\ge N\right\}\right|
 \le\frac{C E_0(0)^2}{\nu h^2}e^{-cN}.
 }
 \tag{TL.758}
\]

This is an occupancy theorem, not an event count: an abstract schedule can
still place many short visits in the set on the left.  Its role in the
packing is exact.  It pays the branch in which critical height is spread
through increasingly many octaves; it leaves the concentrated branch, in
which one actual heat layer carries a fixed fraction of the current height.

That last statement is carrier-native.  The complete pair layers in
(TL.737) satisfy

\[
 \boxed{
 c\,M_d(t)
 \le\sup_{a>0}q_a(t)
 \le C\,M_d(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.759}
\]

For the lower inequality, take \(a\simeq2^{-2k}\) at an octave attaining
\(M_d\); the multiplier \(1-e^{-a|\xi|^2}\) is bounded below on that
octave and \(a^{-1/2}\simeq2^k\).  For the upper inequality, decompose
(TL.737) dyadically.  The coefficient of \(A_k\) in \(q_a\) is comparable
to

\[
 \frac{1-e^{-x_k}}{\sqrt{x_k}},
 \qquad x_k=a2^{2k},
\]

and the dyadic translates of this function have a uniformly bounded sum.
No maximizing chord, shell source, or independent envelope has been added.

Consequently, on the concentrated branch \(H_d/M_d\le N\), some complete
pair layer obeys

\[
 \boxed{
 q_a(t)\ge\frac{c}{N}H_d(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.760}
\]

Equations (TL.753) and (TL.760) now attach a definite deformation--loss
charge to every first-receiving concentrated layer.  Equations
(TL.757)--(TL.758) separately control diffuse temporal occupancy.  The
remaining signed packing step is not permitted to merge those two readings
into a scalar source square: it must assign the endpoint layer in (TL.760)
to its first crossing, carry \(\mathcal Q_a\mathfrak c_a=4\mathfrak p_a\)
through the completed scale faces, and use (TL.757) only on cells whose
breadth ratio is actually large.

## 71. One fixed heat depth turns the ultraviolet diagonal into derivative-depth transport

The joint limit \(\tau\downarrow0\), \(t\uparrow T_*\) in (TL.727) is not
the only way to resolve increasingly fine scales.  Fix once and for all one
heat depth \(\bar\tau>0\), put \(x=\bar\tau|\xi|^2\), and define the Poisson
multipliers

\[
 \omega_m(x):=e^{-2x}\frac{(2x)^m}{m!},
 \qquad m=0,1,2,\ldots .
\]

They form an exact partition of unity.  Accordingly, the positive depth
atoms

\[
 \boxed{
 \varepsilon_m(t)
 :=\frac12\langle u(t),
 \omega_m(\bar\tau\Lambda^2)u(t)\rangle
 \ge0
 }
 \tag{TL.761}
\]

obey

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\varepsilon_m(t)=E_0(t),
 \qquad
 \rho_m(\log\bar\tau,t)=m\varepsilon_m(t)
 \quad(m\ge1).
 }
 \tag{TL.762}
\]

Thus derivative depth does not introduce another reserve.  At one fixed
physical heat depth it is a positive partition of the same kinetic energy
whose continuously translated form is (TL.693).

The Poisson tails are exactly the current multipliers already present in
(TL.708):

\[
 q_n(x)=\sum_{m=n}^{\infty}\omega_m(x).
 \tag{TL.763}
\]

Adjoin the zeroth completed current

\[
 J_0^{\rm VPI}:=0,
 \qquad
 J_0^\nu:=\nu\|\Lambda u\|_2^2,
 \qquad J_0:=J_0^\nu,
\]

and, for every \(m\ge0\), put

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 P_m(t)&:=\operatorname{Re}\langle
 B(u,u),\omega_m(\bar\tau\Lambda^2)u\rangle,\\
 D_m(t)&:=\nu\langle\Lambda u,
 \omega_m(\bar\tau\Lambda^2)\Lambda u\rangle
 =\frac{\nu(m+1)}{\bar\tau}\varepsilon_{m+1}(t).
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.764}
\]

Equations (TL.710) and (TL.763) then give the exact depth-cell law

\[
 \boxed{
 J_m-J_{m+1}=P_m+D_m,
 \qquad
 \varepsilon_m'+J_m-J_{m+1}=0.
 }
 \tag{TL.765}
\]

Every finite derivative-depth face in (TL.765) is the same completed VPI
current with the two opposite orientations.  Moreover,

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{m\ge0}P_m=\langle B(u,u),u\rangle=0,
 \qquad
 \sum_{m\ge0}D_m=\nu\|\Lambda u\|_2^2=2\nu E_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.766}
\]

Hence the nonlinear row only redistributes the one fixed-depth population,
while the sum of its simultaneous positive rows is exactly the ordinary
datum-finite kinetic-energy loss.

This fixed-depth partition retains the critical height.  Define

\[
 \mathscr H_{\bar\tau}(t)
 :=\bar\tau^{-1/2}\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}
 \sqrt{m+1}\,\varepsilon_m(t).
 \tag{TL.767}
\]

If \(N\) is Poisson with mean \(2x\), then

\[
 \sum_{m\ge0}\sqrt{m+1}\,\omega_m(x)
 =\mathbb E\sqrt{N+1}.
\]

Jensen gives the upper bound
\(\mathbb E\sqrt{N+1}\le\sqrt{2x+1}\).  Interpolation between the first
and second moments of \(N+1\) gives

\[
 \mathbb E\sqrt{N+1}
 \ge\frac{(\mathbb E(N+1))^{3/2}}
 {(\mathbb E(N+1)^2)^{1/2}}
 \ge\frac1{\sqrt3}\sqrt{2x+1}.
\]

Consequently there are universal constants \(0<c<C<\infty\) such that

\[
 \boxed{
 c\bigl(H(t)+\bar\tau^{-1/2}E_0(t)\bigr)
 \le\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}(t)
 \le C\bigl(H(t)+\bar\tau^{-1/2}E_0(t)\bigr).
 }
 \tag{TL.768}
\]

Since \(E_0(t)\le E_0(0)\), boundedness of \(H\) is equivalent to
boundedness of \(\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}\) at any one fixed
\(\bar\tau>0\).  Fine-scale accumulation can therefore be followed as
\(m\to\infty\) at fixed heat depth; no freely moving physical cutoff is
needed.

Let \(a_m=\sqrt{m+1}\) and
\(\delta_m=a_m-a_{m-1}=\sqrt{m+1}-\sqrt m\) for \(m\ge1\).  Multiplying
(TL.765) by \(a_m\) and summing a finite depth ladder gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \frac d{dt}\sum_{m=0}^{N}a_m\varepsilon_m
 +J_0+\sum_{m=1}^{N}\delta_mJ_m
 ={}&a_NJ_{N+1}.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.769}
\]

For every compact classical time interval, smoothness makes
\(a_NJ_{N+1}\to0\).  Thus

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr H_{\bar\tau}'(t)
 =-\bar\tau^{-1/2}
 \left[J_0(t)+\sum_{m=1}^{\infty}\delta_mJ_m(t)\right].
 }
 \tag{TL.770}
\]

The positive part of the bracket in (TL.770) has an exact spectral meaning.
Since \(q_m(x)=\mathbb P(N\ge m)\),

\[
 1+\sum_{m\ge1}\delta_mq_m(x)
 =\mathbb E\sqrt{N+1}.
\]

Therefore the simultaneous viscous part of (TL.770) is

\[
 \boxed{
 \bar\tau^{-1/2}
 \left[J_0^\nu+\sum_{m\ge1}\delta_mJ_m^\nu\right]
 =\nu\bar\tau^{-1/2}
 \int |\xi|^2\mathbb E\sqrt{N_{2x}+1}
 |\widehat u(\xi)|^2\,d\xi,
 }
 \tag{TL.771}
\]

which is comparable to

\[
 \nu\|\Lambda^{3/2}u\|_2^2
 +\nu\bar\tau^{-1/2}\|\Lambda u\|_2^2.
\]

Equations (TL.761)--(TL.771) perform the same signed boundary cancellation
as (TL.703)--(TL.727), but move the only possible noncompact exterior to
\(m=\infty\) at one fixed heat depth.  The remaining fast estimate must act
on the signed depth current in (TL.769); estimating each \(P_m\) positively
before that sum would recreate the critical source norm rather than use the
depth cancellation.

## 72. The exact depth source--loss square identifies the critical valuation that must remain signed

The atom law (TL.765) has a canonical source--loss quadratic form.  Whenever
\(\varepsilon_{m+1}>0\), Cauchy--Schwarz with the adjacent Poisson weights
and

\[
 \frac{\omega_m(x)^2}{\omega_{m+1}(x)}
 =\frac{m+1}{2x}\omega_m(x)
\]

gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \frac{\bar\tau}{m+1}
 \frac{|P_m|^2}{\varepsilon_{m+1}}
 \le
 \left\langle\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u),
 \omega_m(\bar\tau\Lambda^2)
 \Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\right\rangle .
 }
 \tag{TL.772}
\]

The left side is defined as zero when the adjacent stock vanishes; then the
same weighted Cauchy--Schwarz identity gives \(P_m=0\).  Summing before any
record selection yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{m\ge0}\frac{|P_m|^2}{D_m}
 \le\frac1\nu\|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.773}
\]

This is the exact unweighted depth action: it is the smallest positive
quadratic form obtained by pairing each complete source atom with its own
simultaneous viscous atom.  It is below the critical valuation.  Indeed,
multiplying (TL.772) by \(\sqrt{(m+1)/\bar\tau}\), summing, and using the
Poisson estimate in (TL.768) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{m\ge0}\sqrt{\frac{m+1}{\bar\tau}}
 \frac{|P_m|^2}{D_m}
 \le\frac C\nu
 \left(
 \|\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)\|_2^2
 +\bar\tau^{-1/2}\|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2
 \right).
 }
 \tag{TL.774}
\]

The second term is lower order at the fixed heat depth.  The first is the
actual critical tangent.  The standard product estimates read

\[
 \|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2
 \le C H E_1,
 \qquad
 \|\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)\|_2^2
 \le C H\|\Lambda^{3/2}u\|_2^2.
 \tag{TL.775}
\]

The unnormalized right side of (TL.773) is not itself paid by the kinetic
energy law.  Its state-normalized form is.  With the integrand defined as
zero at the zero solution, (TL.775) and the ordinary energy identity give

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\frac1{H(t)}
 \sum_{m\ge0}\frac{|P_m(t)|^2}{D_m(t)}\,dt
 \le\frac C\nu\int_0^TE_1(t)\,dt
 \le\frac{C E_0(0)}{2\nu^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.775a}
\]

Thus a cellwise Young inequality applied to (TL.765) before the depth sum
returns the familiar critical wall in the first term of (TL.774).  This
calculation fixes the operation required next: apply signed second-depth
descent to the complete sum in (TL.769), so that the \(\delta_m\)-weighted
source is joined to its adjacent negative trough before any use of
(TL.772).  Only a remainder carrying the reciprocal state normalization in
(TL.775a), rather than the critical factor in (TL.774), is datum-finite.
In fixed-depth coordinates, this is exactly the requested order

\[
 \boxed{
 \text{cancel every shared derivative-depth face}
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 \text{select the first record}
 \;\longrightarrow\;
 \text{apply source--loss positivity only to the exposed remainder}.
 }
 \tag{TL.776}
\]

## 73. The critical source sees only the joint scale--handedness defect

The fixed-depth scalar population does not contain the full geometry of its
nonlinear source.  That geometry is supplied by two exact orthogonalities of
the same velocity field.  Let

\[
 \mathcal C:=\operatorname{curl},
 \qquad
 B:=\mathbb P(u\cdot\nabla u).
\]

For a smooth decaying divergence-free field,

\[
 \boxed{
 \langle B,u\rangle=0,
 \qquad
 \langle B,\mathcal Cu\rangle=0.
 }
 \tag{TL.777}
\]

The first identity is kinetic-energy conservation by the complete quadratic
current.  For the second, use

\[
 (u\cdot\nabla)u=(\mathcal Cu)\times u
 +\nabla\frac{|u|^2}{2}.
\]

The cross product is pointwise perpendicular to \(\mathcal Cu\), the gradient
is orthogonal to the divergence-free vorticity, and the Leray projection does
not change the pairing.  Thus (TL.777) is one joined incompressible tangent,
not a separation of the evolution into two mechanisms.

Use the homogeneous Sobolev inner product

\[
 (f,g)_s:=\langle\Lambda^sf,\Lambda^sg\rangle.
\]

For every real \(s\) for which the displayed rows are finite, define the
energy--helicity centered defect

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal V_s^{\rm eh}
 :=\min_{\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb R}
 \frac12\bigl\|
 \Lambda^s(\Lambda-\alpha I-\beta\mathcal C)u
 \bigr\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.778}
\]

Let \((\alpha_s,\beta_s)\) be any minimizer and put

\[
 A_s:=\Lambda-\alpha_sI-\beta_s\mathcal C.
\]

The normal equations are

\[
 \boxed{
 (A_su,u)_s=0,
 \qquad
 (A_su,\mathcal Cu)_s=0.
 }
 \tag{TL.779}
\]

If the two spanning vectors are dependent, these equations are read as the
orthogonal projection onto their actual span; the residual \(A_su\) is still
unique.  In the helical decomposition
\(\mathcal Cu_\pm=\pm\Lambda u_\pm\), the multiplier of \(A_s\) is

\[
 \bigl[(1-\beta_s)|\xi|-\alpha_s\bigr]\widehat u_+
 +\bigl[(1+\beta_s)|\xi|-\alpha_s\bigr]\widehat u_-.
 \tag{TL.780}
\]

Consequently \(\mathcal V_s^{\rm eh}=0\) whenever each nonzero helical sector
is carried on one frequency sphere, even when the two spheres have different
radii.  This contains both exact degeneracies already visible earlier: a
one-frequency field and a pure-helicity field cannot produce critical-height
growth.

The source itself is exactly centered by this defect.  From (TL.777), for
every \(\alpha,\beta\),

\[
 \boxed{
 P_H=-\langle B,\Lambda u\rangle
 =-\langle B,(\Lambda-\alpha I-\beta\mathcal C)u\rangle.
 }
 \tag{TL.781}
\]

At \(s=1/2\), choose the minimizer in (TL.778).  The same product estimate as
in (TL.673), now after both invariant directions have been removed, gives

\[
 \boxed{
 |P_H|
 \le C E_1\sqrt{\mathcal V_{1/2}^{\rm eh}}.
 }
 \tag{TL.782}
\]

Indeed \(\|B\|_{3/2}\le CE_1\), while Sobolev embedding gives
\(\|A_{1/2}u\|_3\le C\|\Lambda^{1/2}A_{1/2}u\|_2\).
Since orthogonal projection also gives

\[
 E_{3/2}
 =\mathcal V_{1/2}^{\rm eh}+\mathcal P_{1/2}^{\rm eh},
 \qquad
 \mathcal P_{1/2}^{\rm eh}\ge0,
 \tag{TL.783}
\]

the complete critical balance sharpens to

\[
 \boxed{
 H'
 \le CE_1\sqrt{\mathcal V_{1/2}^{\rm eh}}
 -2\nu\mathcal V_{1/2}^{\rm eh}
 -2\nu\mathcal P_{1/2}^{\rm eh}.
 }
 \tag{TL.784}
\]

Thus neither common amplitude, a single occupied radius, nor handedness by
itself can supply a positive critical tangent.  A positive tangent must be a
simultaneous departure from the energy and helicity directions of the same
field, and the resulting defect already lies in the exact viscous Sobolev row.

The defect has its own exact tower law.  Differentiating at the minimizing
\((\alpha_s,\beta_s)\) produces no \(\alpha_s'\) or \(\beta_s'\) terms because
of (TL.779).  Since \(A_s\), \(\Lambda\), and \(\mathcal C\) commute,

\[
 \boxed{
 (\mathcal V_s^{\rm eh})'
 +2\nu\mathcal W_s^{\rm eh}
 =\mathcal Q_s^{\rm eh},
 }
 \tag{TL.785}
\]

where

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal W_s^{\rm eh}
 &:=\frac12\|\Lambda^{s+1}A_su\|_2^2,\\
 \mathcal Q_s^{\rm eh}
 &:=-\langle B,\Lambda^{2s}A_s^2u\rangle.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.786}
\]

Moreover the next minimization can only reduce the same residual:

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal W_s^{\rm eh}\ge\mathcal V_{s+1}^{\rm eh}.
 }
 \tag{TL.787}
\]

Equations (TL.777)--(TL.787) supply the anisotropic row required by the
fixed-depth construction.  They also specify how it must be used: the
Poisson population handles scale storage and shared-face cancellation, while
the energy--helicity projection deletes the two source directions that are
incapable of critical growth before a positive record remainder is estimated.
The exposed remainder is therefore not an arbitrary scalar depth transfer;
it is the signed source \(\mathcal Q_s^{\rm eh}\) of the same centered defect
tower.

## 74. Loss-centering pays every narrow-depth positive record increment

The atom law (TL.765) contains a second exact centering which acts after all
completed depth faces have cancelled.  Keep the fixed heat depth
\(\bar\tau\), set

\[
 a_m:=\sqrt{m+1},
 \qquad
 K(t):=\sum_{m\ge0}a_m\varepsilon_m(t)
 =\sqrt{\bar\tau}\,\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}(t),
 \tag{TL.788}
\]

and define the simultaneous source action and critical loss rate

\[
 \mathcal A(t):=\sum_{m\ge0}\frac{|P_m|^2}{D_m},
 \qquad
 \mathcal L(t):=\sum_{m\ge0}a_mD_m.
 \tag{TL.789}
\]

Use the zero convention already fixed in Section 72.  Put
\(D_\bullet:=\sum_mD_m\).  When \(D_\bullet>0\), let

\[
 \bar a_D:=\frac{\mathcal L}{D_\bullet},
 \qquad
 \mathcal V_D:=\sum_{m\ge0}D_m(a_m-\bar a_D)^2;
 \tag{TL.790}
\]

when \(D_\bullet=0\), set \(\bar a_D=\mathcal V_D=0\).  This distribution
is not a second fluid or a freely selected measure: \(D_m\) is the positive
row of the same completed atom current in (TL.764).

Let

\[
 \mathcal S:=-\sum_{m\ge0}a_mP_m
\]

be the signed nonlinear contribution to \(K'\).  Since
\(\sum_mP_m=0\), the loss mean may be subtracted without changing it:

\[
 \mathcal S=-\sum_{m\ge0}(a_m-\bar a_D)P_m.
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz now gives the exact post-cancellation estimate

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal S^2\le\mathcal A\mathcal V_D,
 \qquad
 K'=\mathcal S-\mathcal L,
 \qquad
 K'\le\sqrt{\mathcal A\mathcal V_D}-\mathcal L.
 }
 \tag{TL.791}
\]

The negative term in (TL.791) is the same simultaneous loss used to center
the source; it has not been estimated separately and then recombined.

Fix \(\kappa>0\) and call a time loss-concentrated when

\[
 \mathcal V_D\le\kappa\mathcal L.
\]

On this set, maximizing the right side of
\(\sqrt{\kappa\mathcal A\mathcal L}-\mathcal L\) over
\(\mathcal L\ge0\) yields

\[
 \boxed{
 [K']_+\,\mathbf1_{\{\mathcal V_D\le\kappa\mathcal L\}}
 \le\frac\kappa4\mathcal A.
 }
 \tag{TL.792}
\]

Using (TL.768), (TL.775a), and \(K=\sqrt{\bar\tau}\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}\)
therefore gives the datum-finite positive logarithmic variation

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T
 [\partial_t\log\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}]_+
 \mathbf1_{\{\mathcal V_D\le\kappa\mathcal L\}}\,dt
 \le
 \frac{C\kappa}{\sqrt{\bar\tau}}
 \int_0^T\frac{\mathcal A}{H}\,dt
 \le
 \frac{C\kappa E_0(0)}{\nu^2\sqrt{\bar\tau}}.
 }
 \tag{TL.793}
\]

Thus all first-record strengthening carried while the loss population remains
narrow in derivative depth is already paid by the normalized action of
Section 72.  In particular, such times can contribute only finitely much to
the logarithm of the fixed-depth critical moment and cannot support an
unbounded record ladder.

The complementary set has an exact, carrier-native meaning.  Variance may be
written without a chosen center as

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal V_D
 =\frac1{2D_\bullet}
 \sum_{m,r\ge0}D_mD_r(a_m-a_r)^2
 =\frac\nu{\bar\tau}
 \min_{c\in\mathbb R}
 \sum_{r\ge1}r\varepsilon_r(\sqrt r-c)^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.794}
\]

Moreover

\[
 \mathcal L
 =\frac\nu{\bar\tau}
 \sum_{r\ge1}r^{3/2}\varepsilon_r.
 \tag{TL.795}
\]

Hence the only positive record contribution not covered by (TL.793) is a
state in which the irreversible row of the same field simultaneously occupies
separated derivative depths:

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal V_D>\kappa\mathcal L.
 }
 \tag{TL.796}
\]

This is a strict reduction of the fast term.  A one-depth or asymptotically
one-depth pulse lies in (TL.792), not in the remainder.  The broad remainder
in (TL.796) must now be inserted into the signed commutator (TL.728)--(TL.731)
and the energy--helicity centering (TL.781), so that separated depths are
joined by their actual common VPI triads before the record-positive part is
taken.  No independent breadth source has been introduced.

## 75. The ultraviolet broad source is common to both helicities

The energy--helicity centering can be placed directly on the fixed-depth
Poisson population.  Let

\[
 \Pi_\pm:=\frac12(I\pm\Lambda^{-1}\mathcal C),
 \qquad u_\pm:=\Pi_\pm u,
\]

and define

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \varepsilon_m^\pm
 &:=\frac12\langle u_\pm,
 \omega_m(\bar\tau\Lambda^2)u_\pm\rangle,\\
 P_m^\pm
 &:=\operatorname{Re}\langle B,
 \omega_m(\bar\tau\Lambda^2)u_\pm\rangle,\\
 D_m^\pm
 &:=\nu\langle\Lambda u_\pm,
 \omega_m(\bar\tau\Lambda^2)\Lambda u_\pm\rangle.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.797}
\]

These are orthogonal readings of the same atom law:

\[
 \varepsilon_m=\varepsilon_m^++\varepsilon_m^-,
 \qquad
 P_m=P_m^++P_m^-,
 \qquad
 D_m=D_m^++D_m^-.
 \tag{TL.798}
\]

Let

\[
 s(x):=\sum_{m\ge0}\sqrt{m+1}\,\omega_m(x)
 =\mathbb E\sqrt{N_{2x}+1},
\]

and write

\[
 S_{\bar\tau}(\Lambda)
 :=\bar\tau^{-1/2}s(\bar\tau\Lambda^2).
\]

Then

\[
 \mathscr H_{\bar\tau}^\pm
 :=\bar\tau^{-1/2}\sum_{m\ge0}\sqrt{m+1}\,
 \varepsilon_m^\pm
 =\frac12\langle u_\pm,S_{\bar\tau}(\Lambda)u_\pm\rangle,
 \tag{TL.799}
\]

and \(\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}=\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}^+
+\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}^-\).  If \(N_\lambda\) is Poisson with mean
\(\lambda\), Jensen gives
\(\mathbb E\sqrt{N_\lambda+1}\le\sqrt{\lambda+1}\), while

\[
 \mathbb E|\sqrt{N_\lambda}-\sqrt\lambda|
 \le\frac{\mathbb E|N_\lambda-\lambda|}{\sqrt\lambda}
 \le1.
\]

Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 |s(x)-\sqrt{2x}|\le1
 \qquad(x\ge0).
 }
 \tag{TL.800}
\]

Thus

\[
 S_{\bar\tau}(\Lambda)
 =\sqrt2\Lambda+R_{\bar\tau}(\Lambda),
 \qquad
 \|R_{\bar\tau}\|_{L^2\to L^2}
 \le\bar\tau^{-1/2}.
 \tag{TL.801}
\]

Let \(\mathcal S_\pm\) denote the nonlinear contribution to
\((\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}^\pm)'\).  Since
\(u_+-u_-=\Lambda^{-1}\mathcal Cu\), (TL.777) and (TL.801) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal S_+-\mathcal S_-
 &=-\langle B,S_{\bar\tau}(\Lambda)(u_+-u_-)\rangle\\
 &=-\langle B,R_{\bar\tau}(\Lambda)
 \Lambda^{-1}\mathcal Cu\rangle.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.802}
\]

The leading critical multiplier has cancelled exactly by helicity
conservation.  The remainder is one derivative lower:

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathcal S_+-\mathcal S_-|
 \le\bar\tau^{-1/2}
 \|\Lambda^{-1}B\|_2\|\Lambda u\|_2
 \le C\bar\tau^{-1/2}\sqrt H\,E_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.803}
\]

By (TL.768), there is a datum-dependent
\(h_0\simeq\bar\tau^{-1/2}E_0(0)\) such that
\(\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}\ge h\ge h_0\) implies
\(H\ge c h\).  The ordinary energy identity therefore gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\{\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}\ge h\}}
 \frac{|\mathcal S_+-\mathcal S_-|}
 {\mathscr H_{\bar\tau}}\,dt
 \le
 \frac{C E_0(0)}{\nu\sqrt{\bar\tau h}}
 \xrightarrow[h\to\infty]{}0.
 }
 \tag{TL.804}
\]

Hence an unbounded fixed-depth record cannot be financed by repeatedly
switching the nonlinear source from one handedness to the other.  At the
ultraviolet record levels the source is asymptotically one common delivery to
both helical rows; only their simultaneous losses may differ.
