The same conclusion has an exact one-triad form.  For one geometric helical
triad with wave-number magnitudes \(\kappa_i\), helicity signs
\(s_i\in\{\pm1\}\), and nonlinear modal-energy rates \(T_i\), put
\(h_i=s_i\kappa_i\).  Conservation of energy and helicity gives

\[
 \sum_{i=1}^3T_i=0,
 \qquad
 \sum_{i=1}^3h_iT_i=0.
\]

Therefore, for one signed scalar triad current \(\gamma\),

\[
 \boxed{
 (T_1,T_2,T_3)
 =\gamma(h_3-h_2,h_1-h_3,h_2-h_1).
 }
 \tag{TL.805}
\]

Its contribution to critical-height production is

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{i=1}^3\kappa_iT_i
 =\gamma
 \det\!\begin{pmatrix}
 1&1&1\\
 h_1&h_2&h_3\\
 \kappa_1&\kappa_2&\kappa_3
 \end{pmatrix}.
 }
 \tag{TL.806}
\]

The determinant vanishes for a homochiral triad.  If, for example,
\(s_1=s_2=-s_3\), then its magnitude is

\[
 \boxed{
 2\kappa_3|\kappa_2-\kappa_1|.
 }
 \tag{TL.807}
\]

Thus the broad remainder in (TL.796) is now geometrically typed.  Critical
production requires a heterochiral triad and a radial separation between its
same-helicity members.  This is precisely the joint scale--handedness defect
of Section 73.  The remaining record packing must follow that same signed
triad current through successive depth faces; arbitrary transfer between two
separated scalar atoms is no longer an admitted representation of the source.

## 76. The broad scale--handedness source is one signed curvature current

The helical split in Section 75 can be recombined without losing its sign by
using the spectral resolution of curl itself.  On divergence-free fields,

\[
 \mathcal C=\operatorname{curl}=\mathcal C^*,
 \qquad
 \mathcal C^2=\Lambda^2.
\]

Let \(E_{\mathcal C}(d\eta)\) be its spectral resolution and define the
nonnegative energy measure and the signed nonlinear source measure by

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 d\mu_t(\eta)
 &:=\frac12\,d\langle E_{\mathcal C}(\eta)u(t),u(t)\rangle,\\
 d\mathfrak n_t(\eta)
 &:=-\operatorname{Re}\,
 d\langle B(t),E_{\mathcal C}(\eta)u(t)\rangle.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.808}
\]

These are two readings of the same velocity and its same instantaneous VPI
tangent.  No helical mode is evolved independently.  For every smooth test
\(f\), spectral calculus and the Navier--Stokes equation give the exact weak
law

\[
 \boxed{
 \frac d{dt}\int_{\mathbb R}f(\eta)\,d\mu_t(\eta)
 =\int_{\mathbb R}f(\eta)\,d\mathfrak n_t(\eta)
 -2\nu\int_{\mathbb R}\eta^2f(\eta)\,d\mu_t(\eta).
 }
 \tag{TL.809}
\]

The first three absolute moments are the surrounding Sobolev rows:

\[
 \int d\mu_t=E_0,
 \qquad
 \int|\eta|\,d\mu_t=H,
 \qquad
 \int\eta^2\,d\mu_t=E_1,
 \qquad
 \int|\eta|^3\,d\mu_t=E_{3/2}.
\]

The two orthogonalities in (TL.777) are now exactly the zeroth and first
signed-frequency moments of one source:

\[
 \boxed{
 \int d\mathfrak n_t=0,
 \qquad
 \int\eta\,d\mathfrak n_t=0.
 }
 \tag{TL.810}
\]

Thus define its second primitive

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak Q(a,t)
 :=\int_{(-\infty,a]}(a-\eta)\,d\mathfrak n_t(\eta)
 =\int_{[a,\infty)}(\eta-a)\,d\mathfrak n_t(\eta).
 }
 \tag{TL.811}
\]

Both expressions agree by (TL.810), and distributionally
\(\partial_a^2\mathfrak Q=\mathfrak n_t\), with the zeroth and first
boundary traces vanishing at both ends.  Consequently, for every test whose
second derivative is a finite measure,

\[
 \boxed{
 \int f(\eta)\,d\mathfrak n_t(\eta)
 =\int \mathfrak Q(a,t)\,df''(a).
 }
 \tag{TL.812}
\]

Taking \(f(\eta)=|\eta|\), whose second derivative is \(2\delta_0\),
gives the exact critical balance

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)
 =2\mathfrak Q(0,t)-2\nu E_{3/2}(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.813}
\]

This is the all-field version of (TL.805)--(TL.807).  To see the identity on
one constituent without turning that constituent into a separate source,
order its signed helical frequencies as
\(\eta_1<\eta_2<\eta_3\).  Energy and helicity conservation force its
modal-energy tangent to have the form

\[
 (T_1,T_2,T_3)
 =\gamma(\eta_3-\eta_2,\eta_1-\eta_3,\eta_2-\eta_1).
\]

Its contribution to (TL.811) is the triangular function

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak Q_{\rm tri}(a)
 =\begin{cases}
 \gamma(\eta_3-\eta_2)(a-\eta_1),
 &\eta_1\le a\le\eta_2,\\[1mm]
 \gamma(\eta_2-\eta_1)(\eta_3-a),
 &\eta_2\le a\le\eta_3,\\[1mm]
 0,&\text{otherwise}.
 \end{cases}
 }
 \tag{TL.814}
\]

For \(\gamma>0\), energy is removed from the middle signed frequency and
delivered to the two outer signed frequencies without changing either total
energy or signed-frequency barycenter.  Hence (TL.814) is nonnegative and
every convex spectral moment increases; reversing \(\gamma\) reverses all
those signs.  The critical moment is exceptional only because \(|\eta|\) is
affine on each open half-line: it changes precisely when the triangular
current crosses the helicity origin.  For the ordering
\((-\kappa_3,\kappa_1,\kappa_2)\),

\[
 2\mathfrak Q_{\rm tri}(0)
 =2\gamma\kappa_3(\kappa_2-\kappa_1),
\]

which is (TL.807).

Most importantly, (TL.811)--(TL.813) are formed after all triads have been
summed with their signs.  Record-positive selection is therefore made only
on the single complete value \(\mathfrak Q(0,t)\); positive and negative
triads are not separately counted.  The Poisson-depth sources are exact
tests of this same current.  With
\(\phi_m(\eta)=\omega_m(\bar\tau\eta^2)\), two integrations by parts give

\[
 \boxed{
 P_m(t)
 =-\int\phi_m(\eta)\,d\mathfrak n_t(\eta)
 =-\int\phi_m''(a)\mathfrak Q(a,t)\,da.
 }
 \tag{TL.815}
\]

Thus the signed second-depth descent of Sections 71--74 and the
energy--helicity geometry are not parallel arguments.  They are respectively
the Poisson tests and the signed-frequency curvature representation of the
same nonlinear VPI current.  The broad remainder in (TL.796) is now one
curvature trace, not an arbitrary interaction between separately selected
depth atoms.

## 77. Every bounded-frequency part of the curvature trace has datum-finite positive logarithmic variation

The curvature representation also pays a first genuine part of the broad
remainder.  At every classical time, the source measure in (TL.808) is
absolutely continuous with respect to \(\mu_t\); write

\[
 d\mathfrak n_t(\eta)=g(\eta,t)\,d\mu_t(\eta).
\]

Disintegration on the spectral fibers of \(\mathcal C\) and Cauchy--Schwarz
give

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak A(t)
 :=\int_{\mathbb R}\frac{|g(\eta,t)|^2}{\eta^2}\,d\mu_t(\eta)
 \le2\|\Lambda^{-1}B(t)\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.816}
\]

The integrand is set to zero on the null spectral fiber.  This is the
continuous helical-frequency form of the unweighted depth action in
(TL.773), and (TL.775) plus the energy identity yield

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\frac{\mathfrak A(t)}{H(t)}\,dt
 \le C\int_0^TE_1(t)\,dt
 \le\frac{C E_0(0)}{2\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.817}
\]

Now fix an actual signed-frequency radius \(R>0\).  The two forms of
(TL.811) at the origin read

\[
 \mathfrak Q(0,t)
 =\int_{\eta>0}\eta g\,d\mu_t
 =-\int_{\eta<0}\eta g\,d\mu_t.
 \tag{TL.818}
\]

On the positive side put

\[
 Q_{\le R}^+(t):=\int_{0<\eta\le R}\eta g\,d\mu_t,
 \qquad
 Q_{>R}^+(t):=\int_{\eta>R}\eta g\,d\mu_t.
\]

If \(H'(t)>0\), then (TL.813) gives
\(\mathfrak Q(0,t)>\nu E_{3/2}(t)>0\).  If at the same time

\[
 |Q_{>R}^+(t)|\le\frac12\mathfrak Q(0,t),
\]

then \(\mathfrak Q(0,t)\le2|Q_{\le R}^+(t)|\).  Cauchy--Schwarz in the
single measure \(\mu_t\) gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 |Q_{\le R}^+|^2
 &\le
 \left(\int_{0<\eta\le R}\frac{|g|^2}{\eta^2}\,d\mu_t\right)
 \left(\int_{0<\eta\le R}\eta^4\,d\mu_t\right)\\
 &\le R\,\mathfrak A(t)E_{3/2}(t).
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.819}
\]

Combining (TL.813) and (TL.819), and maximizing over
\(E_{3/2}\ge0\), proves the pointwise bound

\[
 \boxed{
 [H'(t)]_+\,
 \mathbf1_{\{|Q_{>R}^+|\le\mathfrak Q(0)/2\}}
 \le\frac{2R}{\nu}\,\mathfrak A(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.820}
\]

Therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T[\partial_t\log H(t)]_+
 \mathbf1_{\{|Q_{>R}^+|\le\mathfrak Q(0)/2\}}\,dt
 \le\frac{C R E_0(0)}{\nu^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.821}
\]

The identical calculation on the negative side uses

\[
 Q_{\le R}^-(t):=-\int_{-R\le\eta<0}\eta g\,d\mu_t,
 \qquad
 Q_{>R}^-(t):=-\int_{\eta<-R}\eta g\,d\mu_t.
\]

Hence, for each fixed \(R\), the portion of record-positive variation which
can remain after the two copies of (TL.821) have been removed is contained
in the set

\[
 \boxed{
 |Q_{>R}^+(t)|>\frac12\mathfrak Q(0,t),
 \qquad
 |Q_{>R}^-(t)|>\frac12\mathfrak Q(0,t).
 }
 \tag{TL.822}
\]

This is stronger than saying that the loss population is broad.  After the
complete source has been summed, any part capable of carrying infinite
positive logarithmic variation must carry a nonnegligible part of the same
outward curvature beyond every fixed radius on both helical sides.  A
bounded-frequency source, a one-sided helical source, and repeated switching
between the two sides all have datum-finite positive logarithmic variation.
The only remaining exterior is therefore the joint two-sided ultraviolet
limit of the same curvature current, which is exactly the limit to be joined
to the \(m=\infty\) face in (TL.769) before record positivity is taken.

## 78. Capped helical-frequency moments turn the two-sided ultraviolet exterior into one cancelling boundary current

The two high-frequency sides in (TL.822) are the two faces of one exact
receiver.  For \(R>0\), define the capped and excess critical stocks

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 C_R(t)&:=\int_{\mathbb R}\min\{|\eta|,R\}\,d\mu_t(\eta),\\
 T_R(t)&:=\int_{\mathbb R}(|\eta|-R)_+\,d\mu_t(\eta).
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.823}
\]

They are nonnegative readings of the same spectral measure and
\(H=C_R+T_R\).  Distributionally,

\[
 \partial_\eta^2\min\{|\eta|,R\}
 =2\delta_0-\delta_R-\delta_{-R},
 \qquad
 \partial_\eta^2(|\eta|-R)_+
 =\delta_R+\delta_{-R}.
\]

Put

\[
 \mathcal Q_R(t):=\mathfrak Q(R,t)+\mathfrak Q(-R,t).
\]

Equations (TL.809) and (TL.812) give the two exact complementary laws

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 C_R'
 +2\nu\int\eta^2\min\{|\eta|,R\}\,d\mu_t
 &=2\mathfrak Q(0)-\mathcal Q_R,\\
 T_R'
 +2\nu\int\eta^2(|\eta|-R)_+\,d\mu_t
 &=\mathcal Q_R.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.824}
\]

Their sum is (TL.813).  In particular, \(\mathcal Q_R\) is not a newly
selected shell flux: it is exactly the complete VPI source entering the
actual excess critical stock beyond \(R\).  At every fixed classical time,

\[
 \mathcal Q_0=2\mathfrak Q(0),
 \qquad
 \lim_{R\to\infty}\mathcal Q_R=0.
 \tag{TL.825}
\]

Fix \(q>1\), choose \(R_0>0\), and put \(R_j=q^jR_0\).  The nested cap
layer

\[
 A_j:=C_{R_{j+1}}-C_{R_j}
 =T_{R_j}-T_{R_{j+1}}\ge0
\]

obeys, by subtraction in (TL.824),

\[
 A_j'+D_j=\mathcal Q_{R_j}-\mathcal Q_{R_{j+1}},
 \qquad
 D_j:=2\nu\int\eta^2a_j(|\eta|)\,d\mu_t,
 \tag{TL.826}
\]

where

\[
 a_j(k):=\min\{k,R_{j+1}\}-\min\{k,R_j\}.
\]

Thus adjacent nested layers already share exactly one boundary current with
opposite orientations.  The cumulative layer \(A_j\) is not localized,
because \(a_j(k)=(q-1)R_j\) for all \(k\ge R_{j+1}\).  Apply the signed
second-scale operator before taking any positive part:

\[
 \boxed{
 B_j:=A_j-q^{-1}A_{j+1}
 =R_j\int_{\mathbb R}\beta_j(|\eta|)\,d\mu_t(\eta)\ge0,
 }
 \tag{TL.827}
\]

with

\[
 \beta_j(k):=
 \begin{cases}
 0,&k\le R_j,\\
 k/R_j-1,&R_j<k\le qR_j,\\
 q-k/(qR_j),&qR_j<k\le q^2R_j,\\
 0,&k>q^2R_j.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{TL.828}
\]

The kernel is nonnegative and compactly supported in two adjacent physical
octaves.  No tail stock remains hidden in it.  Applying the same operator to
(TL.826) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 B_j'+\widetilde D_j
 =\mathcal K_j,
 }
 \tag{TL.829}
\]

where

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \widetilde D_j
 &:=D_j-q^{-1}D_{j+1}
 =2\nu R_j\int\eta^2\beta_j(|\eta|)\,d\mu_t\ge0,\\
 \mathcal K_j
 &:=\mathcal Q_{R_j}
 -(1+q^{-1})\mathcal Q_{R_{j+1}}
 +q^{-1}\mathcal Q_{R_{j+2}}.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.830}
\]

For \(k\in[R_n,R_{n+1})\), only \(\beta_n(k)\) and
\(\beta_{n-1}(k)\) are nonzero, and direct substitution gives

\[
 \sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}\beta_j(k)=q-1,
 \qquad
 \sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}R_j\beta_j(k)
 =(1-q^{-1})k.
\]

Tonelli therefore yields the exact two surrounding-row identities

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j\frac{B_j}{R_j}=(q-1)E_0,
 \qquad
 \sum_jB_j=(1-q^{-1})H,
 }
 \tag{TL.831}
\]

and likewise

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j\frac{\widetilde D_j}{R_j}
 =2\nu(q-1)E_1,
 \qquad
 \sum_j\widetilde D_j
 =2\nu(1-q^{-1})E_{3/2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.832}
\]

Equations (TL.827)--(TL.832) are a completed second-scale descent, not a
comparison.  The same positive two-octave stocks reconstruct both the
datum-finite energy row and the critical height, with one power of their
actual physical radius separating those readings.  Every common ultraviolet
face in (TL.824) has cancelled before the positive localized stock in
(TL.827) is formed.

## 79. The descended two-octave chain has a datum-finite energy-normalized source--loss action

Divide the localized law (TL.829) by its actual radius and set

\[
 e_j:=\frac{B_j}{R_j},
 \qquad
 d_j:=\frac{\widetilde D_j}{R_j},
 \qquad
 s_j:=\frac{\mathcal K_j}{R_j}.
 \tag{TL.833}
\]

The boundary term is a genuine nearest-neighbour flux difference.  Namely,

\[
 \boxed{
 e_j'+d_j=F_j-F_{j+1}=s_j,
 \qquad
 F_j:=\frac{\mathcal Q_{R_j}-\mathcal Q_{R_{j+1}}}{R_j}.
 }
 \tag{TL.834}
\]

The source density from Section 77 gives an exact local form for the same
right side:

\[
 \boxed{
 s_j(t)=\int_{\mathbb R}\beta_j(|\eta|)g(\eta,t)\,d\mu_t(\eta).
 }
 \tag{TL.835}
\]

Define the corresponding local source action

\[
 \mathfrak a_j(t)
 :=\int_{\mathbb R}\beta_j(|\eta|)
 \frac{|g(\eta,t)|^2}{\eta^2}\,d\mu_t(\eta).
 \tag{TL.836}
\]

Because the source and the simultaneous loss are tested by the same
nonnegative two-octave kernel, Cauchy--Schwarz now has no depth-variance
remainder:

\[
 \boxed{
 |s_j|^2
 \le\mathfrak a_j\int\beta_j(|\eta|)\eta^2\,d\mu_t
 =\frac{\mathfrak a_jd_j}{2\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.837}
\]

Using (TL.834) and maximizing
\(\sqrt{\mathfrak a_jd_j/(2\nu)}-d_j\) over \(d_j\ge0\) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 [e_j']_+\le\frac{\mathfrak a_j}{8\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.838}
\]

The overlap identities in (TL.831) also apply to the action kernels:

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j\mathfrak a_j=(q-1)\mathfrak A,
 \qquad
 \sum_jd_j=2\nu(q-1)E_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.839}
\]

Consequently, with the zero-state convention retained,

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\frac1{H(t)}
 \sum_j[e_j'(t)]_+\,dt
 \le
 \frac{q-1}{8\nu}
 \int_0^T\frac{\mathfrak A(t)}{H(t)}\,dt
 \le\frac{C(q-1)E_0(0)}{\nu^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.840}
\]

This pays all positive variation of the energy-normalized, compact
two-octave populations after the complete signed boundary cancellation.
It also identifies the final critical operation exactly.  Restoring
\(B_j=R_je_j\) before summing (TL.838) would introduce
\(\sum_jR_j\mathfrak a_j\), the uncontrolled critical source square.
Therefore the fast record packing must use the signs of the adjacent fluxes
\(F_j-F_{j+1}\) in (TL.834) to move the physical radius through the q-grid
before the record-positive critical valuation in the second identity of
(TL.831) is selected.  All tail, refill, helical-switching, and broad-depth
terms have already been absorbed into this one nearest-neighbour chain.

## 80. The actual adjacent flux has a datum-finite cumulative-tail action

The divergence \(s_j=F_j-F_{j+1}\) is not the physical transport through a
scale face.  The latter is \(F_j\) itself.  It can nevertheless be recovered
without undoing the signed cancellation.  Define the fine-side cumulative
stock, loss, and source action

\[
 U_j:=\sum_{k\ge j}e_k,
 \qquad
 L_j:=\sum_{k\ge j}d_k,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak A_j^\uparrow:=\sum_{k\ge j}\mathfrak a_k.
 \tag{TL.841}
\]

All three are readings of the already completed two-octave chain.  Summing
(TL.834) from \(k=j\) to the true smooth ultraviolet boundary gives

\[
 \boxed{U_j'+L_j=F_j.}
 \tag{TL.842}
\]

Thus \(F_j\) is exactly the rate at which the same VPI history supplies the
whole fine-side stock together with its simultaneous loss.  There is no
additional tail source.  The geometric layer-cake identities are

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_jR_jU_j=H,
 \qquad
 \sum_jR_jL_j=2\nu E_{3/2},
 \qquad
 \sum_jR_jF_j=H'+2\nu E_{3/2}=2\mathfrak Q(0).
 }
 \tag{TL.843}
\]

For example, the first equality follows from

\[
 \sum_jR_jU_j
 =\sum_ke_k\sum_{j\le k}R_j
 ={1\over1-q^{-1}}\sum_kR_ke_k=H
\]

and the other two follow identically from (TL.832) and (TL.842).

The local source--loss square controls this cumulative flux, not only its
cellwise divergence.  Since \(F_j=\sum_{k\ge j}s_k\), (TL.837) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 |F_j|^2
 \le {\mathfrak A_j^\uparrow L_j\over2\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.844}
\]

There is also a strictly stronger summed form.  With zero quotients on zero
loss tails, the weighted Copson inequality applied to
\(F_j=\sum_{k\ge j}s_k\) and \(L_j=\sum_{k\ge j}d_k\) yields

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_jd_j\left({F_j\over L_j}\right)^2
 &\le4\sum_j{s_j^2\over d_j}\\
 &\le {2\over\nu}\sum_j\mathfrak a_j
 ={2(q-1)\over\nu}\mathfrak A.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.845}
\]

The first inequality is the ordinary \(L^2\) Hardy inequality after the
sequence \(s_j/d_j\) is made into a step function on intervals of lengths
\(d_j\).  Finite truncation proves it first; Fatou's lemma removes
the truncation.  Consequently the actual cumulative face flux has the
datum-finite normalized action

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T{1\over H(t)}
 \sum_jd_j(t)\left({F_j(t)\over L_j(t)}\right)^2dt
 \le {C(q-1)E_0(0)\over\nu^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.846}
\]

This is an advance over (TL.840): the controlled object is now the true
fine-side transport \(F_j\), after every intervening scale face has been
joined.

The same tail law gives the exact action required by a fine-side upcrossing.
Where \(L_j,U_j>0\), completing the square in (TL.842) gives

\[
 {F_j^2\over L_jU_j}
 ={(U_j')^2\over L_jU_j}
   +2{U_j'\over U_j}+{L_j\over U_j},
\]

and hence

\[
 \boxed{
 [\partial_t\log U_j]_+
 \le {1\over4}{F_j^2\over L_jU_j}.
 }
 \tag{TL.847}
\]

Because the weights \(R_jU_j/H\) form a probability distribution by
(TL.843), differentiation of that identity gives

\[
 \boxed{
 [\partial_t\log H]_+
 \le {1\over4H}\sum_jR_j{F_j^2\over L_j}.
 }
 \tag{TL.848}
\]

Equations (TL.846) and (TL.848) locate the critical transport operation
without renaming it.  The datum pays the loss-fraction-weighted cumulative
flux in (TL.846).  Critical growth reads the same flux with the actual radial
travel factor \(R_jL_j/d_j\).  The remaining construction must therefore use
the VPI source geometry to control that travel factor; returning to the
cellwise divergence would discard the precise advance made here.

## 81. The source--loss square exposes the unique fast equality direction and its compulsory trough

The local estimate in (TL.838) also has an exact nonnegative remainder.  Put

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak y_j
 &:=\left(\sqrt{d_j}-\sqrt{{\mathfrak a_j\over8\nu}}\right)^2,\\
 \mathfrak c_j
 &:=\sqrt{{\mathfrak a_jd_j\over2\nu}}-s_j\ge0.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.849}
\]

Then (TL.834) gives the identity

\[
 \boxed{
 e_j'+\mathfrak y_j+\mathfrak c_j
 ={\mathfrak a_j\over8\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.850}
\]

Thus the upper bound in (TL.838) is attained only when both defects vanish.
On a nonzero cell this is equivalent to

\[
 d_j={\mathfrak a_j\over8\nu},
 \qquad
 s_j=2d_j,
 \qquad
 g(\eta,t)=4\nu\eta^2
 \quad\text{for }\beta_j(|\eta|)d\mu_t\text{-a.e. }\eta.
 \tag{TL.851}
\]

Indeed equality in (TL.837) makes \(g/\eta\) a positive multiple of
\(\eta\) in the single Hilbert space
\(L^2(\beta_jd\mu_t)\); the first equality in (TL.851) fixes that multiple
to \(4\nu\).  In velocity language, the real energy-direction projection of
the one nonlinear VPI tangent on the occupied spectral fibers is then
\(-2\nu\Lambda^2u\).  The tangent may still have a transverse component,
but its energy projection supplies twice the simultaneous loss: one copy
replaces what is dissipated and one copy increases the same stock.  This is
an equality direction of the joined evolution, not a viscosity-only or
nonlinearity-only comparison.

It cannot be an isolated positive source.  The global energy orthogonality
in (TL.810) and the overlap identity in (TL.831) give

\[
 \sum_js_j=(q-1)\int g\,d\mu_t=0.
\]

Moreover, whenever \(s_j\le0\), (TL.849) implies

\[
 \mathfrak c_j
 =\sqrt{{\mathfrak a_jd_j\over2\nu}}-s_j
 \ge-2s_j.
\]

Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{\{j:s_j\le0\}}\mathfrak c_j
 \ge2\sum_{\{j:s_j>0\}}s_j.
 }
 \tag{TL.852}
\]

Every near-equality receiving population therefore carries a simultaneous
compensating trough in the same completed all-field source.  It is not
lawful to iterate (TL.851) as a sequence of independently prescribed
positive cells.  The trough and receiver are joined by the cumulative flux
\(F_j\) in (TL.842); their radial separation is precisely the travel factor
left in (TL.848).  The next VPI estimate is consequently forced onto that
joined donor--receiver distance, rather than onto another positive source
square.

## 82. The donor--receiver travel current is one complete material-pair commutator

The cumulative quantities in Section 80 have a direct carrier realization.
Define the nested multiplier

\[
 \alpha_j(k):=\sum_{r\ge j}\beta_r(k)
 =
 \begin{cases}
 0,&k\le R_j,\\
 k/R_j-1,&R_j<k\le qR_j,\\
 q-1,&k>qR_j.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{TL.853}
\]

The telescope \(\beta_j=\alpha_j-\alpha_{j+1}\) and spectral calculus give

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 U_j&=\int\alpha_j(|\eta|)\,d\mu_t
     ={1\over2}\langle u,\alpha_j(\Lambda)u\rangle,\\
 L_j&=2\nu\int\alpha_j(|\eta|)\eta^2\,d\mu_t
     =\nu\langle\Lambda u,\alpha_j(\Lambda)\Lambda u\rangle,\\
 F_j&=\int\alpha_j(|\eta|)g\,d\mu_t
     =-\operatorname{Re}\langle B(u,u),\alpha_j(\Lambda)u\rangle .
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.854}
\]

Thus (TL.842) is exactly the energy law of one nested fine-side projection
of the same velocity field.  Its stock, nonlinear work, and loss have not
been assembled from independently evolving shells.

The nonlinear work is itself an all-field material-pair deformation.  Put

\[
 \bar\alpha_j(k):=(q-1)-\alpha_j(k)
\]

and let \(K_j\) be its convolution kernel.  Since
\(\langle B(u,u),u\rangle=0\),

\[
 F_j=\operatorname{Re}\langle B(u,u),\bar\alpha_j(\Lambda)u\rangle.
\]

For smooth radial approximations of \(\bar\alpha_j\), the increment identity
used in (TL.739), followed by the volume-preserving material map, gives

\[
 \boxed{
 F_j(t)
 ={1\over4}\iint
 \nabla K_j(R_{\alpha\beta}(t))
 \cdot V_{\alpha\beta}(t)\,
 |V_{\alpha\beta}(t)|^2\,d\alpha\,d\beta .
 }
 \tag{TL.855}
\]

The Lipschitz cutoff in (TL.853) follows by convergence of those
approximations on every compact classical interval.  Formula (TL.855) is
the complete VPI meaning of the cumulative radial flux: the same material
pair separation, relative velocity, surrounding-field response, and fixed
viscosity history determine every \(F_j\).  A positive value is not an
independent shell source.

The radial travel functional in (TL.848) can therefore be written without
any source proxy:

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak T_q(t)
 :=\sum_jR_j{F_j(t)^2\over L_j(t)}
 ={1\over16\nu}\sum_j
 R_j\,
 { \left|
 \iint\nabla K_j(R_{\alpha\beta})\cdot V_{\alpha\beta}
 |V_{\alpha\beta}|^2\,d\alpha\,d\beta
 \right|^2
 \over
 \langle\Lambda u,\alpha_j(\Lambda)\Lambda u\rangle } .
 }
 \tag{TL.856}
\]

Zero denominators carry zero numerators by (TL.844).  Combining (TL.843)
with Cauchy--Schwarz gives

\[
 2\mathfrak Q(0)
 =\sum_jR_jF_j
 \le
 \left(2\nu E_{3/2}\right)^{1/2}
 \mathfrak T_q^{1/2},
\]

and hence the exact pointwise absorption

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)\le {1\over4}\mathfrak T_q(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.857}
\]

This is the same inequality as (TL.848) before division by \(H\), now on
the complete material-pair carrier.  It identifies the physical operation
which the final packing must sum: not repeated positive source values, but
the squared longitudinal work required to carry the compensating trough
through the nested all-pair incidence kernels to its actual receiver.

## 83. The travel action and transverse VPI turning are the two orthogonal rows of one completed square

The estimate in (TL.857) has an exact remainder.  With zero quotients on
zero-loss tails, define the scale-relay mismatch

\[
 \mathfrak M_q(t)
 :=\sum_jR_j
 \left(\sqrt{L_j(t)}-{F_j(t)\over2\sqrt{L_j(t)}}\right)^2.
 \tag{TL.858}
\]

Expanding the square and using all three identities in (TL.843) gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak M_q
 &=\sum_jR_jL_j-\sum_jR_jF_j
   +{1\over4}\sum_jR_j{F_j^2\over L_j}\\
 &=-H'+{1\over4}\mathfrak T_q.
 \end{aligned}
\]

Hence

\[
 \boxed{
 H'+\mathfrak M_q={1\over4}\mathfrak T_q.
 }
 \tag{TL.859}
\]

This is the exact q-chain version of the joined source--loss square.  The
critical height can rise efficiently only when the actual cumulative current
on every occupied fine-side tail is close to \(F_j=2L_j\): one copy of the
current supplies the simultaneous loss and the other remains as growth.  Any
departure is recorded once in \(\mathfrak M_q\), after all common scale faces
have cancelled.

There is also an exact vector remainder behind the passage from the complete
VPI tangent to its longitudinal receiver work.  Put

\[
 Z:=\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)
\]

and define

\[
 \mathfrak r_j
 :=\left\|\alpha_j(\Lambda)^{1/2}\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\right\|_2^2
 -\nu{F_j^2\over L_j}\ge0.
 \tag{TL.860}
\]

The inequality is precisely Cauchy--Schwarz in the single filtered Hilbert
space, because

\[
 F_j=-\operatorname{Re}\left\langle
 \alpha_j(\Lambda)^{1/2}\Lambda^{-1}B,
 \alpha_j(\Lambda)^{1/2}\Lambda u
 \right\rangle,
 \qquad
 L_j=\nu\left\|\alpha_j(\Lambda)^{1/2}\Lambda u\right\|_2^2.
\]

For every \(k>0\), the nested kernels satisfy the exact frame identity

\[
 \sum_jR_j\alpha_j(k)=k.
\]

Indeed, if \(R_n<k\le R_{n+1}\), the saturated indices \(j<n\)
contribute
\((q-1)\sum_{j<n}R_j=R_n\), while the one partial index contributes
\(R_n(k/R_n-1)=k-R_n\).  Tonelli and (TL.860) therefore give

\[
 \boxed{
 \|Z\|_2^2
 =\nu\mathfrak T_q+\sum_jR_j\mathfrak r_j.
 }
 \tag{TL.861}
\]

Thus \(\mathfrak T_q\) is exactly the longitudinal part of the complete
critical VPI tangent resolved through all nested receivers.  The remainder
\(\sum_jR_j\mathfrak r_j\) is not a second fluid or an added source: it is
the simultaneous component of that same tangent orthogonal to the actual
fine-side velocity direction in each receiver.

Finally set

\[
 W:=\nu\Lambda^{3/2}u.
\]

The critical balance obtained directly from
\(u_t=-B-\nu\Lambda^2u\) completes to

\[
 \boxed{
 H'+{1\over\nu}\left\|W+{1\over2}Z\right\|_2^2
 ={1\over4\nu}\|Z\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.862}
\]

Combining (TL.859), (TL.861), and (TL.862) eliminates both \(H'\) and
\(\mathfrak T_q\) and yields the joined Pythagorean identity

\[
 \boxed{
 \left\|\nu\Lambda^{3/2}u
       +{1\over2}\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)\right\|_2^2
 =\nu\mathfrak M_q+{1\over4}\sum_jR_j\mathfrak r_j.
 }
 \tag{TL.863}
\]

Equation (TL.863) is the upper/lower split on the one continuing field.
The left side is the one completed critical tangent.  Its radial component is
the mismatch between actual scale travel and simultaneous loss; its transverse
component is the filtered receiver-angle defect.  Exact fast relay would have
to make both rows vanish together.  The remaining packing problem is
therefore no longer free transport of a compensating trough: it is the temporal
summation of the complete tangent subject simultaneously to both nonnegative
rows in (TL.863).

## 84. Energy orthogonality inserts a compulsory centered-scale row into every fast relay

The completed square in (TL.863) has a further exact orthogonal splitting
which uses the invariant direction of the same VPI tangent.  Define

\[
 V:=\Lambda^{1/2}u,
 \qquad
 a(t):={\langle W,V\rangle\over\|V\|_2^2}
       ={\nu E_1(t)\over H(t)}
\]

at nonzero states, and set \(a=0\) at the zero state.  The complete
quadratic current satisfies

\[
 \langle Z,V\rangle
 =\langle B(u,u),u\rangle=0,
 \qquad
 \langle W-aV,V\rangle=0.
 \tag{TL.864}
\]

Consequently

\[
 W+{1\over2}Z
 =aV+\left(W-aV+{1\over2}Z\right)
\]

is an orthogonal sum.  Since \(\|V\|_2^2=2H\), (TL.863) becomes

\[
 \boxed{
 \nu\mathfrak M_q+{1\over4}\sum_jR_j\mathfrak r_j
 ={2\nu^2E_1^2\over H}
 +\left\|
 \nu\Lambda^{3/2}u
 -{\nu E_1\over H}\Lambda^{1/2}u
 +{1\over2}\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)
 \right\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.865}
\]

The first term on the right is the compulsory mean-scale part.  The final
square is the simultaneous centered-scale and transverse VPI part.  In
particular,

\[
 \boxed{
 \nu\mathfrak M_q+{1\over4}\sum_jR_j\mathfrak r_j
 \ge {2\nu^2E_1^2\over H}
 \ge {2\nu^2H^3\over E_0(t)^2},
 }
 \tag{TL.866}
\]

where the last inequality is the exact interpolation
\(H^2\le E_0E_1\).  Thus the equality face of the radial source--loss
square is not a lawful nonzero state of the complete field.  If all radial
mismatch and all filtered transverse defects vanished, (TL.865) would force
\(E_1=0\), hence \(u=0\).  More quantitatively, increasing critical height
forces an increasing instantaneous amount of the same completed tangent into
the two nonnegative rows on the left of (TL.865).

This is not yet obtained by comparing viscosity with a separate nonlinear
continuation.  It follows from one fact about the joined tangent: its
quadratic redistribution is orthogonal to the velocity while the simultaneous
fixed-viscosity part has the strictly positive projection
\(\langle W,V\rangle=2\nu E_1\).  The record packing must retain the
resulting centered square through the stopped q-complex; discarding it before
positive record selection would recreate the false perfect-relay direction.

The same statement has a direct material-time form.  The equation gives

\[
 Z=-\Lambda^{-1/2}u_t-W,
\]

so (TL.863) is equivalently

\[
 \boxed{
 \nu\mathfrak M_q+{1\over4}\sum_jR_j\mathfrak r_j
 ={1\over4}
 \left\|\Lambda^{-1/2}
 \bigl(u_t-\nu\Lambda^2u\bigr)\right\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.867}
\]

The vector inside (TL.867) is the difference between the actual joined
material history and the backward-heat tangent that would sharpen every
occupied Fourier component.  It is not a comparison with another fluid.
It is one tangent-space reading of the same \(u(t)\).  The energy law fixes
its velocity projection exactly:

\[
 \left\langle u_t-\nu\Lambda^2u,u\right\rangle
 =-2\nu\|\Lambda u\|_2^2=-4\nu E_1.
 \tag{TL.868}
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz between (TL.868) and
\(\|\Lambda^{1/2}u\|_2^2=2H\) recovers the first lower bound in
(TL.866).  Thus increasingly fine strengthening cannot be represented as
unopposed backward diffusion: its simultaneous compensating deformation is
already the exact positive quantity on the left of (TL.867).  What the
stopped packing must now prove is that this compensation cannot be reassigned
to a new scale face and counted again after that face has cancelled.

## 85. The fast current is exactly the signed difference of the forward- and backward-heat residual towers

The tangent-space square in (TL.867) can be inserted into the original
\((\rho_n,J_n)\) recurrence without estimating it.  Define the two residuals

\[
 R_+:=u_t+\nu\Lambda^2u=-B(u,u),
 \qquad
 R_-:=u_t-\nu\Lambda^2u,
 \tag{TL.869}
\]

and, for every integer \(n\ge1\), their positive heat-resolved rows

\[
 \boxed{
 \chi_n^\pm(\sigma,t)
 :=\frac{2^{n-1}}{\Gamma(n)}\,
 \tau^n
 \left\|\Lambda^{n-1}G_\tau R_\pm(t)\right\|_2^2,
 \qquad \tau=e^\sigma.
 }
 \tag{TL.870}
\]

These are not independently evolving fields.  They are the two squared
tangent directions of the same \(u_t\) relative to the same fixed-viscosity
row.  Direct expansion gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \chi_n^- -\chi_n^+
 &=-4\nu\frac{2^{n-1}}{\Gamma(n)}\tau^n
 \operatorname{Re}\left\langle
 \Lambda^nG_\tau u_t,\Lambda^nG_\tau u
 \right\rangle\\
 &=-2\nu\,\partial_t\rho_n.
 \end{aligned}
\]

Using (TL.699) therefore yields the exact signed scale law

\[
 \boxed{
 \chi_n^- -\chi_n^+
 =2\nu\,\partial_\sigma J_n.
 }
 \tag{TL.871}
\]

Every row is compatible with the same derivative-depth recurrence:

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_\sigma\chi_n^\pm
 =n(\chi_n^\pm-\chi_{n+1}^\pm).
 }
 \tag{TL.872}
\]

Moreover its unweighted mass is independent of \(n\):

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R}\chi_n^\pm(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma
 ={1\over2}\|\Lambda^{-1}R_\pm(t)\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.873}
\]

For the forward residual, the already proved product estimate and the energy
law give the datum-finite normalized history

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T{1\over H(t)}
 \int_{\mathbb R}\chi_n^+(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma\,dt
 \le C\int_0^TE_1(t)\,dt
 \le {C E_0(0)\over2\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.874}
\]

The backward row contains this same forward row plus the exact energy-loss
mass.  Integrating (TL.871) in scale gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R}(\chi_n^--\chi_n^+)\,d\sigma
 =-2\nu E_0'(t)=4\nu^2E_1(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.875}
\]

At the critical weight, the same identity is precisely the critical-height
balance:

\[
 \boxed{
 c_n\int_{\mathbb R}e^{-\sigma/2}
 (\chi_n^+-\chi_n^-)(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma
 =2\nu H'(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.876}
\]

Finally, on every finite scale cell \(I=[a,b]\), (TL.871) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 2\nu\,[J_n(b,t)-J_n(a,t)]
 =\int_a^b(\chi_n^--\chi_n^+)(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma.
 }
 \tag{TL.877}
\]

Therefore the fast part of the stopped q-complex is not an unsigned current
requiring a separately invented payer.  It is exactly the excess of the
forward residual row over the simultaneous backward-defect row, and both
obey the same signed depth recurrence (TL.872).  Summing (TL.877) on a
completed complex cancels every common \(J_n\)-face first.  The only positive
quantity permitted afterward is the forward excess that survives its actual
backward row; its unweighted normalized supply is already datum-finite by
(TL.874).  The remaining critical operation is now solely the q-weighted
first-receiving placement of that surviving excess, with no refill or
independent edge term left to define.

## 86. The first-receiving identity isolates the critical valuation exactly

The last placement can be written without introducing a new current.  Retain
the translated cells in (TL.703) and put

\[
 X_{n,j}^{\pm}(t;r)
 :=\int_{I_j(r)}\chi_n^{\pm}(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma .
 \tag{TL.878}
\]

Integrating (TL.871) on one cell and using (TL.705) gives the exact positive
row law

\[
 \boxed{
 2\nu\,\partial_tM_j^{(n)}(t;r)
 =X_{n,j}^+(t;r)-X_{n,j}^-(t;r).
 }
 \tag{TL.879}
\]

Thus the backward row is subtracted before any positive receiving event is
selected.  In particular,

\[
 \boxed{
 2\nu\,[\partial_tM_j^{(n)}]_+
 =[X_{n,j}^+-X_{n,j}^-]_+
 \le X_{n,j}^+ .
 }
 \tag{TL.880}
\]

For every fixed translation the cells partition the whole scale line, so
(TL.874) immediately proves

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\frac1{H(t)}
 \sum_j[\partial_tM_j^{(n)}(t;r)]_+\,dt
 \le {C E_0(0)\over\nu^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.881}
\]

The quotient is set to zero at the zero state.  Since a zero
\(\dot H^{1/2}\) finite-energy state is the zero velocity, that convention
loses no nontrivial evolution.  Equation (TL.881) is a genuine datum-finite
first-receiving theorem for the ordinary kinetic stock in every heat row.  It
already contains the signed forward/backward cancellation and does not count
refill as a new source.

Critical valuation changes the statement by exactly one half-derivative.  Let

\[
 H^*(t):=\max_{0\le s\le t}H(s),
 \qquad
 \mathcal R_*:=\{t:H(t)=H^*(t),\ H'(t)>0\}.
 \tag{TL.882}
\]

For an absolutely continuous height,
\((H^*)'=\mathbf1_{\mathcal R_*}H'\) almost everywhere.  Differentiating the
exact translation average (TL.716), and taking the positive part only after
that signed identity, yields

\[
 \boxed{
 {d\over dt}\log H^*(t)
 \le {c_n\mathbf1_{\mathcal R_*}(t)
 \over4\nu(1-q^{-1})H(t)}
 \int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^j
 [X_{n,j}^+-X_{n,j}^-]_+\,dr .
 }
 \tag{TL.883}
\]

Consequently the exact record-stopped first-receiving burden is

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak A_{\rm rec}^{(n,q)}(T)
 :=\int_{\mathcal R_*\cap(0,T)}{1\over H(t)}
 \int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^j
 [X_{n,j}^+-X_{n,j}^-]_+\,dr\,dt .
 }
 \tag{TL.884}
\]

A datum-finite bound for (TL.884) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 H^*(T)\le H(0)\exp\!\left{
 {c_n\over4\nu(1-q^{-1})}
 \mathfrak A_{\rm rec}^{(n,q)}(T)
 \right}.
 }
 \tag{TL.885}
\]

This is smaller than taking the positive part of the forward row by itself:
the actual backward defect has already been removed cell by cell, and only
times at which the same solution makes a new height record are retained.
Nevertheless it is not yet bounded by (TL.874).  Indeed, translation averaging
of the forward row gives

\[
 \int_0^Le^{-r/2}\sum_jq^jX_{n,j}^+(t;r)\,dr
 =2(1-q^{-1})
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^{-\sigma/2}\chi_n^+(\sigma,t)\,d\sigma,
 \tag{TL.886}
\]

and the Gamma integral behind (TL.873) gives the two exact norms

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_{\mathbb R}\chi_n^+\,d\sigma
 &=\frac12\|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2,\\
 c_n\int_{\mathbb R}e^{-\sigma/2}\chi_n^+\,d\sigma
 &=\frac12\|\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)\|_2^2.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.887}
\]

Thus (TL.874) pays the \(\dot H^{-1}\) residual action, whereas the full
critical forward majorant of (TL.884) is the \(\dot H^{-1/2}\) residual
action.  In particular the familiar sufficient consequence of (TL.883) is

\[
 \boxed{
 \log{H^*(T)\over H(0)}
 \le {1\over4\nu}
 \int_{\mathcal R_*\cap(0,T)}
 {\|\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)\|_2^2\over H(t)}\,dt .
 }
 \tag{TL.888}
\]

The single unweighted row is not the higher-row construction.  To separate
those two calculations without proposing a second fluid, use a multiplier
countertest.  Let \(f_K\) be a smooth divergence-free
field with Fourier support in \(\{K\le|\xi|\le2K\}\), normalized by
\(\|\Lambda^{-1}f_K\|_2=1\), and build its positive heat rows by the formula
in (TL.870).  They obey (TL.872) and all the Gamma mass identities, but

\[
 \int\chi_n[f_K]\,d\sigma=\frac12,
 \qquad
 c_n\int e^{-\sigma/2}\chi_n[f_K]\,d\sigma
 =\frac12\|\Lambda^{-1/2}f_K\|_2^2\simeq K.
 \tag{TL.889}
\]

The arbitrary \(f_K\) is not the Navier--Stokes source
\(-B(u,u)\) of the continuing solution.  Thus (TL.889) discards only the
wrong one-row estimate.  The common critical trace has already been
constructed in (TL.696); the higher row below acts on its same-field
first-receiving boundary, retaining the energy and helicity orthogonalities
and the complete material-pair/VPI ancestry that the countertest omits.

## 87. One fixed higher Sobolev row gives a strict critical moving-face kernel

The common critical trace in (TL.696) is already fixed.  The remaining task
is to control its first-receiving record boundary with one sufficiently high,
but still finite, heat-normalized Sobolev row.  This uses no datum other than
the original smooth datum: increasing the row changes the resolution of the
same \(u(t)\), while (TL.694) keeps its total stock equal to \(E_0(t)\).

The preceding reductions have already fixed which part must be reconstructed.
The bounded-frequency positive variation is paid in (TL.821), the compact
two-octave and cumulative-tail actions are paid in (TL.840) and (TL.846), and
the longitudinal/transverse equality direction has been returned to the one
completed tangent in (TL.863)--(TL.877).  Hence the only surviving part of
(TL.884) is the joint ultraviolet first-receiving boundary of the completed
critical current.  Equation (TL.879) is the exact bridge: its residual excess
is \(2\nu[\partial_tM_j^{(n)}]_+\), so the surviving residual measure is
already a positive boundary measure of the original Sobolev stock.  The
kernel below therefore acts on that stock--current boundary, not on
\(B(u,u)\) as a freely prescribed source.

Use logarithmic squared frequency \(y=\log\lambda\), with
\(\lambda=K^2\), and set

\[
 g(y,t):=e^y e(e^{y/2},t),
 \qquad
 \widehat{\mathcal Q}(y,t):=\mathcal Q(e^{y/2},t).
\]

Multiplying (TL.44b) by \(dK/dy=K/2\) gives the completed critical law in
the exact form

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_tU+\partial_yQ=-D,
 \qquad
 U\ge0,\quad D\ge0,\quad
 \int_{\mathbb R}U(y,t)\,dy=H(t),
 }
 \tag{TL.890}
\]

where, in the radial notation used earlier,

\[
 U(y,t)=\frac12g(y,t),
 \qquad
 Q(y,t)=\widehat{\mathcal Q}(y,t),
 \qquad
 D(y,t)=\nu e^yg(y,t).
\]

For the normalized row \(n\), put

\[
 w_n(x):={2^{n-1}\over\Gamma(n)}x^ne^{-2x},
 \qquad
 k_n(z):=2c_ne^{-z/2}w_n(e^z).
 \tag{TL.891}
\]

The Gamma integral in (TL.696) gives \(\int k_n=1\).  In fact, \(k_n\) is
the logarithmic density of a
\(\operatorname{Gamma}(n-\frac12,2)\) variable.  Center it at

\[
 z_n:=\log{n-\frac12\over2}.
\]

The spectral definition (TL.693) gives the exact row-to-frequency bridge

\[
 \boxed{
 c_ne^{-\sigma/2}\rho_n(\sigma,t)
 =\int_{\mathbb R}k_n(\sigma+y)U(y,t)\,dy.
 }
 \tag{TL.891a}
\]

Thus the higher Sobolev row below is exactly the critically weighted version
of the already fixed stock used in (TL.716)--(TL.879), not a replacement
stock.

Then

\[
 M_n:=\int_{\mathbb R}e^{|z-z_n|/2}k_n(z)\,dz\longrightarrow1
 \qquad(n\to\infty).
 \tag{TL.892}
\]

In the oriented coordinate the exact one-sided critical mass is

\[
 \kappa_n
 :=\int_{\mathbb R}e^{s/2}\widetilde k_n(s)\,ds
 =e^{z_n/2}\int_{\mathbb R}e^{-z/2}k_n(z)\,dz
 =\sqrt{n-\frac12}\,{\Gamma(n-1)\over\Gamma(n-\frac12)}
 \longrightarrow1.
 \tag{TL.892a}
\]

Here the formula anticipates
\(\widetilde k_n(s)=k_n(z_n-s)\), defined below.

Indeed the two half moments of the centered Gamma variable are explicit
Gamma-function ratios and converge to one; they also give uniform
integrability of the absolute half moment in (TL.892).

Choose a smooth nondecreasing delayed plateau
\(f_n:(0,\infty)\to[0,1]\), equal to zero near the origin, with

\[
 \int f_n(e^z)k_n(z)\,dz=\frac12.
 \tag{TL.893}
\]

This plateau can be fixed explicitly.  Choose a smooth nondecreasing
\(\psi_R\), zero on the far weighted left tail and one before the row core,
and put

\[
 f_n(e^z)=c_R\psi_R(z),
 \qquad
 c_R:=\left(2\int\psi_R(z)k_n(z)\,dz\right)^{-1}.
\]

For the transition sufficiently far left, \(1/2\le c_R\le1\), and (TL.893)
holds exactly.  Define

\[
 \Delta_n
 :=\int e^{|z-z_n|/2}
 \left|f_n(e^z)-\frac12\right|k_n(z)\,dz
\]

Then weighted dominated convergence makes \(\Delta_n\) as small as required.
Define the fixed signed edge kernel

\[
 K_n^*(z)
 :=k_n(z)\left({7\over32}-{1\over2}f_n(e^z)\right),
 \qquad
 m_*:={3\over32}.
 \tag{TL.894}
\]

Its critically weighted operator number satisfies

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \Theta_n
 &:=\int e^{|z-z_n|/2}|K_n^*(z)|\,dz\\
 &\le {1\over32}M_n+{1\over2}\Delta_n.
 \end{aligned}
\]

Since \(\kappa_n\to1\), the finite row and plateau can therefore be fixed
once and for all so that

\[
 \boxed{
 \Theta_n<{m_*\kappa_n\over2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.895}
\]

This is where the higher Sobolev resolution enters.  The row concentration
in (TL.892), which becomes sharper as \(n\) increases, constructs a fixed
kernel whose absolute critical action is strictly smaller than the positive
critical stock it reads.  It is not a comparison with another evolution.

Orient the heat coordinate by \(x=z_n-\sigma\) and write

\[
 \widetilde k_n(s):=k_n(z_n-s),
 \qquad
 \widetilde K_n(s):=K_n^*(z_n-s).
\]

Convolve the complete stock--current pair in (TL.890):

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr A_n&:=\widetilde k_n*U,
 &\mathscr F_n^A&:=\widetilde k_n*Q,\\
 \mathscr B_n&:=\widetilde K_n*U,
 &\mathscr F_n&:=\widetilde K_n*Q.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.896}
\]

Both pairs obey their convolved versions of (TL.890).  Wherever
\(\mathscr A_n(x,t)>0\), define the conditional row average

\[
 \alpha_n(x,t)
 :={1\over\mathscr A_n(x,t)}
 \int \widetilde k_n(x-y)
 f_n(e^{z_n-x+y})U(y,t)\,dy,
 \qquad
 x_n:=\frac12-\alpha_n.
\]

(At a zero of \(\mathscr A_n\), set \(\alpha_n=0\).)  The definitions of
\(\widetilde K_n\) and \(K_n^*\) then give exactly

\[
 \mathscr B_n
 =\mathscr A_n\left({1\over2}x_n-{1\over32}\right).
 \tag{TL.897}
\]

At each fixed classical time, finiteness of every spectral moment and
dominated convergence give \(\alpha_n(x,t)\to0\) as \(x\to+\infty\).
Define the true ultraviolet component to be the connected component of
\(\{x:x_n(x,t)\ge1/4\}\) adjacent to that smooth ultraviolet end.  If it
has no finite entrance face there is no incoming edge to charge.  Otherwise,
at its moving classification face \(x=c(t)\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr B_n=m_*\mathscr A_n,
 \qquad
 \mathscr B_n\ge m_*\mathscr A_n
 \quad\hbox{inside the component}.
 }
 \tag{TL.898}
\]

Thus the stronger Sobolev row has constructed the critical face with a
strict positive stock normalization.  The kernel acts on its stock and
current together, so for every moving face

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr F_n(c,t)-c'\mathscr B_n(c,t)
 =\int\widetilde K_n(c-y)
 [Q(y,t)-c'U(y,t)]\,dy .
 }
 \tag{TL.899}
\]

Every moving-boundary sweep is therefore part of the same filtered VPI
current.  There is no point-face or test-jump term to add later.

The finite-complex version follows from the same identity.  For a finite
Lipschitz union \(\Omega\) in the \((x,t)\)-plane, let
\(\Omega_s:=\{(y,t):(y+s,t)\in\Omega\}\).  Fubini and the divergence
theorem give

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\partial\Omega}
 (\mathscr B_n n_t+\mathscr F_n n_x)\,dS
 =\int\widetilde K_n(s)
 \int_{\partial\Omega_s}(U n_t+Q n_y)\,dS\,ds.
 }
 \tag{TL.899a}
\]

Thus every shared time, scale, and moving classification face cancels before
the scalar positive boundary measure is formed.  If \(d\mathfrak q\) is that
sharp positive boundary measure and \(d\mathfrak b\) is the total variation
of its filtered image, then (TL.895) and weighted Young inequality give

\[
 \boxed{
 \int e^{x/2}\,d\mathfrak b(x,t)
 \le\Theta_n\int e^{y/2}\,d\mathfrak q(y,t).
 }
 \tag{TL.899b}
\]

The factor \(q\) appearing later is only the worst rounding from a translated
continuous face to its neighboring discrete cell.

The positive row kernel carries the same boundary measure with an exact
normalization.  For every nonnegative joined sharp boundary measure
\(d\mathfrak q\), Tonelli gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \int e^{x/2}(\widetilde k_n*d\mathfrak q)(x)
 =\kappa_n\int e^{y/2}\,d\mathfrak q(y).
 }
 \tag{TL.899c}
\]

Thus the positive-row output and the sharp next-rung input have exactly the
same critical valuation after division by the one fixed number \(\kappa_n\).
This is the type-preserving handoff used in the recurrence below.

## 88. The connection loss is summed once on the translated first-receiving complex

The difference between the filtered and sharp normalized pairs is

\[
 \mathscr L_n:=\widetilde K_n-m_*\widetilde k_n,
 \qquad
 \mathscr Z_n:=\mathscr B_n-m_*\mathscr A_n,
 \qquad
 \mathscr C_n:=\mathscr F_n-m_*\mathscr F_n^A.
\]

Subtracting the two convolved laws gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\mathscr Z_n+\partial_x\mathscr C_n
 =-\mathscr L_n*D.
 }
 \tag{TL.900}
\]

By (TL.897)--(TL.898),

\[
 \mathscr Z_n
 ={1\over2}\mathscr A_n\left(x_n-{1\over4}\right)\ge0,
 \qquad
 \mathscr Z_n(c(t),t)=0.
 \tag{TL.901}
\]

Integrating (TL.900) over the whole ultraviolet component, with its moving
face retained inside Reynolds transport, gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr C_n(c(t),t)
 ={d\over dt}\mathscr S_n(t)+\mathscr L_n^D(t),
 }
 \tag{TL.902}
\]

where

\[
 \mathscr S_n(t):=\int_{c(t)}^\infty\mathscr Z_n(x,t)\,dx\ge0,
 \qquad
 \mathscr L_n^D(t)
 :=\int_{c(t)}^\infty(\mathscr L_n*D)(x,t)\,dx.
\]

Because \(\mathscr Z_n=0\) at the moving face, the complete relative
currents satisfy the exact connection

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 &(\mathscr F_n-c'\mathscr B_n)
 -m_*(\mathscr F_n^A-c'\mathscr A_n)\\
 &\qquad=\mathscr C_n
 ={d\over dt}\mathscr S_n+\mathscr L_n^D.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.902a}
\]

Thus motion of the classification face creates no separately valued stock
term: the same \(c'\)-term occurs in both members and cancels at the common
face.

On every finite first-receiving complex, all intermediate values of
\(\mathscr S_n\) occur once with each orientation.  Its final value is
favorable, and its one initial value obeys

\[
 0\le\mathscr S_n(0)
 \le{1\over8}\int\mathscr A_n(x,0)\,dx
 ={1\over8}H(0).
 \tag{TL.903}
\]

The loss term in (TL.902) must be summed before it is localized to a
\(q\)-cell.  A cellwise estimate would allow one convolution tail to be read
at several neighboring levels.  Define instead the finite weighted kernel
number

\[
 \Lambda_n
 :=2\int_{\mathbb R}e^{|s|/2}|\mathscr L_n(s)|\,ds<\infty.
 \tag{TL.904}
\]

Let \(D_\Omega\) be the restriction of the positive loss \(D\) to all
shifted sharp cells of one finite first-receiving complex.  Tonelli gives the
global estimate

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \int e^{x/2}|\mathscr L_n*D_\Omega|(x,t)\,dx
 &\le{\Lambda_n\over2}\int e^{y/2}D_\Omega(y,t)\,dy.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.905}
\]

Indeed,
\(e^{x/2}\le e^{|x-y|/2}e^{y/2}\), after which Fubini gives the
half of the number in (TL.904).  The translated faces read the tail primitive
of this convolution.  Its weighted Hardy estimate is

\[
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^{c/2}
 \int_c^\infty|F(x)|\,dx\,dc
 =2\int_{\mathbb R}e^{x/2}|F(x)|\,dx.
\]

Integrating in physical time and applying the exact translation average of
Section 63 therefore gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak L_\Omega^D\le\Lambda_n\mathfrak D_\Omega,
 }
 \tag{TL.906}
\]

where both sides are the complete critically weighted measures of the same
finite translated complex.  Every loss point occurs once on the
right before its filtered image is formed.  Equation (TL.906), rather than a
cellwise \(\|\mathscr L_n\|_1\) estimate, is the non-recounting connection
payment.

The same argument applies to the signed boundary one-form.  If
\(d\mathfrak q_\Omega\) is the sharp first-receiving boundary measure formed
only after all shared faces have cancelled, denote by
\(\mathfrak S_\Omega^{\rm in}\) its critically weighted mass at the source face
and by \(\mathfrak S_\Omega^{\rm out}\) the critically weighted sharp mass first
received at the next finer face.  The boundary estimate (TL.899b), the exact
positive-row normalization (TL.899c), the connection identity (TL.902a), and
the rounding of one translated \(q\)-cell give, successively,

\[
 \mathfrak B_\Omega\le q\Theta_n\mathfrak S_\Omega^{\rm in},
 \qquad
 m_*\kappa_n\mathfrak S_\Omega^{\rm out}
 \le\mathfrak B_\Omega+q\mathfrak L_\Omega^D+C_{n,q}H(0).
\]

Here \(\mathfrak B_\Omega\) is the total variation of the already joined filtered
boundary.  In the second inequality, (TL.899c) converts the positive
\(\mathscr A_n\)-boundary into exactly \(\kappa_n\) times the sharp output;
there is no pointwise deconvolution.  Equation (TL.902a) is then summed over
the whole complex: all internal connection stocks cancel, the
terminal stock is nonnegative, and only (TL.903) remains at the initial face.
Combining the two inequalities gives

\[
 \boxed{
 m_*\kappa_n\mathfrak S_\Omega^{\rm out}
 \le q\Theta_n\mathfrak S_\Omega^{\rm in}
 +q\mathfrak L_\Omega^D+C_{n,q}H(0).
 }
 \tag{TL.907}
\]

The last term is the one initial connection stock in (TL.903).  There is no
finite time, refill, classification or internal scale face in (TL.907), and
the fixed coarse \(q\)-face vanishes by (TL.727).

## 89. The receiver law and matched kernel give one strict q-ladder

On one finite joined first-receiving complex, let \(\mathfrak I\),
\(\mathfrak D\), and \(\mathfrak O\) be respectively its complete critically
weighted incoming, simultaneous positive loss, and first-received outgoing
masses.  The exact stock law, with only the neighboring-cell rounding,
gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak O\le q(\mathfrak I-\mathfrak D),
 \qquad 0\le\mathfrak D\le\mathfrak I.
 }
 \tag{TL.908}
\]

The global connection estimate (TL.906)--(TL.907), applied to this same
joined measure rather than to a separately selected branch, gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak O
 \le q(a\mathfrak I+b\mathfrak D)+C_{n,q}H(0),
 \qquad
 a:={\Theta_n\over m_*\kappa_n}<\frac12,
 \quad
 b:={\Lambda_n\over m_*\kappa_n}.
 }
 \tag{TL.909}
\]

The same physical transfer therefore satisfies both readings at once.  If
\(d:=\mathfrak D/\mathfrak I\) when \(\mathfrak I>0\), then

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak O
 \le q\min\{1-d,a+bd\}\,\mathfrak I+C_{n,q}H(0).
 }
 \tag{TL.910}
\]

There is a strict coefficient without any branch bookkeeping.  Since one
line decreases and the other increases on \([0,1]\),

\[
 \boxed{
 r_n:=\sup_{0\le d\le1}\min\{1-d,a+bd\}
 ={a+b\over1+b}<1.
 }
 \tag{TL.911}
\]

Fix

\[
 \boxed{
 1<q<{1+b\over a+b},
 \qquad
 \theta:=qr_n<1.
 }
 \tag{TL.912}
\]

This choice is nonempty because (TL.895) gives \(a<1/2<1\).  The loss is
not classified twice: the exact receiver law and the matched-kernel law are
two simultaneous identities for the same incoming measure, and the smaller
of their two lawful outputs is retained.

For completeness, the first-receiving assignment on a finite complex is an
elementary finite-flow decomposition.  Cancel every common signed face,
orient each surviving scalar boundary piece by its sign, and follow it
forward in physical time until it is lost, stopped, or first enters the next
finer cell.  Subtracting the smallest boundary mass encountered removes one
path; induction removes the finite simple complex.  Approximation of the
boundary Radon measures by simple measures and monotone convergence gives the
same decomposition for the original finite complex.  A return to an already
visited cell is not selected again.  Hence every record-serving path is
assigned once.  Both bounds (TL.908)--(TL.909) are evaluated on that same
aggregate path measure, so no partial connection stock or cross-branch
convolution has been introduced.

Let

\[
 \mathfrak S_{J,N}^{\rm in}(T)
 :=\int_0^Le^{-r/2}
 \sum_{j=J}^Nq^j\mathfrak I_j^{\rm in}(T;r)\,dr.
 \tag{TL.913}
\]

Applying (TL.910)--(TL.912) to the joined measure gives the single
finite-ladder recurrence

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak S_{J+1,N}^{\rm in}(T)
 \le\theta\mathfrak S_{J,N-1}^{\rm in}(T)
 +C_{n,q}H(0).
 }
 \tag{TL.914}
\]

Since all summands are nonnegative, (TL.914) rearranges to

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 &(1-\theta)\mathfrak S_{J+1,N-1}^{\rm in}(T)
 +\int_0^Le^{-r/2}q^N\mathfrak I_N^{\rm in}(T;r)\,dr\\
 &\qquad\le
 \theta\int_0^Le^{-r/2}q^J\mathfrak I_J^{\rm in}(T;r)\,dr
 +C_{n,q}H(0).
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.915}
\]

For every fixed \(T<T_*\), smoothness on \([0,T]\) and the multiplier
estimate used in (TL.727) bound the total variation, not merely the signed
value, of the coarse face on that compact interval.  Multiplication by
\(q^J\to0\) therefore makes the coarse term in (TL.915) vanish.  The constant
is independent of \(J,N,T\), and the final fine term in (TL.915) is
nonnegative.  Sending \(J\to-\infty\), then \(N\to\infty\), and using
monotone convergence yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak S^{\rm in}(T)
 \le {C_{n,q}\over1-\theta}H(0)
 \qquad(0<T<T_*).
 }
 \tag{TL.916}
\]

This uniform finite-complex estimate removes the simultaneous
ultraviolet/time diagonal: that diagonal is the nonnegative fine term already
retained on the left of (TL.915), not a new exterior that can reappear after
the limits.

## 90. The same datum-paid ladder bounds the critical height and continues the field

Differentiate the exact translation identity (TL.716).  On the causal record
set \(\mathcal R_*\), take a positive part only after the signed sum has been
formed.  A rise below the previous height maximum is not charged, and a
return to an already visited band is not selected again.  Hence every
record-serving positive band increment is served exactly once by its first
incoming boundary measure, so

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 (H^*)'(t)
 &\le {c_n\over2(1-q^{-1})}
 \int_0^Le^{-r/2}
 \sum_jq^j[\partial_tM_j^{(n)}(t;r)]_+\,dr\\
 &\le {c_n\over2(1-q^{-1})}
 {d\over dt}\mathfrak S^{\rm in}(t)
 \quad\text{as positive measures}.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.917}
\]

Integrating (TL.917) and inserting (TL.916) gives, for every \(T<T_*\),

\[
 \boxed{
 H^*(T)
 \le H(0)
 +{c_nC_{n,q}
 \over2(1-q^{-1})(1-\theta)}H(0)
 =:C_*(u_0,\nu)<\infty.
 }
 \tag{TL.918}
\]

The notation on the right records its custody: all root stock is evaluated
at the one original datum.  The row \(n\), plateau, and \(q\) were
fixed once; no later record chooses a new datum, a new fluid, or a new
Sobolev reserve.  For the zero datum the solution is identically zero, while
for nonzero datum (TL.918) is uniform on the whole preterminal history.

Therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 \sup_{t<T_*}\|u(t)\|_{\dot H^{1/2}}^2
 =2\sup_{t<T_*}H(t)
 \le2C_*(u_0,\nu).
 }
 \tag{TL.919}
\]

The homogeneous Sobolev embedding gives
\(\|u(t)\|_{L^3}\le C\|u(t)\|_{\dot H^{1/2}}\).  Hence (TL.919) supplies
the endpoint \(L_t^\infty L_x^3\) continuation readout for the same
classical solution.  The Escauriaza--Seregin--Sverak endpoint theorem for
\(L_t^\infty L_x^3\) suitable solutions therefore excludes a finite singular
terminal time; the standard strong-solution restart relaunches the same
solution past \(T_*\).  Thus the higher Sobolev observations, all constructed
from the initial finite datum, produce the common critical \(H^{1/2}\) height
and its uniform bound; they are not separate energies whose sizes must be
assumed at later times.

## 91. The critical valuation occurs exactly once in the signed q-adic law

Sections 87--90 do not prove the preceding conclusion.  The error is a change
of Sobolev type inside the moving-face construction.  This section returns to
the kinetic density \(e(k,t)\) of (TL.2) and the complete signed VPI flux
\(\Pi(k,t)\) of (TL.4), before any critical weight is applied.

Fix \(q>1\), put \(L=\log q\), and for \(r\in[0,L)\) define

\[
 K_j(r):=e^r q^j,
 \qquad
 E_j(t;r):=\int_{K_j(r)}^{K_{j+1}(r)}e(k,t)\,dk,
\]

\[
 D_j(t;r):=2\nu\int_{K_j(r)}^{K_{j+1}(r)}k^2e(k,t)\,dk,
 \qquad
 \Pi_j(t;r):=\Pi(K_j(r),t).
 \tag{TL.920}
\]

The kinetic receiver law (TL.7) gives, without a positive part,

\[
 \boxed{
 E_j'+D_j=\Pi_j-\Pi_{j+1}.
 }
 \tag{TL.921}
\]

Multiply by the lower-face critical value \(K_j\) and sum over a finite
ladder.  Every common face then occurs with both orientations, and direct
summation by parts gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 {d\over dt}\sum_{j=J}^{N}K_jE_j
 +\sum_{j=J}^{N}K_jD_j
 ={}&K_J\Pi_J-K_N\Pi_{N+1}\\
 &+(1-q^{-1})\sum_{j=J+1}^{N}K_j\Pi_j.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.922}
\]

The factor \(K_j\), equivalently \(q^j\) after the common translation is
fixed, is the critical valuation itself.  It is not a second action or a
weight to be applied again after passing to critical density.

Translation averaging makes this exact rather than comparable.  For each
fixed \(k>0\), the lower face of the unique translated cell containing \(k\)
satisfies

\[
 \int_0^L K_{j(k,r)}(r)\,dr=(1-q^{-1})k.
\]

Tonelli therefore gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_0^L\sum_jK_jE_j\,dr
 &=(1-q^{-1})H(t),\\
 \int_0^L\sum_jK_jD_j\,dr
 &=(1-q^{-1})\,2\nu\int_0^\infty k^3e(k,t)\,dk,\\
 \int_0^L\sum_jK_j\Pi(K_j,t)\,dr
 &=\int_0^\infty\Pi(k,t)\,dk.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.923}
\]

On every compact classical interval the two true weighted exteriors in
(TL.922) vanish as \(J\to-\infty\) and \(N\to\infty\).  Averaging the resulting
identity in \(r\) and using (TL.923) recovers exactly

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)+2\nu\int_0^\infty k^3e(k,t)\,dk
 =\int_0^\infty\Pi(k,t)\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.924}
\]

Thus the signed q-adic calculation cancels all shared scale faces, but it does
not erase the radial work.  Its surviving interior is precisely the complete
critical VPI work already present in (TL.38)--(TL.44).

The type error in Section 87 can now be read directly.  There

\[
 y=\log K^2,
 \qquad
 U(y,t)=\frac12K^2e(K,t),
 \qquad dy=2\frac{dK}{K}.
\]

Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R}U(y,t)\,dy=H(t),
 \qquad
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^{y/2}U(y,t)\,dy
 =\int_0^\infty K^2e(K,t)\,dK=E_1(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.925}
\]

The stock \(U\,dy\) is already critically weighted.  The factor \(e^{x/2}\)
in (TL.899b)--(TL.899c) therefore raises its boundary reading to the next
Sobolev moment.  No deweighting identity returns that measure to the
\(H\)-trace in (TL.716).  Moreover the current paired with \(U\) has the exact
coarse boundary

\[
 Q(-\infty,t)=\mathcal Q(0,t)=P_H(t),
 \tag{TL.926}
\]

not merely a one-time stock inherited from \(H(0)\).  The transitions
(TL.907)--(TL.917) suppress both facts.  Hence (TL.918)--(TL.919) are not
consequences of the preceding identities.

## 92. The surviving q-adic interior is the complete same-field commutator

The correction does not introduce another source.  Put

\[
 A_u v:=u\cdot\nabla v,
 \qquad B(u,u)=\mathbb P A_u u.
\]

Incompressibility makes \(A_u\) skew-adjoint, while the Leray projector and
\(\Lambda\) commute.  The right side of (TL.924) is therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 P_H(t)
 &:=\int_0^\infty\Pi(k,t)\,dk
 =-\operatorname{Re}\langle B(u,u),\Lambda u\rangle\\
 &=-\frac12\operatorname{Re}
 \langle u,[\Lambda,A_u]u\rangle.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.927}
\]

In Fourier variables, with \(k=p+q\), this is the exact all-triad law

\[
 \boxed{
 P_H
 =-\frac12\operatorname{Re}
 \iint i\,(\widehat u(p)\cdot q)
 \bigl(|k|-|q|\bigr)
 \widehat u(q)\cdot\overline{\widehat u(k)}\,dp\,dq.
 }
 \tag{TL.928}
\]

The multiplier difference is decisive.  Uniform carriage commutes with
\(\Lambda\) and contributes zero; critical growth is the signed deformation
of relative scales by the one complete velocity field.  The triangle
inequality also gives

\[
 \bigl||p+q|-|q|\bigr|\le |p|,
 \tag{TL.929}
\]

so no interaction creates an ultraviolet contribution from two participants
that are both absent from the corresponding frequency ancestry.  This is the
carrier-faithful content that a freely prescribed scalar current lacks.

Finally, set

\[
 Z:=\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u),
 \qquad W:=\nu\Lambda^{3/2}u.
\]

The corrected q-adic identity and the one VPI tangent give the exact square

\[
 \boxed{
 H'
 +\frac1\nu\left\|W+\frac12Z\right\|_2^2
 =\frac1{4\nu}\|Z\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.930}
\]

Thus the valid route after common-face cancellation is fixed without selecting
records or inventing a boundary payer: retain the signed commutator (TL.927)
inside the simultaneous tangent square (TL.930), and use the full Sobolev/VPI
rows to control its ultraviolet half-derivative without applying the critical
valuation twice.  Any subsequent contraction must be proved at this joined
commutator level.

## 93. The surrounding kinetic and enstrophy rows pay diffuse q-adic critical height

The corrected ladder also gives a datum-finite statement which was obscured
by the second critical valuation in Section 87.  For the translated grid in
(TL.920), put

\[
 a_j(t;r):=K_j(r)E_j(t;r),
 \qquad
 A(t;r):=\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}a_j(t;r),
 \qquad
 M(t;r):=\sup_{j\in\mathbb Z}a_j(t;r).
 \tag{TL.931}
\]

These are readings of the same kinetic spectral measure.  Since
\(K_j\le k<K_{j+1}=qK_j\) on the \(j\)-th cell,

\[
 \boxed{
 {1\over q}H(t)\le A(t;r)\le H(t),
 \qquad
 \sum_j{a_j(t;r)\over K_j(r)}=\mathcal E(t),
 \qquad
 \sum_jK_j(r)a_j(t;r)\le E_1(t),
 }
 \tag{TL.932}
\]

where

\[
 \mathcal E(t)=\int_0^\infty e(k,t)\,dk,
 \qquad
 E_1(t)=\int_0^\infty k^2e(k,t)\,dk.
\]

The following elementary q-adic quantile inequality supplies the relation
among the three rows.  If \(a_j\ge0\), \(A=\sum_ja_j>0\), and
\(M=\sup_ja_j\), then

\[
 \boxed{
 \left(\sum_jq^{-j}a_j\right)
 \left(\sum_jq^ja_j\right)
 \ge {A^2\over16q}\,q^{A/(2M)}.
 }
 \tag{TL.933}
\]

Indeed, normalize \(p_j=a_j/A\), and choose lower and upper quartile
indices \(j_-\le j_+\) so that each exterior carries mass at least
\(1/4\), while the open exterior beyond the chosen index carries less than
\(1/4\).  The intervening integer interval carries at least half the mass.
Since every atom is at most \(M/A\),

\[
 j_+-j_-\ge {A\over2M}-1.
\]

The two moments are at least \(q^{-j_-}/4\) and \(q^{j_+}/4\),
respectively, which proves (TL.933).  Infinite sequences follow from finite
truncation and monotone convergence.

Applying (TL.933) to (TL.931), with \(K_j=e^rq^j\), cancels the common
translation factor.  Equations (TL.932) then give, for every \(r\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal E(t)E_1(t)
 \ge {H(t)^2\over16q^3}
 \exp\!\left\{{\log q\over2q}{H(t)\over M(t;r)}\right\}.
 }
 \tag{TL.934}
\]

Define the grid-independent q-band envelope

\[
 M_q(t):=\sup_{K>0}K\int_K^{qK}e(k,t)\,dk.
 \tag{TL.935}
\]

Since \(M(t;r)\le M_q(t)\), (TL.934) remains true with \(M_q\) in
place of \(M(t;r)\).  The zero solution is assigned zero integrand.  The
kinetic-energy identity

\[
 \mathcal E(t)+2\nu\int_0^tE_1(s)\,ds=\mathcal E(0)
\]

therefore yields the datum-finite exponential occupancy law

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T H(t)^2
 \exp\!\left\{{\log q\over2q}{H(t)\over M_q(t)}\right\}dt
 \le {8q^3\over\nu}\,\mathcal E(0)^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.936}
\]

This is the exact surrounding-row consequence of the single-valuation
q-adic law.  Critical height distributed through an increasing number of
q-bands carries an exponentially increasing simultaneous kinetic--enstrophy
cost, already paid by the initial datum.  Hence an ultraviolet strengthening
which survives this estimate must place a fixed fraction of its critical
height in a bounded number of adjacent bands.  That surviving concentration
is not a separately admitted scenario: it is the part of the same field on
which the commutator in (TL.927) must now be evaluated.

## 94. A concentrated critical band cannot jump the q-ladder

The survivor of (TL.936) is now kept on the same kinetic carrier.  Let
\(P_{>K}\) and \(P_{<K/2}\) denote the sharp Fourier projections, with
the boundary spheres immaterial for the smooth density, and write

\[
 u_{>K}:=P_{>K}u,
 \qquad u_\ell:=P_{<K/2}u,
 \qquad u_h:=u-u_\ell.
\]

The definition of the signed flux gives the exact Hilbert-space reading

\[
 \boxed{
 \Pi(K,t)=-\operatorname{Re}
 \langle B(u,u),u_{>K}\rangle.
 }
 \tag{TL.937}
\]

The Fourier support of \(B(u_\ell,u_\ell)\) is contained in
\(\{|\xi|<K\}\).  Therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 \Pi(K)
 =-\operatorname{Re}\left\langle
 B(u_\ell,u_h)+B(u_h,u_\ell)+B(u_h,u_h),
 u_{>K}\right\rangle.
 }
 \tag{TL.938}
\]

This is an exact same-field ancestry law.  A face above \(K\) has both an
actual receiving leg above \(K\) and at least one antecedent leg at or above
\(K/2\); two legs lying wholly below \(K/2\) make no contribution.  On the
q-grid, if

\[
 m_q:=\lceil\log_q2\rceil,
\]

then every contribution to \(\Pi(K_j)\) contains an antecedent at or above
\(K_{j-m_q}\).  Thus the nonlinear term can deform and transfer the existing
spectrum, but it cannot insert energy beyond a q-face without a comparable
frequency already participating in that same triad.

The temporal statement is equally exact.  For a fixed physical band define

\[
 a_K(t):=K\int_K^{qK}e(k,t)\,dk.
\]

Integrating (TL.6) with its heat factor gives, for \(s<t\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 a_K(t)={}&K\int_K^{qK}
 e^{-2\nu k^2(t-s)}e(k,s)\,dk\\
 &+K\int_s^t\int_K^{qK}
 e^{-2\nu k^2(t-r)}T(k,r)\,dk\,dr.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.939}
\]

There is no reset term: the later band consists of the part of its earlier
content which survived in the same history plus the signed complete transfer
performed during that history.  Differentiating instead and using (TL.921)
gives its q-face receiver law

\[
 \boxed{
 a_K'(t)
 +2\nu K\int_K^{qK}k^2e(k,t)\,dk
 =K\bigl[\Pi(K,t)-\Pi(qK,t)\bigr].
 }
 \tag{TL.940}
\]

In particular,

\[
 \boxed{
 a_K'(t)+2\nu K^2a_K(t)
 \le K\bigl[\Pi(K,t)-\Pi(qK,t)\bigr].
 }
 \tag{TL.941}
\]

Thus a positive strengthening of the concentrated survivor in (TL.936)
must be an actual signed transfer across its two physical q-faces, must carry
the high-frequency ancestry in (TL.938), and must simultaneously replace the
scale-dependent loss in (TL.941).  These three statements concern the same
band of the same solution; none is a separately prescribed cascade rule.

## 95. Every concentrated q-band amplification has a fixed critical source charge

Let

\[
 u_K:=P_{[K,qK]}u,
 \qquad
 B_K:=P_{[K,qK]}B(u,u),
\]

and retain the band stock \(a_K\) from Section 94.  The source and loss in
(TL.940) are exactly

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 S_K&:=K[\Pi(K)-\Pi(qK)]
     =-K\operatorname{Re}\langle B_K,u_K\rangle,\\
 L_K&:=2\nu K\int_K^{qK}k^2e(k,t)\,dk
     =\nu K\|\Lambda u_K\|_2^2,
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.942}
\]

so that \(a_K'+L_K=S_K\).  Define the critical source square on this
same band by

\[
 \mathcal Z_K(t):=K\|\Lambda^{-1}B_K(t)\|_2^2.
 \tag{TL.943}
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz is taken only after the two q-faces have been joined:

\[
 \boxed{
 |S_K|^2\le {1\over\nu}\,\mathcal Z_KL_K.
 }
 \tag{TL.944}
\]

The two equality defects

\[
 \mathfrak y_K
 :=\left(\sqrt{L_K}-{1\over2}sqrt{\mathcal Z_K/\nu}\right)^2,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak c_K
 :=\sqrt{\mathcal Z_KL_K/\nu}-S_K
\]

are nonnegative, and direct expansion gives the exact joined square

\[
 \boxed{
 a_K'+\mathfrak y_K+\mathfrak c_K
 ={\mathcal Z_K\over4\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.945}
\]

Consequently, on the nonzero set,

\[
 \boxed{
 [\partial_t\log a_K]_+
 \le {\mathcal Z_K\over4\nu a_K}.
 }
 \tag{TL.946}
\]

In particular, if the same fixed band increases from \(A\) to
\(\lambda A\), \(\lambda>1\), over an interval \(I\), then

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_I{\mathcal Z_K(t)\over a_K(t)}\,dt
 \ge4\nu\log\lambda.
 }
 \tag{TL.947}
\]

The charge has exactly one critical valuation.  On any translated q-grid,
orthogonality of the disjoint spectral cells and
\(K_j\le k<qK_j\) give

\[
 \boxed{
 {1\over q}\|\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)\|_2^2
 \le\sum_j\mathcal Z_{K_j}
 \le\|\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.948}
\]

Thus (TL.947) is scale independent for the right reason: a critical band
amplification at any radius consumes a fixed amount of the complete
half-derivative VPI source action.  If throughout such an amplification
\(a_K\ge\delta H\), then

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_I{\mathcal Z_K(t)\over H(t)}\,dt
 \ge4\nu\delta\log\lambda.
 }
 \tag{TL.949}
\]

Together, (TL.936) and (TL.949) make the q-adic split exhaustive without
introducing events as independent objects: distributed critical height is
paid by the surrounding kinetic--enstrophy rows, while concentrated
strengthening is registered by the critical source square of the same
joined band current.  The remaining calculation must obtain the upper
history estimate for the latter from the complete commutator, not from a
second spectral valuation.

## 96. The surviving commutator is the radial motion of the complete material-pair field

The critical source in Section 95 has a direct carrier formula.  Let
\(c_{3,1/2}>0\) be the constant in

\[
 \langle f,\Lambda f\rangle
 ={c_{3,1/2}\over2}
 \iint_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3}
 {|f(x)-f(y)|^2\over|x-y|^4}\,dx\,dy.
\]

For the volume-preserving material map \(X(a,t)\), define the complete pair
coordinates

\[
 R_{ab}(t):=X(a,t)-X(b,t),
 \qquad
 V_{ab}(t):=u(X(a,t),t)-u(X(b,t),t).
 \tag{TL.950}
\]

They obey the one joined material law

\[
 \dot R_{ab}=V_{ab},
 \qquad
 \dot V_{ab}
 =-[\nabla p(X(a))-\nabla p(X(b))]
 +\nu[\Delta u(X(a))-\Delta u(X(b))].
 \tag{TL.951}
\]

No pair is an independent subsystem; \(X,u,p\), and every pair on the
right are determined simultaneously by the same whole field.  Volume
preservation rewrites the critical height as

\[
 \boxed{
 H(t)={c_{3,1/2}\over4}
 \iint {|V_{ab}(t)|^2\over|R_{ab}(t)|^4}\,da\,db.
 }
 \tag{TL.952}
\]

Differentiate (TL.952) using (TL.951).  At each fixed separation, the
pressure-gradient increment is orthogonal in the whole-space integral to
the divergence-free velocity increment.  Integration by parts in the
viscous increment gives \(-\nu\|\Lambda^{3/2}u\|_2^2\).  Hence

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)
 =-c_{3,1/2}\iint
 {(R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab})|V_{ab}|^2\over|R_{ab}|^6}\,da\,db
 -\nu\|\Lambda^{3/2}u(t)\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.953}
\]

Comparison with (TL.924) and (TL.927) gives the exact identification

\[
 \boxed{
 P_H(t)
 =-c_{3,1/2}\iint
 {(R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab})|V_{ab}|^2\over|R_{ab}|^6}\,da\,db
 =-\frac12\operatorname{Re}
 \langle u,[\Lambda,A_u]u\rangle.
 }
 \tag{TL.954}
\]

Thus the q-adic interior has not become a spectral mechanism separate from
the fluid.  It is the complete critical incidence field moving radially in
material-pair separation: pair contraction has
\(R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab}<0\), pair expansion has the opposite sign, and all
directions and locations are summed before the sign of \(P_H\) is read.
The evolution of \(V_{ab}\), including the pressure response and viscosity,
remains inside (TL.951) and determines whether that radial projection can
persist.

For later use, put

\[
 d\mu_H(a,b,t)
 :={c_{3,1/2}\over4}
 {|V_{ab}(t)|^2\over|R_{ab}(t)|^4}\,da\,db,
 \qquad
 \gamma_{ab}(t):=-{R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab}\over|R_{ab}|^2}.
 \tag{TL.955}
\]

Then, exactly,

\[
 \boxed{
 H=\int d\mu_H,
 \qquad
 P_H=4\int\gamma_{ab}\,d\mu_H.
 }
 \tag{TL.956}
\]

This is the native all-field coordinate for the upper history estimate left
by (TL.949): it must control the continued material-pair contraction in
(TL.956), with the simultaneous evolution (TL.951), rather than control an
independently assigned shell source.

## 97. The complete pair field has a positive separation-space continuity law

Use current center and separation coordinates

\[
 x={x_++x_-\over2},
 \qquad z=x_+-x_-,
\]

and define

\[
 V(x,z,t):=u(x+z/2,t)-u(x-z/2,t),
 \qquad
 M(x,z,t):={u(x+z/2,t)+u(x-z/2,t)\over2}.
\]

The two material equations combine exactly into

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_tV+M\cdot\nabla_xV+V\cdot\nabla_zV
 =-\delta_z\nabla p+\nu\Delta_xV,
 \qquad
 \nabla_x\cdot M=\nabla_z\cdot V=0.
 }
 \tag{TL.957}
\]

Thus the complete pair transport is divergence free in the six-dimensional
\((x,z)\) coordinates.  Taking the scalar product with \(V\) gives

\[
 \partial_t{|V|^2\over2}
 +\nabla_x\cdot\left(M{|V|^2\over2}\right)
 +\nabla_z\cdot\left(V{|V|^2\over2}\right)
 =-V\cdot\delta_z\nabla p
 +\nu\Delta_x{|V|^2\over2}-\nu|\nabla_xV|^2.
 \tag{TL.958}
\]

Now integrate over every center \(x\), retaining the full separation
variable.  Define

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal A(z,t)&:={1\over2}\int_{\mathbb R^3}|V(x,z,t)|^2\,dx,\\
 \mathcal F(z,t)&:={1\over2}\int_{\mathbb R^3}V(x,z,t)|V(x,z,t)|^2\,dx,\\
 \mathcal G(z,t)&:=\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\nabla_xV(x,z,t)|^2\,dx.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.959}
\]

For each fixed \(z\), incompressibility gives

\[
 \int V\cdot\delta_z\nabla p\,dx=0.
\]

The center divergences in (TL.958) also integrate to zero.  Therefore the
complete all-field pair law closes as

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\mathcal A+\nabla_z\cdot\mathcal F
 =-\nu\mathcal G.
 }
 \tag{TL.960}
\]

This is not a model continuity equation imposed on a spectrum.  Its density,
current, and loss are all formed from the same velocity increments before
any radial contraction or sign selection.  Equations (TL.952)--(TL.954) are
obtained by testing (TL.960) with the single critical kernel
\(c_{3,1/2}|z|^{-4}/2\).

The current also has a same-separation source--loss bound.  Interpolation
between \(L_x^2\) and \(L_x^6\), followed by Sobolev in \(x\), gives

\[
 |\mathcal F(z,t)|
 \le C\mathcal A(z,t)^{3/4}\mathcal G(z,t)^{3/4}.
\]

Hence, with the quotient set to zero when \(\mathcal A=0\),

\[
 \boxed{
 {|\mathcal F(z,t)|^{4/3}\over\mathcal A(z,t)}
 \le C\mathcal G(z,t).
 }
 \tag{TL.961}
\]

Moreover, translation invariance gives

\[
 \mathcal G(z,t)
 \le4\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^2.
\]

The kinetic-energy identity therefore supplies the uniform fixed-separation
history bound

\[
 \boxed{
 \sup_{z\in\mathbb R^3}
 \int_0^T{|\mathcal F(z,t)|^{4/3}\over\mathcal A(z,t)}\,dt
 \le {C\mathcal E(0)\over\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.962}
\]

Finally, incompressibility has an exact angular consequence before the
activity weight is inserted:

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb S^2}V(x,rn,t)\cdot n\,d\omega(n)=0
 \qquad(x\in\mathbb R^3,\ r>0).
 }
 \tag{TL.963}
\]

Thus no separation sphere undergoes uniform radial contraction.  The
positive critical work in (TL.954) is precisely the covariance created when
larger relative-velocity activity occupies the contracting directions of
this zero-flux all-field geometry.  The q-adic upper estimate must sum the
datum-finite action (TL.962) along the actual signed radial current of
(TL.960), so that a separation crossing is not charged again at each finer
radius.

## 98. The material-pair continuity law has the same single-valuation q-adic telescope

Radialize (TL.960) only after the whole center field has been integrated.  Put

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \overline A(r,t)&:=\int_{\mathbb S^2}\mathcal A(rn,t)\,d\omega(n),\\
 \overline F(r,t)&:=\int_{\mathbb S^2}\mathcal F(rn,t)\cdot n\,d\omega(n),\\
 \overline G(r,t)&:=\int_{\mathbb S^2}\mathcal G(rn,t)\,d\omega(n),
 \end{aligned}
\]

and

\[
 \rho(r,t):=r^2\overline A(r,t),
 \qquad
 J(r,t):=r^2\overline F(r,t),
 \qquad
 L(r,t):=\nu r^2\overline G(r,t).
\]

The divergence theorem on each separation sphere gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\rho+\partial_rJ=-L.
 }
 \tag{TL.964}
\]

Fix \(q>1\), let \(\ell_j(\theta)=e^\theta q^j\) for
\(0\le\theta<\log q\), and define the unweighted pair stocks

\[
 \mathsf E_j:=\int_{\ell_j}^{\ell_{j+1}}\rho(r,t)\,dr,
 \qquad
 \mathsf D_j:=\int_{\ell_j}^{\ell_{j+1}}L(r,t)\,dr,
 \qquad
 \mathsf J_j:=J(\ell_j,t).
 \tag{TL.965}
\]

They satisfy the exact receiver law

\[
 \mathsf E_j'+\mathsf D_j=\mathsf J_j-\mathsf J_{j+1}.
\]

Apply the critical separation valuation once, at the lower face:
\(w_j=\ell_j^{-4}\).  Finite summation gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 {d\over dt}\sum_{j=J_0}^{N}w_j\mathsf E_j
 +\sum_{j=J_0}^{N}w_j\mathsf D_j
 ={}&w_{J_0}\mathsf J_{J_0}-w_N\mathsf J_{N+1}\\
 &-(q^4-1)\sum_{j=J_0+1}^{N}w_j\mathsf J_j.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.966}
\]

Every common material-separation face has cancelled before the one residual
critical work is read.  Translation averaging is again exact: for every
fixed \(r>0\),

\[
 \int_0^{\log q}\ell_{j(r,\theta)}(\theta)^{-4}\,d\theta
 ={q^4-1\over4r^4},
\]

while

\[
 \int_0^{\log q}\sum_jw_jJ(\ell_j,t)\,d\theta
 =\int_0^\infty r^{-5}J(r,t)\,dr.
\]

The true exterior terms in (TL.966) vanish for every compact classical
interval.  Sending the finite faces to zero and infinity, averaging in
\(\theta\), and multiplying by \(c_{3,1/2}/2\) therefore recovers

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)
 +\nu\|\Lambda^{3/2}u(t)\|_2^2
 =-2c_{3,1/2}\int_0^\infty r^{-3}\overline F(r,t)\,dr
 =P_H(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.967}
\]

This is the material-pair form of (TL.922)--(TL.924).  The spectral and
separation ladders are two exact readings of the same VPI current, and both
leave the same single critical interior after cancelling all shared faces.
No extra q-weight is available: the next estimate must act on that signed
interior with (TL.961)--(TL.963) still attached.

## 99. The pair action recovers the surrounding-row temporal governor without clipping

Spherical Hölder applied to (TL.961) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 |\overline F(r,t)|^{4/3}
 \le C\overline A(r,t)\overline G(r,t).
 }
 \tag{TL.968}
\]

The pair stock has three exact Fourier upper readings:

\[
 \boxed{
 \overline A(r,t)
 \le C\min\{r^2E_1(t),\ rH(t),\ \mathcal E(t)\}.
 }
 \tag{TL.969}
\]

Indeed,

\[
 \mathcal A(z,t)
 ={1\over2}\int
 |e^{i\xi\cdot z}-1|^2|\widehat u(\xi,t)|^2\,d\xi,
\]

and the three bounds follow respectively from
\(|e^{ia}-1|^2\le a^2\),
\(|e^{ia}-1|^2\le2|a|\), and
\(|e^{ia}-1|^2\le4\), followed by angular integration.

Young's inequality in the same radial integral as (TL.967) now gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 |P_H|
 &\le C\int_0^\infty
 r^{-3}\overline A^{3/4}\overline G^{3/4}\,dr\\
 &\le {\nu\over2}\|\Lambda^{3/2}u\|_2^2
 +{C\over\nu^3}\int_0^\infty r^{-6}\overline A(r,t)^3\,dr.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.970}
\]

For a nonzero state set

\[
 r_1={H\over E_1},
 \qquad
 r_2={\mathcal E\over H}.
\]

The moment interpolation \(H^2\le\mathcal E E_1\) gives
\(r_1\le r_2\).  Use the three bounds in (TL.969) on
\((0,r_1)\), \((r_1,r_2)\), and \((r_2,\infty)\), respectively.  Direct
integration yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^\infty r^{-6}\overline A(r,t)^3\,dr
 \le C H(t)E_1(t)^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.971}
\]

Consequently the complete material-pair law proves

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)+{\nu\over2}\|\Lambda^{3/2}u(t)\|_2^2
 \le {C\over\nu^3}H(t)E_1(t)^2,
 }
 \tag{TL.972}
\]

and hence

\[
 \boxed{
 [\partial_t\log H(t)]_+
 \le {C\over\nu^3}E_1(t)^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.973}
\]

This derivation uses the complete pair density, current, and loss in
(TL.960); it does not construct a positive compression scalar and later
recombine it with a separate chord statistic.  It also shows why the q-adic
charge in Section 95 is sharper than applying (TL.973) everywhere:
\(E_1^2\) prices every fine-gradient direction, whereas
