\(\mathcal Z_K/a_K\) prices only the signed joined source which actually
strengthens the occupied q-band.

## 100. The heat-normalized tower is a single-valued resolution of kinetic stock

The valid tower calculation can be placed directly over the kinetic density
in Section 91.  Set

\[
 x=\log k,
 \qquad
 \varepsilon(x,t):=e^x e(e^x,t),
 \qquad
 \pi(x,t):=\Pi(e^x,t),
\]

and

\[
 d(x,t):=2\nu e^{2x}\varepsilon(x,t).
\]

Multiplying the spectral energy law by \(k=e^x\) gives the exact kinetic
log-scale balance

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\varepsilon+\partial_x\pi=-d.
 }
 \tag{TL.974}
\]

Its unweighted mass and its first exponential moment are, respectively,

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R}\varepsilon(x,t)\,dx=\mathcal E(t),
 \qquad
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^x\varepsilon(x,t)\,dx=H(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.975}
\]

Thus the critical factor in the corrected q-adic law is exactly the one
factor \(e^x=k\).

The heat-normalized rows in (TL.693) are positive convolutions of this same
kinetic stock.  Define

\[
 \kappa_n(z):={2^n\over\Gamma(n)}e^{nz}e^{-2e^z},
 \qquad
 \eta_n(s):=2\kappa_n(-2s),
 \qquad
 R_n(y,t):=2\rho_n(-2y,t).
\]

Then direct substitution in (TL.693) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 R_n(\,\cdot\,,t)=\eta_n*\varepsilon(\,\cdot\,,t),
 \qquad
 \eta_n\ge0,
 \qquad
 \int_{\mathbb R}\eta_n(s)\,ds=1.
 }
 \tag{TL.976}
\]

The only critical valuation of this convolution is its first exponential
moment.  Its kernel number is

\[
 m_n:=\int_{\mathbb R}e^s\eta_n(s)\,ds
 =\sqrt2\,{\Gamma(n-\frac12)\over\Gamma(n)}
 ={1\over c_n},
 \tag{TL.977}
\]

so (TL.694) and (TL.696) become

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R}R_n(y,t)\,dy=\mathcal E(t),
 \qquad
 c_n\int_{\mathbb R}e^yR_n(y,t)\,dy=H(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.978}
\]

More generally, for every finite signed measure \(d\zeta\) for which the
moment exists, Fubini gives the exact eigenmeasure identity

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^y\,(\eta_n*d\zeta)(y)
 =m_n\int_{\mathbb R}e^x\,d\zeta(x).
 }
 \tag{TL.979}
\]

Consequently the higher heat rows sharpen the log-frequency observation but
do not create a second critical stock or a smaller critical valuation.  The
row recurrence (TL.697)--(TL.700), the signed affine descent
(TL.701), and the translated q-grid identities (TL.703)--(TL.719) remain
valid precisely in their original kinetic-stock form: their single factor
\(e^{-\sigma/2}\), equivalently \(e^x\), is the factor already displayed in
(TL.975).  Applying another \(e^{y/2}\) after passing to the \(U\) of
(TL.890) instead evaluates \(E_1\), as (TL.925) proves.

In particular, signed descent removes shared-face recounting, while the
surviving critical q-current is unchanged up to the fixed normalization
\(m_n\).  Any strict q-contraction must therefore be produced by the actual
completed VPI current and its simultaneous loss, not by applying the row
kernel a second time.

## 101. The surviving q-current remains joined to one scale-uniform VPI cone

Use the localized second heat row \((\rho,\mathcal J)\) of
(TL.638)--(TL.646), whose reorientation is \(R_2(y,t)=2\rho(-2y,t)\)
in Section 100.
Its completed current is

\[
 \mathcal J=\mathcal J_{\rm VPI}+\mathcal J_\nu,
 \qquad
 \mathcal J_\nu\ge0,
\]

and the same-field multiplier estimate (TL.660) says, with

\[
 \beta(t)=\sup_j2^{j/2}\|\Delta_ju(t)\|_2,
 \qquad
 \vartheta(t)={C\beta(t)\over\nu},
\]

that

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathcal J_{\rm VPI}(e^\sigma,t)|
 \le\vartheta(t)\mathcal J_\nu(e^\sigma,t)
 \quad\hbox{at every heat depth }\sigma.
 }
 \tag{TL.980}
\]

This estimate is imposed before the q-faces are selected.  Therefore every
finite signed q-complex inherits, after all common faces cancel, the
pointwise one-current inequality

\[
 \boxed{
 [-\mathcal J]_+
 \le(\vartheta-1)_+\mathcal J_\nu.
 }
 \tag{TL.981}
\]

The orientation here is physical: negative \(\mathcal J\) is the part capable
of carrying kinetic stock toward smaller heat depth and hence increasing its
single critical valuation.  Equation (TL.981) says that such motion is not a
free shell source; it is the negative projection of the complete VPI current
at a time when the same field's active critical octave is large enough to
reverse its simultaneous irreversible orientation.

The coefficient itself is already controlled in the exact surrounding-row
sense:

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\vartheta(t)^4\,dt
 \le {C\mathcal E_0^2\over\nu^5}.
 }
 \tag{TL.982}
\]

Combining (TL.646) and (TL.981) gives, without an absolute value on the
completed current,

\[
 \boxed{
 [H'(t)]_+
 \le {1\over\sqrt{2\pi}}
 (\vartheta(t)-1)_+
 \int_0^\infty\tau^{-3/2}
 \mathcal J_\nu(\tau,t)\,d\tau.
 }
 \tag{TL.983}
\]

The same integral is exactly the critical viscous row, not a second
frequency weight introduced by the proof.  Equations (TL.980)--(TL.983)
therefore give the corrected first q-adic split.  Times with
\(\vartheta\le1\) carry no fineward critical current at any depth.  On the
remaining times the diffuse case is paid by (TL.936), while the concentrated
case has \(H\lesssim_q M_q\simeq\beta^2\) and its strengthening is the
same material-pair/source charge in (TL.949) and (TL.967), not a heat-row
boundary manufactured by convolution.

## 102. The q-face inequality is taken after the common signed faces cancel

Return to the translated heat grid of (TL.703).  For a finite ladder write

\[
 A_{J,N}^{(2)}(t;r)
 :=\sum_{j=J}^Nq^jM_j^{(2)}(t;r)
\]

and abbreviate
\(\mathcal J_j=\mathcal J(e^{s_j(r)},t)\).  The receiver identity
(TL.706), with no sign selection on an individual face, is

\[
 {d\over dt}A_{J,N}^{(2)}
 =q^N\mathcal J_{N+1}-q^J\mathcal J_J
 +\mathfrak C_{J,N},
 \qquad
 \mathfrak C_{J,N}
 :=-(1-q^{-1})\sum_{j=J+1}^Nq^j\mathcal J_j.
 \tag{TL.984}
\]

The two displayed endpoint currents are the only exterior faces.  Every
other face has already occurred once with each orientation before the
positive part of the surviving interior is taken.  Equations
(TL.981) and (TL.984) therefore imply

\[
 \boxed{
 [\mathfrak C_{J,N}]_+
 \le
 (1-q^{-1})(\vartheta-1)_+
 \sum_{j=J+1}^Nq^j\mathcal J_{\nu,j}.
 }
 \tag{TL.985}
\]

This is the corrected signed q-adic estimate.  It has one \(q^j\) valuation,
inherited from the kinetic stock in (TL.704), and no event ledger.

Translation averaging is exact.  Every \(\sigma\) is one face
\(s_j(r)\), and \(e^{-r/2}q^j=e^{-\sigma/2}\).  Hence

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^{2\log q}e^{-r/2}
 \sum_jq^j\mathcal J_{\nu,j}(t;r)\,dr
 =
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^{-\sigma/2}
 \mathcal J_\nu(e^\sigma,t)\,d\sigma.
 }
 \tag{TL.986}
\]

After the two true exterior faces are sent to the smooth coarse and fine
limits, (TL.985)--(TL.986) recover (TL.983).  Thus the q-adic and continuous
heat-depth calculations now agree exactly: shared signed transport cancels,
the single critical valuation survives, and every positive remainder is a
negative projection of the complete VPI current against its own simultaneous
viscous current.

## 103. A concentrated q-band has no nonlinear self-source

The concentrated branch can be reduced before applying a magnitude estimate.
Let \(P_K\) be the divergence-free Fourier projection to
\(\{K\le|\xi|<qK\}\), and write

\[
 v=P_Ku,
 \qquad
 w=(I-P_K)u.
\]

For the pressure-completed trilinear form

\[
 b(a,b,c):=\operatorname{Re}
 \langle\mathbb P(a\cdot\nabla b),c\rangle
\]

incompressibility gives

\[
 b(a,b,c)=-b(a,c,b).
 \tag{TL.987}
\]

In particular,

\[
 b(v,v,v)=0,
 \qquad
 b(w,v,v)=0.
\]

Expanding the exact band work in (TL.942) and using these two cancellations
gives

\[
 \boxed{
 S_K
 =-K\,b(u,u,v)
 =K\,b(v,v,w)-K\,b(w,w,v).
 }
 \tag{TL.988}
\]

Thus the band does not supply its own nonlinear strengthening.  The first
term is exchange between the band and the part of the same field receiving
the output of two band inputs; Fourier support restricts that part of \(w\)
to frequencies below \(2qK\).  In the second term, two complementary inputs
produce a band output, and at least one input has frequency at least \(K/2\).
Both are complete same-field exchanges; the projection already contains the
incompressibility and pressure response.

The critical atom therefore obeys the exact receiver law

\[
 \boxed{
 a_K'
 +\nu K\|\Lambda v\|_2^2
 =
 K\,b(v,v,w)-K\,b(w,w,v).
 }
 \tag{TL.989}
\]

If \(w=0\), the right side vanishes identically.  A successor strengthening
is consequently not a smaller copy generated by an isolated active band:
it is an actual transfer through the complementary part of the one field.
When all q-bands are summed, those exchanges retain their signs and recombine
into the commutator current

\[
 \boxed{
 -\sum_jK_j b(u,u,P_{K_j}u)
 =-\operatorname{Re}\langle B(u,u),\Lambda_q u\rangle
 =-\frac12\operatorname{Re}
 \langle u,[\Lambda_q,u\cdot\nabla]u\rangle,
 }
 \tag{TL.990}
\]

where \(\Lambda_q=\sum_jK_jP_{K_j}\) is the single-valued q-step
approximation of \(\Lambda\).  Equation (TL.990) is the exchange form to be
combined with (TL.985): estimate the signed complementary work only after
the q-sum, so donor loss and receiver gain are not billed as independent
actions.

The same exchange has zero kinetic valuation before the critical factor is
applied:

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j{S_{K_j}\over K_j}
 =-\sum_j b(u,u,P_{K_j}u)
 =-b(u,u,u)=0.
 }
 \tag{TL.991}
\]

Hence the surviving q-current is literally the change of scale of kinetic
energy already present in the one field.  The zero-mass signed exchange in
(TL.991) is valued once by \(K_j\) in (TL.990); there is no second source
and no second critical weight.

## 104. The reciprocal \(L_t^4L_{\rm pair}^2\) law is quadratic, not a parity family

For current endpoints \(x,y\), Kelvin inversion gives

\[
 |\dot Y(x,y,t)|
 ={|\delta_{x-y}u(y,t)|\over|x-y|^2}.
\]

Consequently, for every \(p>0\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \|\dot Y(t)\|_{L^p_{x,y}}^p
 =
 \iint_{\mathbb R^3\times\mathbb R^3}
 { |u(x,t)-u(y,t)|^p\over|x-y|^{2p}}\,dx\,dy.
 }
 \tag{TL.992}
\]

For \(3/2<p<3\), this is exactly the Gagliardo seminorm

\[
 \boxed{
 \|\dot Y(t)\|_{L^p_{x,y}}^p
 =[u(t)]_{\dot W^{\,2-3/p,p}}^p,
 }
 \tag{TL.993}
\]

up to the fixed normalization convention.  The range is forced by the actual
pair geometry.  Near \(x=y\),
\(\delta_{x-y}u\sim\nabla u\,(x-y)\), so the radial integral is
\(\int_0^\varepsilon r^{2-p}\,dr\), which diverges at and above \(p=3\).
At large separation the radial integral behaves like
\(\int^\infty r^{2-2p}\,dr\), which requires \(p>3/2\) for a generic
decaying field.

Under the Navier--Stokes scaling
\(u_\lambda(x,t)=\lambda u(\lambda x,\lambda^2t)\),

\[
 \|\dot Y[u_\lambda](t)\|_{L^p_{x,y}}
 =
 \lambda^{\,3-6/p}
 \|\dot Y[u](\lambda^2t)\|_{L^p_{x,y}}.
 \tag{TL.994}
\]

More generally, on a time interval transformed with the same solution,

\[
 \boxed{
 \|\dot Y[u_\lambda]\|_{L_t^aL_{x,y}^p}
 =\lambda^{\,3-6/p-2/a}
 \|\dot Y[u]\|_{L_t^aL_{x,y}^p}.
 }
 \tag{TL.994a}
\]

Thus \(p=2\) is the unique scale-invariant reciprocal-pair exponent.  At that
exponent (TL.992) is the exact critical identity

\[
 H(t)={1\over4\pi^2}\|\dot Y(t)\|_{L^2_{\rm pair}}^2.
 \tag{TL.995}
\]

The surrounding-row interpolation bound
\(\int H^2dt<\infty\) therefore gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \dot Y\in L_t^4L_{\rm pair}^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.996}
\]

There is a narrow neighboring energy-class family, but it is governed by
Sobolev interpolation rather than parity.  For

\[
 2\le p\le{12\over5},
 \qquad
 \theta={7\over2}-{6\over p}\in\left[{1\over2},1\right],
 \qquad
 a={2\over\theta},
\]

the equal-index Sobolev embedding and the two surrounding energy rows give

\[
 \|\dot Y(t)\|_{L^p_{\rm pair}}
 \lesssim
 \|u(t)\|_{\dot H^\theta}
 \le
 \|u(t)\|_2^{1-\theta}\|\nabla u(t)\|_2^\theta,
\]

and hence

\[
 \boxed{
 \|\dot Y\|_{L_t^aL_{\rm pair}^p}^a
 \le {C_p\over\nu}\|u_0\|_2^a,
 \qquad
 {2\over a}+{6\over p}={7\over2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.996a}
\]

Only the endpoint \((a,p)=(4,2)\) in this paid family is the exact quadratic
critical-height identity (TL.995); the other members measure different
fractional Sobolev readouts of the same field.

The time exponent four comes from squaring the quadratic height; it is not
paired with the spatial exponent by even--odd parity.  In particular,
\(L_t^1L_{\rm pair}^3\) is logarithmically divergent on the pair diagonal
for every nonconstant smooth field, and \(L_t^6L_{\rm pair}^4\) is more
singular there.  No temporal
exponent repairs a spatial divergence occurring at almost every fixed time.
For \(p=2\), (TL.994a) also shows that the scale-critical time exponent is
\(a=\infty\), not four.  The continuation
target is not to replace \(L_{\rm pair}^2\) by either exponent.  It is exactly
the temporal upgrade

\[
 \boxed{
 L_t^4L_{\rm pair}^2
 \longrightarrow
 L_t^\infty L_{\rm pair}^2,
 }
 \tag{TL.997}
\]

derived from the same completed q-current.  This fixes the role of the
reciprocal representation in the active argument: it supplies a
datum-finite occupancy law at the unique critical pair exponent, while
(TL.985) and (TL.988)--(TL.991) must prevent that same \(L^2_{\rm pair}\)
speed from forming arbitrarily narrow temporal peaks.

## 105. The cancelled q-current has a datum-finite cumulative-tail speed

The zero-mass exchange in (TL.991) has an exact fine-side primitive.  Retain
one translated q-grid from (TL.920), and set

\[
 \mathscr E_j^\uparrow:=\sum_{m\ge j}E_m,
 \qquad
 \mathscr D_j^\uparrow:=\sum_{m\ge j}D_m,
 \qquad
 s_j:=\Pi_j-\Pi_{j+1}.
 \tag{TL.998}
\]

At the smooth ultraviolet boundary \(\Pi_\infty=0\).  Summing the signed
receiver law (TL.921), before taking any positive part, gives

\[
 \boxed{
 (\mathscr E_j^\uparrow)'+\mathscr D_j^\uparrow=\Pi_j,
 \qquad
 \Pi_j=\sum_{m\ge j}s_m.
 }
 \tag{TL.999}
\]

The q-adic critical stock and its simultaneous loss are the single first
moments of these tails:

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_jK_jE_j=(1-q^{-1})\sum_jK_j\mathscr E_j^\uparrow,
 \qquad
 \sum_jK_jD_j=(1-q^{-1})\sum_jK_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow.
 }
 \tag{TL.1000}
\]

Let \(u_j=P_{[K_j,K_{j+1})}u\),
\(B_j=P_{[K_j,K_{j+1})}B(u,u)\), and
\(z_j:=\|\Lambda^{-1}B_j\|_2^2\).  These are orthogonal readings of the
same completed field.  The exact band source obeys

\[
 s_j=-\operatorname{Re}\langle B_j,u_j\rangle,
 \qquad
 D_j=\nu\|\Lambda u_j\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 {s_j^2\over D_j}\le {z_j\over\nu},
 \tag{TL.1001}
\]

with zero quotients on zero-loss bands.  Apply the weighted Copson inequality
to the signed sequence \(s_j\) with measure atoms \(D_j\).  Finite
truncation followed by Fatou gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_jD_j
 \left({\Pi_j\over\mathscr D_j^\uparrow}\right)^2
 \le4\sum_j{s_j^2\over D_j}
 \le {4\over\nu}\|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1002}
\]

This is an estimate of the actual cumulative face current after the
intervening q-faces have cancelled; no crossing is counted once per band.
The standard product estimate is at exactly the unweighted level appearing
on the right:

\[
 \boxed{
 \|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2
 \le C\|u\|_4^4
 \le C\|u\|_3^2\|u\|_6^2
 \le C H E_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.1003}
\]

Consequently the normalized cumulative-tail speed has a datum-finite history,
uniformly in the q-grid translation:

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T{1\over H(t)}
 \sum_jD_j(t)
 \left({\Pi_j(t)\over\mathscr D_j^\uparrow(t)}\right)^2dt
 \le {C E_0(0)\over\nu^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1004}
\]

This is the upper action sought at the energy valuation.  Its relation to
critical strengthening is also exact.  If

\[
 v_j:={\Pi_j\over\mathscr D_j^\uparrow},
 \qquad
 A_q:=\sum_jK_jE_j,
\]

then (TL.999)--(TL.1000) give

\[
 \boxed{
 A_q'
 =(1-q^{-1})\sum_jK_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow(v_j-1).
 }
 \tag{TL.1005}
\]

Thus transfer and loss have not been split into competing mechanisms:
\(v_j-1\) is the signed speed of the complete fine-side stock relative to
its own simultaneous loss.  Since \(v-1\le v^2/4\),

\[
 \boxed{
 [A_q']_+
 \le {1-q^{-1}\over4}
 \sum_jK_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow v_j^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1006}
\]

Equations (TL.1004) and (TL.1006) identify, without an event ledger, the
precise remaining geometric operation: the datum pays the cumulative-tail
speed once with local loss \(D_j\), while the critical height reads that same
speed at the actual separation/frequency distance
\(K_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow\).  The next coordinate must retain that distance
inside the complete material-pair current rather than inserting it as a
second spectral weight.

## 106. The critical factor reappears as signed weighted pair motion

The material-pair formula (TL.953)--(TL.954) supplies that coordinate
directly.  Put \(r_{ab}=|R_{ab}|\), and on \(V_{ab}\ne0\) define the radial
incidence cosine

\[
 \chi_{ab}:=-{R_{ab}\cdot V_{ab}\over r_{ab}|V_{ab}|},
 \qquad |\chi_{ab}|\le1,
\]

with \(\chi_{ab}=0\) at \(V_{ab}=0\).  Since
\(|\dot Y_{ab}|=|V_{ab}|/r_{ab}^2\), the complete signed critical work is

\[
 \boxed{
 P_H(t)
 =c_{3,1/2}\iint
 \chi_{ab}(t)\,r_{ab}(t)|\dot Y_{ab}(t)|^3\,da\,db.
 }
 \tag{TL.1007}
\]

The factor \(r_{ab}\) is essential.  It is the geometric travel factor left
by (TL.1006), and it removes the logarithmic pair-diagonal divergence of the
raw \(L^3_{\rm pair}\) norm.  In current coordinates,

\[
 \boxed{
 \iint {|u(x)-u(y)|^3\over|x-y|^5}\,dx\,dy
 =[u]_{\dot W^{2/3,3}}^3,
 \qquad
 |P_H|\le c_{3,1/2}[u]_{\dot W^{2/3,3}}^3.
 }
 \tag{TL.1008}
\]

The magnitude estimate obtained from
\(\dot H^{7/6}\hookrightarrow\dot W^{2/3,3}\) and interpolation is

\[
 |P_H|
 \le C\|u\|_{\dot H^{1/2}}\|u\|_{\dot H^{3/2}}^2.
 \tag{TL.1009}
\]

The VPI content is the sign discarded by (TL.1009).  For the complete
center--separation field in (TL.957), set, at fixed \((x,r,t)\),

\[
 a(n):=V(x,rn,t)\cdot n,
 \qquad
 b(n):=|V(x,rn,t)|^2,
 \qquad
 \bar b:={1\over4\pi}\int_{\mathbb S^2}b\,d\omega.
\]

Incompressibility gives \(\int_{\mathbb S^2}a\,d\omega=0\), and therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb S^2}a(n)b(n)\,d\omega
 =\int_{\mathbb S^2}a(n)(b(n)-\bar b)\,d\omega.
 }
 \tag{TL.1010}
\]

Thus critical growth is not uniform radial contraction.  It is the signed
covariance between radial motion and angular variation of the relative-speed
activity in the one incidence sphere.  A spherical Poincare--Gagliardo--
Nirenberg estimate, obtained by subtracting the angular mean of \(V\), gives

\[
 \left|\int_{\mathbb S^2}(V\cdot n)|V|^2\,d\omega\right|
 \le C
 \left(\int_{\mathbb S^2}|V|^2\,d\omega\right)
 \left(\int_{\mathbb S^2}|\nabla_{\mathbb S^2}V|^2\,d\omega\right)^{1/2}.
 \tag{TL.1011}
\]

Writing

\[
 A_\omega(x,r,t):=\int_{\mathbb S^2}|V(x,rn,t)|^2\,d\omega,
 \qquad
 B_\omega(x,r,t):=\int_{\mathbb S^2}
 |\nabla_{\mathbb S^2}V(x,rn,t)|^2\,d\omega,
\]

and applying Cauchy--Schwarz only after (TL.1010), one obtains the
carrier-native bound

\[
 \boxed{
 |P_H(t)|^2
 \le C H(t)\,\mathfrak A_{\rm ang}(t),
 \qquad
 \mathfrak A_{\rm ang}(t)
 :=\int_0^\infty\!\int_{\mathbb R^3}
 r^{-4}A_\omega B_\omega\,dx\,dr.
 }
 \tag{TL.1012}
\]

This is the direct all-field version of the travel factor in (TL.1006).
It neither uses the divergent raw \(L^3_{\rm pair}\) norm nor constructs an
independent active weight: \(A_\omega\), \(B_\omega\), and their sign in
(TL.1010) are simultaneous readings of the same incidence sphere.  The next
evolution calculation is therefore fixed on \(\mathfrak A_{\rm ang}\): carry
its material-time derivative under (TL.957), retaining the pressure response,
angular transport, and center diffusion in their joined signs before any
positive temporal selection.

## 107. Center diffusion and separation diffusion are the same VPI row

Before differentiating the angular covariance, the genuine-increment
constraint must be imposed.  From

\[
 V(x,z,t)=u(x+z/2,t)-u(x-z/2,t)
\]

one has pointwise

\[
 \boxed{
 \Delta_xV(x,z,t)=4\Delta_zV(x,z,t)
 =\Delta u(x+z/2,t)-\Delta u(x-z/2,t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1013}
\]

Consequently the one pair equation (TL.957) has the two identical readings

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_tV+M\cdot\nabla_xV+V\cdot\nabla_zV
 =-\delta_z\nabla p+\nu\Delta_xV
 =-\delta_z\nabla p+4\nu\Delta_zV.
 }
 \tag{TL.1014}
\]

Thus center smoothing and separation smoothing are not distinct processes.
They are the same fixed-viscosity tangent evaluated in two coordinates of the
same complete pair field.  In polar separation coordinates,

\[
 \Delta_z
 =\partial_r^2+{2\over r}\partial_r
  +{1\over r^2}\Delta_{\mathbb S^2},
 \tag{TL.1015}
\]

so the angular deformation in (TL.1012) occurs inside the same row as radial
transport and the two neighboring separation faces.

This joining is visible in the critical kernel.  On the annulus
\(\Omega_{\varepsilon,R}=\{z:\varepsilon<|z|<R\}\), integration by parts
with \(w(z)=|z|^{-4}\) gives, for every fixed center,

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_{\Omega_{\varepsilon,R}}wV\cdot\Delta_zV\,dz
 ={}&-\int_{\Omega_{\varepsilon,R}}w|\nabla_zV|^2\,dz
 +6\int_{\Omega_{\varepsilon,R}}|z|^{-6}|V|^2\,dz\\
 &+\int_{\partial\Omega_{\varepsilon,R}}
 \left(wV\cdot\partial_nV
 -\frac12\partial_nw\,|V|^2\right)dS.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1016}
\]

For a nonzero smooth increment, the two displayed bulk terms need not have
finite limits separately as \(\varepsilon\downarrow0\); their diagonal
parts are cancelled by the inner face in (TL.1016).  On the other hand, the
center reading has no such artificial split:

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}\!\int_{\Omega_{\varepsilon,R}}
 wV\cdot\Delta_xV\,dz\,dx
 =-\int_{\mathbb R^3}\!\int_{\Omega_{\varepsilon,R}}
 w|\nabla_xV|^2\,dz\,dx
 }
 \tag{TL.1017}
\]

Equations
(TL.1013), (TL.1016), and (TL.1017) prove that a positive angular-gradient
term cannot be detached from its radial and face terms and promoted as an
independent entropy production.  The lawful next operation is the q-annular
version of (TL.1016): sum adjacent separation annuli with their signs, cancel
the shared radial faces, and only then contract the surviving angular
covariance from (TL.1010) against the joined viscous remainder.

## 108. The reciprocal material configuration has datum-controlled finite-horizon reach

The paid exponent in Section 104 has a direct same-label consequence which
does not require a record or a selected scale.  From (TL.995), (TL.626), and
Bochner integration on the fixed material-pair labels,

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \|\dot Y\|_{L_t^4L_{a,b}^2}^4
 &=(4\pi^2)^2\int_0^T H(t)^2dt
 \le{(4\pi^2)^2E_0(0)^2\over2\nu},\\
 \|Y(t)-Y(s)\|_{L_{a,b}^2}
 &\le C E_0(0)^{1/2}\nu^{-1/4}|t-s|^{3/4}.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1018}
\]

Although \(Y(t)\) itself need not belong to global \(L^2_{a,b}\) on the
infinite label space, every difference \(Y(t)-Y(s)\) does; (TL.1018) places
the continuing reciprocal configuration in the affine space
\(Y(0)+L^2_{a,b}\).  It is therefore a genuine finite-reach statement about
the same initial pair field, not a comparison between independently chosen
configurations.

More quantitatively, if a measurable fixed set of material pairs
\(\Gamma\) satisfies

\[
 |Y_{ab}(t)-Y_{ab}(s)|\ge\delta
 \qquad((a,b)\in\Gamma),
\]

then

\[
 \boxed{
 \delta^2|\Gamma|
 \le C E_0(0)\nu^{-1/2}|t-s|^{3/2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1019}
\]

Thus any succession carrying a fixed amount of the complete pair population
through a fixed reciprocal distance has a positive material-time thickness.
For critical-height continuation the remaining q-annular calculation must
derive that population--distance product directly from the signed critical
current in (TL.966)--(TL.967); speed growth alone cannot be substituted for
endpoint displacement.  Equations (TL.1004), (TL.1010)--(TL.1017), and
(TL.1018) now supply its three already-paid rows: cumulative current speed,
joined angular/radial deformation, and finite reciprocal reach, all on the
same labelled history.

## 109. The upper paid pair exponent supplies quantitative participation

The endpoint \(p=12/5\) of the real interpolation family (TL.996a) is the
already-paid row which prevents critical reciprocal speed from concentrating
on a vanishing pair population without being registered.  With
\(F_{ab}=\dot Y_{ab}\), volume preservation and (TL.996a) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\|F(t)\|_{L_{a,b}^{12/5}}^2dt
 \le {C\over\nu}\|u_0\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1020}
\]

At a fixed time let \(\Gamma\) be any measurable set of the original pair
labels carrying a fraction \(0<\alpha\le1\) of the critical speed:

\[
 \int_\Gamma|F|^2\,da\,db
 \ge\alpha\int|F|^2\,da\,db.
\]

Hölder's inequality at exponent \(12/5\) gives

\[
 \int_\Gamma|F|^2
 \le\|F\|_{12/5}^2|\Gamma|^{1/6}.
\]

Using (TL.995), every such carrier therefore satisfies the quantitative
all-field participation law

\[
 \boxed{
 |\Gamma|
 \ge
 \left({4\pi^2\alpha H(t)\over
 \|F(t)\|_{12/5}^2}\right)^6.
 }
 \tag{TL.1021}
\]

This statement neither selects a new fluid population nor asserts that one
instantaneous superlevel set persists.  It applies to every measurable set of
the fixed datum labels that actually carries the stated fraction.  Combined
with (TL.1020), it gives the exact exhaustive alternative needed by the
q-annular transport: either a critical receiving layer is carried by a
quantitatively nonvanishing set of the same material pairs, or its attempted
population concentration creates a correspondingly large value of the
already datum-paid \(L_{a,b}^{12/5}\) row.

For a q-annulus in reciprocal radius,

\[
 \Gamma_{K,q}(t):=\{(a,b):K\le|Y_{ab}(t)|<qK\},
\]

the lower bound (TL.1021) is compatible with the geometric crossing distance:
any fixed labels which pass completely from \(|Y|<K\) to \(|Y|\ge qK\)
undergo displacement at least \((q-1)K\).  The remaining signed step is now
specific: use the already-cancelled q-current to extract the same-label
throughgoing subset before applying (TL.1021) and the reach bound (TL.1019).
Taking a fresh active set at each time would discard precisely the identity
which (TL.999), (TL.1014), and the material labels preserve.

## 110. Complete pair activity is transported and killed, never independently rebuilt

The throughgoing population is already present in the positive continuity
law (TL.960).  On \(\{\mathcal A>0\}\), define the two simultaneous readouts

\[
 b(z,t):={\mathcal F(z,t)\over\mathcal A(z,t)},
 \qquad
 \kappa(z,t):={\nu\mathcal G(z,t)\over\mathcal A(z,t)},
\]

and set them to zero on \(\{\mathcal A=0\}\), where necessarily
\(\mathcal F=0\).  Then the exact all-center pair law is

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\mathcal A+\nabla_z\cdot(\mathcal A b)
 =-\kappa\mathcal A,
 \qquad \mathcal A\ge0,\quad\kappa\ge0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1022}
\]

Neither \(b\) nor \(\kappa\) is a prescribed transport or loss.  They are
the energy-weighted velocity and center-gradient loss of the same increment
field in (TL.959).  Equation (TL.1022) is therefore the carrier-level
no-reset law: after the whole center field is included, pair activity can
cross a separation boundary or be removed there, but no positive source
creates a replacement population.

Let the actual activity inside a separation ball be

\[
 \mathcal M(r,t):=\int_{|z|<r}\mathcal A(z,t)\,dz,
\]

with outward current and accumulated loss

\[
 \Phi(r,t):=\int_{|z|=r}\mathcal F(z,t)\cdot n\,dS,
 \qquad
 \mathcal L(r,t):=\nu\int_{|z|<r}\mathcal G(z,t)\,dz.
\]

The divergence theorem gives the exact nested receiver law

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\mathcal M(r,t)+\Phi(r,t)=-\mathcal L(r,t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1023}
\]

An increase of inner activity is consequently an actual inward crossing
\(\Phi<0\) exceeding the simultaneous removal; it cannot be inserted as a
new inner stock.

The critical height is the single negative-fourth moment of this density.
Using \(r^{-4}=4\int_r^\infty s^{-5}ds\) and Tonelli in (TL.952) gives the
positive layer-cake identity

\[
 \boxed{
 H(t)
 ={c_{3,1/2}\over2}
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}{\mathcal A(z,t)\over|z|^4}\,dz
 =2c_{3,1/2}\int_0^\infty r^{-5}\mathcal M(r,t)\,dr.
 }
 \tag{TL.1024}
\]

Substitution of (TL.1023), with the same smooth coarse and fine limits as in
(TL.966)--(TL.967), yields

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)
 =-2c_{3,1/2}\int_0^\infty r^{-5}
 \bigl(\Phi(r,t)+\mathcal L(r,t)\bigr)\,dr.
 }
 \tag{TL.1025}
\]

This is the inner-ball version of the signed q-adic telescope.  On radii
\(r_j=e^\theta q^{-j}\), subtracting (TL.1023) at adjacent radii makes the
same flux \(\Phi(r_j)\) occur with opposite signs before the one critical
factor \(r_j^{-4}\) is applied.  Hence every surviving positive contribution
to (TL.1025) is carried by activity which actually enters a new inner q-layer
and survives its simultaneous loss.  The population in (TL.1021) can be
taken from this one positive throughgoing activity measure only after its
center motion has also been retained.  The marginal law (TL.1022) proves that
there is no independent positive rebuilding, but center integration allows
activity to move between material centers.  The complete lift of that motion
is derived next rather than being renamed as a fresh label population.

## 111. The complete six-dimensional activity current retains every carrier change

Put

\[
 e_V(x,z,t):={|V(x,z,t)|^2\over2},
 \qquad
 \delta_zp(x,t):=p(x+z/2,t)-p(x-z/2,t).
\]

The pressure increment in (TL.958) is an exact center divergence, because

\[
 \delta_z\nabla p=\nabla_x\delta_zp,
 \qquad
 \nabla_x\cdot V=0,
 \qquad
 V\cdot\delta_z\nabla p
 =\nabla_x\cdot(V\,\delta_zp).
 \tag{TL.1026}
\]

Likewise \(\nu\Delta_xe_V\) is retained as center flux.  Thus the complete
pair-energy equation is the positive killed continuity law

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_te_V
 +\nabla_x\cdot\mathcal C_x
 +\nabla_z\cdot\mathcal C_z
 =-\nu|\nabla_xV|^2,
 }
 \tag{TL.1027}
\]

with the one joined current

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal C_x
 :=M e_V+V\,\delta_zp-\nu\nabla_xe_V,
 \qquad
 \mathcal C_z:=V e_V.
 }
 \tag{TL.1028}
\]

Both current components vanish wherever \(e_V=0\), although the displayed
gradient loss need not vanish pointwise there.  Therefore on the positive set one may
write \((\mathcal C_x,\mathcal C_z)=e_V(c_x,V)\), with
\(c_x=\mathcal C_x/e_V\), without introducing an external drift.  Equation
(TL.1027) says that the same positive pair activity either moves through the
complete \((x,z)\) incidence field or is removed by the displayed loss.  Its
separation velocity is exactly \(V\); any change of the material center which
could otherwise look like a newly selected carrier is the explicit center
component \(\mathcal C_x\) of this same current.

The diffusive part of that center motion already has a pointwise action bound.
Since \(\nabla_xe_V=V\cdot\nabla_xV\),

\[
 \boxed{
 {|-\nu\nabla_xe_V|^2\over e_V}
 \le2\nu^2|\nabla_xV|^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1029}
\]

Thus movement of activity between centers through the diffusive row is paid
by the same killing term in (TL.1027).  The pressure part is also not a free
source: its unweighted center action is

\[
 {|V\,\delta_zp|^2\over e_V}=2|\delta_zp|^2.
 \tag{TL.1030}
\]

Both center rows have datum-controlled fixed-separation histories.  First,
translation invariance and (TL.1029) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \sup_{z\in\mathbb R^3}
 \int_0^T\!\int_{\mathbb R^3}
 {|-\nu\nabla_xe_V|^2\over e_V}\,dx\,dt
 \le C\nu E_0(0).
 }
 \tag{TL.1031}
\]

Second, the pressure is the Riesz-transform reading of \(u\otimes u\), so

\[
 \|p(t)\|_2^2
 \le C\|u(t)\|_4^4
 \le C H(t)E_1(t).
\]

Together with \(\|\delta_zp\|_2\le2\|p\|_2\), (TL.1030), and the energy
identity, this yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \sup_{z\in\mathbb R^3}
 \int_0^T{1\over H(t)}
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}{|V\,\delta_zp|^2\over e_V}\,dx\,dt
 \le {C E_0(0)\over\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1032}
\]

The zero-state convention is used in (TL.1032).  These estimates are taken
at fixed separation before any critical radial valuation, just as the
cumulative current action in (TL.1004) is taken before its travel factor.

After integration in \((x,z)\) against a q-annular separation test, its
opposite center faces cancel before the radial critical value is selected.
The active descent must therefore carry the signed combined center current
\(V\delta_zp-\nu\nabla_xe_V\), not estimate pressure as an independent force.
Equations (TL.1027)--(TL.1030) reduce the fixed-label lift to that signed
q-annular center-face cancellation while preserving the actual separation
velocity needed by (TL.1019).

## 112. Matched q-scales have a strict joined angular defect

The activity factor in (TL.1012) is not independent of the center loss.  At
fixed \((r,t)\), write

\[
 A_\omega(x,r):=\int_{\mathbb S^2}|V(x,rn)|^2d\omega,
 \qquad
 G_\omega(x,r):=\int_{\mathbb S^2}|\nabla_xV(x,rn)|^2d\omega.
\]

Direct differentiation and Cauchy--Schwarz give the center Fisher inequality

\[
 \boxed{
 { |\nabla_xA_\omega|^2\over A_\omega}
 \le4G_\omega.
 }
 \tag{TL.1033}
\]

Applying the three-dimensional Sobolev inequality to \(\sqrt{A_\omega}\)
and then using (TL.1033) yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}A_\omega(x,r)^2dx
 \le C\,\overline A(r)^{1/2}\overline G(r)^{3/2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1034}
\]

Thus the same loss which removes pair activity also controls concentration of
its all-center weight.

The angular row has an exact complementary Fourier form.  Set

\[
 \mathcal B(r):=\int_{\mathbb R^3}\!\int_{\mathbb S^2}
 |\nabla_{\mathbb S^2}V(x,rn)|^2d\omega\,dx
\]

and define its affine ceiling

\[
 \mathcal B_0(r)
 :=r^2\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\widehat u(\xi)|^2
 \int_{\mathbb S^2}
 \bigl(|\xi|^2-(\xi\cdot n)^2\bigr)d\omega\,d\xi.
\]

The increment multiplier gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal B(r)
 &=r^2\int|\widehat u(\xi)|^2
 \int_{\mathbb S^2}
 \bigl(|\xi|^2-(\xi\cdot n)^2\bigr)
 \cos^2\!\left({r\xi\cdot n\over2}\right)d\omega\,d\xi,\\
 \mathcal B_0(r)-\mathcal B(r)
 &=r^2\int|\widehat u(\xi)|^2
 \int_{\mathbb S^2}
 \bigl(|\xi|^2-(\xi\cdot n)^2\bigr)
 \sin^2\!\left({r\xi\cdot n\over2}\right)d\omega\,d\xi,\\
 0&\le\mathcal B_0(r)-\mathcal B(r)
 \le {r^2\over4}\overline G(r).
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1035}
\]

The second line is the angular part of the same sine-square loss appearing in
\(\nabla_xV\); it is not an added coercive term.

The q-matched version is strict.  Let
\(v=P_{[K,qK]}u\), form its increment \(V_K\), and suppose

\[
 0<c\le rK\le C<\infty.
\]

For \(|\xi|\in[K,qK]\), the number
\(a=r|\xi|/2\) lies in the compact interval
\([c/2,Cq/2]\).  The continuous angular ratio

\[
 {\int_{\mathbb S^2}(1-(\widehat\xi\cdot n)^2)
 \sin^2(a\widehat\xi\cdot n)d\omega
 \over
 \int_{\mathbb S^2}(1-(\widehat\xi\cdot n)^2)d\omega}
\]

is strictly positive there.  Hence a number
\(\delta=\delta(c,C,q)>0\) exists such that

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal B_{0,K}(r)-\mathcal B_K(r)
 \ge\delta\mathcal B_{0,K}(r),
 \qquad
 \mathcal B_K(r)\le(1-\delta)\mathcal B_{0,K}(r).
 }
 \tag{TL.1036}
\]

Combining the lower half of (TL.1036) with the last line of (TL.1035) gives
the explicit joined-loss consequence

\[
 \boxed{
 \overline G_K(r)
 \ge {4\delta\over r^2}\mathcal B_{0,K}(r)
 \simeq_{\delta}\|\nabla v(t)\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1037}
\]

This is the strict upper/lower split at the actual q-scale.  A concentrated
critical population may approach the affine angular ceiling only by leaving
its matched separation layer; while it remains matched, the same joined
increment carries the uniform sine-square defect (TL.1036), and (TL.1035)
places that defect inside the simultaneous loss.  Together with
(TL.1033)--(TL.1034), the next q-sum can retain both the activity weight and
this strict defect before the positive critical current is selected.

## 113. The joined pair current has a datum-finite quadratic action at every fixed separation

The exponent calculation in Section 104 has a second direct consequence for
the positive current in (TL.1027).  Retain the joined nondiffusive center row

\[
 \mathcal C_x^0:=M e_V+V\,\delta_zp
\]

and the separation row \(\mathcal C_z=Ve_V\).  On \(\{e_V>0\}\),

\[
 { |\mathcal C_z|^2\over e_V}={1\over2}|V|^4,
 \qquad
 { |\mathcal C_x^0|^2\over e_V}
 \le2e_V|M|^2+4|\delta_zp|^2.
\]

For every fixed separation \(z\), translation invariance and
\(M=(u_++u_-)/2\), \(V=u_+-u_-\), give

\[
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}
 \bigl(e_V|M|^2+|V|^4\bigr)dx
+\|\delta_zp\|_2^2
\le C\bigl(\|u\|_4^4+\|p\|_2^2\bigr).
\]

The pressure is the same-field Riesz-transform reading of \(u\otimes u\),
and the surrounding Sobolev rows give

\[
 \|p\|_2^2\le C\|u\|_4^4
 \le C\|u\|_3^2\|u\|_6^2
 \le C H E_1.
\]

Consequently the transport, pressure, and separation parts of the one current
satisfy the fixed-separation quadratic action

\[
 \boxed{
 \sup_{z\in\mathbb R^3}
 \int_0^T{1\over H(t)}\int_{\mathbb R^3}
 { |\mathcal C_x^0|^2+|\mathcal C_z|^2\over e_V}
 \,dx\,dt
 \le {C E_0(0)\over\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1038}
\]

The zero-state convention is used as before.  This is a true quadratic
transport action: if \(b_z=\mathcal C_z/e_V=V\), then its separation part is
\(\int e_V|b_z|^2/H\).  It is not the divergent raw
\(L^3_{\rm pair}\) or \(L^4_{\rm pair}\) reciprocal-speed norm.

The diffusive center row remains part of the same current.  Equations
(TL.1029) and the energy identity give

\[
 \boxed{
 \sup_z\int_0^T\!\int
 {|-\nu\nabla_xe_V|^2\over e_V}\,dx\,dt
 \le C\nu E_0(0).
 }
 \tag{TL.1039}
\]

Therefore, for every height floor \(h>0\), recombining
\(\mathcal C_x=\mathcal C_x^0-\nu\nabla_xe_V\) only after the two estimates
have been made yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \sup_z\int_{\{H\ge h\}}{1\over H(t)}\int
 { |\mathcal C_x|^2+|\mathcal C_z|^2\over e_V}\,dx\,dt
 \le C E_0(0)\left({1\over\nu}+{\nu\over h}\right).
 }
 \tag{TL.1040}
\]

Thus changing the material center, carrying activity through separation, and
the simultaneous pressure and diffusive center motions all possess one
datum-finite quadratic history on every nonzero critical-height tail.  No
fresh carrier can be inserted between those rows.

## 114. A cumulative q-annular receiver obeys an exact signed passage inequality

Let \(0\le\Psi\le1\) be a compactly supported Lipschitz test on the complete
\((x,z)\) space and set

\[
 \mathfrak m_\Psi(t):=\iint e_V\Psi\,dx\,dz,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak l_\Psi(t):=\nu\iint|\nabla_xV|^2\Psi\,dx\,dz.
\]

Testing (TL.1027) before taking any sign gives

\[
 \mathfrak m_\Psi(t)-\mathfrak m_\Psi(s)
 +\int_s^t\mathfrak l_\Psi(r)dr
 =\int_s^t\!\iint
 (\mathcal C_x,\mathcal C_z)\cdot\nabla_{x,z}\Psi\,dx\,dz\,dr.
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz is now applied to this one completed current, with the
critical height inserted once as the common state normalization:

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 &\left|
 \mathfrak m_\Psi(t)-\mathfrak m_\Psi(s)
 +\int_s^t\mathfrak l_\Psi(r)dr
 \right|^2\\
 &\quad\le
 \left(
 \int_s^t{1\over H(r)}\iint_{\operatorname{supp}\nabla\Psi}
 { |\mathcal C_x|^2+|\mathcal C_z|^2\over e_V}
 \right)
 \left(
 \int_s^tH(r)\iint e_V|\nabla\Psi|^2
 \right).
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1041}
\]

Both integrals in (TL.1041) are over \(dx\,dz\,dr\).  The identity preceding
it shows why the loss is on the same side as the later stock: the quantity on
the left is the actual activity which crossed the test boundary, including
the activity removed while doing so.

For the q-adic specialization, take \(\Psi(x,z)=\psi_{r,q}(z)\), where
\(\psi_{r,q}=1\) on \(|z|\le r\), \(\psi_{r,q}=0\) on \(|z|\ge qr\), and

\[
 |\nabla_z\psi_{r,q}|\le{C\over(q-1)r}.
\]

All center faces then cancel exactly and only \(\mathcal C_z\) occurs on the
right.  The first factor in (TL.1041), (TL.1038), and the volume of the
transition annulus give

\[
 \int_s^t{1\over H}\iint_{r<|z|<qr}
 {|\mathcal C_z|^2\over e_V}
 \le C_q r^3\int_s^tE_1(v)dv.
\]

On the same annulus, the critical identity (TL.1024) gives

\[
 \iint_{r<|z|<qr}e_V\,dx\,dz\le C_qr^4H(t).
\]

Substitution in the second factor of (TL.1041) proves the cumulative
q-annular passage law

\[
 \boxed{
 \left|
 \mathfrak m_{r,q}(t)-\mathfrak m_{r,q}(s)
 +\int_s^t\mathfrak l_{r,q}(v)dv
 \right|^2
 \le
 C_q r^5
 \left(\int_s^tE_1(v)dv\right)
 \left(\int_s^tH(v)^2dv\right).
 }
 \tag{TL.1042}
\]

Using the two surrounding-row datum bounds in (TL.626), this immediately
gives the fixed-radius history estimate

\[
 \boxed{
 \left|
 \mathfrak m_{r,q}(t)-\mathfrak m_{r,q}(s)
 +\int_s^t\mathfrak l_{r,q}(v)dv
 \right|
 \le {C_qE_0(0)^{3/2}\over\nu}\,r^{5/2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1042a}
\]

This estimate is cumulative rather than cellwise.  On a translated q-grid,
subtracting two neighboring tests makes their common radial face occur with
both signs before (TL.1042) is used.  It therefore gives the exact
current--distance inequality for the source-free activity which the
population lower bound (TL.1021) and reciprocal reach law (TL.1019) require.

The power \(r^5\) also fixes the remaining valuation issue without inventing
one.  The lower quadratic action in (TL.1038) pays actual finite-distance
transport.  A critical receiver multiplies the same passage by \(r^{-4}\),
so the missing scale factor cannot come from another application of
Cauchy--Schwarz.  It must be retained from the centered scale direction of
the complete tangent itself.

## 115. Critical work couples only to centered frequency width

That centered direction is exact.  Write the kinetic spectral moments as

\[
 E_0=\int e(k)dk,
 \qquad H=\int k e(k)dk,
 \qquad E_1=\int k^2e(k)dk,
 \qquad E_{3/2}=\int k^3e(k)dk.
\]

At a nonzero state define the critical spectral probability, its mean, and
its variance by

\[
 d\pi_H(k):={k e(k)\over H}dk,
 \qquad m_H:={E_1\over H},
 \qquad \sigma_H^2:={E_{3/2}\over H}-m_H^2.
 \tag{TL.1043}
\]

Retain the three vectors already present in the completed tangent,

\[
 V_0:=\Lambda^{1/2}u,
 \qquad W:=\nu\Lambda^{3/2}u,
 \qquad Z:=\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u).
\]

Energy orthogonality and spectral calculus give

\[
 \boxed{
 \langle Z,V_0\rangle=0,
 \qquad
 \|W-\nu m_HV_0\|_2^2
 =2\nu^2H\sigma_H^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1044}
\]

Since
\(P_H=-\nu^{-1}\operatorname{Re}\langle Z,W\rangle\), the energy-direction
part of \(W\) cancels from the nonlinear critical work exactly:

\[
 \boxed{
 P_H
 =-{1\over\nu}\operatorname{Re}
 \langle Z,W-\nu m_HV_0\rangle,
 \qquad
 |P_H|^2\le2H\sigma_H^2\|Z\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1045}
\]

Thus a one-radius state has zero nonlinear critical work.  More generally,
using \(\|Z\|_2^2\le CE_1^2\) and the exact balance

\[
 H'+2\nu E_{3/2}=P_H,
\]

every positive critical-height time satisfies

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)>0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 {\sigma_H(t)\over m_H(t)}
 \ge {c\nu\over\sqrt{H(t)}}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1046}
\]

Indeed, positivity gives
\(P_H>2\nu H(m_H^2+\sigma_H^2)\); squaring and applying (TL.1045) yields
\(4\nu^2(m_H^2+\sigma_H^2)^2
 <C Hm_H^2\sigma_H^2\), which implies (TL.1046).

This identifies the correct q-resolution for the next signed descent.  On a
height slab \(h\le H\le2h\), the scale mesh must resolve logarithmic width
comparable to \(\nu/\sqrt h\).  A fixed relative-width contraction is not
the VPI statement: the actual current can rise only through the centered
frequency direction in (TL.1044), and the q-annular passage in (TL.1042)
must be summed at that state-determined width while its common faces retain
their signs.  Equation (TL.1046) alone does not assign a fixed population to
different cells; a small far tail may carry part of the variance.  The next
q-sum must therefore retain the entire centered second moment, including its
actual distance, rather than infer a cell count from the variance.

## 116. The height-adapted q-grid retains the complete centered scale row

Fix a height slab \(h\le H(t)\le2h\) and choose

\[
 \varepsilon_h:=\varepsilon_0
 \min\left\{1,{\nu\over\sqrt h}\right\},
 \qquad q_h:=e^{\varepsilon_h},
 \tag{TL.1047}
\]

where the universal \(\varepsilon_0>0\) is chosen below.  This q-grid is fixed
throughout the slab; it is not changed from one cell or time to another.  For
a translation \(r\in[0,\log q_h)\), let \(Q_{h,r}(k)\) be the lower face of
the unique cell \([e^rq_h^j,e^rq_h^{j+1})\) containing \(k\).  Then

\[
 |k-Q_{h,r}(k)|\le(1-q_h^{-1})k.
\]

Consequently, in the critical probability from (TL.1043),

\[
 \|k-Q_{h,r}(k)\|_{L^2(d\pi_H)}
 \le(1-q_h^{-1})\sqrt{m_H^2+\sigma_H^2}.
 \tag{TL.1048}
\]

At every time in the slab with \(H'(t)>0\), (TL.1046) and a sufficiently small
universal choice of \(\varepsilon_0\) make the right side at most
\(\sigma_H/4\).  The reverse and direct triangle inequalities therefore give,
uniformly in the grid translation,

\[
 \boxed{
 {9\over16}\sigma_H^2
 \le
 \sum_j\bigl(K_j(r)-m_H\bigr)^2
 \pi_H\bigl([K_j(r),K_{j+1}(r))\bigr)
 \le {25\over16}\sigma_H^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1049}
\]

No population hypothesis enters (TL.1049).  A remote small tail is retained
with its full squared distance from the mean.

Let \(P_j\) denote these disjoint spectral cells and
\(V_{0,j}:=P_j\Lambda^{1/2}u\).  Since
\(\|V_{0,j}\|_2^2/(2H)=\pi_H([K_j,K_{j+1}))\), (TL.1044) and (TL.1049) give
the q-adic centered-row equivalence

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j\|\nu(K_j-m_H)V_{0,j}\|_2^2
 \simeq
 \|W-\nu m_HV_0\|_2^2
 =2\nu^2H\sigma_H^2,
 }
 \tag{TL.1050}
\]

with universal constants on the positive-growth portion of the slab.

The same centering is compatible with the signed q-current.  For the exact
band exchanges \(s_j=-\operatorname{Re}\langle B,P_ju\rangle\), energy
orthogonality gives \(\sum_js_j=-\langle B,u\rangle=0\).  Hence

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j(K_j-m_H)s_j
 =\sum_jK_js_j
 =-\operatorname{Re}\langle B,\Lambda_q u\rangle,
 \qquad
 \Lambda_q:=\sum_jK_jP_j.
 }
 \tag{TL.1051}
\]

Thus subtracting the moving mean does not manufacture a connection term in
the nonlinear exchange: its coefficient multiplies the exactly vanishing
kinetic source.  Equations (TL.1049)--(TL.1051) place the centered width, the
q-current, and energy orthogonality on one completed q-complex.  The next
operation is now fixed: apply the cumulative-tail cancellation (TL.999)--
(TL.1004) at \(q=q_h\), retain the signed coefficient \(K_j-m_H\) through the
sum, and use the full quadratic current passage (TL.1041) only after those
common faces have cancelled.

## 117. A universal scale--separation kernel tethers every q-band to its matched pair layer

The spectral and pair readings used above have an exact positive common
disintegration.  Let

\[
 d\mathfrak e(\xi):={1\over2}|\widehat u(\xi)|^2d\xi.
\]

Plancherel and angular integration give

\[
 \overline A(r)
 =8\pi\int_{\mathbb R^3}
 \left(1-\frac{\sin(r|\xi|)}{r|\xi|}\right)d\mathfrak e(\xi).
 \tag{TL.1052}
\]

Insert (TL.1052) into the pair identity
\(H=(c_{3,1/2}/2)\int_0^\infty r^{-2}\overline A(r)dr\), and put
\(s=r|\xi|\).  The result is

\[
 \boxed{
 H
 =\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\xi|\,d\mathfrak e(\xi)
 \int_0^\infty\Omega(s)ds,
 \qquad
 \Omega(s):=4\pi c_{3,1/2}
 {1-\sin s/s\over s^2},
 \qquad
 \int_0^\infty\Omega(s)ds=1.
 }
 \tag{TL.1053}
\]

The kernel is nonnegative, finite at the origin, integrable at infinity, and
universal.  Equation (TL.1053) is therefore not a comparison between a
spectral object and a pair object.  It is the positive disintegration of the
same critical quadratic field by frequency \(|\xi|\) and its dimensionless
physical separation \(s=r|\xi|\).

Apply this identity to one q-cell
\(u_j=P_{[K_j,qK_j)}u\), and write

\[
 H_j:={1\over2}\|\Lambda^{1/2}u_j\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 \overline A_j(r):=\int_{\mathbb S^2}\mathcal A_j(rn)d\omega.
\]

Choose fixed \(0<c<C\) with \(C>cq\), and set

\[
 \alpha_{c,C,q}:=\int_{cq}^{C}\Omega(s)ds>0.
\]

For every \(|\xi|\in[K_j,qK_j)\), the interval
\([c|\xi|/K_j,C|\xi|/K_j]\) contains \([cq,C]\).  Hence (TL.1053) gives the
uniform matched-layer admission

\[
 \boxed{
 {c_{3,1/2}\over2}
 \int_{c/K_j}^{C/K_j}r^{-2}\overline A_j(r)dr
 \ge\alpha_{c,C,q}H_j.
 }
 \tag{TL.1054}
\]

Thus every critical q-band places a fixed fraction of its actual activity at
physical separations \(r\simeq K_j^{-1}\).  No vortex label, maximizing
chord, or independently chosen physical scale has been inserted.

At the height-adapted q-grid of Section 116, multiply (TL.1054) by
\((K_j-m_H)^2\) and sum.  Equations (TL.1049)--(TL.1050) yield, on the
positive-growth part of the slab,

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j(K_j-m_H)^2
 {c_{3,1/2}\over2}
 \int_{c/K_j}^{C/K_j}r^{-2}\overline A_j(r)dr
 \ge c_*H\sigma_H^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1055}
\]

This is the all-field admission of the centered scale row.  A small remote
spectral tail appears with both its true critical mass and its true squared
distance; it cannot satisfy (TL.1049) while disappearing from the matched
pair field.

The simultaneous loss occupies the same matched region.  Applying
(TL.1037) to \(u_j\) for \(c/K_j\le r\le C/K_j\), and then integrating with
the same critical pair factor, gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \nu{c_{3,1/2}\over2}
 \int_{c/K_j}^{C/K_j}r^{-2}\overline G_j(r)dr
 \ge c_{**}\nu K_j^2H_j.
 }
 \tag{TL.1056}
\]

Equations (TL.1054)--(TL.1056) complete the scale-to-pair bridge required by
(TL.1041): the centered q-width which alone can receive nonlinear critical
work is carried by a fixed positive portion of the same pair activity, and
that portion retains the joined matched loss.  The remaining signed
calculation must now place the cubic current through this positive joint
disintegration before summing adjacent q-faces; it no longer has to assume or
reconstruct a correspondence between a frequency band and a physical pair
layer.

## 118. The centered q-current is one antisymmetric scale transfer

Retain one translated q-grid and first truncate it to finitely many
orthogonal spectral cells; the sharp boundary spheres are immaterial for the
smooth spectral density.  Write \(u_j=P_ju\), and for the completed trilinear form
of (TL.987) define

\[
 T_{j\leftarrow\ell}^{\,k}
 :=-b(u_k,u_\ell,u_j).
 \tag{TL.1057}
\]

The advecting row \(u_k\) is kept inside the same interaction.  Skew symmetry
in the last two entries gives the exact exchange antisymmetry

\[
 \boxed{
 T_{j\leftarrow\ell}^{\,k}
 =-T_{\ell\leftarrow j}^{\,k},
 \qquad
 s_j=\sum_{k,\ell}T_{j\leftarrow\ell}^{\,k}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1058}
\]

Thus the source of one receiving cell is the loss of another cell inside the
same triadic interaction.  In particular, for any scalar \(m(t)\),

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_j(K_j-m)s_j
 &=\frac12\sum_{j,k,\ell}
   (K_j-K_\ell)T_{j\leftarrow\ell}^{\,k}\\
 &=\sum_jK_js_j.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1059}
\]

The first equality counts each oriented exchange and its reverse together;
the second uses \(\sum_js_j=0\).  Taking \(m=m_H\) proves directly, at the
triad level, that the centered scale direction in (TL.1051) introduces no
new source and that its scale distance occurs exactly once.

The same statement has the cumulative-face form required by the q-adic
receiver.  With \(s_j=\Pi_j-\Pi_{j+1}\), finite summation by parts gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_{j=J}^{N}(K_j-m)s_j
 ={}&(K_J-m)\Pi_J-(K_N-m)\Pi_{N+1}\\
 &+(1-q^{-1})\sum_{j=J+1}^{N}K_j\Pi_j.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1060}
\]

On every compact classical interval the two exterior terms vanish in the
smooth coarse and fine limits used in (TL.922).  Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 -\operatorname{Re}\langle B,(\Lambda_q-m_H)u\rangle
 =(1-q^{-1})\sum_jK_j\Pi_j
 =\frac12\sum_{j,k,\ell}
 (K_j-K_\ell)T_{j\leftarrow\ell}^{\,k}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1061}
\]

No positivity has been taken in (TL.1061).  A transfer which crosses several
q-faces appears once through its endpoint scale difference; equivalently,
that difference is the signed Abel sum of the intervening common faces.  It
is therefore not legitimate to charge such a transfer once per crossed face
after taking positive parts.

Finally average over the translation \(r\in[0,\log q)\).  The exact lower-face
identity in (TL.923) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^{\log q}
 \sum_j(K_j(r)-m_H)s_j(r)\,dr
 =(1-q^{-1})P_H(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1062}
\]

Equations (TL.1057)--(TL.1062) identify the object which must be contracted
against (TL.1054)--(TL.1056): not the absolute value of each shell source and
not a list of face crossings, but the one signed donor--receiver scale
distance generated by the complete cubic VPI interaction.  The remaining
contraction may now group triads by their unique largest receiving scale,
insert that receiver's matched pair layer, and sum the signed reverse partner
before applying a quadratic estimate.

## 119. The same scale distance is the signed pair-layer current

The triadic distance in (TL.1061) has an exact positive pair
disintegration.  Put

\[
 \varphi(s):=1-\frac{\sin s}{s},
 \qquad
 M_r:=\varphi(r\Lambda).
 \tag{TL.1063}
\]

The normalization in (TL.1053) is precisely the strong multiplier identity

\[
 \boxed{
 \Lambda
 =4\pi c_{3,1/2}\int_0^\infty r^{-2}M_r\,dr
 }
 \tag{TL.1064}
\]

on every smooth preterminal state.  Thus this is not a comparison between a
q-band and a chosen physical radius: it is the exact resolution of the
single critical multiplier by all separation spheres.

Define the activity, loss, and signed source of one such sphere by

\[
 h_r:=\frac12\langle u,M_ru\rangle
      =\frac{\overline A(r)}{8\pi},
 \qquad
 d_r:=\nu\langle\Lambda u,M_r\Lambda u\rangle
      =\frac{\nu\overline G(r)}{8\pi},
 \qquad
 s_r:=-\operatorname{Re}\langle B,M_ru\rangle.
 \tag{TL.1065}
\]

The radial all-center law (TL.964), with
\(J(r)=r^2\overline F(r)\), gives the exact joined receiver

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_th_r+d_r=s_r,
 \qquad
 s_r=-\frac1{8\pi r^2}\partial_rJ(r).
 }
 \tag{TL.1066}
\]

Consequently the complete critical balance is

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 H&=4\pi c_{3,1/2}\int_0^\infty r^{-2}h_r\,dr,\\
 2\nu E_{3/2}
   &=4\pi c_{3,1/2}\int_0^\infty r^{-2}d_r\,dr,\\
 P_H&=4\pi c_{3,1/2}\int_0^\infty r^{-2}s_r\,dr
     =-\frac{c_{3,1/2}}2
       \int_0^\infty r^{-4}\partial_rJ(r)\,dr .
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1067}
\]

All three lines use the same positive multiplier \(M_r\).  In the last line
the sign of the physical separation current is retained.  On a finite
q-annular complex, integrate the final expression in (TL.1067) annulus by
annulus before taking a positive part.  Every internal value
\(J(e^\theta q^j)\) then occurs with both orientations; only the two true
exterior currents and the signed interior Abel coefficient survive.

Equations (TL.1061) and (TL.1067) become the same identity after the exact
translation average in (TL.1062).  That average replaces the lower-face
q-multiplier by \((1-q^{-1})\Lambda\), and (TL.1064) then replaces an
exact triadic frequency difference \(\kappa-\lambda\) by

\[
 4\pi c_{3,1/2}\int_0^\infty r^{-2}
 \bigl[\varphi(r\kappa)-\varphi(r\lambda)\bigr]\,dr.
 \tag{TL.1068}
\]

Equation (TL.1066) says that the resulting multiplier difference is exactly
the radial passage of the complete pair activity.  Hence the matched
admission in (TL.1054)--(TL.1056) and the signed cubic current are now on one
common measure: the positive sphere stock \(h_r\), its simultaneous loss
\(d_r\), and its signed radial current \(J\).  No shell source square and no
independent scale-to-pair correspondence is needed to make that
translation-averaged identification.

## 120. Every separation sphere has a datum-paid radial VPI speed

The signed current in (TL.1066) has a quadratic action which retains its
actual sphere activity.  Set

\[
 \mathcal Q_4(r,t)
 :=\int_{\mathbb S^2}\int_{\mathbb R^3}
 |V(x,rn,t)|^4\,dx\,d\omega(n).
 \tag{TL.1069}
\]

Since
\[
 \overline F(r)
 =\frac12\int_{\mathbb S^2}\int_{\mathbb R^3}
 (V\cdot n)|V|^2\,dx\,d\omega,
\]
Cauchy--Schwarz on the one sphere gives

\[
 |\overline F(r)|^2
 \le\frac12\,\overline A(r)\mathcal Q_4(r).
 \tag{TL.1070}
\]

For every fixed \(z\), interpolation and the two surrounding Sobolev rows
give

\[
 \|\delta_zu\|_4^4
 \le\|\delta_zu\|_3^2\|\delta_zu\|_6^2
 \le C\|u\|_{\dot H^{1/2}}^2\|\nabla u\|_2^2
 \le C H E_1.
 \tag{TL.1071}
\]

The constant is independent of \(z\); angular integration therefore gives
\(\mathcal Q_4(r)\le C H E_1\).  Recalling
\(J=r^2\overline F\) and \(h_r=\overline A/(8\pi)\), (TL.1070) becomes

\[
 \boxed{
 \frac{J(r,t)^2}{H(t)\,r^4h_r(t)}
 \le C E_1(t)
 }
 \tag{TL.1072}
\]

with the zero quotient convention.  The energy identity now yields the
uniform same-history action

\[
 \boxed{
 \sup_{r>0}\int_0^T
 \frac{J(r,t)^2}{H(t)\,r^4h_r(t)}\,dt
 \le\frac{CE_0(0)}{\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1073}
\]

Equivalently, if

\[
 v_r(t):=\frac{\overline F(r,t)}{\overline A(r,t)}
 =\frac{J(r,t)}{8\pi r^2h_r(t)}
\]

is the signed radial velocity of the whole sphere activity, then
(TL.1073) is, up to the fixed normalization,

\[
 \sup_{r>0}\int_0^T\frac{h_r(t)}{H(t)}|v_r(t)|^2dt
 \le\frac{CE_0(0)}{\nu}.
 \tag{TL.1074}
\]

This is the radial specialization of the joined current action in
(TL.1038), but (TL.1070) keeps the exact sphere stock required by
(TL.1066).  It is deliberately not integrated against \(r^{-4}dr\):
doing that before the q-annular faces cancel would recreate the free
ultraviolet trace.  The height-slab contraction must instead follow the
signed current through the first q-annular passage selected by (TL.1067),
then apply (TL.1073) at that same passage radius.

## 121. A monotone q-radius has datum-finite complete record passage

The moving-radius form of the same all-pair law retains the part swept by the
moving boundary.  Let \(R:[t_0,T]\to(0,\infty)\) be absolutely continuous
and nonincreasing, and define

\[
 C_R(t):=\int_0^{R(t)}\rho(r,t)\,dr,
 \qquad
 D_R(t):=\int_0^{R(t)}L(r,t)\,dr,
 \qquad
 \mathcal I_R(t):=-J(R(t),t).
 \tag{TL.1075}
\]

Reynolds transport applied directly to (TL.964) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 C_R'=\mathcal I_R-D_R+R'\rho(R).
 }
 \tag{TL.1076}
\]

Thus an increase of the actual inner pair activity is supplied by the inward
current \(\mathcal I_R\), after both simultaneous removal and the activity
discarded by contraction of the chosen radius are retained.

Let \(C_R^*(t)=\sup_{t_0\le s\le t}C_R(s)\), and let
\(\mathcal R_R\) be its full contact set, including maintenance plateaux.
At almost every contact time,

\[
 \mathcal I_R
 =(C_R^*)'+D_R-R'\rho(R)\ge0.
 \tag{TL.1077}
\]

The elementary increment bounds used in (TL.969) give, uniformly in \(r\),

\[
 \rho(r,t)=r^2\overline A(r,t)\le C E_0(0)r^2,
 \qquad
 L(r,t)=\nu r^2\overline G(r,t)\le C\nu E_1(t)r^2.
 \tag{TL.1078}
\]

Consequently

\[
 \sup_tC_R(t)\le CE_0(0)R(t_0)^3,
\]

\[
 \int_{t_0}^TD_R(t)\,dt
 \le C R(t_0)^3\nu\int_{t_0}^TE_1(t)\,dt
 \le CE_0(0)R(t_0)^3,
\]

and

\[
 \int_{t_0}^T[-R'(t)]\rho(R(t),t)\,dt
 \le CE_0(0)\int_{t_0}^T[-R']R^2dt
 \le CE_0(0)R(t_0)^3.
 \tag{TL.1079}
\]

Integrating (TL.1077) therefore proves

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathcal R_R}\mathcal I_R(t)\,dt
 \le CE_0(0)R(t_0)^3.
 }
 \tag{TL.1080}
\]

This is a moving-scale theorem on the complete carrier, not a fixed-radius
estimate reused at later radii.  A continuously shrinking q-selector cannot
recount inward passage: envelope increase, joined loss, and swept activity
are all charged in (TL.1077) before the current is integrated.

The current seen by a face moving with \(R\) is
\[
 \widetilde J_R:=J(R)-R'\rho(R).
\]
Equation (TL.1076) reads
\(C_R'+D_R=-\widetilde J_R\).  Hence on the same contact set,

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathcal R_R}[-\widetilde J_R(t)]\,dt
 =C_R^*(T)-C_R^*(t_0)
  +\int_{\mathcal R_R}D_R(t)\,dt
 \le CE_0(0)R(t_0)^3.
 }
 \tag{TL.1080a}
\]

This relative-current version is the one which enters each moving cell in
(TL.1085); the boundary sweep has not been omitted, but absorbed into the
actual face velocity before the record current is selected.

For the critical q-sum the factor \(R^{-4}\) must still be applied only
after neighboring moving radii have been joined.  Differentiating the
normalized inner stock displays the exact migration row,

\[
 \boxed{
 \left(\frac{C_R}{R^4}\right)'
 =\frac{\mathcal I_R-D_R}{R^4}
 +\frac{-R'}{R^5}\bigl(4C_R-R\rho(R)\bigr).
 }
 \tag{TL.1081}
\]

The last bracket is not an extra source.  It is
\(-R^5\partial_R(C_R/R^4)\), so in a homothetically moving q-grid it is the
signed common-face sweep of the same critical stock.  It must cancel across
the full translated q-family before the positive height-slab contribution is
selected.  Equations (TL.1080)--(TL.1081) now supply both parts of that
calculation: datum-finite actual passage on each monotone q-radius and the
exact signed generator which prevents the moving selector itself from
creating critical height.

## 122. The complete moving q-grid cancels its own sweep exactly

Let \(R_0(t)>0\) be absolutely continuous and put, for
\(0\le\theta<\log q\),

\[
 R_j(t;\theta):=R_0(t)e^\theta q^j,
 \qquad
 \gamma(t):=\frac{R_0'(t)}{R_0(t)},
 \qquad
 w_j(t;\theta):=R_j(t;\theta)^{-4}.
 \tag{TL.1082}
\]

Define the moving cell stock and loss

\[
 E_j:=\int_{R_j}^{R_{j+1}}\rho(r,t)\,dr,
 \qquad
 D_j:=\int_{R_j}^{R_{j+1}}L(r,t)\,dr,
 \tag{TL.1083}
\]

and the current relative to the moving face

\[
 \widetilde J_j
 :=J(R_j,t)-R_j'\rho(R_j,t)
 =J(R_j,t)-\gamma R_j\rho(R_j,t).
 \tag{TL.1084}
\]

Reynolds transport and (TL.964) give the exact moving receiver

\[
 \boxed{
 E_j'+D_j=\widetilde J_j-\widetilde J_{j+1}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1085}
\]

Multiply by the one critical value \(w_j\) and sum a finite ladder.  Since
\(w_j'=-4\gamma w_j\), summation by parts gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \frac d{dt}\sum_{j=J_0}^{N}w_jE_j
 +\sum_{j=J_0}^{N}w_jD_j
 ={}&w_{J_0}\widetilde J_{J_0}
     -w_N\widetilde J_{N+1}\\
 &-(q^4-1)\sum_{j=J_0+1}^{N}w_j\widetilde J_j
 -4\gamma\sum_{j=J_0}^{N}w_jE_j.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1086}
\]

The two terms containing the grid velocity cancel only after the translated
family is kept whole.  Direct changes of variables give

\[
 \int_0^{\log q}\sum_jw_jR_j\rho(R_j)\,d\theta
 =\int_0^\infty r^{-4}\rho(r)\,dr,
 \tag{TL.1087}
\]

whereas

\[
 \int_0^{\log q}\sum_jw_jE_j\,d\theta
 =\frac{q^4-1}{4}
  \int_0^\infty r^{-4}\rho(r)\,dr.
 \tag{TL.1088}
\]

Substitution of \(\widetilde J_j=J_j-\gamma R_j\rho_j\) into
(TL.1086), followed by (TL.1087)--(TL.1088), proves

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^{\log q}
 \left[
 (q^4-1)\gamma\sum_jw_jR_j\rho(R_j)
 -4\gamma\sum_jw_jE_j
 \right]d\theta=0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1089}
\]

Thus a moving observation scale supplies no critical production.  After the
coarse and fine exteriors vanish, translation averaging of (TL.1086)
returns exactly (TL.967), with the signed physical current \(J\) and joined
loss \(L\), independently of \(R_0(t)\).  In particular the
height-adapted q-grid of Section 116 may move with the actual scale history:
its sweep generator in (TL.1081) cancels before any record-positive current
is selected.

## 123. The whole moving q-complex is one cumulative inner-stock current

The cancellation in Section 122 can be written without referring to a
separate record at any face.  For the same moving radii put

\[
 C_j(t):=\int_0^{R_j(t)}\rho(r,t)\,dr,
 \qquad
 \mathscr L_j(t):=\int_0^{R_j(t)}L(r,t)\,dr,
 \qquad
 Q_j(t):=-\widetilde J_j(t).
 \tag{TL.1090}
\]

Equations (TL.1076) and (TL.1084) give the exact cumulative receiver

\[
 \boxed{C_j'+\mathscr L_j=Q_j.}
 \tag{TL.1091}
\]

Since \(E_j=C_{j+1}-C_j\), finite summation by parts gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \sum_{j=J_0}^{N}w_jE_j
 ={}&w_NC_{N+1}-w_{J_0}C_{J_0}
 +(q^4-1)\sum_{j=J_0+1}^{N}w_jC_j.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1092}
\]

The fine exterior vanishes because smooth increments give
\(C(R)=O(R^5)\) as \(R\downarrow0\); the coarse exterior vanishes because
\(C(R)=O(E_0(0)R^3)\) as \(R\uparrow\infty\).  The same calculation applies
to the loss.  Hence, on the complete grid,

\[
 \boxed{
 A_q:=\sum_jw_jE_j=(q^4-1)\sum_jw_jC_j,
 \qquad
 D_q:=\sum_jw_jD_j=(q^4-1)\sum_jw_j\mathscr L_j.
 }
 \tag{TL.1093}
\]

Differentiating (TL.1093), using \(w_j'=-4\gamma w_j\), and substituting
(TL.1091) now yields the single whole-complex identity

\[
 \boxed{
 A_q'+D_q
 =(q^4-1)\sum_jw_jQ_j-4\gamma A_q.
 }
 \tag{TL.1094}
\]

Thus the current which changes the critical q-stock is not a collection of
independently selected face records.  It is the signed weighted sum of the
cumulative relative currents \(Q_j\), formed before any positive temporal
part is taken.

Translation averaging makes this statement independent of the moving base
radius.  In addition to (TL.1088), the change of variables
\(r=R_0e^\theta q^j\) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^{\log q}\sum_jw_jQ_j\,d\theta
 =-\int_0^\infty r^{-5}J(r,t)\,dr
 +\gamma\int_0^\infty r^{-4}\rho(r,t)\,dr.
 }
 \tag{TL.1095}
\]

The last term in (TL.1095) cancels \(-4\gamma\int A_qd\theta\) exactly by
(TL.1088).  Therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 {d\over dt}\int_0^{\log q}A_q\,d\theta
 +\int_0^{\log q}D_q\,d\theta
 =-(q^4-1)\int_0^\infty r^{-5}J(r,t)\,dr.
 }
 \tag{TL.1096}
\]

Equation (TL.1096) is precisely the critical-height balance, up to the fixed
normalization in (TL.967).  It fixes the height-slab operation: stop and
contract the one translation-averaged cumulative current in (TL.1095), not
the positive variations of the individual \(C_j\).

## 124. The same viscosity row belongs inside the radial separation current

The center-diffusion form (TL.1027) is not the only exact reading of the
viscosity row.  Use \(\Delta_xV=4\Delta_zV\) from (TL.1013) before forming
the pair energy, and set

\[
 \mathcal G_z(z,t):=\int_{\mathbb R^3}|\nabla_zV(x,z,t)|^2\,dx.
 \tag{TL.1097}
\]

Then the same complete pair equation is

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_te_V
 +\nabla_x\!\cdot(Me_V+V\,\delta_zp)
 +\nabla_z\!\cdot(Ve_V-4\nu\nabla_ze_V)
 =-4\nu|\nabla_zV|^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1098}
\]

After all centers are integrated, define

\[
 \widehat{\mathcal F}(z,t)
 :=\mathcal F(z,t)-4\nu\nabla_z\mathcal A(z,t).
\]

Equation (TL.1098) becomes the positive killed separation law

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\mathcal A+\nabla_z\cdot\widehat{\mathcal F}
 =-4\nu\mathcal G_z.
 }
 \tag{TL.1099}
\]

This has not added a second diffusion mechanism.  Equations (TL.960) and
(TL.1099) are the center and separation divergences of the same term
\(\nu\Delta_xV=4\nu\Delta_zV\).

The complete separation current has a quadratic action against the activity
it actually transports.  Cauchy--Schwarz gives

\[
 { |\nabla_z\mathcal A|^2\over\mathcal A}
 \le2\mathcal G_z,
 \qquad
 { |\mathcal F|^2\over\mathcal A}
 \le\frac12\int_{\mathbb R^3}|V(x,z,t)|^4\,dx,
 \tag{TL.1100}
\]

with zero quotients on the zero set.  Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 { |\widehat{\mathcal F}|^2\over\mathcal A}
 \le
 2{ |\mathcal F|^2\over\mathcal A}
 +64\nu^2\mathcal G_z.
 }
 \tag{TL.1101}
\]

The first term is paid by the surrounding-row estimate used in (TL.1038).
Moreover \(\sup_z\mathcal G_z(z,t)\le CE_1(t)\).  Thus every height floor
\(h>0\) obeys

\[
 \boxed{
 \sup_{z\in\mathbb R^3}
 \int_{\{H\ge h\}}
 { |\widehat{\mathcal F}(z,t)|^2
  \over H(t)\mathcal A(z,t)}\,dt
 \le
 CE_0(0)\left({1\over\nu}+{\nu\over h}\right).
 }
 \tag{TL.1102}
\]

Radialize this complete current only now:

\[
 \widehat J(r,t)
 :=r^2\int_{\mathbb S^2}
 \widehat{\mathcal F}(rn,t)\cdot n\,d\omega(n),
 \qquad
 \widehat L(r,t)
 :=4\nu r^2\int_{\mathbb S^2}\mathcal G_z(rn,t)\,d\omega(n).
 \tag{TL.1103}
\]

Then (TL.1099) gives

\[
 \boxed{\partial_t\rho+\partial_r\widehat J=-\widehat L.}
 \tag{TL.1104}
\]

Weighted spherical Cauchy--Schwarz gives

\[
 {\widehat J(r,t)^2\over\rho(r,t)}
 \le r^2\int_{\mathbb S^2}
 { |\widehat{\mathcal F}(rn,t)|^2\over\mathcal A(rn,t)}
 \,d\omega(n).
 \tag{TL.1105}
\]

Integrating (TL.1105) through one physical q-annulus and applying
(TL.1102) proves the datum-finite complete radial action

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\{H\ge h\}}\int_R^{qR}
 {\widehat J(r,t)^2\over H(t)\rho(r,t)}\,dr\,dt
 \le
 C(q^3-1)R^3E_0(0)
 \left({1\over\nu}+{\nu\over h}\right).
 }
 \tag{TL.1106}
\]

Replacing \((J,L)\) by \((\widehat J,\widehat L)\) on every *finite* moving
q-complex leaves the receiver and sweep algebra in Sections 121--123
unchanged.  The fine exterior must now remain attached to the fine loss until
their common affine part has cancelled.  Indeed, for the diagonal tangent
\(V^{(1)}(x,z)=\nabla u(x)z\),

\[
 4\nu\Delta_z\frac{|V^{(1)}|^2}{2}
 -4\nu|\nabla_zV^{(1)}|^2=0.
 \tag{TL.1107}
\]

Thus the apparent \(R^{-1}\) critical divergence of the positive separation
loss is exactly the opposite divergence of its diffusive inner face; neither
is a lawful fine-limit object by itself.  Equations (TL.1104)--(TL.1107)
provide the joined finite-annulus operation needed by the height-slab
descent: cancel the common affine face through the complete q-complex first,
then apply the quadratic action (TL.1106) to the surviving radial passage.
The nonlinear separation transport, viscous face motion, and positive
separation loss consequently remain one current throughout that operation.

## 125. The complete q-annular passage has an explicit height-slab modulus

Let \(I=[s,t]\) lie in one connected component of \(\{H\ge h\}\), and let
\(\psi_{R,q}\) be radial, equal to one on \((0,R]\), equal to zero on
\([qR,\infty)\), and satisfy

\[
 |\psi_{R,q}'|\le{1\over(q-1)R}.
 \tag{TL.1108}
\]

Define the actual tested stock and separation loss

\[
 \widehat{\mathfrak m}_{R,q}(t)
 :=\int_0^\infty\rho(r,t)\psi_{R,q}(r)\,dr,
 \qquad
 \widehat{\mathfrak l}_{R,q}(t)
 :=\int_0^\infty\widehat L(r,t)\psi_{R,q}(r)\,dr.
\]

Testing (TL.1104) before taking a sign gives

\[
 \widehat{\mathfrak m}_{R,q}(t)
 -\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{R,q}(s)
 +\int_I\widehat{\mathfrak l}_{R,q}(v)\,dv
 =\int_I\!\int_R^{qR}\widehat J(r,v)\psi_{R,q}'(r)\,dr\,dv.
 \tag{TL.1109}
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz is applied to this completed current with weight
\(H\rho\).  The first factor is (TL.1106).  For the second, the critical
pair identity gives, at every time,

\[
 \int_R^{qR}\rho(r,t)\,dr
 \le Cq^4R^4H(t).
\]

Using (TL.626) for \(\int H^2\), one obtains

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 &\left|
 \widehat{\mathfrak m}_{R,q}(t)
 -\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{R,q}(s)
 +\int_I\widehat{\mathfrak l}_{R,q}(v)\,dv
 \right|^2\\
 &\qquad\le
 C{q^4(q^3-1)\over(q-1)^2}
 R^5E_0(0)^3
 \left({1\over\nu^2}+{1\over h}\right).
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1110}
\]

For the height-adapted grid (TL.1047), on the nontrivial range
\(h\ge\nu^2\), this reads

\[
 \boxed{
 \left|
 \Delta_I\widehat{\mathfrak m}_{R,q_h}
 +\int_I\widehat{\mathfrak l}_{R,q_h}
 \right|
 \le
 {C E_0(0)^{3/2}\over\nu^{3/2}}
 h^{1/4}R^{5/2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1111}
\]

The factor \(h^{1/4}R^{5/2}\) is the exact finite-layer passage scale
supplied by the already-paid quadratic current.  It is to be paired with the
single critical valuation of the centered q-current in (TL.1061)--(TL.1062),
not summed as an absolute passage over all radii.  In particular, the
\((q_h-1)^{-1/2}\) in (TL.1111) records the real cost of resolving a thinner
height-adapted annulus; it cannot be hidden inside a fixed-q constant during
the height-slab contraction.

## 126. Centered scale distance is one joined heat-profile metric

The scale factor in (TL.1061) has an exact quadratic heat representation.
For \(\kappa,\lambda>0\), define

\[
 \mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)
 :=\int_0^\infty\tau^{-3/2}
 \left(e^{-\tau\kappa^2}-e^{-\tau\lambda^2}\right)^2d\tau.
 \tag{TL.1112}
\]

The subscript records that this is the profile of the same heat row; the
number itself is independent of the value of \(\nu\).  Evaluation of the
Laplace integral gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)
 =2\sqrt\pi\left(
 2\sqrt{\kappa^2+\lambda^2}
 -\sqrt2\,\kappa-\sqrt2\,\lambda
 \right)
 ={4\sqrt\pi(\kappa-\lambda)^2
  \over2\sqrt{\kappa^2+\lambda^2}
       +\sqrt2(\kappa+\lambda)}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1113}
\]

Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 {4\sqrt\pi\over2+\sqrt2}
 { (\kappa-\lambda)^2\over\kappa+\lambda}
 \le\mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)
 \le\sqrt{2\pi}
 { (\kappa-\lambda)^2\over\kappa+\lambda}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1114}
\]

Thus the linear donor--receiver distance in (TL.1059) is the geometric mean
of two simultaneous readings of the same scales:

\[
 |\kappa-\lambda|
 \simeq
 \left[(\kappa+\lambda)
 \mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)\right]^{1/2}.
 \tag{TL.1115}
\]

Neither factor in (TL.1115) may be averaged independently.  Let
\(d\pi_H\) be the critical spectral probability in (TL.1043), and take two
independent scale coordinates \(\kappa,\lambda\) under that same measure.
The variance identity and (TL.1114) give

\[
 \boxed{
 2\sigma_H^2
 =\iint(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 \,d\pi_H(\kappa)d\pi_H(\lambda)
 \simeq
 \iint(\kappa+\lambda)
 \mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)
 \,d\pi_H(\kappa)d\pi_H(\lambda).
 }
 \tag{TL.1116}
\]

In particular, the positive-growth width law (TL.1046) becomes

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)>0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \iint(\kappa+\lambda)
 \mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)
 \,d\pi_H(\kappa)d\pi_H(\lambda)
 \ge {c\nu^2m_H(t)^2\over H(t)}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1117}
\]

Equation (TL.1116) is the heat-resolved version of the matched pair
admission (TL.1055).  A small remote spectral population retains both its
heat-profile displacement and its large simultaneous scale; replacing the
double integral by the product of its two marginals would lose exactly that
tail.  The height-slab contraction must therefore act on the joined kernel
\((\kappa+\lambda)\mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)\) before selecting the
positive centered q-current.

## 127. The surrounding Sobolev rows form an exact dissipative determinant

Write the kinetic spectral moments as

\[
 M_n(t):=\int_0^\infty k^ne(k,t)\,dk,
 \qquad
 M_0=E_0,\qquad M_1=H,\qquad M_2=E_1,\qquad M_3=E_{3/2},
 \tag{TL.1118}
\]

and let \(N_n:=\int_0^\infty k^nT(k,t)\,dk\) be the corresponding signed
nonlinear moment.  The exact spectral balance is

\[
 M_n'=N_n-2\nu M_{n+2},
 \qquad N_0=0.
 \tag{TL.1119}
\]

The first nontrivial Hankel determinant of the surrounding rows is

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr V_1
 :=M_1M_3-M_2^2
 =H^2\sigma_H^2
 ={1\over2}\iint
 \kappa\lambda(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda
 \ge0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1120}
\]

Differentiating with (TL.1119) gives one joined VPI law

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr V_1'+2\nu\mathscr D_1=\mathscr P_1,
 }
 \tag{TL.1121}
\]

where

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr D_1
 &:={M_3^2+M_1M_5-2M_2M_4}\\
 &=\frac12\iint
 \kappa\lambda(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 (\kappa^2+\lambda^2)
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda
 \ge0,\\
 \mathscr P_1
 &:=N_1M_3+M_1N_3-2M_2N_2.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1122}
\]

Thus viscosity does not act on the variance determinant as a collection of
separate Sobolev losses.  It multiplies the same two-scale displacement in
(TL.1120) by the simultaneous scale square
\(\kappa^2+\lambda^2\), producing the nonnegative joined dissipation
\(\mathscr D_1\).

The nonlinear side has the same centered scale-current form.  With

\[
 g_H(k):=M_1k^3-2M_2k^2+M_3k
 =Hk\bigl[(k-m_H)^2+\sigma_H^2\bigr],
 \tag{TL.1123}
\]

one has

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr P_1
 =\int_0^\infty g_H(k)T(k)\,dk
 =\frac12\iiint
 \bigl[g_H(\kappa)-g_H(\lambda)\bigr]
 \,d\mathfrak T(\kappa,\lambda;\zeta).
 }
 \tag{TL.1124}
\]

Here \(d\mathfrak T\) is the continuous antisymmetric triad exchange whose
q-cell version is (TL.1057)--(TL.1058); the advecting scale \(\zeta\) remains
inside the same transfer.  Formula (TL.1124) follows by pairing every
oriented donor--receiver exchange with its reverse.  Hence every common
q-face cancels before \(\mathscr P_1\) is read, exactly as in (TL.1061).

The determinant detects every positive critical-height tangent at a
datum-fixed magnitude.  Combining (TL.1046) with
\(m_H=E_1/H\ge H/E_0(0)\) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)>0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \mathscr V_1(t)
 =H(t)^2\sigma_H(t)^2
 \ge {c\nu^2\over E_0(0)^2}H(t)^3.
 }
 \tag{TL.1125}
\]

Equations (TL.1121)--(TL.1125) convert the height-slab problem into a
same-tower contraction: the positive tangent can occur only while the
two-scale determinant is cubically large in \(H\), its irreversible row is
the positive action \(\mathscr D_1\), and its complete rebuilding is the one
signed q-cancelled source \(\mathscr P_1\).  No individual higher Sobolev
moment has been promoted to an independent payer.

## 128. Schur normalization removes the linear critical refinancing term

Divide the determinant in (TL.1120) by its common critical mass.  The resulting
Schur complement is

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_1
 :={\mathscr V_1\over H}
 =M_3-{M_2^2\over M_1}
 =H\sigma_H^2
 =\int_0^\infty k(k-m_H)^2e(k)\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1126}
\]

Differentiate the displayed function of \((M_1,M_2,M_3)\) and use
(TL.1119).  All motion of \(m_H=M_2/M_1\) is already included by the chain
rule, and one obtains

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_1'+2\nu\mathscr R_1=\mathscr S_1,
 }
 \tag{TL.1127}
\]

with

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr R_1
 &:=M_5-2m_HM_4+m_H^2M_3
 =\int_0^\infty k^3(k-m_H)^2e(k)\,dk\ge0,\\
 \mathscr S_1
 &:=N_3-2m_HN_2+m_H^2N_1
 =\int_0^\infty k(k-m_H)^2T(k)\,dk.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1128}
\]

The term \(H\sigma_H^2N_1\) present in the unnormalized source
\(\mathscr P_1\) has cancelled identically.  It was the linear critical
height direction carried by differentiating the common mass \(H\), not a
new deformation of the spectral shape.  Equation (TL.1127) therefore
records only the centered shape change of the same spectrum.

The source retains the exact antisymmetric q-current.  Put

\[
 q_m(k):=k(k-m)^2.
\]

Then

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr S_1
 ={1\over2}\iiint
 \bigl[q_{m_H}(\kappa)-q_{m_H}(\lambda)\bigr]
 \,d\mathfrak T(\kappa,\lambda;\zeta),
 }
 \tag{TL.1129}
\]

and the endpoint factor is exactly

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 q_m(\kappa)-q_m(\lambda)
 &=(\kappa-\lambda)
 \bigl[\kappa^2+\kappa\lambda+\lambda^2
       -2m(\kappa+\lambda)+m^2\bigr]\\
 &=(a-b)\bigl[m(a+b)+a^2+ab+b^2\bigr],
 \qquad a=\kappa-m,\quad b=\lambda-m.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1130}
\]

Thus every contribution to \(\mathscr S_1\) carries both the actual
donor--receiver distance and a centered displacement from the instantaneous
mean.  A constant source, a common translation of all scales, and the linear
critical-height direction have all disappeared before any positive part is
taken.  Combining the first factor in (TL.1130) with (TL.1115) keeps its heat
profile and simultaneous scale joined inside the same triad.

Finally, (TL.1046) and the energy--critical interpolation give the sharper
positive-tangent admission

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)>0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \mathscr Q_1(t)=H(t)\sigma_H(t)^2
 \ge {c\nu^2\over E_0(0)^2}H(t)^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1131}
\]

The height-slab descent may consequently be performed on (TL.1127): its
stock is already quadratically coercive in every positive critical tangent,
its loss \(\mathscr R_1\) is nonnegative, and its source (TL.1129) contains
only the centered signed q-transfer left after common-face cancellation.

## 129. The normalized determinant controls its own successor loss

The Schur stock in (TL.1126) is the variance row already isolated in the
Hilbert factorization of Section 34:

\[
 \boxed{\mathcal S_H=2\mathscr Q_1.}
 \tag{TL.1132}
\]

Its successor loss has a sharp one-line coercivity.  Under the critical
probability \(d\pi_H\),

\[
 \sigma_H^2
 =\mathbb E_{\pi_H}\!\left[k(k-m_H)\right].
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz therefore gives

\[
 \sigma_H^4
 \le\mathbb E_{\pi_H}\!\left[k^2(k-m_H)^2\right].
\]

Using (TL.1126)--(TL.1128), this is exactly

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr R_1
 \ge H\sigma_H^4
 ={\mathscr Q_1^2\over H}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1133}
\]

Hence the full VPI evolution of the centered row obeys the Riccati-form law

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_1'
 +{2\nu\over H}\mathscr Q_1^2
 \le\mathscr S_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.1134}
\]

This inequality has not separated viscosity from the joined evolution: it
is the consequence of the exact identity (TL.1127) after the successor row
of that same identity is contracted against its own stock.

On the positive critical tangent, (TL.1131) strengthens (TL.1133) to

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)>0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \mathscr R_1(t)
 \ge {c\nu^4\over E_0(0)^4}H(t)^3,
 \qquad
 2\nu\mathscr R_1(t)
 \ge {c\nu^5\over E_0(0)^4}H(t)^3.
 }
 \tag{TL.1135}
\]

Thus every positive critical-height tangent contains, simultaneously, a
cubic-in-height irreversible successor row.  The only term capable of
maintaining that tangent in (TL.1127) is the centered signed source
\(\mathscr S_1\); the linear height source and all common q-faces have already
cancelled from it.

## 130. The centered source and successor loss form one exact square

The source in (TL.1128) has a Hilbert representation which preserves its
centering.  Let

\[
 Z_1:=\Lambda^{-1/2}(\Lambda-m_H)B(u,u),
 \qquad
 G_1:=\Lambda^{3/2}(\Lambda-m_H)u.
 \tag{TL.1136}
\]

Self-adjointness of the spectral multipliers gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr S_1=-\operatorname{Re}\langle Z_1,G_1\rangle,
 \qquad
 \|G_1\|_2^2=2\mathscr R_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.1137}
\]

Substitution in (TL.1127) and completion of the square yield the exact
joined identity

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_1'
 +\nu\left\|G_1+{Z_1\over2\nu}\right\|_2^2
 ={1\over4\nu}\|Z_1\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1138}
\]

In particular,

\[
 \boxed{
 [\mathscr Q_1']_+
