 \le {1\over4\nu}\|Z_1\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 \mathscr Q_1'+\nu\mathscr R_1
 \le {1\over2\nu}\|Z_1\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1139}
\]

The right side is not the uncentered critical source square.  Its exact
spectral reading is

\[
 \boxed{
 \|Z_1\|_2^2
 =\int_{\mathbb R^3}
 {\bigl(|\xi|-m_H\bigr)^2\over|\xi|}
 |\widehat{B(u,u)}(\xi)|^2\,d\xi.
 }
 \tag{TL.1140}
\]

Thus a nonlinear tangent parallel to the current spectral scale has zero
innovation charge even when its uncentered magnitude is large.  Conversely,
new scale shape can enter \(\mathscr Q_1\) only through the actual distance
of the complete nonlinear output from \(m_H\).  On the height-adapted q-grid,
that distance is exactly the signed donor--receiver factor already retained
in (TL.1061), (TL.1115), and (TL.1130); it is not an independently imposed
source weight.

## 131. Only the joined parallel source projection can rebuild the determinant

Assume first that \(G_1\ne0\), and take the real Hilbert projection

\[
 Z_1^\parallel
 :={\operatorname{Re}\langle Z_1,G_1\rangle
    \over\|G_1\|_2^2}G_1,
 \qquad
 Z_1^\perp:=Z_1-Z_1^\parallel.
 \tag{TL.1141}
\]

The two components are orthogonal in the real inner product.  Expanding the
square in (TL.1138) shows that the term
\(\|Z_1^\perp\|_2^2/(4\nu)\) occurs once on each side and cancels exactly.
Thus

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_1'
 +\nu\left\|G_1+{Z_1^\parallel\over2\nu}\right\|_2^2
 ={1\over4\nu}\|Z_1^\parallel\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1142}
\]

Define the signed same-state participation coefficient

\[
 a_1(t)
 :=-{\operatorname{Re}\langle Z_1,G_1\rangle
      \over\|G_1\|_2^2}
 ={\mathscr S_1\over2\mathscr R_1},
 \tag{TL.1143}
\]

and put \(a_1=0\) when \(G_1=0\).  Equations (TL.1127) and (TL.1137) then
reduce to the exact scalar relation

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_1'=2(a_1-\nu)\mathscr R_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.1144}
\]

Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_1'>0
 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 a_1>\nu,
 \qquad
 \|Z_1^\parallel\|_2^2=2a_1^2\mathscr R_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.1145}
\]

This is the lawful scalar reduction of the complete source--loss tangent.
Large transverse nonlinear output is irrelevant to determinant rebuilding;
large parallel output with \(a_1\le\nu\) is simultaneously absorbed in the
same row.  The height-slab contraction therefore needs to control only the
positive excess \((a_1-\nu)_+\mathscr R_1\), retaining its signed q-current
definition (TL.1129), rather than the magnitude \(\|Z_1\|_2^2\).

## 132. The lower Schur row supplies the datum-finite shape measure

Apply the same Schur operation one Sobolev step lower.  Put

\[
 m_0:={M_1\over M_0},
 \qquad
 \mathscr Q_0
 :=M_2-{M_1^2\over M_0}
 =\int_0^\infty(k-m_0)^2e(k)\,dk.
 \tag{TL.1146}
\]

The kinetic-energy identity and positivity give immediately

\[
 \boxed{
 0\le\mathscr Q_0(t)\le M_2(t)=E_1(t),
 \qquad
 \int_0^T\mathscr Q_0(t)\,dt
 \le{E_0(0)\over2\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1147}
\]

This stock also has an exact complete evolution.  Differentiating
(TL.1146) with (TL.1119) yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_0'+2\nu\mathscr R_0=\mathscr S_0,
 }
 \tag{TL.1148}
\]

where

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr R_0
 &:=M_4-2m_0M_3+m_0^2M_2
 =\int_0^\infty k^2(k-m_0)^2e(k)\,dk\ge0,\\
 \mathscr S_0
 &:=N_2-2m_0N_1
 =\int_0^\infty(k-m_0)^2T(k)\,dk.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1149}
\]

As in (TL.1133), Cauchy under the kinetic spectral probability gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr R_0\ge{\mathscr Q_0^2\over M_0}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1150}
\]

The two adjacent Schur rows have an exact one-scale relation.  Their Hankel
determinants are

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 M_0\mathscr Q_0
 &=\frac12\iint(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda,\\
 M_1\mathscr Q_1
 &=\frac12\iint\kappa\lambda(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1151}
\]

On \(\{\mathscr Q_0>0\}\), define the carrier-native shape scale

\[
 \boxed{
 \kappa_{\rm sh}(t)
 :={\mathscr Q_1(t)\over\mathscr Q_0(t)}
 ={1\over m_0(t)}
 {\displaystyle\iint\kappa\lambda(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda
  \over
  \displaystyle\iint(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda},
 }
 \tag{TL.1152}
\]

and set it to zero when \(\mathscr Q_0=0\).  Then, exactly,

\[
 \boxed{\mathscr Q_1=\kappa_{\rm sh}\mathscr Q_0.}
 \tag{TL.1153}
\]

The scale \(\kappa_{\rm sh}\) is not a maximizing shell or an external
frequency ruler.  It is the product scale \(\kappa\lambda\), averaged by the
actual two-frequency deformation measure
\((\kappa-\lambda)^2e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\), and divided by the same field's
kinetic mean scale.

Finally, (TL.509), (TL.1132), and (TL.1153) show that every critical-height
doubling interval \(I\) satisfies

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_I\kappa_{\rm sh}(t)\mathscr Q_0(t)\,dt
 =\int_I\mathscr Q_1(t)\,dt
 \ge {\nu\log2\over C_{\rm bil}^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1154}
\]

Thus the lower surrounding row already supplies a datum-finite positive
measure \(\mathscr Q_0(t)dt\), while the critical record reads its first
same-field shape-scale moment.  The signed q-adic contraction is precisely
the operation which must prevent that paid measure from moving its entire
first moment through successively larger values of \(\kappa_{\rm sh}\).

## 133. The adjacent Schur rows are one q-adic deformation measure

The ratio in (TL.1152) has a positive carrier before it is collapsed to one
number.  For a nonzero state define

\[
 d\mu_{\rm sh,t}(\kappa,\lambda)
 :={1\over2M_0(t)}(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 e(\kappa,t)e(\lambda,t)\,d\kappa d\lambda,
 \qquad
 \chi_t(\kappa,\lambda):={\kappa\lambda\over m_0(t)}.
 \tag{TL.1155}
\]

Use the zero measure for the zero solution.  Equations (TL.1151)--(TL.1153)
then become the two moment identities

\[
 \boxed{
 \mu_{\rm sh,t}((0,\infty)^2)=\mathscr Q_0(t),
 \qquad
 \int\chi_t\,d\mu_{\rm sh,t}=\mathscr Q_1(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1156}
\]

Thus \(\mathscr Q_0\) and \(\mathscr Q_1\) are not two separately evolving
stocks.  They are respectively the mass and first product-scale moment of
the same two-frequency deformation of the same kinetic spectrum.

Fix \(q>1\), and form the moving product-scale cells

\[
 \Omega_j(t):=\{q^j\le\chi_t<q^{j+1}\},
 \qquad
 \mu_j(t):=\mu_{\rm sh,t}(\Omega_j(t)).
 \tag{TL.1157}
\]

Positivity and (TL.1156) give the exact q-adic comparison

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_0=\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}\mu_j,
 \qquad
 \sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}q^j\mu_j
 \le\mathscr Q_1
 \le q\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}q^j\mu_j.
 }
 \tag{TL.1158}
\]

Equivalently, if
\(U(s,t):=\mu_{\rm sh,t}\{\chi_t>s\}\), layer cake gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_1(t)=\int_0^\infty U(s,t)\,ds,
 \qquad
 (q-1)\sum_jq^jU(q^{j+1},t)
 \le\mathscr Q_1(t)
 \le(q-1)\sum_jq^jU(q^j,t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1159}
\]

The cells move because \(m_0(t)\) is a readout of the continuing spectrum.
Consequently their boundary sweep is part of their exact derivative; it is
not a new transfer source.  Equations (TL.1155)--(TL.1159) are the positive
q-adic resolution on which that moving-boundary cancellation must be
performed before any temporal positive part is taken.

## 134. The kinetic Schur determinant pays the critical shape moment at height

Do not normalize the lower determinant while differentiating its kinetic
mass.  Put

\[
 \Delta_0:=M_0\mathscr Q_0=M_0M_2-M_1^2.
 \tag{TL.1160}
\]

The moment law (TL.1119) gives the exact joined evolution

\[
 \boxed{
 \Delta_0'+2\nu\mathscr D_0=\mathscr P_0,
 }
 \tag{TL.1161}
\]

where

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr D_0
 &:=M_0M_4+M_2^2-2M_1M_3
   =M_0\mathscr R_0+M_2\mathscr Q_0,\\
 \mathscr P_0
 &:=M_0N_2-2M_1N_1=M_0\mathscr S_0.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1162}
\]

The loss has the same two-frequency carrier as (TL.1155):

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr D_0
 &=\frac12\iint(\kappa-\lambda)^2(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda\\
 &=M_0\int(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)\,d\mu_{\rm sh,t}.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1163}
\]

Since \(\kappa^2+\lambda^2\ge2\kappa\lambda\), (TL.1156) yields the
sharp carrier-level coercivity

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr D_0
 \ge2M_0m_0\int\chi_t\,d\mu_{\rm sh,t}
 =2H\mathscr Q_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.1164}
\]

This is the surrounding-row payment missing from the isolated
\(\mathscr Q_1\)-law.  The same deformation which carries critical
scale-shape is multiplied, inside the determinant's own viscous row, by the
simultaneous square scale.  Nothing has been estimated one Sobolev row at a
time.

The consequence is naturally q-adic in height.  Let \(I=[s,t]\) be a
last-passage slab satisfying

\[
 H(s)=h,
 \qquad H(t)=qh,
 \qquad h\le H(r)\le qh\quad(r\in I).
 \tag{TL.1165}
\]

The differential inequality behind (TL.509), together with
\(\mathcal S_H=2\mathscr Q_1\), gives

\[
 \int_I\mathscr Q_1(r)\,dr
 \ge{\nu\log q\over C_{\rm bil}^2}.
 \tag{TL.1166}
\]

Combining (TL.1164)--(TL.1166) proves

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_I\mathscr D_0(r)\,dr
 \ge {2\nu h\log q\over C_{\rm bil}^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1167}
\]

Thus the determinant charge of successive q-height slabs grows with their
actual height.  The remaining upper calculation is now fixed on the signed
source \(\mathscr P_0\) in the same determinant identity (TL.1161), rather
than on an independently named record action.

## 135. The determinant source is one centered signed scale current

The nonlinear term in (TL.1161) retains the energy cancellation \(N_0=0\).
With \(T=-\partial_k\Pi\), integration by parts gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr S_0
 =N_2-2m_0N_1
 =2\int_0^\infty(k-m_0)\Pi(k,t)\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1168}
\]

Equivalently, in the antisymmetric triad current of (TL.1124),

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr S_0
 ={1\over2}\iiint
 (\kappa-\lambda)(\kappa+\lambda-2m_0)
 \,d\mathfrak T(\kappa,\lambda;\zeta).
 }
 \tag{TL.1169}
\]

Both factors are forced by the one continuing spectrum: the first is the
signed donor--receiver displacement and the second is its displacement from
the same field's kinetic mean.  In particular, on every translated q-grid,
(TL.1168) is partitioned as

\[
 \mathscr S_0
 =2\sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}
 \int_{K_j}^{K_{j+1}}(k-m_0)\Pi(k,t)\,dk,
 \tag{TL.1170}
\]

before any sign selection.  Adjacent cells therefore share the same signed
current; no cell owns a separate source.

There is also an exact Hilbert reading.  Define

\[
 g_0:=(\Lambda-m_0)u,
 \qquad
 G_0:=\Lambda g_0,
 \qquad
 Z_0:=\Lambda^{-1}(\Lambda-m_0)B(u,u).
 \tag{TL.1171}
\]

Then

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_0={1\over2}\|g_0\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 \mathscr R_0={1\over2}\|G_0\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 \mathscr S_0=-\operatorname{Re}\langle Z_0,G_0\rangle.
 }
 \tag{TL.1172}
\]

Projecting \(Z_0\) onto the real line generated by \(G_0\), set

\[
 a_0:={\mathscr S_0\over2\mathscr R_0}
 \tag{TL.1173}
\]

when \(\mathscr R_0>0\), and set \(a_0=0\) otherwise.  The perpendicular
source cancels from the completed square exactly as in Section 131, leaving

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_0'=2(a_0-\nu)\mathscr R_0,
 \qquad
 \Delta_0'
 =2M_0(a_0-\nu)\mathscr R_0-2\nu M_2\mathscr Q_0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1174}
\]

Hence (TL.1167) is rebuilt only by the signed parallel excess already
contained in the common q-current (TL.1168)--(TL.1170).  A magnitude of the
full nonlinear output is not the object to be summed.

## 136. Product-scale migration has an exact adjacent-row law

The motion of the q-cells in Section 133 can now be differentiated without
inventing a sweep source.  On \(\{\mathscr Q_0>0\}\), equations
(TL.1127) and (TL.1148) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \kappa_{\rm sh}'
 ={\mathscr S_1-\kappa_{\rm sh}\mathscr S_0
   -2\nu(\mathscr R_1-\kappa_{\rm sh}\mathscr R_0)
  \over\mathscr Q_0}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1175}
\]

The complete source in this equation is again one signed q-current.  Put

\[
 \psi_t(k)
 :=k(k-m_H)^2-\kappa_{\rm sh}(k-m_0)^2.
 \tag{TL.1176}
\]

The defining identity \(\mathscr Q_1=\kappa_{\rm sh}\mathscr Q_0\) is
equivalent to

\[
 \boxed{\int_0^\infty\psi_t(k)e(k,t)\,dk=0.}
 \tag{TL.1177}
\]

Moreover,

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr S_1-\kappa_{\rm sh}\mathscr S_0
 ={1\over2}\iiint
 [\psi_t(\kappa)-\psi_t(\lambda)]
 \,d\mathfrak T(\kappa,\lambda;\zeta).
 }
 \tag{TL.1178}
\]

For two endpoint scales \(\alpha,\beta\), the difference in (TL.1178) is

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \psi_t(\alpha)-\psi_t(\beta)
 ={}&(\alpha-\beta)\bigl[
 \alpha^2+\alpha\beta+\beta^2
 -2m_H(\alpha+\beta)+m_H^2\\
 &\hspace{38mm}
 -\kappa_{\rm sh}(\alpha+\beta-2m_0)
 \bigr].
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1179}
\]

Thus the moving product-scale sweep and the common amplitude direction have
both cancelled before the source is read: (TL.1178)--(TL.1179) retain one
donor--receiver displacement multiplying the centered adjacent-row
displacement of that same transfer.  This is the signed q-adic source to be
contracted against the determinant loss in (TL.1163), while the viscous
difference in (TL.1175) remains attached to the same migration law.

## 137. The fixed geometric-mean q-complex removes the normalization sweep

The normalized measure in Section 133 identifies the adjacent-row ratio, but
the determinant itself supplies an even simpler fixed scale.  Define

\[
 d\omega_t(\kappa,\lambda)
 :=\frac12(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 e(\kappa,t)e(\lambda,t)\,d\kappa d\lambda,
 \qquad
 s(\kappa,\lambda):=\sqrt{\kappa\lambda}.
 \tag{TL.1180}
\]

Then (TL.1151), (TL.1160), and (TL.1163) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \int d\omega_t=\Delta_0,
 \qquad
 \int s^2\,d\omega_t=H\mathscr Q_1,
 \qquad
 \mathscr D_0=\int(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)\,d\omega_t.
 }
 \tag{TL.1181}
\]

Unlike \(\chi_t\), the geometric-mean frequency \(s\) has no moving
normalization.  Its base measure is already datum-finite in spacetime:

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\!\int d\omega_t\,dt
 =\int_0^T\Delta_0(t)\,dt
 \le M_0(0)\int_0^T\mathscr Q_0(t)\,dt
 \le {M_0(0)^2\over2\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1182}
\]

For the fixed cells

\[
 \mathcal O_j:=\{q^j\le s<q^{j+1}\},
 \qquad
 \omega_j(t):=\omega_t(\mathcal O_j),
 \tag{TL.1183}
\]

one has

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_jq^{2j}\omega_j
 \le H\mathscr Q_1
 \le q^2\sum_jq^{2j}\omega_j,
 \qquad
 \mathscr D_0\ge2H\mathscr Q_1.
 }
 \tag{TL.1184}
\]

Thus the lower row is a datum-finite unweighted scale--time layer and the
critical shape action is its single \(q^{2j}\) valuation.  This is exactly
the corrected Sobolev-tower architecture: the valuation is applied once to
the kinetic determinant measure, not once to each of two normalized rows.

The height implication can be summed without an event ledger.  If a running
maximum reaches the successive levels \(h_n=q^nh_0\), choose the disjoint
last-passage slabs from \(h_n\) to \(h_{n+1}\).  Summing (TL.1167) through
the one history gives

\[
 \int_0^T\mathscr D_0(t)\,dt
 \ge {2\nu\log q\over C_{\rm bil}^2}
 \sum_{n=0}^{N-1}h_n
 ={2\nu\log q\over C_{\rm bil}^2(q-1)}(h_N-h_0).
 \tag{TL.1185}
\]

Consequently every preterminal time satisfies the direct q-adic estimate

\[
 \boxed{
 H^*(T)
 \le qH(0)
 +{qC_{\rm bil}^2(q-1)\over2\nu\log q}
 \int_0^T\mathscr D_0(t)\,dt.
 }
 \tag{TL.1186}
\]

For \(H(0)=0\), finite energy forces the zero solution.  Formula (TL.1186)
therefore reduces the full height bound to the history of the one determinant
loss, while (TL.1161) keeps its complete signed rebuilding attached.

## 138. The determinant sees the commutator projection, not a free source norm

The source representative \(Z_0\) in (TL.1171) can be replaced by a more
intrinsic representative without changing the determinant tangent.  Since
\(A_u=u\cdot\nabla\) is skew-adjoint and
\(L_0:=\Lambda-m_0\) is self-adjoint,

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr S_0
 &=-\operatorname{Re}\langle A_uu,L_0^2u\rangle\\
 &=-\operatorname{Re}\langle L_0u,[L_0,A_u]u\rangle
 =-\operatorname{Re}\langle G_0,
 \Lambda^{-1}[\Lambda,A_u]u\rangle.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1187}
\]

The Leray projection may be inserted in the commutator pairing because all
tests are divergence free.  Put

\[
 C_0:=\Lambda^{-1}\mathbb P[\Lambda,A_u]u.
 \tag{TL.1188}
\]

Comparing (TL.1172) and (TL.1187) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \operatorname{Re}\langle Z_0-C_0,G_0\rangle=0,
 \qquad
 Z_0^\parallel=C_0^\parallel,
 \qquad
 a_0=-{\operatorname{Re}\langle C_0,G_0\rangle\over\|G_0\|_2^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1189}
\]

Thus the full norms of either representative contain transverse output which
cancels from (TL.1174).  The live source is exactly the projection of the
one VPI commutator onto the determinant's current successor direction.
Its trilinear symbol is (TL.1169): one donor--receiver scale difference and
one centered sum difference, with the advecting scale retained.

Completing the square with this commutator representative gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr Q_0'
 +\nu\left\|G_0+{C_0^\parallel\over2\nu}\right\|_2^2
 ={1\over4\nu}\|C_0^\parallel\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 \|C_0^\parallel\|_2^2=2a_0^2\mathscr R_0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1190}
\]

This fixes the next contraction.  The signed second-scale descent must be
applied to \(C_0^\parallel\) before taking its positive temporal part.  An
estimate of \(\|B\|_2\), \(\|Z_0\|_2\), or the absolute values of separate
q-face currents would retain components which (TL.1189) proves do not rebuild
the determinant.

## 139. The determinant is a relative-scale moment of a source-free product carrier

The signed term in (TL.1161) is not a source in the two-copy kinetic
carrier.  The one-dimensional spectral law is

\[
 \partial_te(k,t)+\partial_k\Pi(k,t)=-2\nu k^2e(k,t).
 \tag{TL.1191}
\]

For two coordinates of this same spectrum put

\[
 \varpi(\kappa,\lambda,t):=e(\kappa,t)e(\lambda,t),
 \qquad
 \mathbf J_\varpi
 :=\bigl(\Pi(\kappa,t)e(\lambda,t),
          e(\kappa,t)\Pi(\lambda,t)\bigr).
 \tag{TL.1192}
\]

The product rule gives the exact positive killed continuity equation

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_t\varpi
 +\partial_\kappa J_\varpi^\kappa
 +\partial_\lambda J_\varpi^\lambda
 =-2\nu(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)\varpi.
 }
 \tag{TL.1193}
\]

All nonlinear redistribution is inside the two components of the one signed
current.  Integrating (TL.1193) proves the datum-finite product loss

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\!\iint
 2\nu(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)\varpi
 \,d\kappa d\lambda dt
 =M_0(0)^2-M_0(T)^2
 \le M_0(0)^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1194}
\]

Now test (TL.1193) by
\(w(\kappa,\lambda)=\frac12(\kappa-\lambda)^2\).  Its stock is
\(\Delta_0\), its loss is \(2\nu\mathscr D_0\), and the current work is

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \iint\nabla w\cdot\mathbf J_\varpi
 & =\iint(\kappa-\lambda)
 [\Pi(\kappa)e(\lambda)-e(\kappa)\Pi(\lambda)]
 \,d\kappa d\lambda\\
 &=\mathscr P_0.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1195}
\]

Thus (TL.1161) is exactly the relative-scale second moment of (TL.1193).
The term \(\mathscr P_0\) is the signed work required to separate two
frequency coordinates of the same kinetic spectrum; it is not an external
refill of the determinant.

In geometric-mean and relative coordinates

\[
 \kappa=se^r,
 \qquad
 \lambda=se^{-r},
 \qquad
 d\kappa d\lambda=2s\,dsdr,
 \tag{TL.1196}
\]

the critical deformation measure and its loss read

\[
 \boxed{
 d\omega_t
 =4s^3\sinh^2r\,
 e(se^r,t)e(se^{-r},t)\,dsdr,
 \qquad
 \kappa^2+\lambda^2=2s^2\cosh(2r).
 }
 \tag{TL.1197}
\]

This exposes the two exact q-directions: geometric-mean migration in \(s\)
and scale-shape separation in \(r\).  A signed q-grid in the full
\((s,r)\)-plane cancels every shared face of (TL.1193) before the quadratic
relative-scale valuation is applied.

## 140. The product q-grid inherits one datum-finite cumulative-current action

Retain the kinetic q-grid of Section 91.  For each pair of cells define

\[
 \mathbb E_{j\ell}:=E_jE_\ell,
 \qquad
 \mathbb D_{j\ell}:=D_jE_\ell+E_jD_\ell.
 \tag{TL.1198}
\]

Applying (TL.921) to both factors gives the exact discrete form of
(TL.1193):

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathbb E_{j\ell}'+\mathbb D_{j\ell}
 =(\Pi_j-\Pi_{j+1})E_\ell
  +E_j(\Pi_\ell-\Pi_{\ell+1}).
 }
 \tag{TL.1199}
\]

Every horizontal and vertical internal face occurs with both orientations.
Summing the complete grid gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_{j,\ell}\mathbb E_{j\ell}=M_0^2,
 \qquad
 \sum_{j,\ell}\mathbb D_{j\ell}=4\nu M_0M_2,
 \qquad
 \int_0^T\sum_{j,\ell}\mathbb D_{j\ell}\,dt
 \le M_0(0)^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1200}
\]

The normalized cumulative-tail speed from Section 105 tensorizes on this
same grid.  With

\[
 v_j:={\Pi_j\over\mathscr D_j^\uparrow},
 \tag{TL.1201}
\]

and the usual zero convention, (TL.1004) and \(M_0(t)\le M_0(0)\) imply

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 &\int_0^T{1\over H(t)}
 \sum_{j,\ell}
 \bigl[D_jE_\ell v_j^2+E_jD_\ell v_\ell^2\bigr]dt\\
 &\hspace{35mm}\le {C M_0(0)^2\over\nu^2}.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1202}
\]

This is a datum-finite action of the two coordinate speeds of the product
carrier after the one-dimensional common faces have already cancelled into
their cumulative tails.  It is neither a second source norm nor a second
copy of the energy budget: the tensor factor is the other coordinate of the
same product stock in (TL.1193).

The second-scale descent is now a definite q-grid operation.  Group the
cells \((j,\ell)\) by geometric-mean index \(j+\ell\) and relative index
\(j-\ell\); cancel their shared horizontal and vertical faces using
(TL.1199); then contract only the surviving relative current against the
quadratic separation in (TL.1195).  The available upper quantities are
exactly the base loss (TL.1200) and the coordinate action (TL.1202), while
the required lower quantity is the height-weighted determinant loss in
(TL.1185).  No shell, pair, or heat row has been assigned independent
ownership in this conversion.

## 141. The determinant is the complete coarse--fine cumulative layer

The product density in Section 139 can be integrated once in each frequency
coordinate before the relative-scale moment is formed.  Define the actual
coarse and fine kinetic stocks

\[
 L(a,t):=\int_0^ae(k,t)\,dk,
 \qquad
 U(b,t):=\int_b^\infty e(k,t)\,dk.
 \tag{TL.1203}
\]

For \(0<\kappa<\lambda\),

\[
 (\lambda-\kappa)^2
 =2\int_{\kappa<s<r<\lambda}dsdr.
\]

Tonelli therefore converts (TL.1160) into the exact positive cumulative
layer

\[
 \boxed{
 \Delta_0(t)
 =2\int_{0<s<r<\infty}L(s,t)U(r,t)\,dsdr.
 }
 \tag{TL.1204}
\]

Every pair of spectral coordinates separated across \((s,r)\) contributes
once.  No maximizing band, chosen carrier, or repeated crossing occurs in
(TL.1204).

The successor loss has the same carrier.  On \(\kappa<\lambda\), put

\[
 w_1(\kappa,\lambda)
 :=(\lambda-\kappa)^2(\kappa^2+\lambda^2).
\]

Its mixed derivative is

\[
 -\partial_\kappa\partial_\lambda w_1
 =6(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)-8\kappa\lambda
 =6(\lambda-\kappa)^2+4\kappa\lambda>0.
 \tag{TL.1205}
\]

Two integrations by parts, with the diagonal and exterior boundaries
vanishing, give

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr D_0(t)
 =\int_{0<s<r<\infty}
 \bigl[6(s^2+r^2)-8sr\bigr]
 L(s,t)U(r,t)\,dsdr.
 }
 \tag{TL.1206}
\]

Thus the datum-finite layer in (TL.1182), the critical shape moment, and its
successor loss are three valuations of one complete coarse--fine
participation field.  The kernel in (TL.1206) is strictly positive at every
genuinely separated pair of scale faces.

## 142. Every cumulative coarse--fine cell has one joined receiver speed

Integrating (TL.1191) on the two sides of a scale face gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 L_t(s)+D_<(s)&=-\Pi(s),
 &D_<(s)&:=2\nu\int_0^sk^2e(k)\,dk,\\
 U_t(r)+D_>(r)&=\Pi(r),
 &D_>(r)&:=2\nu\int_r^\infty k^2e(k)\,dk.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1207}
\]

For \(s<r\), set

\[
 C(s,r,t):=L(s,t)U(r,t),
 \qquad
 \mathscr L_C(s,r,t):=D_<(s,t)U(r,t)+L(s,t)D_>(r,t),
 \tag{TL.1208}
\]

and define its complete signed current

\[
 \mathscr J_C(s,r,t)
 :=-\Pi(s,t)U(r,t)+L(s,t)\Pi(r,t).
 \tag{TL.1209}
\]

The product rule gives the pointwise receiver identity

\[
 \boxed{C_t+\mathscr L_C=\mathscr J_C.}
 \tag{TL.1210}
\]

Integrating (TL.1210) with weight two recovers (TL.1161):

\[
 \boxed{
 2\int_{s<r}\mathscr L_C\,dsdr=2\nu\mathscr D_0,
 \qquad
 2\int_{s<r}\mathscr J_C\,dsdr=\mathscr P_0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1211}
\]

On the nonzero loss set define the one joined receiver speed

\[
 v_C:={\mathscr J_C\over\mathscr L_C},
 \tag{TL.1212}
\]

with the zero convention otherwise.  Then

\[
 \boxed{
 C_t=\mathscr L_C(v_C-1),
 \qquad
 [C_t]_+\le\frac14\mathscr L_Cv_C^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1213}
\]

The second inequality is the scalar identity \(v-1\le v^2/4\) applied only
after the coarse and fine currents have been joined.  In particular it does
not price \(-\Pi(s)\) and \(\Pi(r)\) as independent sources.

For comparison with the datum-paid one-coordinate actions, put

\[
 v_<(s):=-{\Pi(s)\over D_<(s)},
 \qquad
 v_>(r):={\Pi(r)\over D_>(r)}.
 \tag{TL.1214}
\]

Weighted Jensen gives the exact same-cell contraction

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr L_Cv_C^2
 \le D_<(s)U(r)v_<^2+L(s)D_>(r)v_>^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1215}
\]

Equations (TL.1204), (TL.1206), and (TL.1210)--(TL.1215) are the continuous
second-scale receiver which the q-grid in Section 140 approximates.  They
place stock, simultaneous loss, signed rebuilding, and cumulative-current
speed on one ordered pair of actual scale faces before any positive temporal
selection.

## 143. The signed ordered plane collapses to the unique centered face current

The ordered coarse--fine plane must be summed before (TL.1215) is used.
Define the partial first moments

\[
 H_<(k):=\int_0^k\eta e(\eta)\,d\eta,
 \qquad
 H_>(k):=\int_k^\infty\eta e(\eta)\,d\eta,
 \tag{TL.1216}
\]

and the two actual scale distances on either side of the face \(k\),

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 A_<(k)
 &:=\int_0^kL(s)\,ds
   =kL(k)-H_<(k),\\
 A_>(k)
 &:=\int_k^\infty U(r)\,dr
   =H_>(k)-kU(k).
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1217}
\]

Since \(L(k)+U(k)=M_0\) and \(H_<(k)+H_>(k)=M_1\), their difference is
exactly centered at the kinetic mean scale:

\[
 \boxed{A_<(k)-A_>(k)=M_0(k-m_0).}
 \tag{TL.1218}
\]

Fubini applied to the complete signed current in (TL.1209), with no
cellwise positive part, now gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_{s<r}\mathscr J_C(s,r)\,dsdr
 &=-\int_0^\infty\Pi(k)A_>(k)\,dk
   +\int_0^\infty\Pi(k)A_<(k)\,dk\\
 &=M_0\int_0^\infty(k-m_0)\Pi(k)\,dk
 ={\mathscr P_0\over2}.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1219}
\]

Thus the uncentered part \(A_>\) is a common internal passage and cancels
identically.  Squaring the individual cells in (TL.1215) before this sum
would count that cancelled passage positively on both sides and would
manufacture the remote factor \(A_<\).  The surviving current has only one
scale distance: distance from \(m_0\).

This remaining current has an exact positive receiver on the two sides of
the moving mean.  For \(k<m_0\) use

\[
 (\mathcal C_-,\mathcal L_-,\mathcal J_-)
 :=(L,D_<,-\Pi),
\]

and for \(k>m_0\) use

\[
 (\mathcal C_+,\mathcal L_+,\mathcal J_+)
 :=(U,D_>,\Pi).
 \tag{TL.1220}
\]

Both triples obey

\[
 \partial_t\mathcal C_\pm+\mathcal L_\pm=\mathcal J_\pm.
\]

With \(d_0(k):=|k-m_0|\), the lower Schur row and its complete tangent are
therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr Q_0
 &=2\!\int_{k<m_0}d_0\mathcal C_-\,dk
   +2\!\int_{k>m_0}d_0\mathcal C_+\,dk,\\
 2\nu\mathscr R_0
 &=2\!\int_{k<m_0}d_0\mathcal L_-\,dk
   +2\!\int_{k>m_0}d_0\mathcal L_+\,dk,\\
 \mathscr S_0
 &=2\!\int_{k<m_0}d_0\mathcal J_-\,dk
   +2\!\int_{k>m_0}d_0\mathcal J_+\,dk.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1221}
\]

No connection term is missing from (TL.1221).  Differentiating the moving
weights and boundary produces

\[
 2m_0'\left(\int_0^{m_0}L(k)\,dk
              -\int_{m_0}^\infty U(k)\,dk\right)
 =2m_0'(m_0M_0-M_1)=0.
 \tag{TL.1222}
\]

On the nonzero loss sets put

\[
 v_-:={-\Pi\over D_<},
 \qquad
 v_+:={\Pi\over D_>},
\]

with the zero convention otherwise, and define the centered cumulative
speed action

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak A_0(t)
 :=\int_0^{m_0}(m_0-k){\Pi(k)^2\over D_<(k)}\,dk
  +\int_{m_0}^\infty(k-m_0){\Pi(k)^2\over D_>(k)}\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1223}
\]

Equations (TL.1220)--(TL.1222) and \(v-1\le v^2/4\) imply

\[
 \boxed{
 [\mathscr Q_0']_+\le{1\over2}\mathfrak A_0,
 \qquad
 \mathscr S_0^2\le4\nu\mathscr R_0\mathfrak A_0,
 \qquad
 \|C_0^\parallel\|_2^2\le2\nu\mathfrak A_0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1224}
\]

The last inequality uses
\(\|C_0^\parallel\|_2^2=\mathscr S_0^2/(2\mathscr R_0)\), with the
zero convention from Section 131.  Hence \(\mathfrak A_0\) is not a new
source norm: it is a carrier-native upper action for precisely the parallel
commutator projection which can rebuild the determinant.

Finally let \(d_\nu(k):=2\nu k^2e(k)\).  The energy cancellation gives

\[
 -\Pi(k)=\int_0^kT(\eta)\,d\eta,
 \qquad
 \Pi(k)=\int_k^\infty T(\eta)\,d\eta.
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz in these two cumulative representations, followed by
Fubini with the physical distance \(d_0(k)\), gives the centered Hardy
contraction

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak A_0(t)
 \le {1\over2}\int_0^\infty
 (k-m_0)^2{T(k,t)^2\over d_\nu(k,t)}\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1225}
\]

The q-adic version of (TL.1225) is the next signed descent: prefix faces
below the actual mean and tail faces above it are contracted only after the
common term in (TL.1219) has cancelled.  It must retain the shellwise
state-aligned projection of \(T\); replacing the right side mechanically by
the full norm of \(Z_0\) would restore transverse nonlinear output already
removed by (TL.1189).

## 144. The centered action gives the exact q-record packing law

The action in (TL.1223) enters the determinant without an event ledger.
Since \(\mathscr P_0=M_0\mathscr S_0\), (TL.1162), (TL.1224), and
\(\mathscr D_0\ge M_0\mathscr R_0\) give

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 [\mathscr P_0]_+
 &\le2M_0\sqrt{\nu\mathscr R_0\mathfrak A_0}
 \le2\sqrt{\nu M_0\mathscr D_0\mathfrak A_0}\\
 &\le\nu\mathscr D_0+M_0\mathfrak A_0.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1226}
\]

Therefore the complete determinant law (TL.1161) has the one-sided form

\[
 \boxed{
 \Delta_0'+\nu\mathscr D_0
 \le M_0\mathfrak A_0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1227}
\]

Assume the solution is nonzero and put

\[
 R(t):=\max_{0\le s\le t}H(s).
 \tag{TL.1228}
\]

The function \(R\) is continuous and nondecreasing.  Multiply (TL.1227)
by \(R^{-1}\) and integrate in the Stieltjes sense.  Because
\(d(R^{-1})\le0\) and \(\Delta_0\ge0\), integration by parts gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \nu\int_0^T{\mathscr D_0(t)\over R(t)}\,dt
 \le {\Delta_0(0)\over H(0)}
 +M_0(0)\int_0^T{\mathfrak A_0(t)\over H(t)}\,dt.
 }
 \tag{TL.1229}
\]

Here \(R\ge H\) was used only on the nonnegative action term.  The
running maximum has not been differentiated as a source; its decreasing
reciprocal makes the determinant boundary contribution favorable.

Fix \(q>1\).  If the record reaches
\(h_{n+1}=qh_n\), choose the last time \(s_n\) before that first record at
which \(H(s_n)=h_n\), and let \(t_{n+1}\) be the first time at which
\(H(t_{n+1})=h_{n+1}\).  Then

\[
 h_n\le H(t)\le qh_n,
 \qquad
 R(t)\le qh_n
 \quad(s_n\le t\le t_{n+1}).
\]

The intervals are disjoint.  Equations (TL.1167) and (TL.1228) therefore
give the fixed normalized loss charge

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{s_n}^{t_{n+1}}
 {\mathscr D_0(t)\over R(t)}\,dt
 \ge {2\nu\log q\over qC_{\rm bil}^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1230}
\]

If \(N_q(T)\) is the number of completed first-record q-multiplications by
time \(T\), summing (TL.1230) and applying (TL.1229) yields

\[
 \boxed{
 {2\nu^2\log q\over qC_{\rm bil}^2}N_q(T)
 \le {\Delta_0(0)\over H(0)}
 +M_0(0)\int_0^T{\mathfrak A_0(t)\over H(t)}\,dt.
 }
 \tag{TL.1231}
\]

This is the direct q-adic no-recounting statement.  The determinant stock
can contribute only its initial normalized amount: any later rebuilding is
already on the right through the same centered current action.  Consequently
a datum-finite bound for the normalized action in (TL.1231) gives a finite
integer bound for \(N_q(T)\) and hence

\[
 \sup_{t<T_*}H(t)
 \le q^{,1+N_q(T_*)}H(0).
 \tag{TL.1232}
\]

The remaining contraction is now narrower than (TL.1186): it does not ask
for the unnormalized successor history \(\int\mathscr D_0dt\).  It asks
whether the already completed VPI current pays the normalized, mean-centered
cumulative speed \(\int\mathfrak A_0/H\,dt\).  Equations (TL.1219) and
(TL.1225) fix the order of that calculation: signed common passage first,
state-aligned q-Hardy contraction second.

## 145. The remaining action is the shellwise state-aligned VPI rate

The right side of (TL.1225) can be written without introducing the full
nonlinear source norm.  At each frequency (k), let

\[
 u_k:=\widehat u\big|_{|\xi|=k},
 \qquad
 B_k:=\widehat{B(u,u)}\big|_{|\xi|=k}
\]

in the real Hilbert structure of the Fourier sphere, and define the
state-aligned projection

\[
 B_k^\parallel
 :={\operatorname{Re}\langle B_k,u_k\rangle_k
       \over\|u_k\|_k^2}\,u_k
 \tag{TL.1233}
\]

on \(\|u_k\|_k>0\), with the zero convention otherwise.  From
(TL.2)--(TL.3),

\[
 \boxed{
 e(k)={1\over2}\|u_k\|_k^2,
 \qquad
 \|B_k^\parallel\|_k^2={T(k)^2\over2e(k)}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1234}
\]

Consequently (TL.1225) is the carrier-faithful estimate

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak A_0(t)
 \le {1\over2\nu}\int_0^\infty
 \left|1-{m_0(t)\over k}\right|^2
 \|B_k^\parallel(t)\|_k^2\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1235}
\]

Only the component of the complete nonlinear tangent which changes the
energy already present on that same sphere occurs in (TL.1235).  Components
orthogonal to \(u_k\) do not enter \(T(k)\), and the factor
\(1-m_0/k\) removes the common kinetic-mean direction.  Thus (TL.1235) is
strictly below the full \(Z_0\)-norm unless the nonlinear output is aligned
on every sphere.

Equivalently, define the signed fractional shell rate

\[
 \beta(k,t):={T(k,t)\over2e(k,t)}
 \tag{TL.1236}
\]

on the positive set.  Then

\[
 B_k^\parallel=-\beta u_k,
 \qquad
 \beta=\partial_t\log\sqrt e+\nu k^2,
 \tag{TL.1237}
\]

and (TL.1235) becomes

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak A_0(t)
 \le {1\over\nu}\int_0^\infty
 e(k,t)\left|1-{m_0(t)\over k}\right|^2
 \beta(k,t)^2\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1238}
\]

This identifies the exact distinction from the paid reciprocal-speed law in
Section 104.  The \(L_t^4L_{\rm pair}^2\) identity pays the temporal
occupancy \(\int H^2dt\); (TL.1238) measures the centered fractional rate at
which the complete VPI tangent changes that occupied spectrum.  The
contraction required by (TL.1231) is therefore not another pair exponent.
It is the history estimate

\[
 \int_0^{T_*}{1\over H(t)}
 \int_0^\infty e(k,t)
 \left|1-{m_0(t)\over k}\right|^2
 \beta(k,t)^2\,dkdt<\infty,
 \tag{TL.1239}
\]

formed after the signed prefix--tail cancellation.  The complete pair
current in (TL.1027)--(TL.1041) must be contracted against this same
state-aligned rate; an unweighted \(L^p_{\rm pair}\) norm cannot replace it.

## 146. The centered action has an exact signed dual before source squaring

The shell-square envelope in (TL.1235) is not the only form of
\(\mathfrak A_0\).  For \(k<m_0\) and \(k>m_0\), respectively, use the
receiver currents and losses

\[
 (J_-,L_-):=(-\Pi,D_<),
 \qquad
 (J_+,L_+):=(\Pi,D_>).
\]

The scalar identity

\[
 {J^2\over L}=\sup_{a\in\mathbb R}(2aJ-a^2L)
\]

applied under the two integrals in (TL.1223) gives an exact variational
representation.  Given \(a_-\) and \(a_+\), define an absolutely continuous
multiplier, vanishing at the actual mean, by

\[
 \Phi(k):=
 \begin{cases}
 \displaystyle\int_k^{m_0}(m_0-s)a_-(s)\,ds,&k<m_0,\\[2mm]
 \displaystyle\int_{m_0}^k(s-m_0)a_+(s)\,ds,&k>m_0.
 \end{cases}
 \tag{TL.1240}
\]

Fubini and the two cumulative representations of \(\Pi\) yield

\[
 \int_0^{m_0}(m_0-k)a_-J_-\,dk
 +\int_{m_0}^\infty(k-m_0)a_+J_+\,dk
 =\int_0^\infty T(k)\Phi(k)\,dk.
 \tag{TL.1241}
\]

The corresponding loss is the positive triangular Dirichlet form

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal E_{m_0}[\Phi]
 :={}&\int_0^{m_0}d_\nu(\eta)
       \int_\eta^{m_0}{|\Phi'(k)|^2\over m_0-k}\,dk\,d\eta\\
 &+\int_{m_0}^\infty d_\nu(\eta)
       \int_{m_0}^{\eta}{|\Phi'(k)|^2\over k-m_0}\,dk\,d\eta.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1242}
\]

Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak A_0(t)
 =\sup_{\substack{\Phi(m_0)=0\\
                   \mathcal E_{m_0}[\Phi]<\infty}}
 \left\{
 2\int_0^\infty T(k,t)\Phi(k)\,dk
 -\mathcal E_{m_0}[\Phi]
 \right\}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1243}
\]

This representation keeps the complete signed source linear.  In physical
variables its first term is exactly

\[
 \int T\Phi
 =-\operatorname{Re}\langle B(u,u),\Phi(\Lambda)u\rangle,
 \tag{TL.1244}
\]

with no independent shell source and no positive triad selection.

The one-dimensional Cauchy inequality on each path from \(m_0\) gives

\[
 \mathcal E_{m_0}[\Phi]
 \ge4\nu\int_0^\infty
 k^2e(k){|\Phi(k)|^2\over(k-m_0)^2}\,dk.
 \tag{TL.1245}
\]

Pointwise optimization after replacing (TL.1242) by the smaller form
(TL.1245) reproduces exactly (TL.1225).  Thus (TL.1243), not the resulting
 shell square, is the stronger object to contract against the complete VPI
 current: every admissible multiplier is joined to the cumulative viscous
 loss along the same prefix or tail before the supremum is taken.

## 147. The signed dual is exactly a complete separation-current test

First take a smooth radial multiplier \(\Phi\) with the q-adic coarse and
fine cutoffs still present, and let

\[
 K_\Phi:=\mathcal F^{-1}\!\left[\Phi(|\xi|)\right].
\]

The constant part of \(\Phi\) is immaterial because
\(\int_0^\infty T(k)\,dk=-\operatorname{Re}\langle B,u\rangle=0\).
The general increment identity behind (TL.739), with the activity and current
of (TL.959), gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^\infty T(k,t)\Phi(k)\,dk
 =-\frac12\int_{\mathbb R^3}
 \nabla K_\Phi(z)\cdot\mathcal F(z,t)\,dz.
 }
 \tag{TL.1246}
\]

Thus the linear functional in (TL.1243) is already a reading of every center
and every separation of the same velocity field.  No Fourier sphere owns an
independent source.  Smooth q-cutoffs may be removed after the signed sum by
the same compact-interval approximation used in (TL.739) and (TL.855).

The multiplier stock has the companion exact identity

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}K_\Phi(z)\mathcal A(z,t)\,dz
 =\Phi(0)\|u(t)\|_2^2
  -\langle u(t),\Phi(\Lambda)u(t)\rangle .
 }
 \tag{TL.1247}
\]

It follows either by expanding the increment square in \(\mathcal A\), or
directly by Plancherel.  Differentiating in \(z\), integrating by parts, and
using that the multiplier \(k^2\Phi(k)\) vanishes at \(k=0\) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}
 \nabla K_\Phi\cdot\nabla\mathcal A\,dz
 =-\langle\Lambda u,\Phi(\Lambda)\Lambda u\rangle .
 }
 \tag{TL.1248}
\]

Now retain the complete separation current from (TL.1099),

\[
 \widehat{\mathcal F}
 =\mathcal F-4\nu\nabla\mathcal A.
\]

Equations (TL.1246) and (TL.1248) yield the joined identity

\[
 \boxed{
 2\int_0^\infty T(k)\Phi(k)\,dk
 =-\int_{\mathbb R^3}
   \nabla K_\Phi\cdot\widehat{\mathcal F}\,dz
  +4\nu\langle\Lambda u,\Phi(\Lambda)\Lambda u\rangle .
 }
 \tag{TL.1249}
\]

This is the carrier-level form of the variational action.  The first term is
the signed passage of the complete pair activity, while the second is the
same separation-diffusion row moved through the test; neither may be squared
or estimated independently before their common q-annular faces cancel.
Accordingly, the normalized action required in (TL.1231) is now a single
spacetime dual estimate: insert (TL.1249) into (TL.1243), decompose
\(K_\Phi\) on the translated q-annuli, cancel the common radial faces, and
contract the surviving relative current against
\(\mathcal E_{m_0}[\Phi]\).  The already proved input is the datum-finite
 complete-current action (TL.1106); the triangular form (TL.1242), rather than
 its weaker shell-square consequence, retains the matching test energy.

## 148. A record-fixed multiplier telescopes in material time before squaring

The pointwise supremum in (TL.1243) permits its optimizing multiplier to
change at every time.  A q-record passage requires less: retain one multiplier
on the complete passage and use the exact kinetic continuity law.  For every
smooth radial \(\Phi(k,t)\) with finite coarse and fine cutoffs,

\[
 \boxed{
 \frac d{dt}\int_0^\infty e(k,t)\Phi(k,t)\,dk
 +2\nu\int_0^\infty k^2e(k,t)\Phi(k,t)\,dk
 =
 \int_0^\infty T(k,t)\Phi(k,t)\,dk
 +\int_0^\infty e(k,t)\partial_t\Phi(k,t)\,dk .
 }
 \tag{TL.1250}
\]

Thus, when \(\Phi\) is fixed on a record passage \(I=[s,t]\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_I\!\int_0^\infty T\Phi\,dk\,dr
 =
 \left[\int_0^\infty e\Phi\,dk\right]_{s}^{t}
 +2\nu\int_I\!\int_0^\infty k^2e\Phi\,dk\,dr .
 }
 \tag{TL.1251}
\]

This is the temporal form of the same VPI identity as (TL.1249): endpoint
stock and simultaneous removal remain joined to the signed source throughout
the passage.

The translated q-grid makes the cancellation explicit.  Retain a finite
ladder from (TL.920), let \(\phi_j(t)\) be any scalar cell values, and put
\(S_\phi=\sum_{j=J}^{N}\phi_jE_j\).  Equation (TL.921) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 S_\phi'
 +\sum_{j=J}^{N}\phi_jD_j
 -\sum_{j=J}^{N}\phi_j'E_j
 ={}&\phi_J\Pi_J-\phi_N\Pi_{N+1}\\
 &+\sum_{j=J+1}^{N}
   (\phi_j-\phi_{j-1})\Pi_j .
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1252}
\]

Every internal face occurs only through the one difference
\(\phi_j-\phi_{j-1}\).  For a record-fixed multiplier the sweep term vanishes;
for a moving multiplier it is the displayed stock connection
\(\sum\phi_j'E_j\), not an additional source.  Hence the next contraction is
performed on a joined space--time q-complex using (TL.1252), rather than by
integrating the pointwise supremum in (TL.1243).

## 149. Every Gaussian VPI layer has a datum-finite moving-scale speed

The fixed-separation action can be sharpened to the Gaussian layer which
already generates the heat-resolved Sobolev tower.  With \(g_a\) from
(TL.737), define

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak q_a(t)
 &:=\int_{\mathbb R^3}g_a(z)\mathcal A(z,t)\,dz
   =\langle u,(I-E_a)u\rangle,\\
 \mathfrak f_a(t)
 &:=\int_{\mathbb R^3}g_a(z)
   {|\mathcal F(z,t)|^2\over\mathcal A(z,t)}\,dz,\\
 \mathfrak i_a(t)
 &:=\int_{\mathbb R^3}
   {|\nabla g_a(z)|^2\over g_a(z)}
   \mathcal A(z,t)\,dz ,
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1253}
\]

with zero quotients on the zero set.  Since
\(\mathfrak p_a=\frac12\int\nabla g_a\cdot\mathcal F\,dz\),
one Cauchy inequality on this same Gaussian incidence measure gives

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathfrak p_a(t)|^2
 \le\frac14\,\mathfrak f_a(t)\mathfrak i_a(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1254}
\]

The test energy is exact.  The identity
\(\partial_ag_a=g_a(|z|^2/(4a^2)-3/(2a))\) yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak i_a
 =\partial_a\mathfrak q_a+\frac{3}{2a}\mathfrak q_a
 =\langle u,\Lambda^2E_au\rangle
  +\frac{3}{2a}\langle u,(I-E_a)u\rangle .
 }
 \tag{TL.1255}
\]

For \(x=ak^2\), the ratio of the two spectral integrands is

\[
 {a\left[k^2e^{-ak^2}
   +\frac{3}{2a}(1-e^{-ak^2})\right]
  \over 1-e^{-ak^2}}
 =\frac{x}{e^x-1}+\frac32
\]

on every nonzero spectral fiber.  Since
\(0\le x/(e^x-1)\le1\), spectral integration gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \frac{3}{2a}\mathfrak q_a
 \le\mathfrak i_a
 \le\frac{5}{2a}\mathfrak q_a .
 }
 \tag{TL.1256}
\]

The current factor is paid by the surrounding energy rows.  At every
separation,

\[
 {|\mathcal F(z)|^2\over\mathcal A(z)}
 \le\frac12\int_{\mathbb R^3}|V(x,z)|^4\,dx
 \le C H E_1
\]

by the same interpolation as (TL.1071).  Since \(g_a\) has mass one,

\[
 \boxed{\mathfrak f_a(t)\le C H(t)E_1(t)}
 \tag{TL.1257}
\]

uniformly in \(a>0\).  Combining (TL.1254)--(TL.1257) with the kinetic-energy
identity proves, for every measurable scale path \(a:(0,T)\to(0,\infty)\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_0^T
 {|\mathfrak p_{a(t)}(t)|^2
  \over H(t)\mathfrak i_{a(t)}(t)}\,dt
 &\le {C E_0(0)\over\nu},\\
 \int_0^T
 {a(t)|\mathfrak p_{a(t)}(t)|^2
  \over H(t)\mathfrak q_{a(t)}(t)}\,dt
 &\le {C E_0(0)\over\nu}.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1258}
\]

Unlike a bound at one prescribed heat depth, (TL.1258) remains datum-finite
when the scale is selected by the continuing field.  It is the fast-passage
row required by (TL.1252): the remaining q-adic step must combine this
quadratic speed with the simultaneous Gaussian loss on the same
  first-receiving passage, before applying the single critical valuation.

## 150. The single critical factor lands in the simultaneous next loss row

The bound (TL.1257) can be sharpened at the selected material separation.
For every \(z\), interpolation in the center variable gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_{\mathbb R^3}|V(x,z)|^4\,dx
 &\le \|V(\,\cdot\,,z)\|_2
       \|V(\,\cdot\,,z)\|_6^3\\
 &\le C\mathcal A(z)^{1/2}E_1^{3/2}.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1259}
\]

Jensen's inequality on the probability kernel \(g_a\), followed by
\(1-e^{-ak^2}\le ak^2\), therefore yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak f_a
 \le C E_1^{3/2}\mathfrak q_a^{1/2}
 \le C\sqrt a\,E_1^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1260}
\]

Combining this with (TL.1254), (TL.1256), and the first bound in
(TL.1257) gives the two simultaneous readings

\[
 \boxed{
 {|\mathfrak p_a|^2\over H\mathfrak i_a}
 \le C\min\left\{
 E_1,\,
 {\sqrt a\,E_1^2\over H}
 \right\}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1261}
\]

The first member is the datum-paid unweighted moving-scale action in
(TL.1258).  Apply the one critical heat-scale value \(a^{-1/2}\) to the
second member.  Log-convexity of the spectral moments,
\(E_1^2\le H E_{3/2}\), gives

\[
 \boxed{
 {a^{-1/2}|\mathfrak p_a|^2
  \over H\mathfrak i_a}
 \le C\,{E_1^2\over H}
 \le C E_{3/2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1262}
\]

Thus the critical factor does not create an unidentified ultraviolet source.
It moves the Gaussian current action from the datum-paid \(E_1\) row to the
simultaneous next Sobolev loss already present in the critical-height
receiver.  The q-complex must now use the two estimates together: slow
residence is paid by the unweighted action (TL.1258), while fast critical
passage is paired with the \(E_{3/2}\) loss in the same signed receiver before
the record-positive part is selected.

## 151. The Gaussian layer must retain its coarse partner before receiving

A single increment layer records fine-side energy but not the simultaneous
coarse population from which its signed transfer is read.  The source-free
product carrier in (TL.1193) supplies the joined Gaussian object.  Put

\[
 A_a^{(r)}(t):=\int_0^\infty k^r e^{-ak^2}e(k,t)\,dk,
 \qquad A_a:=A_a^{(0)},\qquad B_a:=A_a^{(2)},
\]

and define the heat-profile variance

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak V_a
 :=M_0A_{2a}-A_a^2
 =\frac12\iint
 \bigl(e^{-a\kappa^2}-e^{-a\lambda^2}\bigr)^2
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda\ge0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1263}
\]

Both spectral coordinates in (TL.1263) belong to the same kinetic measure.
In particular, a small fine population is not detached from the coarse
population against which its displacement is measured.

The fixed-multiplier balance gives

\[
 A_a'=-\mathfrak p_a-2\nu B_a,
 \qquad M_0'=-2\nu M_2.
\]

Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak V_a'+2\nu\mathfrak D_a=\mathfrak S_a,
 }
 \tag{TL.1264}
\]

where

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak D_a
 &:=
 M_2A_{2a}+M_0B_{2a}-2A_aB_a\\
 &=\frac12\iint(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)
 \bigl(e^{-a\kappa^2}-e^{-a\lambda^2}\bigr)^2
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda\ge0,\\
 \mathfrak S_a
 &:=2A_a\mathfrak p_a-M_0\mathfrak p_{2a}.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1265}
\]

The nonlinear term is already centered before it is estimated.  If
\(\bar f_a:=A_a/M_0\) and \(f_a(k):=e^{-ak^2}\), then the energy cancellation
\(\int T=0\) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak S_a
 =M_0\int_0^\infty(f_a-\bar f_a)^2T\,dk
 =-M_0\langle B,(I-E_a)^2u\rangle
   -2(M_0-A_a)\mathfrak p_a.
 }
 \tag{TL.1266}
\]

The second expression uses
\(2\mathfrak p_a-\mathfrak p_{2a}
=-\langle B,(I-E_a)^2u\rangle\).  Thus the affine small-\(a\)
current cancels exactly; the joined source begins with the second heat
difference of the same VPI tangent.

This centered product layer has a datum-finite receiver speed.  Put
\(\psi_a=(f_a-\bar f_a)^2\).  The loss in (TL.1265) has the exact
decomposition

\[
 \mathfrak D_a
 =M_0\int k^2\psi_a e\,dk
  +{M_2\over M_0}\mathfrak V_a
 \ge M_0\int k^2\psi_a e\,dk .
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz, (TL.1234), and the unweighted product estimate (TL.1003)
therefore give

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 |\mathfrak S_a|^2
 &\le
 M_0^2
 \left(\int\psi_a{T^2\over k^2e}\,dk\right)
 \left(\int\psi_a k^2e\,dk\right)\\
 &\le C M_0H E_1\,\mathfrak D_a .
 \end{aligned}
\]

Hence, uniformly along every measurable heat-scale path,

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T
 {|\mathfrak S_{a(t)}(t)|^2
  \over M_0(t)H(t)\mathfrak D_{a(t)}(t)}\,dt
 \le {C M_0(0)\over\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1267}
\]

All zero denominators use the zero convention.  For a fixed heat face,
putting \(v_a=\mathfrak S_a/(2\nu\mathfrak D_a)\) in (TL.1264) gives

\[
 [\mathfrak V_a']_+
 \le {|\mathfrak S_a|^2\over8\nu\mathfrak D_a}.
 \tag{TL.1268}
\]

Equations (TL.1263)--(TL.1268) replace the isolated-layer receiver by the
coarse--fine heat-profile receiver of the one product history.  Its
unweighted speed is datum-paid, its loss already contains both simultaneous
frequencies, and its leading ultraviolet source has cancelled.  A moving
choice of \(a\) still carries the stock connection
\(a'\partial_a\mathfrak V_a\); that term is cancelled only on the complete
translated q-complex, exactly as in (TL.1252), before the single critical
factor \(a^{-1/2}\) is applied.

## 152. The translated product profiles give one critically valued q-row

Fix \(q>1\), write \(L=\log q\), and use the translated frequencies from
(TL.920).  Set

\[
 K_j(r)=e^rq^j,\qquad a_j(r)=K_j(r)^{-2},
\]

and, initially on a finite ladder,

\[
 \mathcal V_q(r,t):=\sum_jK_j(r)\mathfrak V_{a_j(r)}(t),
 \quad
 \mathcal D_q(r,t):=\sum_jK_j(r)\mathfrak D_{a_j(r)}(t),
\]

\[
 \mathcal S_q(r,t):=\sum_jK_j(r)\mathfrak S_{a_j(r)}(t).
\]

For fixed \(r\) every \(a_j(r)\) is fixed in material time.  Hence
(TL.1264) sums before any positive part to

\[
 \boxed{\partial_t\mathcal V_q+2\nu\mathcal D_q=\mathcal S_q.}
 \tag{TL.1269}
\]

The scale valuation in (TL.1269) occurs exactly once.  As \(r\) ranges over
one translated cell, the collection \(K_j(r)\) covers \((0,\infty)\) once
in logarithmic measure.  With \(a=K^{-2}\),
\(K\,dK/K=\frac12a^{-3/2}da\).  Therefore monotone convergence and
(TL.1263)--(TL.1265) give the exact translation averages

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak B_0(t)
 &:=\int_0^L\mathcal V_q(r,t)\,dr
 =\frac14\iint\mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda,\\
 \mathfrak B_1(t)
 &:=\int_0^L\mathcal D_q(r,t)\,dr\\
 &=\frac14\iint(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)
 \mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)
 e(\kappa)e(\lambda)\,d\kappa d\lambda .
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1270}
\]

Writing

\[
 d\omega_t(\kappa,\lambda)
 :=\frac12(\kappa-\lambda)^2
 e(\kappa,t)e(\lambda,t)\,d\kappa d\lambda,
\]

the heat-profile equivalence (TL.1114) becomes

\[
 \mathfrak B_0\simeq
 \int{1\over\kappa+\lambda}\,d\omega_t,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak B_1\simeq
 \int{\kappa^2+\lambda^2\over\kappa+\lambda}\,d\omega_t.
 \tag{TL.1271}
\]

Thus the lower determinant lies exactly between the translated product stock
and its simultaneous loss.  Since
\(\kappa^2+\lambda^2\ge(\kappa+\lambda)^2/2\), Cauchy--Schwarz on the one
measure \(d\omega_t\) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \Delta_0(t)^2
 =\left(\int d\omega_t\right)^2
 \le C\,\mathfrak B_0(t)\mathfrak B_1(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1272}
\]

Moreover
\((\kappa-\lambda)^2/(\kappa+\lambda)\le\kappa+\lambda\), so

\[
 \boxed{
 0\le\mathfrak B_0(t)\le C M_0(t)H(t),
 \qquad
 \int_0^T\mathfrak B_0(t)^2\,dt
 \le {C M_0(0)^4\over\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1273}
\]

Finally, integrating (TL.1269) over the translation gives the exact adjacent
row

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak B_0'+2\nu\mathfrak B_1=\mathfrak S_B,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak S_B:=\int_0^L\mathcal S_q(r,t)\,dr .
 }
 \tag{TL.1274}
\]

This is the corrected Sobolev-tower placement.  The critical q-factor turns
the bounded heat-profile variance into \(\mathfrak B_0\); the same factor
turns its product loss into the adjacent row \(\mathfrak B_1\); and
\(\Delta_0\) is their interpolation, not an independently weighted shell
quantity.

## 153. The q-summed product source has one centered state-aligned action

For the centered heat profile in Section 151 define

\[
 \mathfrak X_a(t)
 :=\int_0^\infty
 (f_a(k)-\bar f_a)^2\,{T(k,t)^2\over k^2e(k,t)}\,dk ,
 \tag{TL.1275}
\]

with the zero convention on \(ke=0\).  The calculation preceding
(TL.1267) is the pointwise joined estimate

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathfrak S_a|^2
 \le M_0\,\mathfrak D_a\,\mathfrak X_a .
 }
 \tag{TL.1276}
\]

For one translated q-grid put

\[
 \mathcal X_q(r,t):=\sum_jK_j(r)\mathfrak X_{a_j(r)}(t),
 \qquad
 \mathfrak X_B(t):=\int_0^L\mathcal X_q(r,t)\,dr.
\]

Cauchy--Schwarz first in the joined \(j\)-sum and then in the translation
variable gives

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathfrak S_B|^2
 \le M_0\,\mathfrak B_1\,\mathfrak X_B .
 }
 \tag{TL.1277}
\]

The remaining action retains the centered scale distance exactly.  Under
the kinetic probability \(d\mu(\lambda)=e(\lambda)d\lambda/M_0\), Jensen
gives

\[
 (f_a(k)-\bar f_a)^2
 \le\int(f_a(k)-f_a(\lambda))^2\,d\mu(\lambda).
\]

The same translation calculation as (TL.1270) therefore proves

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak X_B
 \le {1\over2M_0}\iint
 \mathfrak d_\nu(k,\lambda)e(\lambda)
 {T(k)^2\over k^2e(k)}\,d\lambda dk .
 }
 \tag{TL.1278}
\]

No uncentered critical source norm has yet been inserted in (TL.1278).
The heat metric vanishes when the occupied scale \(k\) coincides with its
same-field partner \(\lambda\), exactly as the product stock and loss do.
Using only the coarse inequality in (TL.1114) would give

\[
 \mathfrak X_B
 \le C\int_0^\infty
 (k+m_0){T(k)^2\over k^2e(k)}\,dk,
 \tag{TL.1279}
\]

which restores the free \(\dot H^{-1/2}\) source square and is not used in
the receiver.

Instead combine (TL.1274) and (TL.1277) before any such enlargement:

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak B_0'
 +\nu\mathfrak B_1
 \le {M_0\over4\nu}\mathfrak X_B .
 }
 \tag{TL.1280}
\]

Thus the q-adic contraction has been reduced to a matched pair of integrals
over the same heat-profile distance:

\[
 \mathfrak B_1
 ={1\over4}\iint(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)
 \mathfrak d_\nu(\kappa,\lambda)e(\kappa)e(\lambda),
\]

while \(\mathfrak X_B\) is bounded by (TL.1278).  The next operation is made
inside this common \((k,\lambda)\) measure: insert the complete VPI
state-aligned rate \(T(k)/(k e(k))\), retain its signed q-current origin, and
split its slow part into the datum-paid action (TL.1267) while its fast part
stays against the simultaneous factor \(k^2+\lambda^2\) in
\(\mathfrak B_1\).

## 154. Slow product motion is absorbed inside the same receiver

Introduce only the shorthand

\[
 d\eta_t(k,\lambda)
 :={1\over4}\mathfrak d_\nu(k,\lambda)
 e(k,t)e(\lambda,t)\,dkd\lambda,
 \qquad
 \gamma(k,t):={T(k,t)\over k e(k,t)}.
 \tag{TL.1281}
\]

Then (TL.1270) and (TL.1278) read

\[
 \mathfrak B_0=\int d\eta_t,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak B_1=\int(k^2+\lambda^2)\,d\eta_t,
 \qquad
 M_0\mathfrak X_B\le2\int\gamma(k)^2\,d\eta_t.
 \tag{TL.1282}
\]

This puts the source rate and the simultaneous product loss on one positive
measure.  Split that measure by the actual same-state inequality

\[
 \mathcal F_t
 :=\{(k,\lambda):
 |\gamma(k,t)|>\nu\sqrt{k^2+\lambda^2}\}.
 \tag{TL.1283}
\]

No possible fluid history has been added: \(\mathcal F_t\) is simply the
portion of the already completed product current whose state-aligned
fractional rate exceeds its simultaneous product scale.  On its complement,
(TL.1282) gives

\[
 {M_0\over4\nu}\mathfrak X_B
 \le{\nu\over2}\mathfrak B_1
 +
{1\over2\nu}\int_{\mathcal F_t}\gamma(k)^2\,d\eta_t .
\]

Substitution in (TL.1280) therefore proves the exact slow/fast receiver

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak B_0'
 +{\nu\over2}\mathfrak B_1
 \le {1\over2\nu}
 \int_{\mathcal F_t}\gamma(k,t)^2\,d\eta_t(k,\lambda).
 }
 \tag{TL.1284}
\]

The slow part has disappeared into the same positive loss; it is not an
external damping estimate.  On the surviving set in (TL.1283),

\[
 \gamma^2
 \le{\gamma^4\over\nu^2(k^2+\lambda^2)},
\]

so (TL.1284) also has the fourth-action form

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak B_0'
 +{\nu\over2}\mathfrak B_1
 \le {1\over2\nu^3}
 \int_{\mathcal F_t}
 {\gamma(k,t)^4\over k^2+\lambda^2}\,d\eta_t .
 }
 \tag{TL.1285}
\]

Equation (TL.1285) explains the appearance of a fourth temporal power
without inventing an exponent parity: the fourth power is produced by
restricting the *quadratic* complete-current action to the portion whose
rate has already crossed its simultaneous quadratic scale loss.  The
fourth-action is not yet the paid \(L_t^4L_{\rm pair}^2\) history:
\(\gamma\) is a conditional shell rate, whereas (TL.996) is the fourth
temporal power of the complete reciprocal-pair \(L^2\) speed.  The
first-receiving integral must be formed before dividing by a small receiving
population; otherwise the two fourth powers are not lawfully identified.

## 155. First reception is integrated before conditional rates are formed

The distinction at the end of Section 154 is necessary even for a finite
smooth triad.  If two occupied input modes have fixed amplitude and a
receiving mode has amplitude \(\varepsilon\), then generically

\[
 e_{\rm rec}\simeq\varepsilon^2,
 \qquad T_{\rm rec}\simeq\varepsilon,
 \qquad |\gamma_{\rm rec}|\simeq\varepsilon^{-1}.
\]

The contribution of that mode to the conditional fourth action in
(TL.1285) can therefore grow like \(\varepsilon^{-2}\), while the complete
critical height and the reciprocal-pair \(L^2\) speed remain bounded.  This
does not describe a blowup scenario; it is the ordinary birth of a
previously small receiving component.  It proves that (TL.1285) cannot be
majorized directly by (TL.996).

The birth-safe quantity is the quadratic product action in (TL.1267).
Let \(J=[s,t]\) be any interval on which one fixed product profile is read,
write

\[
 \delta_a(J):=[\mathfrak V_a(t)-\mathfrak V_a(s)]_+,
 \qquad
 D_a(J):=\int_J\mathfrak D_a(r)\,dr,
\]

and suppose \(H(r)\le h_+\) on \(J\).  Integrating (TL.1264) and applying
Cauchy--Schwarz with the actual loss gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \int_J{\mathfrak S_a^2\over\mathfrak D_a}\,dr
 &\ge
 {(\delta_a(J)+2\nu D_a(J))^2\over D_a(J)}\\
 &\ge8\nu\,\delta_a(J).
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1286}
\]

The inequality is understood by continuity when \(D_a(J)=0\).  Since
\(M_0(r)\le M_0(0)\), it follows that

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_J
 {\mathfrak S_a^2\over M_0H\mathfrak D_a}\,dr
 \ge {8\nu\over M_0(0)h_+}\,\delta_a(J).
 }
 \tag{TL.1287}
\]

Now take pairwise disjoint height slabs \(J_n\), choose one fixed heat depth
\(a_n\) on each slab, and define the measurable path \(a(t)=a_n\) there.
The path-uniform upper bound (TL.1267) and (TL.1287) yield the datum-finite
first-reception packing

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_n{\delta_{a_n}(J_n)\over h_{n,+}}
 \le {C M_0(0)^2\over\nu^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1288}
\]

Thus arbitrarily small receiving populations cause no singular quotient:
their complete source is integrated through their actual stock increase and
simultaneous loss.  Equation (TL.1288) is the unweighted half of the q-adic
receiver.  The critical half is not another fourth-power estimate; it is the
single factor \(K_n=a_n^{-1/2}\) multiplying the same newly received product
stock.  That factor must now be identified with the reciprocal distance
crossed by the positive VPI activity which realizes the profile reception.

## 156. The critical product profile is an actual reciprocal-pair speed layer

The product profile in Section 151 is bounded by the Gaussian increment layer
of the same field.  Since \(0\le A_{2a}\le A_a\le M_0\),

\[
 \mathfrak V_a
 =M_0A_{2a}-A_a^2
 \le M_0(M_0-A_{2a})
 ={M_0\over2}\mathfrak q_{2a}.
 \tag{TL.1289}
\]

Let \(K=a^{-1/2}\).  The material formula (TL.738) and Kelvin inversion
\(Y_{ab}=R_{ab}/|R_{ab}|^2\) give

\[
 |\dot Y_{ab}|^2={|V_{ab}|^2\over|R_{ab}|^4}.
\]

Consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 K\mathfrak q_{2a}(t)
 =\iint
 \chi\!\left({|R_{ab}(t)|\over\sqrt a}\right)
 |\dot Y_{ab}(t)|^2\,dadb,
 }
 \tag{TL.1290}
\]

where the fixed nonnegative profile is

\[
 \chi(s):=(8\pi)^{-3/2}s^4e^{-s^2/8}.
\]

Indeed,
\(K g_{2a}(R)|V|^2
=(8\pi)^{-3/2}a^{-2}e^{-|R|^2/(8a)}|V|^2
=\chi(|R|/\sqrt a)|\dot Y|^2\).
Combining (TL.1289)--(TL.1290) yields

\[
 \boxed{
 {K\mathfrak V_a(t)\over M_0(t)}
 \le {1\over2}\iint
 \chi\!\left({|R_{ab}(t)|\over\sqrt a}\right)
 |\dot Y_{ab}(t)|^2\,dadb
 \le C H(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1291}
\]

Thus the critical factor in the q-product receiver is not an imposed
frequency price.  It is exactly the reciprocal-speed participation of the
same material pairs in the physical separation collar
\(|R_{ab}|\simeq\sqrt a\).  Translation of the q-grid merely partitions the
fixed profile \(\chi\); it does not change the pair population.

The surrounding energy rows immediately give the uniform occupancy estimate

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T
 \left({K\mathfrak V_a(t)\over M_0(t)}\right)^2dt
 \le {C M_0(0)^2\over\nu}
 }
 \tag{TL.1292}
\]

for every fixed \(a>0\), and pointwise for any measurable scale choice.
The q-adic first-receiving calculation must now keep (TL.1288) and
(TL.1290) on the same labelled passage: the former pays creation of product
stock, while the latter identifies the critical value of that stock with the
actual reciprocal-speed layer whose complete center--separation continuity
law is (TL.1027).

## 157. Adjacent product profiles have a datum-paid q-scale connection

The same product stock has a Hilbert-space form which makes its scale
connection exact.  In the real \(L^2\) Hilbert structure let

\[
 \Omega_a(t):=u(t)\wedge E_a u(t)
\]

in the exterior square.  Its norm satisfies

\[
 \boxed{\|\Omega_a(t)\|_{\wedge^2L^2}^2=4\mathfrak V_a(t).}
 \tag{TL.1293}
\]

Indeed,
\(\|u\wedge E_au\|^2
=\|u\|_2^2\|E_au\|_2^2-\langle u,E_au\rangle^2\), and the three factors
are \(2M_0\), \(2A_{2a}\), and \(2A_a\).

On the translated q-grid of Section 152 write

\[
 \Omega_j:=\Omega_{a_j},
 \qquad
 \delta_j(\Lambda):=E_{a_{j+1}}-E_{a_j}\ge0.
\]

Then the common field \(u\) gives the exact scale connection

\[
 \boxed{
 \Omega_{j+1}-\Omega_j=u\wedge\delta_j(\Lambda)u.
 }
 \tag{TL.1294}
\]

There is no independent connection stock.  Spectral scaling of

\[
 \delta_j(k)
 =e^{-(k/(qK_j))^2}-e^{-(k/K_j)^2}
\]

gives, for every \(0<s\le1\),

\[
 \sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}K_j^{2s}|\delta_j(k)|^2
 \le C_{q,s}k^{2s}.
\]

Plancherel and \(\|x\wedge y\|\le\|x\|\|y\|\) therefore prove

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_jK_j^{2s}\|\Omega_{j+1}-\Omega_j\|^2
 \le4C_{q,s}M_0E_s.
 }
 \tag{TL.1295}
\]

The two exterior scale faces vanish:
\(\Omega_j\to0\) as \(j\to-\infty\) because \(E_{a_j}u\to0\), and
\(\Omega_j\to u\wedge u=0\) as \(j\to+\infty\).  Weighted discrete Hardy
from the fine face consequently yields

\[
 \boxed{
 K_j^{2s}\mathfrak V_{a_j}
 \le C_{q,s}M_0E_s
 \qquad(0<s\le1).
 }
 \tag{TL.1296}
\]

At \(s=\frac12\), (TL.1296) is the scale-connection proof of the critical
occupancy in (TL.1291).  At \(s=1\), the ordinary energy identity gives the
strictly stronger datum-finite spacetime connection

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\sum_jK_j^2
 \|\Omega_{j+1}(t)-\Omega_j(t)\|^2dt
 \le {C_qM_0(0)^2\over\nu},
 }
 \tag{TL.1297}
\]

and, in particular,

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T K_j^2\mathfrak V_{a_j}(t)\,dt
 \le {C_qM_0(0)^2\over\nu}
 \quad\hbox{uniformly in }j\hbox{ and the grid translation}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1298}
\]

Equation (TL.1297) is a scale-regularity estimate for the simultaneous
stocks \(\Omega_j(t)\).  It is not, by itself, a physical transport law in
the q-direction: changing the heat test from \(a_j\) to \(a_{j+1}\) changes
the readout of the same instantaneous spectrum.  Consequently it cannot be
used as an alternative payer for temporal reception in (TL.1288).  The
actual scale-transfer current remains the signed product current in
(TL.1193).  The next q-time cancellation must therefore be formed with that
current before (TL.1297) is used as stock regularity.

## 158. The first-receiving q-complex is carried by the product VPI current

Let

\[
 f_a(k):=e^{-ak^2},
 \qquad
 w_a(\kappa,\lambda)
 :={1\over2}\bigl(f_a(\kappa)-f_a(\lambda)\bigr)^2.
\]

With \(\varpi\) and \(\mathbf J_\varpi\) from (TL.1192), the product stock
and its simultaneous loss are exactly

\[
 \mathfrak V_a=\iint w_a\varpi\,d\kappa d\lambda,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak D_a
 =\iint(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)w_a\varpi\,d\kappa d\lambda.
 \tag{TL.1299}
\]

Testing the positive killed continuity equation (TL.1193), before taking
any sign, gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak V_a'+2\nu\mathfrak D_a
 =\iint\nabla_{\kappa,\lambda}w_a\cdot
       \mathbf J_\varpi\,d\kappa d\lambda
 =\mathfrak S_a .
 }
 \tag{TL.1300}
\]

Thus the centered source in (TL.1266) is not an independent Gaussian
source.  It is the signed work of the complete two-coordinate current
through the one relative heat-profile test.  Explicitly,

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \nabla w_a\cdot\mathbf J_\varpi
 =2a(f_a(\kappa)-f_a(\lambda))
 \bigl[&-\kappa f_a(\kappa)\Pi(\kappa)e(\lambda)\\
       &+\lambda f_a(\lambda)e(\kappa)\Pi(\lambda)\bigr].
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1301}
\]

On the translated q-grid let \(w_j=w_{a_j}\).  For every finitely
supported sequence \(\eta_j(t)\), put

\[
 \sigma_j:=\eta_{j-1}-\eta_j.
\]

The algebraic identity

\[
 \sum_j\sigma_jw_j
 =\sum_j\eta_j(w_{j+1}-w_j)
\]

and (TL.1300) give, distributionally in time,

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 {d\over dt}\sum_j\sigma_j\mathfrak V_{a_j}
 +2\nu\sum_j\sigma_j\mathfrak D_{a_j}
 ={}&\iint\nabla\!\left[
       \sum_j\eta_j(w_{j+1}-w_j)
      \right]\cdot\mathbf J_\varpi\,d\kappa d\lambda\\
 &+\sum_j\sigma_j'\mathfrak V_{a_j}.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1302}
\]

This is the lawful q-time first-receiving identity.  Every internal q-face
has cancelled inside the single signed test difference
\(w_{j+1}-w_j\); the final term is the actual temporal boundary swept by a
moving first-receiving region.  No norm of
\(\Omega_{j+1}-\Omega_j\) has been substituted for either term.

For comparison, the Gram relation (TL.1294) implies only the simultaneous
stock identity

\[
 \mathfrak V_{a_{j+1}}-\mathfrak V_{a_j}
 ={1\over4}
 \left\langle
  \Omega_{j+1}+\Omega_j,\Omega_{j+1}-\Omega_j
 \right\rangle .
 \tag{TL.1303}
\]

It can control the regularity of the boundary stock in (TL.1302), through
(TL.1297), but it cannot replace the current work in its first line.  The
single remaining contraction is therefore fixed without ambiguity: apply
the critical q-valuation to the signed current test in (TL.1302), keep its
simultaneous product loss, and use the Gram estimate only on the surviving
boundary stock after all common faces have cancelled.

## 159. Product reception has a scale-coercive simultaneous loss

The simultaneous loss in (TL.1299) already strengthens as the selected
heat scale decreases.  Put

\[
 x:=a(\kappa^2+\lambda^2).
\]

The elementary heat-profile estimate

\[
 w_a(\kappa,\lambda)
 \le {a^2\over2}(\kappa^2-\lambda^2)^2
 \le {x^2\over2}
 \tag{TL.1304}
\]

converts this observation into a nonlinear coercivity with no spectral
localization assumption.  If \(\mathfrak V_a>0\), set

\[
 R_a:={\sqrt{\mathfrak V_a}\over M_0}.
\]

Because \(w_a\le1/2\), one has \(0<R_a\le1/\sqrt2\).  Splitting the product
carrier into \(\{x\le R_a\}\) and \(\{x>R_a\}\), (TL.1304) gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak V_a
 &\le {R_a^2\over2}\iint\varpi
     +{1\over R_a}\iint xw_a\varpi\\
 &= {\mathfrak V_a\over2}
    +{a\over R_a}\mathfrak D_a.
 \end{aligned}
\]

Hence, with \(K=a^{-1/2}\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak D_a
 \ge {\mathfrak V_a^{3/2}\over2aM_0}
 ={K^2\over2M_0}\mathfrak V_a^{3/2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1305}
\]

This is the exact mathematical form of the finite-lifetime statement for
the product receiver: concentrating a fixed amount of coarse--fine product
stock into a smaller heat scale necessarily increases the simultaneous
loss of that same stock.  It does not compare two mechanisms or two fluid
histories.

The bound is uniform along a scale path.  Since \(w_a\le1/2\),

\[
 \mathfrak D_a
 \le {1\over2}\iint(\kappa^2+\lambda^2)\varpi
 =M_0M_2.
\]

The kinetic-energy identity \(M_0'=-2\nu M_2\) therefore implies, for every
measurable \(a(t)>0\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T
 {K(t)^2\mathfrak V_{a(t)}(t)^{3/2}\over M_0(t)}\,dt
 \le2\int_0^T\mathfrak D_{a(t)}(t)\,dt
 \le{M_0(0)^2-M_0(T)^2\over2\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1306}
\]

Thus neither a preselected scale nor a moving first-receiving scale can
evade the datum-finite loss by making its heat-profile population small.
Writing the critical stock as

\[
 Z_a:={K\mathfrak V_a\over M_0},
\]

(TL.1306) is equivalently

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T
 (M_0K)^{1/2}Z_{a(t)}^{3/2}\,dt
 \le {M_0(0)^2\over2\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1307}
\]

Equations (TL.1287) and (TL.1305) now keep the two physical facts on one
receiver: creation of \(\mathfrak V_a\) is paid through its complete signed
current and simultaneous loss, while residence of the critically valued
stock is paid increasingly strongly as \(K\) rises.  The q-time descent in
(TL.1302) must combine these two terms before selecting its positive
first-receiving boundary; neither term alone supplies that boundary value.

## 160. A far-ultraviolet critical tail is a definite part of the same product receiver

The product profile does not lose a small receiving population merely
because that population is remote from the kinetic mean.  For \(K>0\), set

\[
 a:=K^{-2},
 \qquad
 L_K:=\int_0^{K/2}e(k)\,dk,
 \qquad
 U_K:=\int_{2K}^{\infty}e(k)\,dk,
\]

and put

\[
 c_*:=\bigl(e^{-1/4}-e^{-4}\bigr)^2>0.
\]

On every cross pair \(\kappa\le K/2\), \(\lambda\ge2K\),

\[
 |f_a(\kappa)-f_a(\lambda)|
 \ge e^{-1/4}-e^{-4}.
\]

Keeping both orientations of this one product pair in (TL.1299) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak V_{K^{-2}}\ge c_*L_KU_K,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak D_{K^{-2}}\ge4c_*K^2L_KU_K.
 }
 \tag{TL.1308}
\]

Let \(m_0=H/M_0\).  If \(K\ge4m_0\), Markov's inequality on the same
kinetic measure gives

\[
 \int_{K/2}^{\infty}e(k)\,dk
 \le{2H\over K}\le{M_0\over2},
\]

and hence \(L_K\ge M_0/2\).  Therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 {K\mathfrak V_{K^{-2}}\over M_0}
 \ge {c_*\over2}KU_K,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak D_{K^{-2}}
 \ge2c_*M_0K^2U_K.
 }
 \tag{TL.1309}
\]

In particular, if the actual far tail carries a fraction \(\alpha\) of the
critical height in the form

\[
 2KU_K\ge\alpha H,
\]

then the same selected product receiver satisfies

\[
 \boxed{
 {K\mathfrak V_{K^{-2}}\over M_0}
 \ge {c_*\alpha\over4}H,
 \qquad
 \mathfrak D_{K^{-2}}
 \ge c_*\alpha M_0KH
 \ge4c_*\alpha H^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1310}
\]

Thus the remote population and the surrounding coarse field are not two
separately normalized objects: their product stock and loss are already a
single positive portion of (TL.1193).

The current entering that tail is equally path-uniform.  On the translated
kinetic q-grid of (TL.920), Cauchy--Schwarz in the exact cumulative identity
\(\Pi_j=\sum_{m\ge j}s_m\) gives

\[
 {\Pi_j^2\over\mathscr D_j^\uparrow}
 \le\sum_{m\ge j}{s_m^2\over D_m}
 \le{1\over\nu}\|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2.
 \tag{TL.1311}
\]

Consequently, for every measurable choice of a q-face \(j(t)\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T
 {\Pi_{j(t)}(t)^2
  \over H(t)\mathscr D_{j(t)}^\uparrow(t)}\,dt
 \le {C M_0(0)\over\nu^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1312}
\]

Here (TL.1003) and the energy identity were used only after the signed tail
faces had collapsed to \(\Pi_j\).  If a fixed tail face is read on an
interval \(J=[s,t]\), its exact receiver law (TL.999) also gives, with

\[
 \delta_j(J):=
 [\mathscr E_j^\uparrow(t)-\mathscr E_j^\uparrow(s)]_+,
 \qquad
 D_j^\uparrow(J):=\int_J\mathscr D_j^\uparrow(r)\,dr,
\]

the birth-safe lower action

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_J{\Pi_j^2\over\mathscr D_j^\uparrow}\,dr
 \ge
 {\bigl(\delta_j(J)+D_j^\uparrow(J)\bigr)^2
  \over D_j^\uparrow(J)}
 \ge4\delta_j(J).
 }
 \tag{TL.1313}
\]

Equations (TL.1309)--(TL.1313) are the exact far-tail first-reception
splice.  They pay the creation and simultaneous loss of the remote tail on
the same cumulative signed current.  The single uncontracted quantity is
now explicit: a critical first reception multiplies the tail increase by
its own crossed face \(K\).  That factor must be retained from the signed
q-face travel in the full first-receiving complex; neither the unweighted
path action (TL.1312) nor the residence estimate (TL.1310) is to be counted
as that travel twice.

## 161. Critical weighting of a moving heat-row component retains its sweep generator

The higher heat rows can be returned to the corrected kinetic log-scale law
without the second critical valuation used in Sections 87--90.  Retain
\(\varepsilon,\pi,d\) from (TL.974), so that

\[
 \partial_t\varepsilon+\partial_x\pi=-d,
 \qquad
 H=\int_{\mathbb R}e^x\varepsilon(x)\,dx.
\]

Let \(\eta=\eta_n\) be one positive normalized row kernel from (TL.976),
with

\[
 m_n=\int_{\mathbb R}e^s\eta(s)\,ds={1\over c_n},
\]

and let \(K_*\) be any fixed signed row kernel with finite exponential
moment.  For a fixed \(m_*>0\), define

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 A&:=\eta*\varepsilon,&F_A&:=\eta*\pi,\\
 B&:=K_* *\varepsilon,&F_B&:=K_* *\pi,\\
 L&:=K_*-m_*\eta,&Z&:=B-m_*A,\\
 C&:=F_B-m_*F_A.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1314}
\]

Then the one kinetic continuity law gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \partial_tZ+\partial_xC=-L*d.
 }
 \tag{TL.1315}
\]

Suppose \((c(t),\infty)\) is a smooth ultraviolet component on which
\(Z\ge0\), with \(Z(c(t),t)=0\).  The unweighted connection stock

\[
 S_0(t):=\int_{c(t)}^\infty Z(x,t)\,dx
\]

obeys the familiar boundary identity

\[
 C(c(t),t)
 =S_0'(t)+\int_{c(t)}^\infty(L*d)(x,t)\,dx.
 \tag{TL.1316}
\]

The critical valuation is not the same identity with an exterior factor
attached.  For

\[
 S_1(t):=\int_{c(t)}^\infty e^xZ(x,t)\,dx,
\]

integration by parts in (TL.1315) gives exactly

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 e^{c(t)}C(c(t),t)
 ={}&S_1'(t)
 -\int_{c(t)}^\infty e^xC(x,t)\,dx\\
 &+\int_{c(t)}^\infty e^x(L*d)(x,t)\,dx.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1317}
\]

The middle term is the critical sweep generator.  It is present even though
the moving-face term \(c'Z(c,t)\) vanishes.  For a homothetically translating
readout it cancels the critical growth of \(S_1\); omitting it converts a
coordinate sweep into a false contraction.

On the complete scale line the generator is an exact VPI moment.  Put

\[
 \ell_1:=\int_{\mathbb R}e^sL(s)\,ds.
\]

Fubini and (TL.927) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathbb R}e^xC(x,t)\,dx
 =\ell_1\int_{\mathbb R}e^y\pi(y,t)\,dy
 =\ell_1P_H(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1318}
\]

Thus the sweep generator is not an independent heat-row obstruction.  By
the energy-direction cancellation (TL.1044)--(TL.1045),

\[
 \boxed{
 \int e^xC\,dx
 =-{\ell_1\over\nu}
 \operatorname{Re}\langle
  \Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u),
  \nu\Lambda^{3/2}u-\nu m_H\Lambda^{1/2}u
 \rangle,
 }
 \tag{TL.1319}
\]

and consequently

\[
 \boxed{
 \left|\int e^xC\,dx\right|^2
 \le2\ell_1^2H\sigma_H^2
 \|\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1320}
\]

In particular, common scale motion cannot strengthen the critical height
without the centered spectral breadth of the same field.  This is the exact
VPI correction to a freely translating scalar spectrum.

There is also a sharp structural reason the generator cannot be deleted by
choosing a more concentrated row kernel.  If a proposed boundary contraction
satisfies

\[
 \Theta_*:=\int e^s|K_*(s)|\,ds<m_*m_n,
\]

then

\[
 \ell_1
 =\int e^sK_*(s)\,ds-m_*m_n<0.
 \tag{TL.1321}
\]

Conversely, \(\ell_1=0\) forces
\(\Theta_*\ge m_*m_n\) by the triangle inequality and removes the strict
contraction.  Hence the lawful higher-row descent has exactly two joined
parts: concentration of the row kernel contracts the non-sweep boundary,
while (TL.1319) returns the surviving critical sweep to the centered VPI
product geometry.  Equations (TL.1270)--(TL.1284) and
(TL.1305)--(TL.1313), rather than another convolution estimate, are the
available receiver and loss for that second part.

## 162. Height normalization cancels the common sweep and leaves a positive three-scale loss

The sweep in (TL.1319) is the common critical dilation of the same lower
Sobolev determinant already carried by (TL.1161).  It can be cancelled
without estimating \(P_H\).  At a nonzero state retain

\[
 m_0={H\over M_0},
 \qquad
 m_H={M_2\over H},
 \qquad
 \boxed{
 \mathscr W_0
 :={\Delta_0\over2H}
 ={M_0\over2}(m_H-m_0)\ge0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1322}
\]

The first variation of the determinant stock with respect to the kinetic
measure is

\[
 q_0(k)
 :=\int_0^\infty(k-\lambda)^2e(\lambda)\,d\lambda
 =M_0k^2-2Hk+M_2.
\]

It has the exact two-mean decomposition

\[
 \boxed{
 q_0(k)
 ={\Delta_0\over H}k
 +M_0(k-m_0)(k-m_H).
 }
 \tag{TL.1323}
\]

Because \(N_0=0\), the determinant source is
\(\mathscr P_0=\int q_0T\).  Hence (TL.1323) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr P_0
 ={\Delta_0\over H}P_H
 +M_0\int_0^\infty
 (k-m_0)(k-m_H)T(k)\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1324}
\]

Differentiate (TL.1322), use
\(\Delta_0'+2\nu\mathscr D_0=\mathscr P_0\) and
\(H'+2\nu M_3=P_H\), and substitute (TL.1324).  The complete \(P_H\)
direction cancels identically:

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr W_0'
 +{\nu\over H}\mathscr L_0
 ={M_0\over2H}
 \int_0^\infty(k-m_0)(k-m_H)T(k)\,dk,
 }
 \tag{TL.1325}
\]

where

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr L_0
 &:=\mathscr D_0-{M_3\over H}\Delta_0\\
 &=M_0\int_0^\infty
 k^2(k-m_0)(k-m_H)e(k)\,dk\ge0.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1326}
\]

The final sign in (TL.1326) is a genuine three-scale inequality, not the
pointwise sign of \((k-m_0)(k-m_H)\).  To prove it, set

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal K(a,b,c):={}&
 a(b^2+c^2-a^2)(b-c)^2\\
 &+b(a^2+c^2-b^2)(a-c)^2\\
 &+c(a^2+b^2-c^2)(a-b)^2.
 \end{aligned}
\]

Symmetrizing the three dummy frequency variables gives exactly

\[
 \boxed{
 H\mathscr L_0
 ={1\over6}\iiint_{(0,\infty)^3}
 \mathcal K(a,b,c)e(a)e(b)e(c)\,dadbdc.
 }
 \tag{TL.1327}
\]

Now order \(0\le a\le b\le c\).  If
\(c^2\le a^2+b^2\), all three coefficients in \(\mathcal K\) are
nonnegative.  If \(c^2>a^2+b^2\), only the last coefficient is negative,
and the middle term alone dominates it.  For \(a>0\), writing
\(A=b/a\) and \(B=c/a\), this is the elementary inequality

\[
 {A\over B}
 {1+B^2-A^2\over B^2-1-A^2}
 \left({B-1\over A-1}\right)^2\ge1,
\]

because the middle fraction exceeds one and

\[
 A(B-1)^2-B(A-1)^2=(B-A)(AB-1)\ge0.
\]

The case \(a=0\) follows directly or by continuity.  Therefore
\(\mathcal K\ge0\), proving (TL.1326).

Finally, the residual source in (TL.1325) is already a signed q-face
current.  Since \(T=-\partial_k\Pi\), integration by parts after the smooth
coarse and fine limits gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr W_0'
 ={M_0\over2H}
 \left[
 \int_0^\infty(2k-m_0-m_H)\Pi(k)\,dk
 -2\nu\int_0^\infty
 k^2(k-m_0)(k-m_H)e(k)\,dk
 \right].
 }
 \tag{TL.1328}
\]

Thus the interior generator exposed in Section 161 is no longer an
uncontracted copy of the target current.  Height normalization cancels the
common dilation exactly.  What remains is one signed transfer measured from
both adjacent Sobolev mean scales, together with the nonnegative simultaneous
three-scale loss generated by that same displacement.  The next q-adic
operation is correspondingly narrower: contract the two-mean face current in
(TL.1328) against \(\mathscr L_0\), retaining the far-tail admission of
Section 160 and the comparable-scale product current before any positive
first reception is selected.

## 163. Every adjacent Sobolev gap has the same sweep-free VPI law

The cancellation in Section 162 is not peculiar to the rows surrounding
\(H=M_1\).  For every integer \(n\ge0\) with \(M_n>0\), define the adjacent
moment means and Hankel gap by

\[
 \boxed{
 m_n:={M_{n+1}\over M_n},
 \qquad
 \mathscr K_n:=M_nM_{n+2}-M_{n+1}^2,
 \qquad
 \mathscr W_n:={\mathscr K_n\over2M_{n+1}}
 ={M_n\over2}(m_{n+1}-m_n)\ge0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1329}
\]

Use the zero convention for the zero solution.  Differentiating with
\(M_j'=N_j-2\nu M_{j+2}\) and cancelling the derivative of the common
denominator gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr W_n'
 +{\nu\over M_{n+1}}\mathscr L_n
 ={M_n\over2M_{n+1}}\mathscr S_n,
 }
 \tag{TL.1330}
\]

where both surviving rows use the same two adjacent mean scales:

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr S_n
 &:=\int_0^\infty
 k^n(k-m_n)(k-m_{n+1})T(k)\,dk,\\
 \mathscr L_n
 &:=M_n\int_0^\infty
 k^{n+2}(k-m_n)(k-m_{n+1})e(k)\,dk\ge0.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1331}
\]

Indeed, the nonlinear numerator left after normalization is

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 &N_nM_{n+2}+M_nN_{n+2}-2M_{n+1}N_{n+1}
 -{\mathscr K_n\over M_{n+1}}N_{n+1}\\
 &\hspace{25mm}=M_n\mathscr S_n,
 \end{aligned}
\]

and the identical calculation with \(N_j\) replaced by \(M_{j+2}\)
produces \(\mathscr L_n\).  Its sign follows from the same pointwise
three-scale kernel as Section 162.  Applying (TL.1327) to the positive
measure \(k^ne(k)\,dk\) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 M_{n+1}\mathscr L_n
 ={1\over6}\iiint
 (abc)^n\mathcal K(a,b,c)
 e(a)e(b)e(c)\,dadbdc\ge0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1332}
\]

The source also remains on the one signed scale current.  Since
\(T=-\partial_k\Pi\),

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr S_n
 =\int_0^\infty
