 \partial_k\!\left[
 k^n(k-m_n)(k-m_{n+1})
 \right]\Pi(k)\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1333}
\]

If

\[
 \mathscr J_n
 :=\int_0^\infty
 k^{n+2}(k-m_n)(k-m_{n+1})e(k)\,dk,
\]

then (TL.1330) has the exact joined source--loss form

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr W_n'
 ={M_n\over2M_{n+1}}
 \bigl(\mathscr S_n-2\nu\mathscr J_n\bigr),
 \qquad
 \mathscr J_n\ge0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1334}
\]

Thus every neighboring pair of Sobolev moments removes its own common
scale motion before the nonlinear tangent is read.  The entire tower is
governed by the same object: a second centered difference of the complete
signed VPI transfer, paired with a nonnegative three-scale loss carrying
the identical two mean factors.  The q-adic packing may therefore descend
through adjacent \(n\)-rows without ever replacing the surviving critical
sweep by an unsigned shell source.

## 164. Positive critical growth enters either the adjacent mean gap or the full far-tail loss

The two-mean law has an exhaustive state-level admission.  Under the
critical probability \(d\pi_H=ke(k)\,dk/H\), put

\[
 \delta_0:=m_H-m_0\ge0.
\]

Because \(\mathbb E_{\pi_H}[k^{-1}]=m_0^{-1}\), one has the exact
arithmetic--harmonic gap identity

\[
 \boxed{
 \delta_0
 =\mathbb E_{\pi_H}
 \left[{(k-m_0)^2\over k}\right],
 \qquad
 \mathscr W_0={M_0\over2}\delta_0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1335}
\]

Fix once and for all \(R\ge16\).  On \(k\le Rm_H\),

\[
 (k-m_H)^2
 \le2(k-m_0)^2+2\delta_0^2
 \le2Rm_H{(k-m_0)^2\over k}+2\delta_0^2.
\]

Since \(0\le\delta_0\le m_H\), splitting the critical variance at
\(Rm_H\) and using (TL.1335) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \sigma_H^2
 \le4Rm_H\delta_0
 +{1\over H}\int_{Rm_H}^\infty k^3e(k)\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1336}
\]

Let \(c_{\rm pg}>0\) be the universal constant in the positive-growth
width law (TL.1046), so that

\[
 H'>0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \sigma_H^2\ge
 {c_{\rm pg}\nu^2m_H^2\over H}.
\]

Combining this with (TL.1336) proves that every positive critical tangent
satisfies at least one of the two alternatives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr W_0\ge {c_{\rm pg}\nu^2\over16R}
 \quad\hbox{or}\quad
 \int_{Rm_H}^\infty k^3e(k)\,dk
 \ge {c_{\rm pg}\nu^2m_H^2\over2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1337}
\]

The second member is already contained in the all-scale product receiver;
no new tail functional is required.  Put \(K_0=Rm_H/4\).  Then
\(K_0\ge4m_0\), so (TL.1309), Tonelli, and
\(\mathfrak B_1=\int_0^\infty\mathfrak D_{K^{-2}}\,dK\) give

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak B_1
 &\ge2c_*M_0\int_{K_0}^\infty
 K^2\int_{2K}^\infty e(k)\,dk\,dK\\
 &\ge {7c_*M_0\over96}
 \int_{4K_0}^\infty k^3e(k)\,dk.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1338}
\]

Here the numerical factor follows from
\(int_{K_0}^{k/2}K^2dK\ge7k^3/192\) for \(k\ge4K_0\).  Therefore the
positive-growth admission is the carrier-native dichotomy

\[
 \boxed{
 H'>0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \mathscr W_0\ge c_R\nu^2
 \quad\hbox{or}\quad
 \mathfrak B_1\ge c_RM_0\nu^2m_H^2,
 }
 \tag{TL.1339}
\]

with \(c_R>0\) depending only on the fixed choice of \(R\).  The first
member is the normalized separation of the two adjacent Sobolev mean scales;
the second is the simultaneous loss of the complete coarse--fine product
receiver integrated over every far receiving scale.  Thus a positive
critical tangent cannot hide simultaneously from both surrounding rows.
The remaining temporal packing now has two exact inputs on the same history:
(TL.1328) governs creation and maintenance of the mean gap, while
(TL.1267), (TL.1302), and (TL.1306) govern creation, transport, and total
loss of the far-tail product receiver.

## 165. The three-scale loss is the lower mean gap times the upper variance plus one successor

The positivity of \(\mathscr L_0\) has an exact adjacent-row factorization.
Under \(d\pi_H=ke(k)\,dk/H\), write
\(X=k\), \(a=m_0\), \(b=m_H\), and
\(\delta_0=b-a\).  Then

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 {\mathscr L_0\over M_0H}
 &=\mathbb E_{\pi_H}[X(X-a)(X-b)]\\
 &=\delta_0\sigma_H^2
   +\mathbb E_{\pi_H}[X(X-b)^2].
 \end{aligned}
\]

Using \(\Delta_0=M_0H\delta_0=2H\mathscr W_0\), this is

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr L_0
 =\Delta_0\sigma_H^2
 +M_0\int_0^\infty
 k^2(k-m_H)^2e(k)\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1340}
\]

Both terms are nonnegative.  In particular,

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr L_0\ge2H\mathscr W_0\sigma_H^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1341}
\]

This proves the sign in (TL.1326) without collapsing its three-scale
meaning: the first term joins the lower adjacent-mean separation to the
upper critical variance, while the second retains the next centered
Sobolev row of the same spectrum.

On a positive critical tangent, (TL.1046) and (TL.1341) give

\[
 \boxed{
 H'>0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 {\nu\over H}\mathscr L_0
 \ge {2c_{\rm pg}\nu^3m_H^2\over H}\mathscr W_0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1342}
\]

Consequently the first member of the dichotomy (TL.1339) satisfies the
height-growing normalized loss bound

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 H'>0,quad \mathscr W_0\ge c_R\nu^2
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 {\nu\over H}\mathscr L_0
 &\ge c_R'\nu^5{m_H^2\over H}\\
 &\ge c_R'\nu^5{H\over M_0(0)^2}.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1343}
\]

The second member of (TL.1339) has, simultaneously,

\[
 \boxed{
 2\nu\mathfrak B_1
 \ge c_R'\nu^3M_0m_H^2
 \ge c_R'\nu^3{H^2\over M_0(0)}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1344}
\]

Equations (TL.1343)--(TL.1344) are the scale-sensitive loss half of the
temporal packing.  They do not compare separate mechanisms: they state that
the same positive critical tangent necessarily occupies one of two
simultaneous loss rows of the surrounding Sobolev/product tower, and the
corresponding loss grows with the height of that same state.  The remaining
q-time step is to retain the matching signed source of (TL.1328) or
(TL.1302), respectively, until these losses have been summed against their
already proved path-uniform current actions.

## 166. The doubly centered source has one matched cumulative-current action

The signed source in (TL.1328) is centered at the midpoint of the two
adjacent Sobolev means.  Put

\[
 c_0:={m_0+m_H\over2},
 \qquad
 \delta_0:=m_H-m_0,
\]

and retain the prefix and tail losses from (TL.1207).  Define

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak A_{\rm mid}
 &:=
 \int_0^{c_0}(c_0-k){\Pi(k)^2\over D_<(k)}\,dk
 +\int_{c_0}^\infty(k-c_0){\Pi(k)^2\over D_>(k)}\,dk,\\
 \mathfrak D_{\rm mid}
 &:=
 \int_0^{c_0}(c_0-k)D_<(k)\,dk
 +\int_{c_0}^\infty(k-c_0)D_>(k)\,dk.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1345}
\]

All zero denominators carry the zero convention.  Since

\[
 \mathscr S_0^{\rm gap}
 :=\int_0^\infty(k-m_0)(k-m_H)T(k)\,dk
 =2\int_0^\infty(k-c_0)\Pi(k)\,dk,
\]

one Cauchy inequality on the two joined sides of the same face gives

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathscr S_0^{\rm gap}|^2
 \le4\mathfrak A_{\rm mid}\mathfrak D_{\rm mid}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1346}
\]

The test loss is exact.  Fubini in the prefix and tail definitions yields

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathfrak D_{\rm mid}
 &=\nu\int_0^\infty k^2(k-c_0)^2e(k)\,dk\\
 &={\nu\over M_0}\mathscr L_0
 +{\nu\delta_0^2\over4}M_2,
 \end{aligned}
\]

because
\((k-c_0)^2=(k-m_0)(k-m_H)+\delta_0^2/4\).  Therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathfrak D_{\rm mid}
 ={\nu\over M_0}\mathscr L_0
 +{\nu\delta_0^2\over4}M_2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1347}
\]

Insert (TL.1346)--(TL.1347) into the exact law (TL.1325), split the two
positive members of \(\mathfrak D_{\rm mid}\), and apply Young only to the
member containing \(\mathscr L_0\).  This gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr W_0'
 +{\nu\over2H}\mathscr L_0
 \le {M_0\over2H}\mathfrak A_{\rm mid}
 +{\mathscr W_0\over H}
 \sqrt{\nu M_2\mathfrak A_{\rm mid}}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1348}
\]

Thus the temporal upper half now uses exactly the same midpoint-centered
prefix--tail current as the residual source, while the simultaneous lower
half is the positive loss already factorized in Section 165.  No full
nonlinear source norm and no independently weighted shell current appears.
The next signed q-descent must control the normalized history of
\(\mathfrak A_{\rm mid}\); (TL.1348) shows precisely how that one action
would absorb the height-growing loss in the adjacent-gap branch.

## 167. Orthogonal q-bands keep exchange, scale travel, and transverse VPI turning in one identity

The pointwise midpoint square in Section 166 is larger than the history which
the energy law pays: forming it before the signed q-faces have been resolved
restores an ultraviolet factor which is absent from the cumulative action.
The correction is to resolve the *velocity field itself* orthogonally and to
complete the VPI tangent only after its kinetic direction has been retained.

Fix \(q>1\), a translation \(\theta\in[0,\log q)\), and a smooth real radial
partition \(p_j\), localized to a fixed q-neighbourhood of
\(K_j=e^\theta q^j\), such that

\[
 \sum_{j\in\mathbb Z}p_j(k)^2=1\qquad(k>0).
\]

Write

\[
 P_j=p_j(\Lambda),\qquad
 u_j=P_ju,\qquad
 B_j=P_jB(u,u),
\]

and introduce the two band rows

\[
 \mathcal H_j={1\over2}\|\Lambda^{1/2}u_j\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 \mathcal E_j={1\over2}\|\Lambda u_j\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 \mu_j={\mathcal E_j\over\mathcal H_j}.
\]

When \(\mathcal H_j=0\), the reciprocal expressions below are read through
their displayed quadratic products and assigned their exact zero-band value.
Set

\[
 V_j=\Lambda^{1/2}u_j,
 \qquad
 W_j=\nu\Lambda^{3/2}u_j,
 \qquad
 Z_j=\Lambda^{-1/2}B_j,
\]

and retain the actual signed kinetic exchange

\[
 s_j=-\operatorname {Re}\langle B_j,u_j\rangle
     =-\operatorname {Re}\langle Z_j,V_j\rangle.
\]

Orthogonality of the partition and kinetic-energy cancellation give

\[
 \sum_j\mathcal H_j=H,
 \qquad
 \sum_j\mathcal E_j=M_2,
 \qquad
 \sum_js_j=-\operatorname {Re}\langle B(u,u),u\rangle=0.
\]

Remove only the component parallel to the occupied band velocity:

\[
 Z_j^\circ=Z_j+{s_j\over2\mathcal H_j}V_j,
 \qquad
 W_j^\circ=W_j-\nu\mu_jV_j.
\]

Then \(Z_j^\circ\perp V_j\) and \(W_j^\circ\perp V_j\).  The band critical
balance and one Hilbert-space square completion now give the exact law

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal H_j'
 =\mu_js_j-2\nu\mu_j^2\mathcal H_j
 +{1\over4\nu}\|Z_j^\circ\|_2^2
 -{1\over\nu}\left\|W_j^\circ+{1\over2}Z_j^\circ\right\|_2^2 .
 }
 \tag{TL.1349}
\]

Indeed,

\[
 \mathcal H_j'
 =-{1\over\nu}\operatorname {Re}\langle W_j,Z_j\rangle
  -{1\over\nu}\|W_j\|_2^2,
\]

and the two orthogonal decompositions above produce (TL.1349) without
discarding transport, pressure, geometry, or viscosity from the one tangent.

Put

\[
 r_j={s_j\over4\nu\mathcal H_j}.
\]

Since

\[
 \|Z_j\|_2^2
 =\|Z_j^\circ\|_2^2+{s_j^2\over2\mathcal H_j},
\]

summing (TL.1349) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 H'
 +2\nu\sum_j\mathcal H_j(\mu_j-r_j)^2
 +{1\over\nu}\sum_j
   \left\|W_j^\circ+{1\over2}Z_j^\circ\right\|_2^2
 ={1\over4\nu}\|\Lambda^{-1/2}B(u,u)\|_2^2 .
 }
 \tag{TL.1350}
\]

This square retains the signed inter-band motion: because \(\sum_js_j=0\),
the \(\mathcal H_j\)-mean of \(r_j\) is zero.  With

\[
 \bar\mu={M_2\over H},
\]

the first positive term therefore has the exact Pythagorean refinement

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j\mathcal H_j(\mu_j-r_j)^2
 =H\bar\mu^2
  +\sum_j\mathcal H_j(\mu_j-\bar\mu-r_j)^2 .
 }
 \tag{TL.1351}
\]

Thus the compulsory mean-scale loss and the fluctuation of actual q-band
travel are paid simultaneously; neither is manufactured by taking the
positive part of a shell flux.

The source side of (TL.1350) also has a datum-finite lower-order history.  Set

\[
 \mathfrak a_q(t)
 :={1\over H(t)}\sum_j{\|Z_j(t)\|_2^2\over K_j}.
\]

The fixed q-localization gives

\[
 \sum_j{\|Z_j\|_2^2\over K_j}
 \simeq_q\|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2.
\]

The critical product estimate (TL.1003) and the energy identity consequently
yield

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T\mathfrak a_q(t)\,dt
 \le C_q\int_0^T
 {\|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2\over H}\,dt
 \le {C_qM_0(0)\over\nu}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1352}
\]

Finally define the harmonic q-scale actually occupied by the complete source,

\[
 \kappa_Z(t)
 :={\sum_j\|Z_j\|_2^2
       \over\sum_jK_j^{-1}\|Z_j\|_2^2},
\]

with zero value when the source vanishes.  Dividing (TL.1350) by \(H\) and
using (TL.1351) gives the exact upper--lower junction

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 {H'\over H}
 &+2\nu\bar\mu^2
 +2\nu\sum_j{\mathcal H_j\over H}
      (\mu_j-\bar\mu-r_j)^2\\
 &+{1\over\nu H}\sum_j
      \left\|W_j^\circ+{1\over2}Z_j^\circ\right\|_2^2
 ={\kappa_Z\over4\nu}\,\mathfrak a_q .
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1353}
\]

The datum-finite quantity is now the actual negative-order action of the same
nonlinear VPI tangent, not the midpoint face-square.  Equation (TL.1353)
displays the source's harmonic q-scale \(\kappa_Z\), but it must not be
estimated after dropping the simultaneous transverse square: source motion
orthogonal to the critical spectral readout occurs on both sides and cancels.
The next q-descent therefore re-expands that exact difference into the two
components which can change \(H\): signed transfer between q-bands and signed
radial motion inside a q-band.  Their common faces are cancelled before either
component is selected positively.

## 168. Translation of the q-grid joins inter-band exchange and intra-band radial work

Return first to what the same fluid field can physically change.  At a fixed
frequency sphere, only the component of the complete nonlinear tangent along
the velocity already present on that sphere changes its kinetic energy.
Components orthogonal to that velocity change phase, direction, or incidence
geometry, but they do not change \(e(k,t)\) and therefore do not contribute to
the critical height.  The cancellation is exact in the q-band variables of
Section 167.

Define the actual critical production in the \(j\)-th smooth band by

\[
 \mathcal P_j
 :=-\operatorname {Re}\langle B_j,\Lambda u_j\rangle .
\]

The parallel--transverse decomposition used in (TL.1349) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal P_j
 =\mu_js_j-{1\over\nu}
   \operatorname {Re}\langle W_j^\circ,Z_j^\circ\rangle,
 \qquad
 \nu\|\Lambda^{3/2}u_j\|_2^2
 =2\nu\mu_j^2\mathcal H_j+{1\over\nu}\|W_j^\circ\|_2^2 .
 }
 \tag{TL.1354}
\]

In terms of the spherical transfer density of (TL.3), these are simply

\[
 s_j=\int_0^\infty p_j(k)^2T(k)\,dk,
 \qquad
 \mathcal P_j=\int_0^\infty k\,p_j(k)^2T(k)\,dk.
\]

Consequently the apparent transverse production in (TL.1354) is exactly the
centered radial work inside the occupied band:

\[
 \boxed{
 g_j:=\mathcal P_j-\mu_js_j
 =\int_0^\infty(k-\mu_j)p_j(k)^2T(k)\,dk
 =-{1\over\nu}\operatorname {Re}
   \langle W_j^\circ,Z_j^\circ\rangle .
 }
 \tag{TL.1355}
\]

Thus the full magnitude \(\|Z_j\|_2\) is not the source presented to \(H\).
Only its scalar projection in (TL.1355) survives; the remaining components
cancel between the two squares in (TL.1350).  Summing the partition gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j(\mu_js_j+g_j)
 =\int_0^\infty kT(k)\,dk
 =P_H,
 \qquad
 \sum_j\nu\|\Lambda^{3/2}u_j\|_2^2
 =2\nu M_3.
 }
 \tag{TL.1356}
\]

Hence (TL.1350) is exactly the original critical balance after an orthogonal
Pythagorean resolution; the harmonic source scale \(\kappa_Z\) is not an
independent proof object.

It remains to show that the two scalar terms \(\mu_js_j\) and \(g_j\) are not
two currents.  Use the translated sharp q-cells of Section 91.  On
\(I_j(r)=[K_j(r),K_{j+1}(r))\), put

\[
 s_j(r)=\int_{I_j(r)}T(k)\,dk,
 \qquad
 \mathcal P_j(r)=\int_{I_j(r)}kT(k)\,dk.
\]

Since \(T=-\partial_k\Pi\), one integration by parts gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 s_j(r)&=\Pi(K_j(r))-\Pi(K_{j+1}(r)),\\
 \mathcal P_j(r)
 &=K_j(r)\Pi(K_j(r))-K_{j+1}(r)\Pi(K_{j+1}(r))
   +\int_{I_j(r)}\Pi(k)\,dk.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1357}
\]

For any fixed translation, the two displayed q-faces cancel between adjacent
cells before a sign is chosen, and the cell interiors tile the positive
frequency axis.  Therefore

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j\mathcal P_j(r)=\int_0^\infty\Pi(k)\,dk=P_H.
 }
 \tag{TL.1358}
\]

Translation now turns every intra-cell radial displacement into an inter-cell
exchange on another member of the same q-grid family.  With
\(L=\log q\), the exact lower-face formula (TL.923) and the signed Abel law
(TL.1060) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 P_H
 &=\int_0^L\sum_jK_j(r)\Pi(K_j(r))\,dr\\
 &={1\over1-q^{-1}}
   \int_0^L\sum_jK_j(r)s_j(r)\,dr.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1359}
\]

Equation (TL.1359) is the required joining.  Signed transfer between q-cells
and signed radial motion within a particular cell are coordinate readings of
one scale current; translation of the same grid converts one reading into the
other.  There is therefore no transverse-source remainder and no second
current to estimate.  The remaining packing calculation acts on the single
cumulative face speed

\[
 v_j(r,t)={\Pi(K_j(r),t)\over\mathscr D_j^\uparrow(r,t)}
\]

already carrying the datum-finite unweighted action (TL.1004), while its
critical q-factor remains inside the signed recurrence (TL.1005).  The next
descent must combine that recurrence with the scale-coercive product loss
(TL.1305)--(TL.1313), rather than restoring a square of the discarded
transverse source.

## 169. The unique scale current has an exact throughput--remaining-lifetime law

The normalized action in (TL.1004) has a continuous form which identifies its
physical denominator.  Define the instantaneous dissipation density and the
dissipation still lying above the face \(k\) by

\[
 d_\nu(k,t):=2\nu k^2e(k,t),
 \qquad
 \mathcal L_\nu(k,t):=\int_k^\infty d_\nu(q,t)\,dq.
\]

On \(\{\mathcal L_\nu>0\}\), put

\[
 v(k,t):={\Pi(k,t)\over\mathcal L_\nu(k,t)},
\]

and use the zero value elsewhere.  The continuous weighted Copson inequality,
followed by Cauchy--Schwarz on each Fourier sphere, gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathcal Q_0(t)
 &:=\int_0^\infty d_\nu(k,t)v(k,t)^2\,dk\\
 &\le4\int_0^\infty{T(k,t)^2\over d_\nu(k,t)}\,dk
 \le {4\over\nu}\|\Lambda^{-1}B(u,u)\|_2^2.
 \end{aligned}
\]

Using (TL.1003) and the kinetic-energy identity therefore yields

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_0^T{\mathcal Q_0(t)\over H(t)}\,dt
 \le {C M_0(0)\over\nu^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1360}
\]

This is the datum-paid speed of the same signed scale current.  Its
denominator is not an independently assigned loss: \(\mathcal L_\nu(k,t)\)
is precisely the remaining viscous lifetime density of the velocity already
present above that same face.

Fubini supplies the other half without an estimate:

\[
 \int_0^\infty\mathcal L_\nu(k,t)\,dk
 =\int_0^\infty k\,d_\nu(k,t)\,dk
 =2\nu M_3(t).
\]

Together with \(P_H=\int_0^\infty\Pi\,dk\), the critical balance becomes

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)
 =\int_0^\infty
 \mathcal L_\nu(k,t)\,[v(k,t)-1]\,dk.
 }
 \tag{TL.1361}
\]

Equations (TL.1360)--(TL.1361) are one throughput--lifetime law.  The first
measures the squared speed of the signed current against the loss actually
available at its face; the second says that critical height rises only where
that same current outruns the complete dissipation tail.  No event ledger and
no second fluid quantity have entered.

The exact q-adic version shows the single factor still to be joined to the
existing product receiver.  For the translated cells of Section 91 write

\[
 h_j:={D_j\over\mathscr D_j^\uparrow},
 \qquad
 v_j={\Pi_j\over\mathscr D_j^\uparrow}.
\]

Here \(h_j\in[0,1]\) is the fraction of the remaining tail loss situated in
the first receiving cell.  For every reference frequency \(\lambda>0\), the
scalar square

\[
 x-1\le ax^2+{1\over4a}-1
 \qquad(a>0)
\]

with \(a=\lambda h_j/(4K_j)\) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 K_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow(v_j-1)
 \le{}&{\lambda\over4}D_jv_j^2\\
 &+K_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow
 \left({K_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow\over\lambda D_j}-1\right).
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1362}
\]

Zero-loss cells are understood by monotone regularization.  After retaining
only the positive part of the second line, summing (TL.1362) in the exact
signed recurrence (TL.1005), with

\[
 \mathcal Q_0(t;r):=\sum_jD_j(t;r)v_j(t;r)^2,
\]

gives

\[
 \boxed{
 A_q'(t;r)
 \le {1-q^{-1}\over4}\lambda\mathcal Q_0(t;r)
 +(1-q^{-1})\mathcal R_\lambda(t;r),
 }
 \tag{TL.1363}
\]

where

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathcal R_\lambda
 :=\sum_jK_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow
 \left({K_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow\over\lambda D_j}-1\right)_+.
 }
 \tag{TL.1364}
\]

The first term in (TL.1363) is the already datum-paid current speed, with no
ultraviolet source norm.  The second is not a generic missing weight.  It has
one literal meaning: the critical face \(K_j\) is carrying a dissipation tail
whose frequency reach \(K_j\mathscr D_j^\uparrow/D_j\) exceeds the chosen
reference \(\lambda\).  The cellwise positive sum in (TL.1364) is not itself
the final action: an empty intermediate cell makes its reciprocal loss
singular and would recount one transfer across several faces.  It identifies
the exact branch which must next be collapsed while still signed.  Maximal
q-blocks are therefore to be grouped by the diffuse multi-cell alternative
(TL.934)--(TL.936) or the remote coarse--fine product population
(TL.1308)--(TL.1313); only the exterior current of each completed block may
then be selected positively.

## 170. Every signed transfer is assigned once to its donor--receiver q-span

The last sentence of Section 169 must be implemented before
\(\mathcal R_\lambda\) is used.  In fact the cellwise reciprocal-loss
remainder is not needed.  The complete trilinear exchange already assigns
every transfer to one donor--receiver span without reference to the cells
between them.

Retain the orthogonal q-cells and the signed triadic exchange
\(T_{j\leftarrow\ell}^{\,k}\) from (TL.1057).  For \(a<b\), set

\[
 \mathcal T_{b,a}:=\sum_kT_{b\leftarrow a}^{\,k}.
\]

The antisymmetry
\(T_{b\leftarrow a}^{\,k}=-T_{a\leftarrow b}^{\,k}\) gives the exact
endpoint-span decomposition

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_jK_js_j
 =\sum_{a<b}(K_b-K_a)\mathcal T_{b,a}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1365}
\]

No absolute value occurs in (TL.1365).  A transfer crossing many q-faces
appears once, with its actual endpoint distance \(K_b-K_a\); an empty cell
between the endpoints creates neither a denominator nor an additional
crossing.  Equation (TL.1365) is the completed-block version of the Abel law
(TL.1060), so the diagnostic remainder \(\mathcal R_\lambda\) in (TL.1364)
is not used as a proof action.

The Fourier support of the same triad makes its physical alternatives
exhaustive.  Suppose \(T_{b\leftarrow a}^{\,k}\ne0\) and

\[
 K_b\ge2qK_a.
\]

For interacting frequencies
\(\xi_b+\xi_a+\xi_k=0\), the cell supports give

\[
 |\xi_b|\ge K_b,
 \qquad
 |\xi_a|<qK_a\le{K_b\over2}.
\]

The triangle inequality therefore forces

\[
 \boxed{
 {K_b\over2}\le|\xi_k|\le(q+\tfrac12)K_b,
 \qquad
 {K_b\over2q}<K_k<(q+\tfrac12)K_b.
 }
 \tag{TL.1366}
\]

Thus a remote donor--receiver span cannot be carried by one isolated high
mode or by two wholly coarse modes.  It contains one coarse endpoint and two
comparable high-frequency legs of the same velocity field.  Conversely, if
\(K_b<2qK_a\), the donor and receiver lie in a fixed finite q-neighbourhood.
Define accordingly

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 \mathscr P_q^{\rm near}
 &:=\sum_{\substack{a<b\\K_b<2qK_a}}
   (K_b-K_a)\mathcal T_{b,a},\\
 \mathscr P_q^{\rm remote}
 &:=\sum_{\substack{a<b\\K_b\ge2qK_a}}
   (K_b-K_a)\mathcal T_{b,a}.
 \end{aligned}
\]

Then

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_jK_js_j
 =\mathscr P_q^{\rm near}+\mathscr P_q^{\rm remote},
 }
 \tag{TL.1367}
\]

and the two terms have different carrier-native contractions.  The near term
is a finite-width q-current and keeps the cumulative same-face action of
(TL.1002)--(TL.1004).  Every summand of the remote term has exactly the
coarse--fine/high--high geometry admitted by the positive two-frequency
carrier in (TL.1193), with its simultaneous loss and coordinate current in
(TL.1199)--(TL.1202).  This is not an analogy: (TL.1366) proves that the
third leg of every remote exchange lies in a fixed enlargement of the fine
endpoint.

Translation averaging preserves the split and reconstructs the exact
critical work:

\[
 \boxed{
 P_H(t)
 ={1\over1-q^{-1}}
  \int_0^{\log q}
  \bigl(\mathscr P_q^{\rm near}(r,t)
       +\mathscr P_q^{\rm remote}(r,t)\bigr)\,dr.
 }
 \tag{TL.1368}
\]

The next contraction can therefore be made without cellwise reach, refill,
or gap terms.  It must sum the near finite-span exchanges against the local
loss first, and sum the remote exchanges on the source-free coarse--fine
product current first; only their two resulting exterior contributions may
enter the positive critical-height recurrence.

## 171. Quadratic span decay absorbs every sufficiently remote triad

The remote geometry in (TL.1366) gives more than an admission statement.
Because the advecting and receiving legs are both at the fine endpoint, the
derivative in \(T_{b\leftarrow a}^{\,k}=-b(u_k,u_a,u_b)\) falls on the
coarse leg.  This produces a strict scale-ratio factor against the
simultaneous fine-scale loss.

Put

\[
 a_j:=K_jE_j={K_j\over2}\|u_j\|_2^2,
 \qquad
 M_q:=\sup_j a_j,
\]

and define the critical viscous loss in one cell by

\[
 \mathcal V_j
 :=2\nu\int_{K_j}^{K_{j+1}}k^3e(k)\,dk
 \simeq_q \nu K_j^3\|u_j\|_2^2.
\]

Bernstein on the coarse q-cell gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \|\nabla u_a\|_\infty
 \le C_qK_a^{5/2}\|u_a\|_2
 \le C_qK_a^2\sqrt{a_a}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1369}
\]

For a remote span \(K_b\ge2qK_a\), (TL.1366) restricts every nonzero
advecting index \(k\) to a fixed q-neighbourhood \(\mathcal N_q(b)\) of
\(b\).  Hence

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 |(K_b-K_a)T_{b\leftarrow a}^{\,k}|
 &\le C_qK_b\|\nabla u_a\|_\infty
        \|u_k\|_2\|u_b\|_2\\
 &\le {C_q\|\nabla u_a\|_\infty\over\nu K_b^2}
       \bigl(\mathcal V_k+\mathcal V_b\bigr).
 \end{aligned}
\]

Using (TL.1369) yields the scale-defective bound

\[
 \boxed{
 |(K_b-K_a)T_{b\leftarrow a}^{\,k}|
 \le {C_q\sqrt{a_a}\over\nu}
      \left({K_a\over K_b}\right)^2
      \bigl(\mathcal V_k+\mathcal V_b\bigr).
 }
 \tag{TL.1370}
\]

This is a same-triad estimate: the coarse deformation rate, both fine legs,
and their viscous loss have not been separated into independently evolving
objects.

For an integer \(m\) greater than the fixed support threshold in
(TL.1366), let

\[
 \mathscr P_q^{\mathrm{far}(m)}
 :=\sum_{\substack{a<b\\b-a\ge m}}
   (K_b-K_a)\mathcal T_{b,a}.
\]

The finite overlap of \(\mathcal N_q(b)\), (TL.1370), and the geometric
series in \(q^{-2(b-a)}\) give

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathscr P_q^{\mathrm{far}(m)}|
 \le {C_q\sqrt{M_q}\over\nu}\,q^{-2m}
      \sum_j\mathcal V_j
 ={2C_q\sqrt{M_q}\over\nu}\,q^{-2m}\,
      \nu M_3.
 }
 \tag{TL.1371}
\]

Given \(0<\varepsilon<1\), choose the measurable integer

\[
 m_*(t)
 :=\max\!\left\{
 m_q^{\mathrm{tri}},
 \left\lceil
 {1\over2\log q}
 \log_+\!\left({C_q\sqrt{M_q(t)}\over\varepsilon\nu}\right)
 \right\rceil
 \right\},
\]

where \(m_q^{\mathrm{tri}}\) is any fixed index width containing the
support range in (TL.1366).  Then

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathscr P_q^{\mathrm{far}(m_*(t))}(t)|
 \le\varepsilon\sum_j\mathcal V_j(t)
 =2\varepsilon\nu M_3(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1372}
\]

Thus no arbitrarily remote exchange remains in the positive critical
balance.  Every span beyond \(m_*(t)\) is absorbed by the simultaneous loss
of its two compulsory fine legs.  The surviving nonlinear work is confined
to endpoint spans

\[
 0<b-a<m_*(t),
\]

whose width grows only logarithmically with the actual critical q-envelope.
The next signed sum must contract this finite-span current before its
time-dependent width is estimated; replacing the remaining spans by
\(m_*(t)\) copies would reintroduce the face recounting removed in
(TL.1365).

## 172. The surviving finite-span current is governed by the critical q-envelope

The finite spans are summed as one commutator, not estimated one endpoint
pair at a time.  Define

\[
 \beta_q(t):=\sup_jK_j^{1/2}\|u_j(t)\|_2
 =\sqrt{2M_q(t)}.
\]

With \(\Lambda_q=\sum_jK_jP_j\), kinetic-energy orthogonality and
skew-adjointness of \(A_u:=u\cdot\nabla\) give

\[
 \boxed{
 \mathscr P_q
 :=\sum_jK_js_j
 =-\operatorname {Re}\langle A_uu,\Lambda_qu\rangle
 =-\frac12\operatorname {Re}
   \langle[\Lambda_q,A_u]u,u\rangle.
 }
 \tag{TL.1373}
\]

This commutator is the complete signed sum in (TL.1365).  A direct
Littlewood--Paley paraproduct calculation gives

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathscr P_q(t)|
 \le C_q\beta_q(t)
       \sum_jK_j^3\|u_j(t)\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1374}
\]

For completeness, the scale factors in (TL.1374) come from the same triad
geometry already used above.  In a low--high interaction the common
advection cancels in (TL.1373); the multiplier difference leaves
\(K_j\|\nabla u_{<j}\|_\infty\|u_j\|_2^2\), and

\[
 \|\nabla u_{<j}\|_\infty
 \le C_q\sum_{\ell<j}K_\ell^{5/2}\|u_\ell\|_2
 \le C_q\beta_qK_j^2.
\]

The high--high and neighboring high--low sums have two comparable largest
frequencies and obey the same bound after Cauchy--Schwarz; their finite
overlap is absorbed into \(C_q\).  Thus (TL.1374) is obtained after the
antisymmetric q-sum, not by replacing each triad with its magnitude.

Since

\[
 \sum_jK_j^3\|u_j\|_2^2\simeq_q2M_3,
\]

translation averaging in (TL.1368) gives

\[
 \boxed{
 |P_H(t)|\le C_q\sqrt{M_q(t)}\,M_3(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1375}
\]

Combining this with the exact critical balance yields

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)
 +\bigl(2\nu-C_q\sqrt{M_q(t)}\bigr)M_3(t)
 \le0.
 }
 \tag{TL.1376}
\]

In particular,

\[
 \boxed{
 H'(t)>0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 M_q(t)>{4\nu^2\over C_q^2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1377}
\]

The q-span calculation has therefore removed both false possibilities:
positive critical work cannot be supplied by an empty scale gap, and it
cannot be supplied by arbitrarily remote endpoints without paying the
simultaneous fine loss.  After all signed spans are recombined, the only
coefficient capable of reversing the critical loss is the actual
concentrated q-band amplitude \(M_q\) of the same field.  The complementary
distributed part remains governed by (TL.936),

\[
 \int_0^T H(t)^2
 \exp\!\left\{c_q{H(t)\over M_q(t)}\right\}dt
 \le {C_qM_0(0)^2\over\nu}.
\]

The next temporal descent must therefore act on the moving maximizer of
\(M_q\) together with its complementary signed donor current.  Estimating
the maximizing band alone would discard the cancellation in (TL.1373) and
recreate an isolated-packet amplifier.

## 173. The moving q-envelope is the limit of one signed all-band law

The maximizer is now retained without selecting a band and then forgetting
its complement.  Write \(L=\log q\), let

\[
 K_j(r)=e^rq^j,
 \qquad 0\le r<L,
\]

and use the sharp orthogonal q-cells on every translated grid.  Set

\[
 a_j(r,t):=K_j(r)E_j(r,t),
 \qquad
 \ell_j(r,t):=K_j(r)D_j(r,t).
\]

The exact band equation is

\[
 a_j'+\ell_j=K_js_j.
\]

For \(1<p<\infty\), define the all-grid power mean

\[
 \Phi_p(t):={1\over L}\int_0^L\sum_j a_j(r,t)^p\,dr,
 \qquad
 \mathcal M_p(t):=\Phi_p(t)^{1/p}.
\]

On every compact preterminal interval the smooth solution gives uniform
decay of the cells at both scale faces.  The following differentiation and
all rearrangements are therefore justified first on finite scale
truncations and then by dominated convergence:

\[
 \boxed{
 {1\over p}\Phi_p'
 +{1\over L}\int_0^L\sum_j a_j^{p-1}\ell_j\,dr
 ={1\over L}\int_0^L\sum_j a_j^{p-1}K_js_j\,dr.
 }
 \tag{TL.1378}
\]

When \(\Phi_p>0\), introduce only the probability reading

\[
 d\Theta_p(r,j)
 :={a_j(r,t)^p\over L\Phi_p(t)}\,dr.
\]

Since \(a_j=K_jE_j\) and \(\ell_j=K_jD_j\), (TL.1378) is equivalently

\[
 \boxed{
 (\log\mathcal M_p)'
 +\int {D_j\over E_j}\,d\Theta_p
 =\int {s_j\over E_j}\,d\Theta_p,
 \qquad
 {D_j\over E_j}\ge2\nu K_j^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1379}
\]

Zero cells contribute zero, by approximation.  Thus the two terms in
(TL.1379) are the simultaneous relative gain and relative loss of the same
q-band population.  They have not been formed from separately normalized
fields.

Most importantly, the source in (TL.1378) is still the complete signed
donor--receiver exchange.  The antisymmetry used in (TL.1365), now with the
time-dependent endpoint value \(K_ja_j^{p-1}\), gives

\[
 \boxed{
 \begin{aligned}
 &{1\over L}\int_0^L\sum_j a_j^{p-1}K_js_j\,dr\\
 &\quad={1\over L}\int_0^L
 \sum_{a<b}
 \bigl(K_ba_b^{p-1}-K_aa_a^{p-1}\bigr)
 \mathcal T_{b,a}\,dr.
 \end{aligned}
 }
 \tag{TL.1380}
\]

There is an equivalent shared-face form.  With

\[
 F_j:=K_j\Pi_j,
\]

the identity \(s_j=\Pi_j-\Pi_{j+1}\) and
\(K_{j-1}=q^{-1}K_j\) yield

\[
 \boxed{
 \sum_j a_j^{p-1}K_js_j
 =\sum_j
 \bigl(a_j^{p-1}-q^{-1}a_{j-1}^{p-1}\bigr)F_j.
 }
 \tag{TL.1381}
\]

Equations (TL.1380)--(TL.1381) prevent a change of maximizing band from
becoming a reset: before any maximum is taken, every gain retains the
oppositely signed endpoint of the same transfer, and every shared q-face
occurs once.

The power means converge to the true grid-independent q-envelope:

\[
 \boxed{
 \lim_{p\to\infty}\mathcal M_p(t)
 =\sup_{0\le r<L,\,j\in\mathbb Z}a_j(r,t)
 =M_q(t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1382}
\]

Indeed \((r,j)\mapsto a_j(r,t)\) belongs to
\(L^1([0,L)\times\mathbb Z)\cap L^\infty\), and its supremum is attained
away from the two scale faces whenever the field is nonzero.  On compact
preterminal intervals the same uniform face decay makes \(M_q\) locally
Lipschitz.  At almost every differentiability time, every weak limit
\(\Theta_\infty\) of \(\Theta_p\) is supported on

\[
 \mathcal A_q(t):=\{(r,j):a_j(r,t)=M_q(t)\},
\]

and (TL.1379) passes to the combined active-envelope law

\[
 \boxed{
 (\log M_q)'
 +\int_{\mathcal A_q}{D_j\over E_j}\,d\Theta_\infty
 =\int_{\mathcal A_q}{s_j\over E_j}\,d\Theta_\infty.
 }
 \tag{TL.1383}
\]

This limit is taken only after the signed sums (TL.1380)--(TL.1381); it does
not assert that an independently selected band evolves on its own.

The finite initial kinetic row already makes the active loss quantitative.
On \(\mathcal A_q(t)\),

\[
 M_q=K_jE_j,
 \qquad E_j\le E_0(t),
 \qquad K_j\ge{M_q\over E_0(t)}.
\]

Consequently (TL.1383) contains the datum-linked lower loss

\[
 \boxed{
 \int_{\mathcal A_q}{D_j\over E_j}\,d\Theta_\infty
 \ge2\nu\left({M_q\over E_0(t)}\right)^2
 \ge2\nu\left({M_q\over E_0(0)}\right)^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1384}
\]

Finally, the scalar inequality \(x-y\le x^2/(4y)\), (TL.1001), and
(TL.1379) give before the envelope limit

\[
 \boxed{
 [\partial_t\log\mathcal M_p]_+
 \le {1\over4\nu L\Phi_p}
 \int_0^L\sum_jK_ja_j^{p-1}z_j\,dr,
 \qquad
 z_j=\|\Lambda^{-1}B_j\|_2^2.
 }
 \tag{TL.1385}
\]

The gain capable of overcoming (TL.1384) is therefore no longer an
unspecified temporal spike.  It is the critically travelled negative-order
source of the same active q-population.  The unweighted source action
\(\sum K_j^{-1}z_j/H\) is datum-finite by (TL.1352); the two powers of the
active scale separating it from (TL.1385) are exactly the reciprocal-pair
distance already represented by (TL.1053)--(TL.1055) and the finite-reach
law (TL.1018).  The next contraction must insert that existing
scale--separation disintegration into (TL.1385) before taking
\(p\to\infty\), so the active q-weight and its material travel remain one
quantity rather than a new ultraviolet trace.

## 174. Remote spans remain absorbable after the moving-envelope weight is attached

The endpoint weight in (TL.1380) does not destroy the remote-span decay of
Section 171.  Fix one translated grid, put

\[
 w_{p,j}:=K_ja_j^{p-1},
\]

and let \(\mathfrak S_{p}^{\rm far(m)}\) denote the part of the signed sum
in (TL.1380) with \(b-a\ge m\).  For one such span write

\[
 R:={K_b\over K_a}=q^{b-a}.
\]

Since \(R\ge2q\) on the remote range,

\[
 \boxed{
 { |w_{p,b}-w_{p,a}|\over K_b-K_a}
 \le C_q\bigl(a_b^{p-1}+R^{-1}a_a^{p-1}\bigr).
 }
 \tag{TL.1386}
\]

Combine (TL.1386) with the same-triad estimate (TL.1370).  The third index
\(k\) remains in the fixed q-neighbourhood \(\mathcal N_q(b)\), so

\[
 \mathcal V_k\simeq_q\nu K_b^2a_k,
 \qquad
 \mathcal V_b\simeq_q\nu K_b^2a_b,
 \qquad
 \ell_j\simeq_q\mathcal V_j.
\]

For \(x,z\ge0\) and \(p>1\), Young's inequality in the form

\[
 x^{p-1}z
 \le {p-1\over p}x^p+{1\over p}z^p
\]

assigns every high--high cross term to the weighted loss of one of its two
actual high legs.  The term containing the coarse endpoint has the extra
factor \(R^{-1}\) in (TL.1386); after the same Young inequality it is
assigned either to the coarse weighted loss or to the corresponding high
weighted loss.  Consequently, with a constant independent of \(p\),

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 &\sum_{k\in\mathcal N_q(b)}
 |(w_{p,b}-w_{p,a})T_{b\leftarrow a}^{\,k}|\\
 &\quad\le
 {C_q\sqrt{M_q}\over\nu}
 \left[
 R^{-2}\sum_{k\in\mathcal N_q(b)}a_k^{p-1}\ell_k
 +R^{-1}a_a^{p-1}\ell_a
 \right].
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1387}
\]

The first term is summed using the finite overlap of
\(\mathcal N_q(b)\); the second is summed using the geometric series in
\(R^{-1}=q^{-(b-a)}\).  This proves the envelope-weighted remote
contraction

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathfrak S_p^{\rm far(m)}(r,t)|
 \le {C_q\sqrt{M_q(t)}\over\nu}\,q^{-m}
 \sum_j a_j(r,t)^{p-1}\ell_j(r,t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1388}
\]

It is uniform in the grid translation and therefore remains true after the
\(r\)-integration in (TL.1378).  Given \(0<\varepsilon<1\), choose

\[
 \boxed{
 m_\varepsilon(t)
 :=\max\!\left\{
 m_q^{\rm tri},
 \left\lceil
 \log_q^+\!\left({C_q\sqrt{M_q(t)}\over\varepsilon\nu}\right)
 \right\rceil
 \right\}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1389}
\]

Then

\[
 \boxed{
 |\mathfrak S_p^{\rm far(m_\varepsilon)}(r,t)|
 \le\varepsilon
 \sum_j a_j(r,t)^{p-1}\ell_j(r,t).
 }
 \tag{TL.1390}
\]

Thus the smooth moving-envelope law retains the earlier remote absorption:
arbitrarily separated endpoints do not produce a new positive term when
the maximum moves.  After (TL.1390), every possible positive envelope
tangent is carried by the single finite q-neighbourhood

\[
 0<b-a<m_\varepsilon(t)
\]

of the active all-band weight.  This remaining term is still kept as the
one signed commutator

\[
 -\frac12\operatorname {Re}
 \langle[\Lambda_{p,r}(t),u\cdot\nabla]u,u\rangle,
 \qquad
 \Lambda_{p,r}(t):=\sum_jK_j(r)a_j(r,t)^{p-1}P_j,
\]

with only the spans below \(m_\varepsilon\) retained.  Its next contraction
must use that an active band has zero self-source and therefore receives
through its actual finite-neighbour complement; replacing that complement
by \(m_\varepsilon\) unsigned copies would lose the cancellation just
preserved in (TL.1380).

## 175. A fixed coarse deformation cannot drive an unbounded q-envelope

The finite-span commutator still contains the possibility that two
neighboring fine bands are transported by a much coarser third triad leg.
The common coarse transport cancels by incompressibility; only deformation
of that coarse leg remains.  This makes its contribution strictly
subquadratic in the active frequency.

Let \(a_j=M_q\) be an active q-cell with lower face \(K=K_j\), and let
\(u_{\le L}=P_{\{k\le L\}}u\), where \(L<K/(4q)\).  Denote by
\(s_j^{\le L}\) the complete portion of the band source whose third triad
leg lies in \(u_{\le L}\).  Fourier support confines the other high leg to
a fixed q-neighbourhood of \(j\).  Commuting the fixed band projection
through \(u_{\le L}\cdot\nabla\), the uncommuted term vanishes, and the
standard kernel commutator bound gives

\[
 \begin{aligned}
 |s_j^{\le L}|
 &\le C_q\|\nabla u_{\le L}\|_\infty
       \|u_{\sim j}\|_2\|u_j\|_2,\\
 { |s_j^{\le L}|\over E_j}
 &\le C_q\|\nabla u_{\le L}\|_\infty.
 \end{aligned}
 \tag{TL.1391}
\]

The second line uses only that every cell in the fixed high-frequency
neighbourhood obeys \(K_\ell E_\ell\le M_q=K_jE_j\).  No low mode has
been treated as an external velocity field.

Bernstein and kinetic-energy orthogonality give the datum estimate

\[
 \boxed{
 \|\nabla u_{\le L}(t)\|_\infty
 \le C L^{5/2}\|u(t)\|_2
 \le C E_0(0)^{1/2}L^{5/2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1392}
\]

For \(0<\varepsilon<1\), set

\[
 L_\varepsilon(K)
 :=\min\!\left\{
 {K\over4q},
 \left({\varepsilon\nu K^2\over C_qE_0(0)^{1/2}}\right)^{2/5}
 \right\}.
\]

Equations (TL.1391)--(TL.1392) then imply

\[
 \boxed{
 { |s_j^{\le L_\varepsilon}|\over E_j}
 \le\varepsilon\nu K_j^2
 \le {\varepsilon\over2}{D_j\over E_j}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1393}
\]

This estimate is uniform over the active set in (TL.1383), so it may be
averaged against \(\Theta_\infty\) after the signed donor--receiver sum has
been formed.  Since every active cell also satisfies
\(K_j\ge M_q/E_0(0)\), the nontrivial branch of the cutoff obeys

\[
 \boxed{
 L_\varepsilon(K_j)
 \ge c_{q,\varepsilon}
 {\nu^{2/5}\over E_0(0)}M_q^{4/5}
 \qquad(M_q\ \hbox{sufficiently large}).
 }
 \tag{TL.1394}
\]

Therefore a fixed finite-frequency part of the initial motion cannot remain
the deformation leg of an unbounded envelope tangent.  After the remote
endpoint absorption (TL.1390), the second endpoint of every surviving span
also satisfies, for sufficiently large \(M_q\),

\[
 \boxed{
 K_{\rm other}
 \ge q^{-m_\varepsilon}K_j
 \ge c_{q,\varepsilon}
 {\nu\over E_0(0)}M_q^{1/2}.
 }
 \tag{TL.1395}
\]

Together with the coarse-leg absorption (TL.1393)--(TL.1394), this proves
that every leg of a surviving source triad tends to infinite frequency with
the active envelope.  The next q-adic contraction can
accordingly be made on the moving ultraviolet *triad population* itself:
the initial smooth tail vanishes there only asymptotically, not identically.
The exact fixed-band heat law (TL.939) and
mpp-vpi-q-envelope-chord-envelope-equivalence-and-terminal-ancestry-20260722.md
show that any nonvanishing terminal population is first *excess over the
heat-surviving earlier tail*. That excess is necessarily the heat-filtered
signed incoming current of the same history rather than a reusable coarse
background. The normalized current law in
mpp-vpi-all-high-signed-current-efficiency-and-unparking-regate-20260722.md
shows that the oriented efficiency needed to beat viscosity is only
\(O(\nu/\sqrt{M_q})\). It follows that phase nonparking alone is not the
missing contraction: the next theorem must control the signed positive
time integral or route it into simultaneous reverse transfer or loss.
